# JewelPrice Pro — Project Context

## What this is
JewelPrice Pro is a bilingual (Greek/English) jewelry pricing Progressive Web App for Lekkas Jewelry, a seasonal jewelry store in Karpathos, Greece. It is used daily in the store by the owner (Sotiris) and an employee.

## Critical technical constraints — never violate these
- The entire app lives in a **single file: `jewelprice.htm`**. Do NOT split it into multiple files, and do NOT create or edit `index.htm` (the file was renamed from index.htm; old references to that name are outdated).
- Hosted on **GitHub Pages**. The app is accessed via its full URL ending in `/jewelprice.htm` — never break or rename this path, as users have it bookmarked/installed.
- Backend: **Firebase Realtime Database** (migrated from JSONBin and jsonstorage.net — do not reintroduce those).
- All UI text must exist in **both Greek and English**, following the app's existing i18n pattern.
- Greek characters CANNOT be encoded in QR codes generated for BarTender UltraLite (SHIFT-JIS limitation). Label codes therefore use parallel Greek/Latin codes.
- QR camera scanning prefers the browser's native **BarcodeDetector** and falls back to an **inlined jsQR** decoder (in a `<script id="jsqr-lib">` block) for desktop browsers that lack it (Chrome on Windows/Linux). `canDecodeQr()` is true where QR decoding works and false on Safari/iOS.
- **Text (OCR) scanning** (v10.6): a "Scan code text" toggle inside the scanner overlay (`toggleScanMode`) reads the **printed reference code** (e.g. `PE0142`, from `formatSeqId`) instead of the QR square — the fallback for sun-faded/scratched tags that killed the old attempt (which tried to OCR the free-form Greek code `ΚΑΛ18Σ4.5`). Uses **Tesseract.js**, lazy-loaded from CDN on first use (`loadTesseract`) and cached in IndexedDB (needs internet the first time only). The engine runs with a narrow char whitelist (`BCEGHKLNOPRTWX0123456789`) + single-line PSM; `extractRefCode` pulls a `[A-Z]{2}\d{4}` token, applies position-aware confusion fixes (O↔0, B↔8, etc.) and validates the 2-letter prefix against `REF_PREFIXES`. The live path crops a **wide, short strip** across the centre whose height scales with the frame **width** (not height) so it stays a tight one-line strip in any orientation — a fixed fraction of frame *height* produced a tall, sparse crop on portrait phones that single-line OCR couldn't read — and it OCRs that strip at a few normalised heights, taking the first valid read (text size varies with how close the tag is held); gallery/photo OCRs the whole image. The live loop accepts a code as soon as one frame reads a valid token that **matches a real inventory item** (`refCodeExists`) — the existence check rejects misreads into non-existent codes, and `lookupCode` then shows the item so a wrong read is caught by eye. (An earlier two-frame agreement gate was removed in v10.7: it caused a ~2s delay and could get stuck showing "Reading…" forever if the user moved the tag away before the second confirming frame.) Text mode shows a dashed **read-zone guide band** (`.qr-textguide`) marking where to place the code, and the frame turns **green** (`.qr-video-wrap.aligned`) the moment a valid code is readable — a live "aligned, hold still" signal driven by OCR success itself (no tilt sensor; readability *is* alignment). Result is fed to `handleScannedCode` → `lookupCode`, which already resolves ref codes to items. Because the Calc scanner now always has a working path (QR where possible, else OCR), the Calc scan icon **shows on all devices including iOS/Safari** — where it opens straight into text mode. **Inventory scanning stays QR-only** (its confirm flow parses QR, not ref codes), so the text toggle is hidden there.
- Users are on mobile: employee uses a Xiaomi Redmi (Android/Chrome), another user is on iPhone (Safari). Test logic must account for both.

## Key features (current — verified against jewelprice.htm at app v10.87)
The app has grown well past the v2.2 list. It is organized into navigable tabs/panels:
**Calc, Inventory, Vendors, Clients, Sales, Sync, Settings, About** (some hidden until enabled/owner mode).

### Pricing & calculation (Calc panel)
- Live gold & silver spot prices in € (auto-refresh ~every 5 min) with a mini price chart; warning + manual entry fallback when the live price can't be fetched; works offline with last saved prices
- Price calculation from metal / weight / karat / vendor + labor category
- Weight-based margin tiers (up to 5) and per-labor-category margin overrides
- Handmade toggle (indicator shown ONLY when active)
- Secondary metal support and a generic "other cost" pass-through
- Stone/gemstone rows with AI price estimation (dealer / wholesale / retail values), a stone-price cache (with a settings-panel manager), and support for many stone types (e.g. Labradorite as of v9.90)
- Discount % per transaction; price rounding (works for gold/silver and Other-type items)
- Customer-facing final price bar (no cost details); breakdown view for owner

### Labels, codes & scanning
- Label code system with parallel Greek/Latin codes for BarTender UltraLite (SHIFT-JIS constraint)
- QR camera scanning with automatic calculation — native BarcodeDetector on Android/Chrome, with an inlined jsQR fallback so it also works on desktop Chrome (Windows/Linux); can also scan a photo of a tag.
- Text (OCR) scanning of the printed reference code (e.g. `PE0142`) as a fallback for faded/damaged QR squares (Tesseract.js, toggle in the scanner). Works on all devices — including iPhone/Safari, which now gets a working camera scan path for the first time.
- Label history; printable code reference card (the old Settings "Duplicate QR Check" scan/fix tool was removed in v10.61 — it flagged the now-intentional shared QRs of coins/similar-items and its "Fix" button mutated weights; the save-time duplicate-QR guard was itself removed in v11.32 — see below)

### Quotes, sales & inventory
- Quote cart (multi-item quotes) with rounding; single/multi A4 print quotes with optional jewelry photo
- Quote history (searchable) and certificate generation
- Inventory management with type/stone filters; sold flow and sale modals
- Bulk chain ("by the gram") sale flow: sells part of a chain batch by weight, deducting from the remaining grams; a rounding-target field sets the exact final price (the entered amount is honoured exactly, discount % is derived from it for display only)
- Sales tracking: sales dashboard, sales search, and a sales leaderboard by employee (monthly drill-down, medal ranking)
- Multi-item sales (whole-cart checkout) are tagged with a shared `saleGroup` id — one record per item is still stored (so all totals/profit/leaderboards stay exact), but the sales list, hero counts, and search collapse them into ONE transaction row ("🛍 N items" badge). Tapping opens a combined detail listing each item (each openable on its own via `showSaleDetail(id, true)`); deleting removes the whole group. Grouping is done at display time by `_groupSalesForDisplay()` (v10.0). The combined view has a whole-transaction "💵 CASH" toggle next to the total (`_toggleGroupCash` → `sdmGroupCashBtn`) that flips `cashSale` on EVERY member record at once and re-derives each item's profit, so cash can be set/cleared for the whole sale in one tap instead of editing items individually (v10.4)
- Sales bonus % per team member (live preview, stored on each sale)
- Purchase-cost tracking for profit calculation; value-since-added ("gain") display
- "Promo Image" generator for Instagram/Facebook (logo + photo + type/description, no price; Story or Post formats, live preview). Uses the high-res photo (`photoUrlHi`) when available so the large canvas stays sharp.

### Clients, team & sync
- Clients panel (client records/modals); a client's purchase-history popup collapses multi-item (cart) sales into one row (`🛍 N items` badge, via `_groupSalesForDisplay`) and each row opens the SALE detail (`showSaleDetail`) — from which the "View Item" button jumps to the sold inventory item (v10.4). The list has a sort menu (name A→Z / Z→A, newest/oldest) and a "date added" range filter with one-tap **Season** chips (v10.9): each client's date-added is derived from its id (`String(Date.now())`) via `getClientDate`; a season chip picks that year's operating window (Apr 1 – Nov 30) into the from/to date inputs, and every client card now shows its date added
- Cloud sync via Firebase Realtime Database across devices; cloud identity (change phone, reopen link, everything restored)
- PIN-protected Owner mode; team management (add employees/managers with personalised install links); employee setup via URL parameter
- Optional Telegram notifications (bot token stored in Sync settings)
- Web Share API sharing / one-tap app share

### Platform & misc
- Installable PWA (inline manifest) on Android & iOS; bilingual Greek/English throughout (`data-i18n` + `ABOUT`/i18n dictionaries)
- Green "new version" banner on load after an update: `checkForUpdate()` reads the changelog entry for the current `APP_VERSION` straight from `ABOUT[lang].changelog`, so it stays in sync automatically (there is no separate list to maintain)
- Bilingual item names shown on quotes per active language
- JSON backup export and a data-recovery/force-restore tool
- **Automatic cloud backups (v10.9):** a once-per-calendar-day rolling snapshot of ALL data nodes (the 9 in `BACKUP_PATHS`: config, quotes, clients, sales, users, stone_cache, labels, qr_images, inventory), stored **privately** at Firebase node `/jewelprice_backups/<ms>` (written with the DB secret, so it stays private — NOT in the public mirror or repo). `autoBackupIfDue()` runs from `autoSync()` on app open, gated to owner/manager devices (those holding `creds.dbSecret`) and to one snapshot per day via `localStorage 'jpro_lastAutoBackup'`; keeps the newest `BACKUP_KEEP` (30) via `pruneCloudBackups()`. Settings → Data Backup adds "☁️ Back Up Now" (`backupNowToCloud`) and "↩️ Restore" (`openCloudBackups` → per-snapshot `restoreCloudBackup`), which takes an automatic `pre-restore` snapshot, PUTs each non-null node back, then reloads. `collectBackupData()` is shared by `exportBackup()` and the cloud backups. Snapshot keys are `String(Date.now())` (once-a-day cadence makes collisions a non-issue)
- Configurable VAT %, default margin %, currency symbol
- **Dual-resolution photos (v10.10):** each uploaded inventory photo produces two ImgBB uploads — a small ~480px `photoUrl` (used everywhere in the app, keeps it light/fast) and a ~1200px/85% `photoUrlHi` (used ONLY by the public showroom, `item.htm`, and promo images). `_photoInputChanged` builds both dataURLs from the original picked file (hi only when the source is >480px, else it reuses the small one) and passes `(dataUrl, hiUrl)` to the picker's `onCapture`; the three inventory save paths (label save, scan-to-inventory, edit item) stash both in parallel pending vars and upload via `uploadInventoryPhoto()` (parallel ImgBB uploads, returns `{url,name,hiUrl}`). `photoUrlHi` is in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` so it reaches `/jewelprice_public`; the showroom lightbox/compare views, `item.htm`, and `drawPromoCanvas` use `photoUrlHi || photoUrl` (grid thumbnails stay on the small `photoUrl` for fast browsing). Existing items only gain a hi-res version when their original photo is re-selected/re-taken (ImgBB can't upscale the already-shrunk 480px image).
- **HD upload tracking (v10.11–v10.20 — REMOVED in v10.21):** a temporary aid (green "HD" thumbnail badge + "HD Photo" filter group) that flagged which items already had a `photoUrlHi`, while the owner re-uploaded all existing photos in high resolution. All 363 photos are now uploaded, so the badge in `renderInvList`, the `hdGroup`/`hd` filter logic (`_invFilters.hd`), and the `invFilterHd*` i18n keys were all removed. The v10.16 `uploadInventoryPhoto` `hiUrl` fallback (`hiUrl: hi ? hi.url : small.url`, so every uploaded photo gets a non-empty `photoUrlHi`) is **kept** — it's correct behaviour independent of the tracking UI.
- **Stone-system review & hardening (v10.15):** three round-trip bugs fixed. (1) **Missing abbreviations** — `Akoya Pearl`, `Freshwater Pearl`, `South Sea Pearl`, `Tahitian Pearl` and `Jade` were in `STONE_TYPES` but absent from `STONE_ABBREV_MAP`, so `stoneToLabelPart` encoded them by full name (with carat → re-scanned as **Diamond**) or `type.slice(0,4)` (no carat → dropped on scan). Added them to `STONE_ABBREV_MAP` (keys `AKOY`/`FRES`/`SOUT`/`TAHI`/`JADE`, matching the historical 4-letter fallback so already-printed no-carat tags now parse) and to `COLORED_FULL`/`COLORED_FULL_EARLY`. (2) **Scan-to-inventory dropped qty/melee** — the invScan item builder hardcoded `qty: 1`, so scanning a `4xRUB`/`tZIR` tag into inventory lost the count/melee; now uses `s.qty || 1`. (3) **Label-save `stonesDesc` from lossy QR** — it rebuilt the description from `parseCodeInput(fullQR)` and merged DOM shape/price **by index**, so a no-carat Diamond (absent from the QR) was dropped and, when any stone was skipped in the code, shape/price shifted onto the wrong stone. Now builds `stonesDesc` directly from the DOM stone rows (the complete source the QR was itself built from), gated by the same stone-section-open check as `buildLabelStrings`, falling back to the QR only when there are no DOM rows. Note: 3 divergent copies of the diamond/colored price-per-carat tables still exist (calc estimate / inv-edit live price / another) — a maintainability risk, left as-is.
- **Photo-only edits don't trigger a tag reprint (v10.14):** the Edit Item reprint prompt now fires only when the **stones actually change** in that edit session, not whenever the rebuilt suffix differs from the stored code. On open, `invEdit` captures a baseline stone suffix (`_invEditStoneRowsEl.dataset.baselineSuffix = _buildStoneQRSuffix(...)`); on save it flags the tag stale only if `_newSuffix !== baseline`. This stops photo-only edits (e.g. the bulk HD-photo re-upload) from nagging for a reprint, including on legacy items whose stored code is malformed (those `+`-form codes stay in the DB and the tolerant parser handles them; editing/reprinting still auto-corrects them when the user does change stones).
- **Stone-code separator fix — skipped leading stone (v10.13):** the stone suffix on a code uses `-` before the FIRST stone and `+` before each subsequent one. The generators (`buildLabelStrings`, `_buildStoneQRSuffix`) previously chose the separator from the stone's **array index**, but a stone can be skipped (e.g. a carat-less Diamond yields no label part via `stoneToLabelPart`). So an item whose first stone was a carat-less Diamond followed by e.g. 4× Ruby produced `KAL18S1.3+4xRUB` (no `-`) instead of `KAL18S1.3-4xRUB`; with no `-`, `parseCodeInput` couldn't split base from stones and the calc showed "Code not found". Fixed by tracking the count of parts **actually emitted** (not the index) in all three generator loops. `parseCodeInput` is now also tolerant of the malformed form (no `-` but a `+` → treat the first `+` as the stone boundary) so already-printed bad tags still scan; and the edit-save + restock QR-regen paths normalise a leading `+` suffix to `-`, so editing/reprinting such an item auto-corrects its code. Existing bad codes in the DB are left as-is (the tolerant parser handles them).
- **Stone melee marking recovered from the QR code on edit (v10.12):** the printed QR code is the authoritative source for a stone's quantity/melee (`t` prefix, e.g. `-tZIR`). Older items whose stored `stonesDesc` lost the "melee" word (built before the recent qty change) used to re-parse as plain qty=1 in the Edit Item form, so simply changing a photo regenerated the code as `ZIR` and forced a spurious "print new tag" prompt. `invEdit` now overlays the qty read from `parseCodeInput(item.qrCode).stones` onto the `stonesDesc`-parsed rows (matched by index; or rebuilds rows straight from the code if `stonesDesc` yields none), so melee is restored on load and, on save, `buildStoneDescription` self-heals the description to include "melee" and the regenerated suffix matches the old one (no reprint prompt).
- **Restock doesn't inflate the gain % (v10.17):** the bulk-chain "+ Restock" flow (`confirmBulkRestock`) used to add grams to an item's weight without touching `initialRetail`, so the newly added stock made the current retail jump while the initial price stayed flat — the "Initial price (+x%)" gain line then ballooned (e.g. adding 57g of 14K chain showed a fake +32% rise). It now measures retail at the current gold price *before* and *after* adding the grams (gold price is identical in that instant, so the whole delta is the value of the new metal) and credits that delta into `initialRetail`, so the gain % reflects only real gold-price movement, not how much stock is on hand.
- **Gain sort & filter (v10.17):** to spot such anomalies fast, the inventory Sort menu gains **"Gain: High → Low"** / **"Gain: Low → High"** (`gain_high`/`gain_low`) and the Filter sheet gains a **"Price Gain"** group with **> +3% / +5% / +10%** single-select threshold pills (`_invFilters.gainMin`, a number; 0 = off). Both are **owner/manager only** (hidden from employees, mirroring the appreciation line). A shared helper `invGainPct(item)` computes an item's gain vs its `initialRetail` (returns `null` for Other-type, sold, or baseline-less items, which sort to the bottom and are excluded by the filter).
- **No silent stone auto-pricing (v10.19):** `renderInvList` previously ran a background self-heal that, for any stone item whose `stoneCost` was left blank (null/undefined — never `> 0` or explicit `0`, which it skipped), re-parsed the QR, looked the stone up in the shared stone-price cache (`lookupStonePriceCache`), and **silently wrote that cached price onto the item and persisted it to Firebase**. Two harms: (1) it changed the item's actual retail (adding a stone cost the owner never entered — e.g. a blank-priced Pearl inheriting the cache's `"Pearl"` wholesale), and (2) it inflated the "Initial price (+x%)" gain, because `initialRetail` was captured (self-heal at ~line 11156) *before* the async patch added the stone cost. Removed entirely (the `data-inv-id` stale-card flag on the price div went too). A blank stone price now simply counts as €0; stones are priced only by explicit user action (manual entry or the ✨ Estimate button in Edit/Scan, both tap-only — neither auto-applies on open). Note: the fix stops *future* silent writes but does not retroactively identify/undo items already auto-priced (no audit field distinguishes an auto-filled `stoneCost` from a typed one; the new Gain sort surfaces anomalous items for manual review). The stone-price cache and the Estimate buttons are unchanged.
- **Stone Price Audit (v10.19 — TEMPORARY, remove after cleanup):** an owner/manager-only Settings section (`stoneAuditSection`, gated in `applyRoleUI`) with a "🔍 Scan Inventory" button (`runStonePriceAudit`) that lists in-stock stone items whose stored `initialRetail` matches the **stone-free** retail more closely than the with-stone retail — the reliable fingerprint of the removed self-heal having added the stone cost *after* the baseline was captured (typed prices always have the stone in the baseline). Each row shows the ref code, stone price (+ "= cache" if it equals the cache wholesale), current/no-stone/initial prices and gain %; "Set stone free" (`stoneAuditFix`, confirm-gated) sets `stoneCost = 0` and re-baselines `initialRetail` to the stone-free retail; "Open" calls `invEdit`. Delete this block, the `stoneAuditSection` markup, the `applyRoleUI` line, and the `stoneAudit*` i18n keys once cleanup is done.
- **Initial-price line shows the date added (v10.18):** the owner/manager "Initial price (+x%)" appreciation line in `renderInvList` now embeds the item's `dateAdded` (formatted via `fmtDate`, Greek DD/MM/YYYY by default), e.g. `Initial price (added 07/07/2026): €744.17 (+5.6%)` / `Αρχική τιμή (προσθήκη 07/07/2026): …`. This lets the owner check a gain % against how much gold has moved since that date. Falls back to the plain `initialPriceLbl` for legacy items with no `dateAdded`.
- **Reference code frozen to the printed tag (v10.20):** the printed ref code (e.g. `NK0342`) was previously *derived live* from `jewelryType` everywhere it appeared (`formatSeqId(seqId, jewelryType)`), so editing an item's type silently rewrote the code across the app, the in-app search, the OCR text-scan lookup **and** the public website — leaving the physical tag (and customers who typed it into the showroom search) pointing at a code that no longer resolved. The prefix is now "frozen" via a stored `item.refType` field the first moment the type is changed in `invEdit` (captures the *previous* type before overwriting), and every display/lookup goes through a new helper `itemRefCode(item)` = `formatSeqId(item.seqId, item.refType || item.jewelryType)`. Legacy items have no `refType` and fall back to the live type — **identical to the old behaviour until the first type change**, so nothing in the existing inventory changes. `refTypeMismatch(item)` flags when `refType !== jewelryType`. The code only re-syncs when the user deliberately reprints a tag: `_withRefSync(id, item, doPrint)` wraps the three reprint paths (`invPrintTagFromCard`, the restock print, and `_promptStoneTagReprint`/`_doStoneReprint`) and, on a mismatch, shows a confirm — "Update & Print `CR0342`" (sets `refType = jewelryType`, saves, re-syncs the public mirror, prints the new code) vs "Keep `NK0342`" (prints the frozen code unchanged). Restock with a **new** seqId clears the inherited `refType` (`delete newItem.refType`) so a fresh number derives from the current type; a kept-ref restock preserves it. The QR square is never touched — only the printed ref-code text — so scanning is unaffected. `refType` was added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`, and `showroom.htm` + `item.htm` gained the same `itemRefCode` helper so the website's display AND search match the physical tag. The new-item label-creation paths (`formatSeqId(_pendingSeqId, …)`) are unchanged (a fresh item's type == its code).
- **Android back-gesture guard (v10.22):** on gesture-navigation phones (e.g. Pixel) the edge back-swipe used to exit the installed PWA in one stroke — because switching tabs / opening modals never pushed browser-history entries, so the first "back" popped the app's only entry and closed it. **The reliable design makes the browser history mirror the tab path proactively:** `_jpTrackPanel` (called from the top of `showPanel`) pushes one *real* history entry on every genuine forward tab change and remembers the tab it came from in `_jpFrames`; a single permanent home-floor guard is pushed once at startup. A back gesture (`popstate` → `_jpHandleBack`) then just *consumes* a pre-existing entry — the code never re-pushes history during the common back paths (re-pushing inside `popstate` is unreliable on Android Chrome and was why the app used to close with no warning). `_jpFrames.length` is the source of truth for depth (not `history.state`), so: (1) if a full-screen overlay/modal is open it's closed the way the user would — `_jpTopOverlay()` finds the topmost visible `position:fixed` element at `z-index ≥ 50000` (real modals are ≥ 99996; tab panels are z:100) and `_jpCloseOverlay()` clicks its own Cancel/close control (skipping destructive/confirming actions), falling back to backdrop-click / hide-or-remove, then re-pushes one entry to keep depth in sync (modals have no frame of their own); (2) else if tab frames remain, it pops one and returns to that tab (no re-push); (3) at the home floor (no frames) a first swipe shows a bilingual "Swipe again to exit" toast (`_jpShowExitToast`) and arms a ~3s window, and only the second swipe calls `history.back()` to actually close the app — and this degrades gracefully (if the re-push ever fails, the confirming swipe still exits). **Calc is a hard home floor:** arriving at Calc (tap or programmatic `showPanel('calc')`) collapses any tab frames back to the floor via `history.go(-n)` (the resulting pops are swallowed with the `_jpUnwind` counter, self-healing after 700ms), so a back gesture from Calc ALWAYS warns rather than walking forward into a tab you tapped through. **Resume re-arm:** a PWA has no API to force-quit itself, so `history.back()` only backgrounds the app on Android; when it returns to the foreground the same page resumes with its history stack emptied, which would let the next back close it with no warning — so `_jpResumeRearm` (on `visibilitychange`→visible, `pageshow` and `focus`) re-pushes a fresh home guard whenever we come back after an exit attempt (`_jpExited`). **Double-fire dwell guard:** Android's edge back-swipe often fires `popstate` twice for a single gesture; at the home floor that used to arm on the first fire and immediately exit on the second, and on other tabs it silently double-stepped. The exit branch requires ≥500 ms since the arming swipe (`_jpArmedAt`) before `history.back()` runs, so a single gesture can only ever ARM; only a deliberate later swipe exits. **Guard must be pushed inside a user gesture (Chrome history-manipulation intervention):** this was *the* real cause of "open on Calc, swipe back → immediate close, no warning". Chrome (≥74) on Android marks any `pushState` made **without a prior user activation** as *skippable*, so a guard pushed at page load is skipped by the back gesture and the PWA closes with **no `popstate`** (so none of the above logic runs). The guard is therefore established from the **first real user gesture** — `_jpEstablishGuard`, wired to capture-phase `pointerdown`/`touchstart`/`keydown`/`click` listeners added in `_jpInitBackGuard` — where Chrome honours it. `_jpGuardUp` makes it idempotent; `_jpResumeRearm` resets `_jpGuardUp=false` so the next gesture rebuilds it after a resume. **Residual limitation:** if the user opens the app and swipes back having touched *nothing* at all (no tap/scroll/keypress), there is no user activation, so Chrome still closes it — unavoidable by design (the same activation requirement also suppresses `beforeunload`), and harmless since no work exists to lose. Any interaction at all (including a scroll's `touchstart`) arms the guard. The guard is enabled only in standalone/installed PWAs or on coarse-pointer (touch) devices, so a desktop browser's Back button is untouched. It also persists the last active tab (`localStorage 'jpro_lastPanel'`) and, on open, reopens it (spirit of "continue where I was") with one seeded `calc` frame so back still returns to Calc first, unless following a `#item=` deep link. Init: `_jpInitBackGuard()` from the boot `DOMContentLoaded` after `checkDeepLinkHash()`.
- **Inventory list performance — instant open + windowing (v10.23):** the Stock tab was the only tab that stalled on open, and the cost grew with the item count (382 → 1000+). Three fixes, all read/render-only (no data writes, no behaviour change): (1) **Instant render from cache + background refresh** — `renderInventory()` now draws immediately from the in-memory `_inventoryCache` (like every other tab) whenever it's already populated, then re-fetches from Firebase in the background and only re-renders if the JSON actually changed *and* the panel isn't scrolled down (`panel.scrollTop < 200`), so a cross-device change still lands without yanking the list. First open of the session still fetches then renders. (2) **Lazy + memoized sort** — the sort comparator used to call `calcInvItemPrice` **4× per comparison unconditionally** (O(n·log n) price calcs even for a "newest" sort that needs no prices). A per-render `Map` memo (`_px`/`_gn`, fresh each render so it can't serve a stale price after spot moves) now prices each item at most once, and price/name/gain values are computed lazily inside the relevant `switch` cases only. `invGainPct`'s filter/sort uses go through the memo too. (3) **Windowed rendering** — instead of building all N cards at once, `renderInvList` stores the full sorted rows in `_invSortedRows` + a `buildCard` closure in `_invBuildCard`, then reveals `INV_PAGE_SIZE` (50) at a time: `_invRenderNextPage()` appends the next page (via `insertAdjacentHTML`), the panel's scroll listener (in `_initInvScrollTop`) calls it as you near the bottom, and `_invMaybeFill()` tops up until the viewport overflows (guarded to no-op when the panel is hidden, `clientHeight===0`). "Jump to card" flows (`_jumpToInvCard`, `handleDeepLinkItem`, the sale→item jump) call `_invEnsureRendered(id)` to page up to a target that isn't in the DOM yet; `invSelectAll` selects every id in `_invSortedRows` (not just rendered cards); newly appended cards re-apply select-mode via `_invRestoreSelectMode`. Measured ~13–16 ms to render at 1000 items with only 50 cards in the DOM.
- **Unisex surfaces under Men and Women (v10.24):** unisex pieces are meant for both men and women, but customers (and the owner) kept forgetting to also tick "Unisex" to see them — so a "Men's crosses" search missed the unisex crosses. Now a gender filter for **Men or Women also matches unisex items** in both the showroom (`showroom.htm` shared `genderMatches()` helper, used by `applyFilters` and the nav-drawer `_navFacetResultCount`) and the app (`jewelprice.htm` inventory filter at the `f.gender` check in `renderInvList`). The **"Unisex" filter option was removed entirely from the showroom** (nav-drawer `genderOrder` no longer lists it; `getFilterOptions` excludes `'unisex'`) since it's now redundant there. In the **app the "Unisex" filter pill is kept** — the owner still uses it, and setting an item's gender to Unisex is what makes it appear in both Men and Women searches. Item gender values are unchanged; only the filter matching/options changed.
- **Cart & rounding bug fixes (v10.25):** two Calc fixes. (1) The **cart item "✕" now removes the item** — `removeFromCart` called a non-existent `_saveQuoteCart()`, which threw a ReferenceError right after the `splice`, so the UI never refreshed (the cart is memory-only; `confirmAddToCart` never persisted it either, so the call was spurious). Removed the stray call. (2) The **round-price "+" button resets per item** — after applying a rounding (button flips to "✕") and adding to cart, the next scanned/calculated item left the button showing "✕", so a new rounding couldn't be applied; the calc-display reset (where `roundPriceInput.value` is cleared) now also sets `roundApplyBtn.textContent = '+'`.
- **Secondary gender — "Also appears under" (v10.26):** an item keeps ONE primary `gender` (drives the badge, backward-compatible) but can now be cross-listed under extra categories via a new optional array field **`alsoGenders`** (subset of `women`/`men`/`children`), so e.g. a children's pendant that grown women also buy shows up under both Children and Women filters. Implemented with shared helpers in `jewelprice.htm` (`genderAlsoGroupHtml`/`_toggleGenderAlso`/`_syncGenderAlso`/`readGenderAlso`, `GENDER_ALSO_OPTS`): a row of toggle pills sits under the gender `<select>` in all three item forms — **label creation** (static `#labelGenderAlsoWrap`, rebuilt fresh each open in `_openLabelModalInner`), **scan-to-inventory**, and **edit item** — the pill equal to the current primary is hidden (`onchange="_syncGenderAlso(...)"`). All three save paths persist `alsoGenders`. The inventory card gender badge shows the primary plus dimmer `+♀️/+♂️/+👶` badges for extras. Matching: the app inventory filter and the showroom `genderMatches(selected, item)` both accept an item as matching a selected gender if it's the primary, in `alsoGenders`, or (unisex) Men/Women. Showroom nav-drawer facet **counts** and `getFilterOptions` genders now bucket an item under every category it matches (incl. unisex→Men+Women and alsoGenders) so counts line up with results. `alsoGenders` was added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` so the showroom mirror carries it. This generalises the v10.24 unisex rule (unisex still auto-spans Men+Women without needing alsoGenders).
- **Item "Color" field (v10.27):** a new optional, first-class **`color`** field on every inventory item (independent of metal — enamel / stone / cord color), added to all three item forms (**label creation** static `#labelColorSelect` populated in `_openLabelModalInner`; **scan-to-inventory** `#invScanColorSelect`; **edit item** `#invEditColorSelect`) and persisted by all three save paths. Backed by the bilingual `ITEM_COLORS` list (20 colors + blank) with helpers `itemColorLabel` / `itemColorSwatchCss` / `itemColorOptionsHtml` (defined next to `GOLD_COLORS`); stored as a **language-neutral key** (`light_blue`) so labels flip with the UI language. **Blank = plain** (stored `null`) → no badge, the piece reads by its metal (Gold/Silver). Shown as a swatch-dotted badge on the inventory card (`_colorBadge`, before the gold-color badge) and filterable via a new **Color** group in the Stock filter sheet (`colorGroup`, `_invFilters.colors`, present-colors-only, generic pill toggle + `applyFilters` check). `color` added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` so the showroom mirror carries it. **This is step 0 of the "similar items" grouped-inventory feature** — see `SIMILAR_ITEMS_DESIGN.md` for the full design (groupId model, grouped cards, batch add/restock, color-split showroom); the color field doubles as the future group color-split dimension. Replaces the color owners used to type by hand into the description.
- **Similar-items grouping — groupId + grouped card (v10.28):** the first functional slice of the "similar items" feature (design in `SIMILAR_ITEMS_DESIGN.md`). Items sharing a hidden **`groupId`** collapse into ONE Stock card, keeping every piece as its own exact `inv_...` record underneath (weight/cost/retail/QR/profit intact) — the same "N exact records, one card" philosophy as multi-item sales. Implemented purely at **display time in `renderInvList`**: after sort/filter, a transform folds items with the same `groupId` into rows shaped `[repId, repItem, members[]]` (representative = first member in sorted order), and a `buildRow` dispatcher renders `buildGroupCard` for grouped rows / the normal `buildCard` for singletons. The grouped card shows the representative photo/desc/ref-code, a **`🔗 N in stock`** badge (count = in-stock members in the filtered view), **one price or a `min – max` range** (owner/manager only), and a **colour breakdown** (blank colour → the piece's metal). Windowing (v10.23) counts a group as one row; `invSelectAll` expands to every member id; `_invEnsureRendered` matches a member id to its group row. **Creating groups:** a 🔗 button in the select-mode bar (`invGroupSelected`, owner/manager, hidden for employees via `applyRoleUI`) stamps a shared `groupId` on the selected items (reusing an existing groupId in the selection so a single item folds into an existing group), `showConfirm`-gated. **Group detail:** `openInvGroup(gid)` opens a bottom sheet listing each in-stock piece (weight, colour, price, QR) with per-piece Mark-sold / Edit and a **"🗑 Delete all (N)"** action (`invDeleteGroup`, v10.43 — one confirmation deletes every in-stock member, for cleaning up a whole batch e.g. coins added by mistake; replaced the old **Ungroup** button, `invUngroup`, which still exists but is no longer wired to any button). `groupId` added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (for the future showroom split). Selling one piece is an ordinary single-item sale (count drops); a group hitting 0 in-stock vanishes like a sold item. Still TODO from the design: batch "Add similar items" create flow, "＋ Add more" restock, "sell N", and the showroom group-by-`groupId` count + colour split (Phase 2).
- **Similar-items "Add similar / Add more" weigh-tap flow (v10.29):** the batch-create + restock slice of the similar-items feature (design steps 4–5, same primitive). `invAddSimilar(sourceId)` (owner/manager) opens a loop sheet that clones the source's shared attributes and, per piece, takes only a **weight** (+ **color**, pre-filled), keeps the **same reference code** (copies `seqId`/`refType`), regenerates the weight-encoded QR (same vendor+cat+weight logic as `invRestock` — `buildQR`), stamps the group's `groupId` (**minting one on the source on the first add** so a single item becomes a group of 2+), re-baselines `initialRetail` to the new piece's own retail (so gain starts at 0, correct for per-gram pricing), saves, and — on "🖨️ Print & Add" — prints the tag directly via `printLabel` (a running "Added: N" counter; "Done" re-renders). Two entry points: **"🔗 Add similar items"** in the Edit Item form (in-stock, non-bulk, owner) — the way to start a fresh batch from the first piece — and **"＋ Add more"** in the group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) — restock into an existing group. Serves both "12 near-identical necklaces from scratch" and "sell 6, add 8 → 14". Still TODO from the design: "sell N at once" from the group card, and the showroom group-by-`groupId` count + colour split (Phase 2).
- **Similar-items "Sell several at once" (v10.30):** the group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) gains a **"Sell several"** quantity stepper (`_invGroupQtyStep`, clamped 1..in-stock-count) + a Sell button. `invSellFromGroup(gid)` takes the first N in-stock members and routes through the **existing** sale flow — N=1 → `openSaleModalFromInventory` (single sale); N>1 → sets `window._cartSaleItems` and calls `openMultiSaleModal`, which tags them with a shared `saleGroup` so the Sales list collapses them into one "🛍 N items" row and stock drops by N (all-roles; employees can sell). For specific pieces (e.g. one pink + one blue) the per-piece Sell buttons in the sheet remain. Completes design step 6.
- **Similar-items showroom split (Phase 2, step 7 — `showroom.htm`, no `APP_VERSION` bump):** the public showroom grid collapses pieces sharing a `groupId` **and** the same `color` into ONE card with a live **"N in stock"** ribbon (`.card-stock-badge`, top-left); different colours of the same group **split into separate public cards** (so a customer sees each colour). The card also shows the decorative `color` as a tag. A ported `ITEM_COLORS` + `itemColorLabel` provides bilingual colour labels; the grouping transform lives in `renderGrid` (keyed by `groupId + color`). Ungrouped items render individually as before. `groupId`/`color` reach the showroom via `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`. This completes the similar-items design (Phase 1 + Phase 2 core). Known minor gaps: showroom nav-drawer facet counts still count individual pieces; no combined mixed-colour multi-sale from the group sheet.
- **Similar-items polish (v10.31 + showroom):** (1) **Full per-piece actions** — the group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) now gives every piece the complete single-card action set (➕ Calc `invLoadToCalc`, Share `invShareSingle`, 🏷️ Print Tag `invPrintTagFromCard`, 💰 Sell `invMarkSold`, ✏️ Edit `invEdit`, 🗑 Delete `invDelete`), each closing the sheet first, so no functionality is lost when items are grouped. (2) **Showroom badge moved** — the "N in stock" count moved from a top-left image ribbon to a quiet bottom-right line in the card body next to the ref code (`.card-foot`/`.card-stock-count`), and the **lightbox detail** now shows an "In stock / N available" row plus the item's Colour (counted by `groupId + color` from `filtered`). Showroom-only parts don't bump `APP_VERSION`; the app-side per-piece actions did (v10.31).
- **Similar-items polish 2 + dup-QR safety net (v10.32):** (1) the grouped Stock card (`buildGroupCard`) now shows the representative's **handmade / gold-colour / gender** badges on the right (mirroring the single card, minus the per-piece decorative colour which the breakdown covers); (2) each piece row in the group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) shows the owner/manager **"Initial price (added DD/MM/YYYY): €X (+Y%)"** line between the piece info and its action buttons; (3) **save-time duplicate-QR guard** added to `saveToInventoryFromLabel` (belt-and-suspenders behind the existing `openLabelModal` pre-check — reloads the cache if empty, and `showConfirm`s "already exists … Save anyway?" so a dup QR can't be saved silently; not reached by the `invAddSimilar` flow which saves via `saveInventoryItem`).
- **Duplicate-QR check now status-agnostic (v10.33):** the add-via-calc duplicate-QR warning (`openLabelModal` pre-check + its `_showDupQrWarning` live preview + the v10.32 save-time guard in `saveToInventoryFromLabel`) previously matched only `status === 'in_stock'` items. A collision with a **sold** item therefore showed the item's photo in the Calc price bar (that lookup at ~line 7888 never filtered status) but produced **no warning** — the reported "duplicate prompt gone" bug. All three checks now match **any** existing item with that QR (preferring an in-stock match for the shown details, else the first match) and tag a non-in-stock match "[sold]/[πωλημένο]", so the owner's globally-unique-QR workflow is enforced regardless of status. The `invRestock` dup-check (which intentionally excludes the sold original being restocked) is unchanged.
- **Stale-stones-on-scan fix (v10.34):** `lookupCode` only CLEARED the calc's stone rows inside its `if (stones && stones.length > 0)` auto-fill block, so scanning a stone-less item **after** a stone item left the previous item's stone rows in place. Those stale stones then rode into the stone-less item's QR (`buildLabelStrings` reads the DOM stone rows), so a Calc-sold item was recorded with a phantom suffix (e.g. `ALO14MM0.6-tZIR`) that didn't match its real inventory qrCode (`ALO14MM0.6`) — the sale's `confirmSale` QR-match (~line 6220) then failed to mark the item sold, and the sale's "View Item" couldn't resolve it. Fixed by resetting the stone state **once, up-front, on every successful `lookupCode` match** (before the Other-type / metal-type branch): clears `stoneRowsList`, clears `stoneCostInput`, and collapses `stoneSectionBody` if open. Stone items re-open the section and re-add their own rows just below (metal path) so nothing is lost. Covers all lookup entry points (typed code, camera QR, OCR — all route through `lookupCode`). NOTE: this does not retroactively fix already-mismatched sales; the owner reconciles those by deleting the bad sale and re-selling the still-in-stock item.
- **Share whole group at once (v10.35):** the grouped Stock card (`buildGroupCard`) gets a share icon in its top row (next to the code, `.inv-share-btn` so it hides in select mode) → `invShareGroup(gid)` collects the group's in-stock members and calls the existing `showInvShareOptions(members)`. That sheet already handles multi-item: Order Sheet + Catalog (with/without price) + Send to Team work for N items, and the Promo/social image is shown only `if (isSingle)` so it auto-hides for a group.
- **Group select = all members (v10.36):** in Stock select-mode, the grouped card's checkbox now calls `invSelectToggleGroup(gid, checked)` (instead of `invSelectToggleItem(repId)`), which adds/removes EVERY in-stock member of the group from `_invSelected` and mirrors the checked state on the representative checkbox. So mixing a single item + a group in a bulk share (catalog/order sheet/send-to-team) or the totals bar covers all the group's pieces, not just the representative. `invSelectAll`/`_invRestoreSelectMode` stay consistent (they key off the rep id, always included).
- **Catalog/order-sheet collapses similar pieces (v10.37):** `invDoShare` now folds the resolved `items` by (`groupId` + `color`) into one representative per bucket (shallow copy carrying a `_stockCount`) before `buildSharePDF` — so a group of 3 identical prints ONE entry, and a mixed group (1 blue / 1 red / 3 green) prints 3 (blue, red, green). Ungrouped items always list individually. `buildSharePDF` draws a "colour · N in stock" line under the metal line (count only when `_stockCount > 1`; colour via `itemColorLabel`). Mirrors the showroom's group+colour collapse. The share-options sheet's header count still reflects the raw selection (pre-collapse) — minor, acceptable.
- **Select-bar redesign + cost-total fix (v10.38):** the Stock select-mode floating bar (`#invShareBar`) was re-laid-out into two rows — row 1 = selected count + "All" (select-all) + "✕" (exit); row 2 = three big equal finger-friendly buttons (Share / Group / Sell) with icon-over-label. New i18n keys `invSelAll`/`invSelGroup`/`invSelSold`. `applyRoleUI` now sets `#invGroupBtn` display to `flex` (not `''`) so hiding it for employees doesn't break the column layout. **Cost total fix:** `updateInvShareBar` summed only `item.purchaseCost` (null for per-gram items → "—"); it now falls back to `calcInvItemPrice(item).baseCost` (metal + labour + stone) so the COST total reflects real production cost when no purchase cost is stored.
- **Coins in inventory — gold & silver (v10.39):** a first-class **"Coin" / «Νόμισμα»** item type (`jewelryType: 'coin'`, ref-code prefix **`GC`** in `JEWELRY_TYPE_PREFIX`) for bullion / historical coins — modelled as `metal:'gold'|'silver'` inventory records flagged **`isCoin: true`**, distinct from jewelry (no vendor / labour / margin / VAT). Backed by **`COIN_CATALOG`** (each entry has `metal`, `gross`, `fineness`, `pure`): **26 gold** (British Sovereign/Half/Quarter, French & Swiss 20 Fr, Austrian Ducat & Corona, American Eagle 1oz/½/¼/1-10 & Double Eagle, Krugerrand ×4, Maple Leaf, Mexican 50 Peso, Greek 20 Drachmai, German 20 Mark, Italian 20 Lire, Turkish 100 Kurush) + **12 silver** (Canadian .800 dollar/50¢/25¢/10¢, US 90% dollar/half/quarter/dime, Greek 20 & 30 Drachmai .835, Silver Maple & Silver Eagle 1oz) + a **Custom** option (type gross weight + fineness). Each coin stores its exact **`coinPure`** grams (source of truth), `coinGross`, `coinFineness`, `coinKey`/`coinName`, and optional **`purchaseCost`**. Helpers: `coinCatalogEntry`/`coinCatalogByMetal`/`coinItemLabel`/`coinMeltValue`/`coinMarkup(metal)`/`coinNumistaUrl`. **Pricing** (`calcInvItemPrice` coin branch): `retail = coinPure × (spot/31.1035) + coinMarkup(metal)` where spot is gold or silver per the coin's metal; markup default **€30 gold / €0 silver** (`state.coinMarkup` / `state.coinMarkupSilver`, editable in Settings, synced via `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`). The Stock/grouped card shows the **live melt value big**; gold adds a "sell from" suggested price, silver at €0 markup shows value only; owner also sees **"Bought: €X"** when `purchaseCost` is set (in the group-detail row, since coins always group). The **grouped card also shows the group TOTAL** (`N× live · N× sell-from`), an owner-only **"Initial price (added DATE): €X (+Y%)" line** (`coinInitialLineHtml`, mirrors regular items' appreciation line) and a direct **Numista 🔗** link in the top row (`coinNumistaUrl(rep)`, next to Share). Gain model (v10.41): `coinBaseline(item)` = `purchaseCost` if set, else `initialMelt` (pure-metal value captured at add-time in `_addCoinConfirm`, alongside `initialRetail`); `coinGainPct` compares the **live melt** to that baseline (same basis as the big number). Coins use this coin-specific gain, not `invGainPct` (which still returns null for them) nor the old appreciation line. Coin `karat` is stored as a **string** (gold `'22'` / silver `'800'`) so the karat filter's `.endsWith` label logic works; the type filter lists **"Coin"** (types are sorted by `INV_JEWELRY_TYPES` order). **Personal "My coins" catalogue:** owner-saved coins live in `state.customCoins` (cloud-synced via `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`), each `{ key:'user_…', metal, name, gross, fineness, pure, custom:true }`. Helpers `customCoins()`/`allCoins()`/`coinEntryLabel(e)` merge them with the built-in `COIN_CATALOG` everywhere (`coinCatalogEntry`/`coinCatalogByMetal` search `allCoins()`), so a saved coin is recognised by identify-by-weight, dropdown, live-valued and grouped like a built-in one (shown with a ★). In the Add-Coin modal a **Custom** coin has a **"💾 Save to My coins"** toggle (default on) → `_addCoinConfirm` de-dupes against a near-identical saved coin (same metal, ~gross/fineness) or persists a new `user_…` entry (pushes config to cloud), then uses that key for the records' `coinKey`/`groupId`. When identify-by-weight finds no match (or "none of these"), an **"➕ Add new coin"** block (`_addCoinStartNew`) pre-fills the weighed gross into the Custom form + a **Numista weight search** link (`coinNumistaWeightUrl`); fineness comes from the owner's metal tester or Numista. Settings → **"My coins"** (`renderCustomCoinsManager`/`deleteCustomCoin`) lists/deletes saved coins. Full auto-identification via the Numista API was deliberately NOT built (needs an exposed API key + CORS; noted as a possible future server-side add-on). **Numista link precision (v10.40):** `coinNumistaUrl` links **directly to the exact Numista piece page** (`…/catalogue/pieces<id>.html`) for single-design **bullion** coins via the **`COIN_NID`** map (Eagles, Double Eagle, Krugerrands, gold/silver Maple, Silver Eagle, Mexican 50 Peso, Canadian 1967 dollar) — their generic names ("Eagle"/"Dollar") make text search useless (buried under look-alikes). Coins **not** in `COIN_NID` (historical multi-variant: sovereigns, Napoleons, ducats, corona, junk silver, Greek, etc.) fall back to a tuned search from **`COIN_NUMISTA`** (issuing country + face value, e.g. `United States 5 Dollars American Gold Eagle`) so the customer can match their own year/portrait. Custom coins fall back to `entry.name`. To pin more coins to exact pages, add `key: <numista piece id>` to `COIN_NID`. **Adding** — the Stock tab's old "📷 Scan QR" button was **replaced** by **"🪙 Add Coin"** (`openAddCoinModal`, owner/manager, gated in `applyRoleUI` via `#invAddCoinBtn`): a **Gold/Silver toggle** (`_addCoinSetMetal`), an **⚖️ identify-by-weight** tool (`_addCoinIdentify` lists catalogue coins of that metal within a ± tolerance, each with a **Numista** 🔗 confirm link and a Pick button — handles same-weight coins like the 6.4516 g LMU group), a catalogue dropdown + Custom, an optional **purchase price**, and quantity → creates N identical records that **auto-group** by deterministic `groupId = 'coin_' + coinKey` (custom: `coin_<metal>_c_<pure×1000>`); all share ONE `GC####` ref code (interchangeable bullion; the sale marks the specific piece sold by record id). *(The scan-to-inventory flow still exists — reached from Calc when a scanned QR isn't found — just not via a Stock-tab button.)* **Selling:** `openSaleModalFromInventory` pre-fills the suggested price, names the coin in the note, and **defaults CASH ON** for coins; profit = sale − (`purchaseCost` if set, else melt via `baseCost = melt`). Coins fold into inventory **totals**: retail (via the gold/silver breakdown) and **pure-metal grams use `item.coinPure` directly** (coin finenesses aren't standard karats). **Coins are hidden from the Stock LIST by default (v10.42):** after `renderInvSummary(filtered)` (which still counts coins, so totals are unchanged), `renderInvList` drops `isCoin` items from the list unless the **"Coin" type filter** is selected (`_invFilters.types.includes('coin')`) or a search is active — value totals never change, only the visible cards. **Also excluded from the PIECE COUNTS (v10.45):** `renderInvSummary` counts coins in `totalFiltered`/`piecesGold`/`piecesSilver` only under the same `_countCoins` condition (coin filter or search); value totals (retail/metal/pure) always include them. **Group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`, v10.45):** the Sell-several / Add-more / Delete-all actions sit at the top and the per-piece list collapses into an expandable dropdown (`_invGroupTogglePieces`, collapsed by default when >8 members) so actions stay reachable without scrolling past e.g. 107 coins. **Filter Type/Vendor dropdowns have a "Select all" row (v10.45)** (`dropGroup`, `data-selall`) — picking all Types incl. Coin is how the owner sees the grand total. Group detail, search, and "Add more" are coin-aware. **Coins are NEVER published to the public showroom** — `publicProjection` returns null for `isCoin`/`jewelryType==='coin'`. *(Future: a dedicated "metal stock"/bullion-bar type for workshop gold/silver — for now Custom coins cover ad-hoc bars.)*
- **Raw metal / scrap stock — gold & silver (v10.48):** a first-class **"Metal" / «Μέταλλο»** item type (`jewelryType: 'metal'`, no ref-code prefix — no printed tag) for the vault metal the owner holds to melt down later (pure gold/silver balls, 14/18/22K old jewellery from customers, scrap silver). Modelled as inventory records flagged **`isMetal: true`** — **one accumulating record per (metal + karat)**: adding more of the same karat tops up the existing pile's `metalGross`/`metalPure`. Each record stores `metal`, `karat` (gold `'24'..'9'` / silver `'999'..'800'` string), `metalFineness` (0..1), `metalGross` (= `weight`, mirror), `metalPure` (source of truth for value), `initialMelt`/`initialRetail` (accumulated melt-value-at-add), and optional accumulated `purchaseCost`. Backed by **`METAL_STOCK_KARATS`** (gold/silver preset karat→fineness lists) + a **Custom ‰** option. Helpers: `metalStockLabel`/`metalStockMelt`/`metalStockBaseline`/`metalStockGainPct`/`metalStockInitialLineHtml`/`_findMetalLot`/`_metalCurrentSpec`/`_refiningLossPct`. **Pricing** (`calcInvItemPrice` `isMetal` branch, placed before the coin branch): `retail = metalValue = melt = coinPure-style pure×spot/31.1035` — **no labour/markup/VAT** (raw metal, not a piece). **Add-Metal flow:** the Stock tab's **"🧱 Add Metal"** button (`openMetalModal('add')`, owner/manager, gated in `applyRoleUI` via `#invAddMetalBtn`) — Gold/Silver toggle, karat dropdown (+ custom ‰), weight, optional **price paid**; `_metalConfirm` merges into the existing metal+karat lot (or creates one). The card's **"＋ Add"** re-opens the modal prefilled to that karat; **"✏️ Edit"** opens `openMetalModal('edit',{editId})` which SETS the absolute weight/karat and rescales the accumulated baselines by the pure-grams ratio; **"🗑 Delete"** removes the lot. **Buy-cheap gain → daily sales:** when a price paid is entered below the metal's **recoverable value** (`melt × (1 − refiningLoss%)`, default **3%**, `state.refiningLoss`, editable in Settings + cloud-synced), the gain (`recoverable − paid`) is booked as a **gain-only profit entry** in today's Sales via `_recordScrapGain` (a sale record with `amount = profit = gain`, `cost = null`, `cashSale: true`, flagged **`scrapGain: true`**, note `♻️ Scrap gain — 30g 14K Gold`) — so day revenue AND profit both rise by exactly the gain, without a real item sale. **Display:** dedicated compact card (`buildCard` `isMetal` early-return — no photo/vendor/QR/Calc/Share) showing the live melt value big, pure/gross/fineness, an owner-only **"Bought: €X"** line when `purchaseCost` set, and the owner-only **"Value when added (DATE): €X (+Y%)"** appreciation line (`metalStockInitialLineHtml`, vs `initialMelt`). **Totals:** metal **value counts** toward Total Retail + Metal Value, and pure grams use `item.metalPure` directly (exact, handles non-standard finenesses) — but metal is **NOT counted in "Pieces"** and is **hidden from the Stock LIST** unless the **"Metal" type filter** is selected (or a search is active), exactly like coins (`renderInvSummary` `_countMetal`, `renderInvList` list-drop). Never grouped (the group-fold guards `item.isMetal ? null : item.groupId`). **Coins/metal are NEVER published to the public showroom** — `publicProjection` returns null for `isMetal`/`jewelryType==='metal'`. Excluded from `invGainPct` (has its own gain line). No sale/melt-out action — the owner Deletes a lot after melting it into workshop stock.
- **Coin legacy-gain fix (v10.49):** coins added before v10.41 (the "Initial price" line) stored only `initialRetail`, which for a coin is **melt + markup**, but no `initialMelt`. `coinBaseline`'s `initialRetail` fallback compared that with-markup baseline against the LIVE **melt** (no markup), so every such coin showed a spurious loss of roughly the markup (e.g. a gold coin added the same afternoon read −7.3%). Fixed by stripping the markup back off in the fallback: `baseline = initialRetail − coinMarkup(item.metal)` (guarded to stay positive). Coins added from v10.41 on carry `initialMelt` and are unaffected; display-time only, no data writes.
- **Desktop layout — wider frame + adjustable stock columns (v10.51, revised v10.52):** the app was a single 480px phone column even on a desktop monitor. Now, at **`@media (min-width:1000px)`** only (phones/tablets untouched — they stay the exact 480px column), the whole frame widens to **1120px** (`.app`, `.bottom-nav`, `#panel-inventory`, `#panel-sales`; `.customer-price-bar`/`#cartPanel` at 560px), and the form/list panels (`#panel-calc`, vendors, clients, settings, sync, about, `.sales-panel-inner`) are capped to **560px centred** so forms stay readable inside the wide frame. **Stock density is user-selectable** via `#invColsSelect` (a desktop-only picker in the filter bar, shown by CSS `#invColsWrap{display:flex}` at ≥1000px): **List / 2 / 3 columns**, applied through `#invList[data-cols]` (`data-cols=1` → block, capped 600px centred = the old single-column look; `2`/`3` → `grid-template-columns:repeat(N,1fr)`; vertical spacing keeps each card's own `margin-bottom`). `setInvCols(v)` persists to `localStorage 'jpro_invCols'`; `applyInvCols()` (called from `renderInvList` and re-syncs the `<select>`) sets `#invList.dataset.cols`. Default **2**. Two desktop bugs fixed as part of the v10.51 work: (1) **scroll-top arrow vanished on desktop** — `#panel-inventory` used `transform:translateX(-50%)` to centre on desktop, which made the panel the containing block for its `position:fixed` children (`#invScrollTopBtn`, `#invNotifBtn`, `#invShareBar`), so they scrolled away with the content / landed under the header. The panel now centres with the base `left:0;right:0;margin:0 auto` (no transform); the floating buttons hug the frame's right-inside edge via `right: max(16px, calc(50vw - <halfFrame-16>px)) !important` (224 for the 480 column at ≥480px, 544 for the 1120 frame at ≥1000px) — the `max()` keeps a 16px gap while the frame is narrower than its max-width. (2) **bottom nav misaligned with inventory in fullscreen** — the base `max-width:480` capped a plain `width:1120`, so the desktop rules override **`max-width`** on `.bottom-nav`/`#panel-sales`/`.customer-price-bar`. Nav and panels share the same centred box. CSS + a tiny persisted setting; no data or behaviour change on mobile.
- **Coin/Metal filters hidden from employees (v10.51):** in `openInvFilterSheet`, after the Type filter list is built, `creds.role === 'employee'` strips `'coin'` and `'metal'` from the type options (they're owner-only concerns; the Add Coin/Add Metal buttons were already employee-gated in `applyRoleUI`). Employees therefore can't filter the stock list to surface coins/scrap metal.
- **Per-item "Hide from showroom" (v10.52):** any inventory item (gold/silver/other — no exceptions) can be individually excluded from the public online showroom via a new boolean field **`hideFromShowroom`**. `publicProjection` returns `null` when it's set (same mechanism as coins/metal), so `syncPublicItem`/`republishPublicShowroom` drop it from `/jewelprice_public` — and toggling it ON for an existing item removes it on the next save. The toggle (a 🙈 button mirroring the handmade toggle) appears in **two** forms: **label creation** (Calc → Tag: static `#labelHideShowroomToggle`, reset to OFF each open in `_openLabelModalInner`, read into the save object as `hideFromShowroom`) and **Edit Item** (`#invEditHideShowroomToggle`, initialised from `item.hideFromShowroom`, saved in `invEdit`). NOT added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (it only gates publishing; it must never be published itself). Existing items have no `hideFromShowroom` → treated as visible (no change). Lets the owner keep walk-in-only stock (e.g. earring backs/clutches, type Other) out of the showroom without asking for code changes. **(v10.53)** hidden pieces are surfaced in the app: a **🙈 badge** on the inventory card (`_hiddenBadge` in `buildCard`, `_grpHidden` in `buildGroupCard`, next to the handmade/gender badges) and a **"🙈 Hidden" pill in the Status filter group** (`statuses` gains `'hidden'`; it's a cross-cutting flag, not a real status — `renderInvList` requires `item.hideFromShowroom` when selected, while the in_stock/sold pills still apply via `_realStatus = f.status.filter(s=>s!=='hidden')`). i18n: `invFilterHidden`.
- **Per-item target-retail fix + blank-cost default (v10.54):** two fixes to the **per-item** labor-category pricing (self-made pieces priced by a Target Retail). (1) **Target overshoot** — the stored `itemMargin` was back-calculated as `(targetExVat / purchaseCost) − 1`, but `calcInvItemPrice` applies the margin to **baseCost** (`metalCost + secondary + stones + laborCost`). These are equal in the normal case (labor = purchaseCost − metal − …), but when the **live metal cost exceeds the entered purchase cost** the labor clamps to 0 and `baseCost > purchaseCost`, so dividing by purchaseCost overshot the target (a 0.20 g 14K gold back with €5 cost / €20 target showed **€54.30**). Both save paths (label creation ~L10944, `invEdit` ~L14065) now divide by the actual `baseCost`, so the target is always honored; **in every normal case the new formula equals the old one**, so existing correctly-priced items are unchanged. (2) **Blank purchase cost → ex-VAT metal cost** — for per-item pieces the owner makes himself (his cost IS the metal), leaving Purchase Cost blank now defaults `purchaseCost` to the live **ex-VAT metal cost** (primary + secondary), in both label creation (`perItemPurchaseCost`) and `invEdit` (via `calcInvItemPrice(item).metalValue + secMetalCost`). Keeps profit and the target-margin correct instead of treating cost as €0. NB: this was a **pre-existing** pricing bug (unrelated to the v10.51–53 desktop/showroom work, which touched no pricing logic).
- **Desktop final-price bar off-centre when maximised (v10.54):** `.customer-price-bar` centres via `left:50%; transform:translateX(-50%)` at ≥480px, but the base rule's `right:0` + `margin:0 auto` were never cleared, over-constraining the fixed box (left+right+width all set) so the leftover width was absorbed into auto margins — ~16px at 1200px but ~196px at 1920px, drifting the box far right only when maximised on a wide desktop monitor. Fixed by adding `right:auto; margin:0` to the ≥480 rule (verified: bar centre == calc-form centre at 1920px). CSS-only.
- **"Add similar items" quantity field + N-copy print (v10.55):** the `invAddSimilar` sheet gained a **Quantity** number input (`#invAddSimQty`, native up/down arrows, min 1 / max 50, default 1) so a batch of identical-weight pieces is added in one tap instead of one-by-one. `addOne` → **`addBatch(doPrint)`** loops the qty, creating N separate `inv_…` records (all same weight/colour → same weight-encoded QR, shared `groupId`/`seqId`). Since all N share ONE label, printing uses a **single job with N copies**: `buildTSPL(data, qrOnly, copies)` and `printLabel(data, statusEl, qrOnly, copies)` gained an optional `copies` arg → TSPL `PRINT 1,N` (default 1, so all other callers are unchanged); `addBatch` calls `printLabel(data, el, false, qty)` once after the loop. Qty resets to 1 after each batch.
- **Grouped-card polish — equal height + handmade badge (v10.56):** two fixes to the "similar items" grouped Stock card (`buildGroupCard`). (1) **Even card heights on desktop** — on the multi-column desktop grid (`@media (min-width:1000px)`, 2/3 columns) the grouped card, which carries less text than a normal card, looked stunted next to its neighbours. The grid's `align-items` was changed from `start` back to the default `stretch` so every card in a row shares the tallest card's height, and `#invList[data-cols="2"/"3"] .inv-card` is now a flex column with `.inv-card-actions { margin-top:auto }` so the leftover space sits between the content and the pinned-to-bottom action row. Scoped to the desktop grid only — mobile (single-column block) and the `data-cols="1"` List view are untouched. (2) **Handmade badge on grouped cards** — `_grpHandmade` now checks whether **any** member of the group is handmade (`members.some(...)`), not just the representative, so a group containing handmade pieces shows the ✋ badge like a single card.
- **"Grouped" status filter (v10.57):** the Stock filter sheet's Status group gains a **"🔗 Grouped"** pill (`statuses` = `['in_stock','sold','grouped','hidden']`, positioned between Sold and Hidden). Like `hidden`, `grouped` is a **cross-cutting flag, not a real status** — `renderInvList` requires `item.groupId && !item.isMetal` when selected, while the in_stock/sold pills still apply via `_realStatus = f.status.filter(s => s!=='hidden' && s!=='grouped')`. Lets the owner narrow to existing similar-items groups (e.g. "Rings + Grouped + Vendor") instead of scanning ungrouped pieces. i18n: `invFilterGrouped`. (Note: coins/metal auto-group, but metal is excluded here and coins only appear when the Coin type filter/search is active anyway.)
- **Group appreciation line (v10.58):** the grouped Stock card (`buildGroupCard`) now shows an owner/manager **"Group initial value: €X (±Y%)"** line under the price (`_grpAppreciation`, i18n `grpInitialLbl`) for non-coin, non-`other` groups — the group analogue of the single card's "Initial price" appreciation. **The gain is aggregated over the members currently in the group's view** (`Σ live retail` vs `Σ initialRetail`, both summed across the same in-stock members), NOT `currentTotal` vs a stored `initialTotal`. This is deliberately count-independent: when a piece sells it leaves `filtered`, so its live price *and* its baseline drop out of both sums together — the % reflects only real metal-price movement, never the smaller total from having fewer pieces. (Coins keep their own per-coin `coinInitialLineHtml`, which is already count-independent; `other`-type has no baseline.)
- **Exact-piece sale restore on delete (v10.59):** identical grouped pieces (added via "Add similar items" at the same weight) and coin batches share the **same `qrCode`**, and sale records only stored `invQr`. So deleting a sale restored the item by the **first** QR match — usually a still-in-stock twin (a silent no-op), leaving the actually-sold piece sold and the group count stuck one low (5→4→*4*). Fixed by storing the exact record id **`invId`** on the sale when sold from inventory: `confirmSale` attaches `_firstSaleRec.invId = window._saleFromInvId` (the first/single piece) and each multi-item record gets `_stdRec.invId = id`. `deleteSaleRecord` now restores by `target.invId` when present, falling back to a QR match **restricted to a `status === 'sold'` item** (so even legacy sales raise the in-stock count instead of no-opping on an in-stock twin); a bulk batch is never flipped (`!invItem.isBulk`). `deleteSaleGroup` inherits the fix (it loops `deleteSaleRecord`). Bulk-chain sales still restore via the separate `bulkInvId`/weight path.
- **Spot-price resilience — no more "cannot fetch price → restart" (v10.60):** the old `fetchSpotPrices` held `goldPriceOz`/`silverPriceOz` in memory only (start `null`, never persisted), gave the fetches **no timeout**, and showed the "Cannot fetch live gold price" banner on **any single failed refresh**. So after the app sat open a while on desktop (screen sleep/wake, wifi blip) one failed 5-min cycle raised the banner and only a restart (fresh successful fetch) cleared it. Fixed with four changes: (1) **persist + restore** the last good price via `localStorage 'jpro_spot'` (`_saveSpotPrices`/`_loadSpotPrices`, restored before the first fetch) so a blip never blanks the price; (2) a **12 s `AbortController` timeout** per request (`_spotFetch`) so a stalled socket can't hang the cycle; (3) the banner now shows **only when the price is genuinely stale** — no successful gold fetch for `SPOT_STALE_MS` (30 min) — tracked by `_spotLastOk`, instead of on the first failure; (4) **self-healing recovery** — a `SPOT_RETRY_MS` (60 s) retry after any failure, plus an immediate refetch on `window 'online'` and on tab refocus (`visibilitychange`), so the price recovers without a restart. An `_spotFetching` in-flight guard stops the interval/retry/focus triggers from piling up concurrent fetches. `showSpotWarning` and the manual-price fallback are unchanged.
- **Inventory-total appreciation % (v10.62):** the Stock summary header shows an owner/manager-only gain % under **both** the "Total Retail Value" and "Metal Value" totals (`#invStatRetailGain` / `#invStatMetalGain`, green up / red down, same visual language as the per-item card line; blank for employees and when there's no baseline). Computed in `renderInvSummary` as a **count-independent aggregate** (like the v10.58 group line): each in-stock piece's live value **and** its baseline enter the running sums together, so adding or selling stock never moves the %, only real metal-price movement does. **Retail** baseline = each item's existing add-date `initialRetail` (`Σ live retail` vs `Σ initialRetail`; no data changes — retail was deliberately left "as is"). **Metal** baseline uses a NEW per-item field **`initialMetalValue`** (the item's `metalValue` at add time) resolved by helper `invMetalBaseline(item, pureGrams)`: coins/raw-metal lots use their existing `initialMelt`; regular gold/silver jewelry uses `initialMetalValue` when present (new items), else falls back **on the fly** to the piece's pure grams × the **20 May 2025** spot in `METAL_BASELINE_ANCHOR` (`{gold:2921.38, silver:29.339}` €/oz — the owner's main buying date; change these two numbers to re-anchor all legacy stock, nothing is written to the DB). `initialMetalValue` is captured going forward at the same create paths that set `initialRetail` — the `renderInvList` self-heal (label items), scan-to-inventory, and "add similar" (re-baselined, not inherited); restock inherits it via `Object.assign` like `initialRetail`; edit does NOT set it (legacy pieces keep the May-2025 anchor). Both coins and regular jewelry contribute to the metal % since the Metal Value total includes them. `initialMetalValue` is NOT in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (internal-only). **No mass write / no backfill** — existing records are untouched; the anchor is computed at read time.
- **Greek all-caps accent removal (v10.63):** Greek typography drops the tonos on words rendered in ALL CAPS (ΑΛΥΣΙΔΑ, not ΑΛΥΣΊΔΑ) while keeping the dialytika (¨). Two mechanisms: (1) **CSS-uppercased text** — declaring the content language `el` makes browsers apply Greek casing to `text-transform: uppercase`, which strips the tonos automatically (verified: it strips even from already-uppercase input). `jewelprice.htm` was already `<html lang="el">` (default lang is `'el'`) so its CSS-uppercased Greek — inventory card types, metal tags, filter chips, headers, and even the JS-`toUpperCase()` hero label — already rendered correctly. **`showroom.htm` was the actual bug** (static `<html lang="en">`): its `.card-type` («ΑΛΥΣΙΔΑ») and `.card-tag` metal pill («ΧΡΥΣΟΣ 14K») kept the accent. Fixed by setting `document.documentElement.lang = lang` inside `applyLang()`, so switching to Greek now flips the document language and the browser strips the tonos (verified live: ΑΛΥΣΙΔΑ / ΧΡΥΣΟΣ / ΜΕΝΤΑΓΙΟΝ / ΧΕΙΡΟΠΟΙΗΤΟ). (2) **Text that bypasses CSS** — three hardcoded all-caps accented literals in `jewelprice.htm` were corrected at the source: the `addVendorBtn` label `ΠΡΟΣΘΉΚΗ`→`ΠΡΟΣΘΗΚΗ`, and the two jsPDF-drawn share-doc titles `ΚΑΤΆΛΟΓΟΣ`→`ΚΑΤΑΛΟΓΟΣ` and `ΦΎΛΛΟ ΠΑΡΑΓΓΕΛΊΑΣ`→`ΦΥΛΛΟ ΠΑΡΑΓΓΕΛΙΑΣ` (canvas/PDF text is not affected by CSS `text-transform`). A strict search confirmed these were the only hardcoded all-caps accented Greek strings; the promo-canvas type/stones are English. Showroom-side change did not need an `APP_VERSION` bump on its own, but the shared change ships as v10.63.
- **Thumbnails show the whole photo (v10.64):** item-photo thumbnails used `object-fit: cover`, which fills the square by cropping — for the shop's tall portrait necklace photos that sliced off the pendant/clasp and left only the middle of the chain, so a piece couldn't be identified from the thumbnail without enlarging it. Changed to `object-fit: contain; background:#fff` (whole image, letterboxed on a white backdrop that blends with the white product shots) in three item-photo spots: the inventory single card (`buildCard`, ~L12471) and grouped card representative (`buildGroupCard`, ~L12605), the Calc customer price-bar thumbnail (`#cpbPhotoThumb`, shown when a scanned/looked-up item appears in the floating price box), and the duplicate-QR warning preview (`_showDupQrWarning`, ~L4987). Left as `cover` (correct there): the scanner video feed and the bulk-chain sell modal photo (`#bulkSellPhotoImg`). CSS-only; no data/behaviour change.
- **Uncropped form previews + photo in the enlarged price view (v10.65):** two follow-ups to the v10.64 thumbnail work. (1) The shared photo-picker preview box (`renderPhotoPicker`, the small square next to Camera/Gallery used by **label creation**, **scan-to-inventory** and **Edit item**) was still `object-fit: cover` in both its freshly-captured (`_photoInputChanged`, ~L10733) and existing-photo (~L10757) branches — switched both to `object-fit: contain; background:#fff` so the whole piece shows. (2) **New:** the maximized/customer-facing price overlay (`priceFullscreenOverlay`, opened by `togglePriceFullscreen()` when the final-price bar is tapped) now shows the item's photo — a new `#pfsPhoto` `<img>` after `#pfsDesc` (max-width `min(72vw,300px)`, max-height 44vh, white card, natural aspect), populated from `lastCalcResult.photoUrl` and hidden when there's no photo. `lastCalcResult.photoUrl` is set by the gold/silver `calculate()` path already; added the same assignment to the **Other-type** calc path (~L21896) so the photo (and the quote modal that also reads `photoUrl`) works for Other items too. Uses the small (~480px) `photoUrl` for fast load — crisp at ~300px. CSS/markup + one assignment; no data change.
- **Enlarged price-view polish (v10.67):** two follow-ups to the v10.65 `#pfsPhoto` addition. (1) **No scroll** — with the photo added the overlay could exceed the viewport and scroll; the overlay is now `overflow:hidden` with tightened padding/gap, and `#pfsPhoto` uses `flex:1 1 auto; min-height:0; width:min(76vw,320px); max-height:46vh; object-fit:contain` so it **fills the remaining vertical space and shrinks on short screens** — the whole customer card always fits without scrolling (verified 812px and 620px tall). NB: the photo needs a *definite* `width` (not `max-width`) or the flex cross-axis collapses it to a sliver. (2) **Closes on tab change** — `showPanel()` now hides `#priceFullscreenOverlay` at the top of every tab switch, so the full-screen price never bleeds/creeps behind another tab. CSS/markup + one guard line.
- **Full changelog on its own page (v10.66):** the changelog history was moved out of `jewelprice.htm` into a new standalone **`changelog.htm`** so the app file stops bloating (see the "current entry inline" rule above). The Info-tab changelog section now shows only the current version (from the single inline entry) plus a **"📜 View the full changelog →"** link (added in `renderAbout`, opens `changelog.htm` in a new tab). `changelog.htm` is a **self-contained bilingual page** (EL/EN toggle reading `localStorage 'jpro_lang'`, dark/gold styling matching `item.htm`) with the **complete v9.29→present history embedded as a `CHANGELOG` JSON array** (137 entries at creation). It reads NO Firebase data — the history is baked into the file — so it carries no security/offline concerns. The full history was reconstructed from git via a one-off extractor (in the session scratchpad: `extract_changelog.py` unions every commit's `changelog` arrays; `gen_changelog_html.py` renders the page). Showroom-style page → does **not** bump `APP_VERSION` on its own, but the paired `jewelprice.htm` change (inline trim + link) ships as v10.66.
- **User Manual moved to standalone `manual.htm` + fully rewritten (v10.68):** the inline manual (in `ABOUT[lang].manual`) was badly out of date (7 sections: pricing, vendors, settings, sync, team, sales, labels) and missing everything added since — Stock, similar-items groups, coins, metal/scrap, clients, quotes/sharing, showroom, backups, OCR scanning, install/offline. Both inline `manual` arrays were **removed** from `jewelprice.htm` (net size reduction) and the manual rendering dropped from `renderAbout`; the Info-tab "📖 User Manual" card is now a button that opens **`manual.htm`** (`window.open('manual.htm','_blank')`). `manual.htm` is a **self-contained bilingual (EL/EN) accordion** of **22 sections** covering the current app (a "Returns & exchange card" section was added after Sales in v11.46; note the manual still predates the whole **Desk** area — reservations, repairs and gift certificates have no section yet), styled like `changelog.htm`/`item.htm`, reads `localStorage 'jpro_lang'`, Expand/Collapse-all controls, first section open. It's static content (no Firebase) — no security surface. To edit the manual, edit `manual.htm`'s `MANUAL = {el:[…],en:[…]}` object directly (does not need an `APP_VERSION` bump on its own; the paired `jewelprice.htm` inline-removal + link ships as v10.68).
- **Inventory CSV export for the accountant (v10.69):** an owner/manager-only Settings section (`exportSection`, gated in `applyRoleUI`, near Data Backup) → **"📊 Export Inventory (CSV)"** opens a bottom-sheet (`openInvExportSheet`/`closeInvExportSheet`, markup `#invExportSheet`) with: **Status** (in-stock only [default] / include sold), a **Type** checklist (only types actually present; **coin & metal unchecked by default** per the owner's "no coins" need), **Value columns** toggles (**Cost / Retail / Pure-metal-grams**, all default on — the three the owner chose), a **Group-by-type-with-subtotals** toggle (default on), and a **date-range** (by add-date) with one-tap **Season chips**. **UI note (v10.74):** the sheet's option controls are tappable **`.inv-fs-pill` buttons** backed by a `_ieOpts` state object (`{status, types:Set, cols:Set, group}`), **not native checkboxes** — the global `input{-webkit-appearance:none;width:100%}` rule rendered real checkboxes as unusable full-width empty boxes on iOS. `openInvExportSheet` is **async and awaits `loadInventory()` when the cache is empty** (the sheet can open without the Stock tab ever running, which otherwise left "Types included" blank); `runInvExport` reads options from `_ieOpts`.
