(`_invExportSeasonChips`/`pickInvExportSeason`, Apr 1–Nov 30 window, reusing the Clients pattern). `runInvExport()` builds the file **read-only** (no data writes): rows sorted by `INV_JEWELRY_TYPES` order then ref code, one row per record (`Ref, Type, Description, Metal, Karat, Color, Weight, [Pure], [Cost], [Retail], Date added, Status`), **per-type subtotal rows** + a **grand-total row**, then a **SUMMARY block** (pure gold/silver grams by karat, totals, cost/retail totals, piece count, and a **"Breakdown by type" table** — v10.75 — of piece counts per type split by metal with a per-karat detail formatted `18K (20) · 14K (20)` / `925 (50)` — parentheses, NOT `925:30`, which Excel auto-converts to a time value; v10.76) and a top **valuation stamp** (date/time + the gold & silver €/oz spot used — retail floats with spot, so the accountant needs the price basis). **Cost** per item = `purchaseCost` if set else `calcInvItemPrice(item).baseCost` (metal+labour+stones; melt for coins/scrap); **Retail** = `calcInvItemPrice(item).retail`; **Pure grams** via `_invPureGrams` (coin `coinPure` / metal `metalPure` / weight×purity). **File format (v10.77):** **always a standard comma-delimited CSV with decimal points** + UTF-8 BOM, in BOTH languages — only the TEXT (headers/labels/metal/status) is translated. (Originally Greek used `;` + decimal-comma per "Greek Excel" convention, but that scrambled in the owner's actual comma-splitting Excel — every `986,42` was cut in half; a Greek-locale desktop Excel is the only place `;`/comma-decimal is "right", and it isn't what the owner uses.) Text fields are quote-escaped; numeric cells are never quoted so they parse as numbers. `stkExportReport` (inventory-check report) uses the same comma/point convention. Helper `_invItemDate(id,item)` resolves an add-date (stored `dateAdded`, else the `inv_<ms>_…` id timestamp). i18n keys `export*`. **`other`-type cost (v10.71 fix):** Other items don't store a plain `purchaseCost`; their gross purchase price is encoded as the **QR code's last numeric segment** and the **supplier (vendor) discount** is applied on top — so the CSV uses helper `_invOtherCost(item)` (gross×(1−vendor.discount%), skipping any stone suffix, falling back to a stored `purchaseCost`) to report the real cost, exactly reproducing the "Cost after discount" the scan breakdown shows. (Earlier the export naively read `purchaseCost` and showed €0 for Other items.) This is **step 1 of the two-part end-of-season toolset** (see the inventory-check entry below, which reuses the same CSV conventions for its variance export).
- **End-of-season inventory check / stocktake (v10.70):** an owner-activated, otherwise-invisible **mode** (NOT a permanent tab) for physically reconciling stock at season close. Self-contained module (`STK_PATH='/jewelprice_stocktake'`, state `_stk`/`_stkActive`) that **reuses the existing QR decode stack** (`decodeQrFromCanvas`/`canDecodeQr`) via its **own** camera + decode loop so it never touches the daily Calc scanner. **Flow:** owner (Settings → `stocktakeSection`, owner/manager-gated in `applyRoleUI`) taps **Start** (`stkStart` → PUT `{active,startedAt,startedBy}`, clearing any prior scans). A floating **`#stkBanner`** ("📋 Inventory check — Scan") then shows on **every** device (all roles), surfaced on app open via `stkRefreshBanner()` called from `autoSync()` + on `visibilitychange`. Tapping it opens the scan overlay (`#stkOverlay`, `stkOpenScanner` — **async, awaits `loadInventory()` if `_inventoryCache` is empty (v10.72 fix): the scanner opens straight from the banner without the Stock tab ever running, so without this every scan resolved to "unknown code" against an empty cache**): live QR camera (`stkStartCamera`/`stkDecodeLoop`, ~1.3 s cooldown after each hit so identical grouped/coin twins are each counted as the user re-presents them) **plus a manual code-entry field** (works on iPhone/Safari, where `canDecodeQr()` is false and the camera path is hidden). Each scan (`stkOnScan`→`stkResolve`) resolves the raw value to an item by **qrCode, else printed ref code** (handles `item.htm?qr=` URLs too), shows instant feedback (✓ ref+name / `[sold!]` / coin-not-counted / unknown), bumps the local "You scanned" counter, and **POSTs a scan record**. **Live duplicate guard (v10.73):** on scanner open, `stkOpenScanner` builds `_stk.expected` (in-stock, non-coin/metal count **per qrCode**) and seeds `_stk.counts` from the session's existing scans (Firebase, so reopening/other devices are respected); in `stkOnScan`, a "countable" in-stock piece whose accepted count for that qr has already reached `expected` is rejected on the spot with a **⚠️ "Already scanned"** warning + double buzz and is **NOT recorded** (so it can't inflate the count or create a false over-count) — while grouped/coin twins that legitimately share a qr are still accepted up to their in-stock count, shown as a `(n/N)` progress tag. Sold/coin/metal/unknown scans bypass the guard (sold-on-shelf & unknown are still recorded as report signals). `{qr,ref,by,at,manual}` to `/jewelprice_stocktake/scans`; a live **Total (all devices)** count polls `scans?shallow=true`. **Finish** (`stkFinishCompare`, owner-only footer/Settings) pulls all scans, runs **`stkBuildReport`** — a **count-aware** comparison (grouped/coin pieces legitimately share a QR, so it compares scanned-count-per-QR vs expected-count-per-QR): **Expected** = in-stock, **non-coin, non-metal**, tagged (`qrCode`) items (**coins & scrap excluded per owner's rule**); buckets = ✅ accounted, 🔴 **missing** (unscanned in-stock pieces, listed with ref/name/retail value + total missing €), 🟡 to-review (**over-count**, **scanned-but-marked-sold** = found-on-shelf/mis-marked, **unknown code**), and coin/metal scans are silently skipped. The report (`stkShowReport`, `#stkReport`) shows summary tiles + lists and a **CSV export** (`stkExportReport`, same Greek-Excel-safe BOM/`;`/decimal-comma conventions as the inventory export). Finishing sets `active=false` (stops everyone scanning) but **keeps the scans** so the owner can **Resume** (`stkResume`, re-arms `active=true`) if a drawer was missed; **Cancel** (`stkCancel`) discards. A lightweight year-over-year snapshot (summary + missing ref codes) is saved to `/jewelprice_stocktake_history/<ms>` (`stkSaveHistory`). **Read-only** w.r.t. inventory — the comparison never mutates stock; it's a report the owner acts on manually. **Privacy:** `/jewelprice_stocktake` and `/jewelprice_stocktake_history` are private nodes (default-deny DB rules; the app writes with the secret) — never public, not in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`, no rules change needed. i18n keys `stk*`.
- **Reference-code number recycling (v10.78):** ref-code numbers come from ONE global counter at Firebase `/jewelprice_seq_counter` (`getNextSeqId`); `formatSeqId(seqId, type)` just prefixes it per type (CR/RG/…), so the numbers are shared across all types, not per-type. Deleting an item used to leave its number permanently unused (a gap). `getNextSeqId` now **recycles the lowest freed number**: it loads the full inventory (if not cached), builds the set of seqIds in use by ANY current item (in-stock OR sold), and returns the lowest `1..maxUsed` gap without touching the counter; only when there are no gaps does it take `max(counter,maxUsed)+1` and PUT the counter (self-heals a stale counter). Falls back to plain `counter+1` if the inventory can't load (never hands out a number that's actually in use). Sold items keep their number (they're still in the cache), so only truly-deleted numbers are reused. Caveat: if a deleted item's physical tag is still in circulation, its number could be reused — acceptable since deletion implies the piece/tag is gone. **"Add similar items" blank weight = group weight (v10.79):** in `invAddSimilar`, leaving the weight box blank now **defaults to the source/group's own weight** (helper `_effWeight()` = typed value, else `parseFloat(source.weight)`), used by both the preview and `addBatch` — so for identical pieces the owner just sets a quantity and prints. A placeholder + label hint ("blank = same as group (Xg)") makes this explicit; the guard only blocks when neither the box nor the source has a usable weight. (This reverses the short-lived v10.78 behaviour that required a weight — the owner wants blank to mean "same as the group".)
- **Navigation restructure — 5 tabs + ☰ More menu (v10.80):** the bottom nav is now 5 everyday tabs (Calc · Stock/inventory · Sales · Clients · **Desk**); **Vendors, Sync, Settings, Info moved into a ☰ "More" dropdown** in the header (`#moreMenu`, `toggleMoreMenu`/`closeMoreMenu`, `.more-item` styling; those `nav-*` buttons keep their ids so `showPanel`/`applyRoleUI` are unchanged and role-gating still applies). New **`panel-desk`** is a combined container with a segmented toggle (`deskShow('reservations'|'orders'|'repairs'|'gift')`, `.desk-seg`, a 2x2 grid since v12.65): **Reservations** (v10.80), **Orders** (v12.65), **Repairs** (v10.89) and **Gift Cards & Credit** (v11.41). `nav-desk` is all-roles; `#panel-desk` added to the desktop 560px-centred rule.
- **Reservations / Layaway (v10.80 first slice, reworked v10.81):** hold in-stock pieces for a customer with optional deposit + installment tracking; a reservation is a **"held sale"** created **from within the sale flow** (not a manual form). Stored as an array at Firebase **`/jewelprice_reservations`** (private; `getReservations`/`saveReservationsLocal`/`push`/`pullReservationsFromCloud`, pulled in `autoSync` for all roles; `localStorage 'jpro_reservations'`); numbered `H-####` via `/jewelprice_reservation_counter`. **Entry points (v10.81):** (1) **Calc single item** — the price bar's SOLD button was split into **SOLD | RESERVE** (`reserveSingleFromCalc`); (2) **Cart (multiple)** — a **RESERVE** button next to "Sell all" (`reserveCartItems`); (3) **Stock item card** — a 🔒 lock icon in the card top row (`invReserveOrView` → reserve, or open the reservation if already reserved; `invReserve`). All three build **resItems** via `_resItemFromResult(name,result)` (calc/cart — locks in `result.finalPrice` incl. discounts/rounding, resolves the inv item by qr/seqRef; supports bulk chains) or `_resItemFromInv(id)` (stock, at live retail), then call the shared **`openReserveDialog(items, source)`**: customer **name/phone/email**, editable **agreed price** (default Σ item prices), optional **deposit** (amount+method — the squished-select bug is fixed with `flex:0 0 auto;width:auto`), and a **"hold until"** date (default +14d). `_resDialogSave` writes the reservation `{id, reservationNo, clientName, clientPhone, clientEmail, items[{invId,isBulk,bulkInvId,soldWeight,qr,ref,desc,photoUrl,price,cost}], itemIds, agreedTotal, payments[], holdUntil, status:'active'|'completed'|'cancelled'|'expired', dateCreated, ...}` and flags the discrete items reserved. **Reserved items** carry `item.reservationId` (`_resMarkItems` sets/clears + re-syncs the public feed) and are **excluded from the showroom** (`publicProjection` returns null when `reservationId` set). **Stuck-flag self-heal (v10.96):** `_resMarkItems` now **loads inventory if the cache is empty** (previously a cancel with Stock never opened silently no-op'd, leaving items flagged forever), and `_resReconcileReservedFlags()` (run fire-and-forget from `renderInvList`, gated on `_resLoaded`) clears any item whose `reservationId` isn't an **active** reservation — so cancelled/deleted holds unlock on the next Stock open; `_resDelete` also releases its items directly. Desk → Reservations = **list** (`renderReservations`, active-first + search, no more "New" button — creation is from the entry points) + **detail** (`openReservationDetail` — items, agreed/paid/balance, hold-until, email, payment log, **Add payment** `_resAddPayment`, **Cancel** `_resCancel` owner/manager-only → releases items). **Completion & lifecycle (v10.82):** detail has **"Complete & sell"** (`_resComplete`) — builds sale items from the reservation `items` (discrete → `{id,item,price}`; bulk → `{id,item,price,isBulk,soldWeight,cost}` which deducts grams), stashes `window._completingReservationId` + `window._cartSaleItems`, calls `openMultiSaleModal` at the agreed prices (carries customer name + a deposit note); when `confirmSale` finishes it calls **`_resFinalizeComplete`** (status→`completed`, clears reserved flags — the items are now sold). **Auto-expiry** `_resCheckExpiry` (run from `autoSync`): any active reservation past `holdUntil` (string compare vs `todayStr()`) → status `expired` + items released back to stock/showroom. **Reserved filter:** the Stock Status group has a **🔒 Reserved** pill (cross-cutting flag like grouped/hidden — `renderInvList` requires `item.reservationId`; `invFilterReserved` i18n). **Sale guard:** `invMarkSold` warns if the item is reserved ("Open reservation" vs "Sell anyway"). **Printed/shared reservation form (v10.88, reworked v10.90):** the detail sheet has **separate 🖨 Print and 📤 Share buttons**. The form is built as **A4 HTML in the same elegant style as the printed quote** (`buildReservationHTML(r)` — branded LEKKAS JEWELRY header + maps QR, `✦ Reservation ✦` title + `resNoFmt`/date, customer block, **item list with per-item photos** (`it.photoUrl`), ref + desc + gold price, agreed/deposit/balance totals in the quote's gold style, hold-until line, bilingual terms, **signature line + dashed stamp box**). **Print** → `printReservationForm(id)` → `openQuoteWindow(html, false)` (opens the branded page and goes straight to the browser print dialog, popup-blocked fallback = overlay). **Share** → `shareReservationForm(id)` → `shareQuoteAsPDF(html, title, false, 'reservation-H-####.pdf')` (renders the HTML via html2canvas → paginated jsPDF → `navigator.share({files})` / download). `shareQuoteAsPDF`/`downloadQuotePDF` gained an optional `fileName` param (defaults preserved). The v10.88 jsPDF `buildReservationPDF` was **removed** (replaced by the HTML form). All dates via `fmtDate`. **Cash/receipt + initial price/discount (v10.91):** the reserve dialog has a **single 💵 Cash toggle** (pressed = cash, unpressed = receipt — the ONE pattern used everywhere, via shared `_cashToggleHtml(id)` / `_toggleCashBtn(id)` / `_isCashBtn(id)` built on the sales `_setCashBtnState`/`_cashLabel`; v10.94 replaced the earlier two-button segmented control) whose state is read as `res.cashSale` and **carried into the sale at completion** — `_resComplete` calls `_setCashBtnState(saleCashToggle, r.cashSale)` after `openMultiSaleModal` so the sale inherits it (cash = no VAT extracted, matching the sales model; the agreed total is unchanged either way). Each reservation item captures **`listPrice`** (pre-discount retail — `result.retail || finalPrice + discountAmt` for calc/cart **and bulk chains** (v10.95 — bulk carries `retail = soldWeight × price-per-gram` and a derived `discountAmt` from the rounding-target, so by-the-gram chains show their discount too), `calcInvItemPrice(...).retail` for stock). **Per-item discount display (v10.93):** `buildReservationHTML` shows, in each item block, the **list price (strikethrough) → −X% (−€Y) → final price** when the item's discount is **≥ 5%** (`DISC_MIN`); the `_lp(it)` helper falls back to the **live inventory retail** (`calcInvItemPrice` on `it.invId`) for reservations made before `listPrice` was captured, so old holds still show the discount (uses current gold, minor drift). The totals show only a **reservation-level Extra discount** (Subtotal + −% when `agreedTotal < Σ item prices` by ≥5%), then **Agreed price**, a **VAT breakdown** for receipts (**hidden entirely for cash** — cash = no VAT anywhere), a **Transaction: 💵 Cash / 🧾 With receipt** line, deposit, balance; the in-app detail sheet shows the same transaction line. **Quotes & certificates cash toggle (v10.93):** the quote modal has a **single 💵 Cash toggle** (`#quoteCashToggle`, shared `_toggleCashBtn`/`_isCashBtn`; `printQuote`/`shareQuote` set `window._quoteIsCash = _isCashBtn('quoteCashToggle')`, reset off in `openQuoteModal`/`reprintQuote`); `buildQuoteHTML` and `buildMultiQuoteHTML` **hide the Price-before-VAT + VAT rows when `window._quoteIsCash`** (certificates never showed VAT). **All three cash toggles are the same single-button pattern (v10.94)** — see the `consistent-ui-patterns` memory. *(NB: the repair claim form `buildRepairPDF` is still the self-contained jsPDF version — convert it to the same HTML style if the owner wants parity.)* **Multi-reserve + badge (v10.99):** Stock **select-mode** has a **🔒 Reserve** action (`invReserveSelected` → `_resItemFromInv` for each selected id → `openReserveDialog`, all-roles), and reserved pieces now show a prominent **`🔒 Reserved`** body badge (`_reservedBadge` in `buildCard`) in addition to the top-row lock icon. Full design in `NEXT_FEATURES_DESIGN.md`. **Dates:** all user-facing dates go through `fmtDate` (`state.dateLocale`) — v10.82 fixed the PDF-header and notification dates that keyed off `lang` instead (see the `dates-use-fmtdate` memory).
- **Repairs & Custom Orders (v10.89):** the second Desk segment (`deskShow('repairs')` → `renderRepairs`). Open a ticket for a customer's **repair** (photo of their item + **required cost estimate**, single or `from–to` range; NO deposit — the jewel is the deposit) or a **custom order** to make (photo/reference + estimate + **required deposit**). Stored as an array at private Firebase **`/jewelprice_repairs`** (`getRepairs`/`saveRepairsLocal`/`pushRepairsToCloud`/`pullRepairsFromCloud`, pulled in `autoSync` for all roles; `localStorage 'jpro_repairs'`); numbered `R-####` via `/jewelprice_repair_counter`. Added to `BACKUP_PATHS` (alongside `reservations`, which was also missing). **Never published** to the showroom (customer PII/photos). Record shape: `{id:'rep_<ms>', ticketNo, type:'repair'|'custom', clientName/Phone/Email, photoUrl/photoUrlHi, description, estLow, estHigh (0 = single value), deposit, depositMethod, datePromised, status:'in_progress'|'ready'|'collected'|'cancelled', notifiedAt, finalPrice, dateCreated/Ready/Collected, createdBy}`. **Create:** `openRepairModal` — type toggle (`_repairSetType`, shows/requires the deposit block for custom), customer name with **autocomplete** (`_repClientAutocomplete`/`_repSelectClient`, own field ids), country-code phone field, email, **photo** (`renderPhotoPicker` → `_repPendingPhoto`/`Hi`, uploaded via `uploadInventoryPhoto` on save), description, estimate low/high, deposit (custom), optional ready-by date (overlay shows `fmtDate`); `_repairSave` validates (name always; estimate required for a **repair** — for a custom order both estimate and deposit are optional since v12.26, and a blank deposit saves it as a **quote**), assigns `ticketNo`, saves, `upsertClientFromSale`. **Detail** (`openRepairDetail`): photo, tappable phone (`_callPhone`), description, estimate/deposit/ready-by/final-price box; status flow **In progress → Ready** (`_repairSetStatus`, stamps `dateReady`) **→ Collected** (`_repairCollectOpen`/`_repairCollectConfirm` — final price + a Cost field (what the job actually cost you), a single 💵 Cash toggle (shared `_cashToggleHtml`/`_isCashBtn`) and a visible "Log as income" toggle button (`_repCollectToggleLog` — replaced a native checkbox that rendered invisible under the global `input{appearance:none;width:100%}` rule; state in overlay `dataset.log`/`dataset.cash`) → sets `collected`/`finalPrice`, and if logged calls **`_recordRepairIncome(price, cost, cashSale, note)`** which pushes a normal sale record (`repairIncome:true`) whose **profit mirrors a sale**: `exVat = cashSale ? price : price/(1+vat/100)`, `profit = exVat − cost`, `cost` stored — so day revenue/profit and the cash (off-the-books) treatment match the sales model. The detail box shows final price (+💵/🧾), cost, and owner-only profit. **Notify customer** (`_repairNotify`, shown when in_progress/ready): pre-filled bilingual "ready for pickup" message via **WhatsApp** (`wa.me/<digits>`), **SMS** (`sms:`) or **Email** (`mailto:`); stamps `notifiedAt` on first send. **Claim form** (`shareRepairForm` → `buildRepairPDF`): branded A4 PDF (own copy of the LEKKAS JEWELRY canvas header + Noto fonts, like `buildReservationPDF`) — title `REPAIR TICKET`/`CUSTOM ORDER`, ticket no/date, **embedded item photo** (loaded from `photoUrlHi||photoUrl`), customer block, description, estimate/deposit/ready-by rows, type-specific bilingual terms (custom = non-refundable deposit), signature line + stamp box; shared via `navigator.share({files})` with download fallback. **Cancel/Delete = owner/manager only** (`_repairCancel`/`_repairDelete`, `isOwnerOrManager` gated). All dates via `fmtDate`. i18n: `repNewTicket`/`repSearchPh` (rest inline bilingual). Full design in `NEXT_FEATURES_DESIGN.md` (Feature B).
- **Stale Stock lens (v10.99, owner/manager):** the last design feature (Feature A) — spot long-sitting pieces and the room to discount them. (1) **"Time in Stock" filter** (`_invFilters.seasonMin`, pills 1+/2+/3+/5+ seasons) in the Stock filter sheet, gated to owner/manager like the gain filter; `_invSeasonsInStock(id,item)` = calendar years since add-date (store runs one season a year). (2) **"🕰️ Stale Stock" report** — a filter-bar button (`#invStaleBtn`, owner-only via `applyRoleUI`) opens `openStaleReport()`: a threshold picker (1/2/3/5, default 2) + a list (oldest first) of in-stock, non-coin/metal pieces past the threshold, each showing photo, ref, description, **time in stock**, **retail**, **metal-value floor** (`calcInvItemPrice.metalValue`), **negotiation room** (retail − floor) and **gain %**, with per-row **Open** (`invEdit`) / **Share** (`invShareSingle`) / **To showroom** (clears `hideFromShowroom`) and a **CSV export** (`_staleExport`, same comma/point/BOM convention). All read-only w.r.t. stock. Discount-first; the metal floor is shown so melting stays an obvious option. i18n `stale*`/`invFilterAge`/`invSeasonWord`. **This completes NEXT_FEATURES_DESIGN.md — all three features (Stale Stock, Repairs, Reservations) are now built.**
- **Showroom facet counts collapse groups (v10.99):** `showroom.htm` `_navFacetResultCount` now folds pieces sharing `groupId + color` into one before counting, so the nav-drawer category counts match the number of cards shown (was counting every individual piece).
- **Light theme (v11.05):** Settings → Appearance toggle (🌙 Dark default / ☀️ Light), persisted in `localStorage 'jpro_theme'`, applied via `data-theme="light"` on `<html>` (a no-flash inline script at the top of `<body>` sets it before paint; `applyTheme`/`initThemeToggle` handle the toggle + boot). Colours are centralised: `:root` holds the dark palette + `--waNN` variables for the 216 translucent-white surfaces (white-on-dark); `:root[data-theme="light"]` overrides them to ivory/charcoal/deep-gold + warm-dark surfaces. **Dark-theme values are byte-identical to before, so dark is unchanged.** New constant `--ink` (#0F0D09) = text ON gold/accent buttons (stays dark in both themes — replaced the 34 `color:var(--dark)` on-accent uses). A few surfaces stay dark in both themes on purpose (fullscreen customer price display, floating select bar, update banner, toasts); the bottom nav + price bar get a light skin. Net size added ≈ 1.9 KB (colours only). Design pass tracked in `DESIGN_AUDIT.md` (items A safety-confirms / B modal close+danger standard / C light theme — all done).
- **Menu/filter/selection UX + gold-color variants (v11.06):** five fixes. (1) **More menu z-index** — the header `☰ More` dropdown (`#moreMenu`) was painted *under* the Stock/Sales panels: those panels are `position:fixed; z-index:100` and live OUTSIDE `.app` (which is `position:relative; z-index:1`), so a dropdown nested in the header could never exceed z:1 at the root. `toggleMoreMenu` now re-parents `#moreMenu` to `<body>` and positions it `fixed` under the `#moreBtn` (via `getBoundingClientRect`), escaping `.app`'s stacking context. (2) **Greek gender filter labels** — `genderWomen/Men/Children/Unisex` existed only in the EN i18n dict, so the Stock filter's gender pills showed English in Greek; added the Greek keys. (3) **Comma multi-term search** — the Stock search (`renderInvList`) now splits the query on commas and matches an item if ANY term hits (OR), so `CR0100, CR0200` shows both. (4) **Selection UX** — new `_invShowSelectedOnly` lens: a **"Selected"** eye-toggle in the select bar (`invToggleShowSelected`/`_syncShowSelBtn`, i18n `invShowSel`) narrows the list to only ticked items (unpress → all return; auto-reset on exit). Plus **long-press any card** (`_initInvLongPress`, ~500 ms, on `#panel-inventory`) enters select mode with that piece ticked — so a selection can start from deep in the list without scrolling up to the Select button (swallows the trailing tap; a >10px move cancels it as a scroll). (5) **"Add similar items" gold color + per-variant photo** — `invAddSimilar` gained a **Gold Color** `<select>` (shown when the source is gold) and a **photo picker** (`renderPhotoPicker`, existing = source photo). `addBatch` applies the chosen `goldColor` to every new piece and, when a photo was captured, uploads it once (`uploadInventoryPhoto`) and stamps `photoUrl`/`photoUrlHi` on the batch (blank → reuse the source's photo). The app **group card colour breakdown** (`buildGroupCard`) now buckets a blank-decorative-colour gold piece by its **gold colour** (`goldColorLabel`), so a yellow+white set reads "1 Gold · 1 White Gold". The **showroom** now splits a group by `groupId + color + goldColor` (grid card, lightbox in-stock count, and `_navFacetResultCount` in `showroom.htm`), so different gold colours of one group show as **separate cards/images** (goldColor was already in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`). App still folds a mixed-gold-colour group into ONE card with the breakdown.
- **Select-bar minimize (v11.07):** the Stock select-mode floating action bar (`#invShareBar`) gained a **minimize** button (chevron, in row 1 next to the ✕ exit) mirroring the Calc price-bar's `togglePriceBar`. `toggleInvShareBar()` hides the bar and shows a small centred **restore pill** (`#invShareBarPill`, chevron-up + live selected count) so items/buttons sitting behind the bar (e.g. the last card's Mark Sold/Edit/Delete when it's the bottom of the list) stay reachable. A `_invBarMinimized` flag makes the minimized state sticky: `updateInvShareBar` respects it (keeps the bar hidden, just refreshes the pill count) instead of force-re-showing the bar on the next selection change. Entering/leaving select mode (`toggleInvSelectMode`) resets the flag and hides the pill.
- **Deselect drops out under the "selected only" lens (v11.08):** while `_invShowSelectedOnly` is active, deselecting a card now removes it from the view immediately — `invSelectToggleItem` (and `invSelectToggleGroup`) delete the card's DOM node (`closest('.inv-card, .inv-group-card')`) on uncheck instead of leaving an unchecked card in place, so a "change all these photos" pass narrows to just the pieces still to do. Display-only (no data write, no full re-render/scroll jump); the lens's row filter still rebuilds correctly on the next real render.
- **Label modal saves ONCE per session + karat/rate in the category dropdown (v11.09):** two fixes. (1) **Reprint no longer duplicates the item** — all four buttons in the label modal (Print, Copy, Print QR only, Save QR as image) call `saveToInventoryFromLabel`, which had no idempotency guard, so pressing Print a second time (the natural reaction to a tag that printed crooked) added the SAME piece to stock again — as did pressing Copy and then Print. The function is now a thin wrapper around the (renamed) `_saveToInventoryFromLabel`, guarded by a `labelData._invSaveState` flag (`'saving'` → `'saved'`) set **synchronously** before the first `await`, so even a rapid double-tap of the fire-and-forget handlers saves once. The flag lives on the label `data` object, which `openLabelModal`/`_openLabelModalInner` rebuild per session, so a genuinely new piece still saves normally; a **failed** save resets the flag to `null` (via `try/finally`) so the next press retries, and `_saveToInventoryFromLabel` now returns `saveInventoryItem`'s ok and only writes `_inventoryCache[id]` on success (a phantom cache entry would otherwise trip the duplicate-QR guard on that retry). On success `_lblMarkSaved` shows a green `#lblSavedNote` ("✓ Saved to stock — PE0599") and relabels `#lblPrintBtn` to "Reprint"/«Επανεκτύπωση». Trade-off (accepted): edits made in the modal *after* the save (description, photo, gender…) are not written — the note points the owner at Stock → ✏️ Edit; adding a second identical piece stays the explicit "🔗 Add similar items" flow (which already handles qty + group + N copies). (2) **Category dropdown shows karat + rate** — new shared helpers `_catKey(c)` (= `name|karat`), `catKaratLabel(c)` (`'18'`→`18K`, `'925s'`→`925`, `'—'`→`''`) and `catOptionLabel(c, isOtherType)` render an option as `Σκουλαρίκια (18K · €22.00/γρ.)` / `Earrings (18K · €22.00/g)`, handling per-item (`📦 ανά τεμάχιο`), flat (`€40.00 σταθερό`) and Other-type vendors (`×2.5`); used by `updateWorkTypeOptions` (Calc) and `updateBulkCatSelect` (bulk chain, which had its own English-only variant). **Why it matters:** the printed code already encodes the karat (`ΚΑΛ18Σ` vs `ΚΑΛ14Σ` — same category abbreviation, different karat), so a vendor can hold **the same category name at two karats** and the codes stay unique — no rename needed. Renaming a category is in fact **unsafe**: the category letter is derived from the name (`getCatAbbr`), and adding a word turns the single-word rule into the multi-word one (`Σκουλαρίκια` → `Σ`, but `Σκουλαρίκια 18κ` → `ΣΚ`), so `ΚΑΛ18Σ`→`ΚΑΛ18ΣΚ` and every already-printed tag stops resolving in `buildCodeMap`/`lookupCode`. To make same-name duplicates fully safe, the two places that matched a category **by name alone** now match by `_catKey` (name+karat): the vendor-save rate-change detector (`oldCats.find`) and `recalibrateCategoryItems`, so correcting the rate on Earrings 14K no longer re-baselines the same vendor's Earrings 18K items. Item pricing was always by `vendorIdx`/`catIdx` and is untouched.
- **Frozen category codes + duplicate category names (v11.10):** the category letters in a printed code (`ΚΑΛ18Σ` → `Σ`) were **re-derived from the category NAME** on every use (`getCatAbbr`/`buildNameToAbbr`), so renaming a category silently changed codes already printed on tags — and because letters are handed out in list order and must stay unique per vendor, renaming one category could shift a **neighbour's** letter and strand tags on a category never touched. A category now carries frozen **`abbr`** (Greek) + **`abbrLatin`** fields; the code is pinned to what its tags say and the name is free to edit. Same philosophy as the frozen `refType` (v10.20). **Freeze point:** `editVendor` computes the current letters via `catAbbrs(cats)` and stamps them on each form row's `dataset` (`addLaborRow(..., abbr, abbrLatin)` / `addVfOtherCatRow(..., abbr, abbrLatin)`) **when the form opens — before any rename can happen**; `saveVendor` reads `row.dataset.abbr/abbrLatin` back onto each category, then `_freezeCatAbbrs(cats)` mints letters for rows that lack them (i.e. newly added ones). All four save paths (`saveVendor` metal + other, add-vendor-modal metal + other) call `_freezeCatAbbrs`, so every category saved from v11.10 on is pinned. **Backward compatibility is the load-bearing part:** `catAbbrs(cats, latin)` returns an array **index-aligned with `cats`** (NOT name-keyed — two categories may now share a name); if **no** category carries a frozen letter, the vendor predates v11.10 and it falls back to the OLD name-keyed `buildNameToAbbr`/`buildLatinNameToAbbr`, so an untouched vendor's codes are byte-identical until the owner opens+saves it (verified: freeze is a no-op, rename-after-freeze changes zero codes). `buildNameToAbbr`/`getCatAbbr` are kept solely for that legacy path. **New scheme** (`deriveCatAbbr(name, used, latin)`, used only for categories created from v11.10 on): **first letter + the next letter of the name that keeps it unique within the vendor** — Σκουλαρίκια → `ΣΚ`, a second → `ΣΟ`, a third → `ΣΥ`; falls back to walking `GREEK_ALPHA_UP`/`LATIN_ALPHA_UP`. It **never returns a digit** — `parseCodeInput` reads a code's trailing digits as the weight, so a digit in the abbreviation would be swallowed into it (`ΚΑΛ14Σ2` + `4.5` → weight `24.5`). The six readers that derived letters (`buildLabelStrings`, `getLatinCodeForCat`, `buildCodeMap`, `renderVendors`, `showDebugCodes`, the printable code card) all go through `catAbbrs` by index. **Duplicate names** now work end-to-end — «Σκουλαρίκια» at 14K €17/g and at 18K €20/g, *and even same-name-same-karat at different rates* (previously both collapsed to one code, silently shadowing each other in `buildCodeMap`). Two identity fixes made that safe: (1) `_catKey(c)` now prefers `'a:' + c.abbr` (unique per row, stable across renames) over `name|karat`, and `saveVendor` resolves the *old* vendor's letters via `catAbbrs` before comparing so the rate-change/"mistake fix" prompt still matches on a legacy vendor's first edit; (2) **`getItemCat` was name-first** (`cats.find(c => c.name === item.catName)`), which would have priced every piece in a second «Σκουλαρίκια» off the FIRST one's rate — it now only trusts the name when it matches **exactly one** category (`filter(...).length === 1`, preserving the index-shift protection the name lookup was there for) and otherwise falls back to `catIdx`, which is exact for the row the piece was actually priced with. *(Items still don't store a `catAbbr`; the `catIdx` fallback covers it. Storing one would additionally survive rows being deleted/reordered — a possible follow-up.)* **Rate display:** `_fmtRate` (`String(parseFloat(r))`) drops trailing zeros — `€17/γρ.` not `€17.00/γρ.`, halves preserved (`€12.5/γρ.`) — used by `catOptionLabel` and the vendor card. **Known limitation:** a device still running a pre-v11.10 app that saves a vendor would strip the `abbr` fields (it doesn't know them), re-arming the rename hazard — update every device that edits vendors. Vendor-name abbreviations were fixed the same way in **v11.11** (see below).
- **Frozen vendor codes + karat follows the category (v11.11):** the two follow-ups to v11.10. (1) **Frozen vendor abbreviations** — the vendor part of a code (`ΚΑΛ18Σ` → `ΚΑΛ`) had the same disease as the category letters, but worse: `getVendorAbbr` takes 3 letters of the vendor NAME **but bumps to 4 when another vendor shares those 3**, so merely **ADDING** a vendor (`ΚΑΛΑΜΑΤΑ` next to `ΚΑΛΟΓΕΡΑΣ`) silently renumbered the *existing* vendor `ΚΑΛ`→`ΚΑΛΟ` and broke every tag it had printed — without the owner touching it (verified: the old derivation does exactly this). A vendor now carries frozen **`abbr`/`abbrLatin`** like its categories. `vendorAbbr(vendor, allVendors, latin)` returns the frozen value, else derives exactly as before (legacy vendor → unchanged codes); all **7** read sites go through it. `_freezeVendorAbbrs(vendors)` pins every existing vendor to what it resolves to *right now*, and must run before the list can change; `_newVendorWithAbbr(vendor)` (used by BOTH add paths — `saveVendor`'s new branch and `saveVendorFromModal`) calls it, then picks the newcomer's abbr via `deriveVendorAbbr(name, used, latin)` (3 letters, 4 if taken, then 5…, then the alphabet) against what's already pinned — so the newcomer yields, never the incumbent. **Rename** is covered by `editVendor` capturing `_editVendorAbbr`/`_editVendorAbbrLatin` **when the form opens** (module vars next to `editingVendorIndex`, cleared in `cancelEditVendor` + `finishVendorSave`); `saveVendor` re-attaches them because `vendor = { name, laborCategories }` is rebuilt from scratch and would otherwise drop them. (2) **Karat follows the category** — `#workType` had no karat handling, so the printed code took its karat from the *category* (`cat.karat`) while the price took it from the *karat selector*: they could silently disagree (price 14K gold, print an `18` code, then scan that tag later and get the 18K price). Picking a category now calls the new shared **`syncKaratToCat(rawKarat)`** ('18' → gold 18K; '925s' → `setMetal('silver')` + purity 925; '—' → left alone), which was **extracted from the identical block already in `lookupCode`** and is now used by both, so a scanned tag and a hand-picked category behave the same. The `workType` change listener is double-guarded (`metal === 'other'` and `vendor.vendorType === 'other'`) so Other-type vendors, whose categories carry no karat, are never dragged to gold. Verified end-to-end in the real form: adding `ΚΑΛΑΜΑΤΑ` leaves `ΚΑΛΟΓΕΡΑΣ`'s codes untouched and takes `ΚΑΛΑ`; renaming a vendor keeps its codes; picking 14K/18K/silver categories moves the selectors correctly; Other-type vendors stay on `other`; the Other-type form round-trip pins and preserves its codes; and the whole v11.10 category regression still passes.
- **Retire-don't-delete + karat-change warning + 3-letter vendor codes (v11.12):** a deep audit of everything that feeds a printed code or an item's pricing identity, prompted by "600+ items, this runs my business". Found and fixed three silent corruptors. (1) **`deleteVendor` had NO inventory check and spliced the array.** Items reference their vendor by **index** into `state.vendors` and *nothing anywhere renumbers `vendorIdx`* (verified: it's written at creation, never repaired) — so deleting a vendor shifted every later vendor down and silently repriced their pieces off the wrong vendor's rates AND changed their codes (verified: deleting the 1st of 3 turned a ΚΑΛΟΓΕΡΑΣ earring @€17/g into a ΒΟΥΤΣΑΣ ring @€30/g and orphaned ΒΟΥΤΣΑΣ's items). Now vendors are **retired, never removed**: `deleteVendor` sets `vendor.deleted = true` (keeping the slot = keeping the identity), `activeVendors()` is the live list, `populateVendorSelect` filters on the **value** (never reindexes), `renderVendors` shows retired vendors dimmed with a «retired» badge + `restoreVendor(i)` (↩️), and `buildCodeMap`/`getItemCat`/`calcInvItemPrice` are untouched so a retired vendor's items still price and its tags still scan. (2) **Same for categories** — deleting a labor row shifted `catIdx` for every row below it; a *unique* name was rescued by `getItemCat`'s name lookup, but once v11.10 allowed **duplicate names** the name could no longer rescue and the item resolved to **nothing** (lost its labor rate). `_retireLaborRow(row)` hides the row + sets `row.dataset.deleted` (a never-saved row with no frozen `abbr` is still removed outright), `saveVendor` persists `deleted: true`, and `updateWorkTypeOptions`/`updateBulkCatSelect` filter on the value. This also fixed a latent hazard in `confirmDeleteLaborRow`, which writes item reassignments to Firebase **immediately, before the vendor is saved** — with no splice, the target `catIdx` stays valid even if the owner then cancels the form. `saveVendor` now requires ≥1 **non-retired** category. (3) **Karat-change warning** — `cat.karat` is the one code ingredient that can't be frozen (it also drives pricing), so changing it silently rewrote the code (`ΚΑΛ14Σ`→`ΚΑΛ18Σ`) and stranded that category's tags. `saveVendor` now collects `karatChanges` (matched by `_catKey`) and `showConfirm`s **before** saving, listing `name: oldCode → newCode`, danger-styled, chained ahead of the existing rate-change prompt via `_afterKarat`/`_promptRateChange` (no nag when nothing changed). (4) **Vendor codes stay 3 letters** — `deriveVendorAbbr` now takes the first TWO letters + the next letter of the name that keeps it unique (ΚΑΛΟΓΕΡΑΣ → `ΚΑΛ`, a later ΚΑΛΑΜΑΤΑ → `ΚΑΑ`, a third → `ΚΑΥ`; BUTTERFLY/BUTLER → `BUT`/`BUL`) instead of growing to 4 — the vendor prefix is in **every** QR and a 4th letter costs space a stone suffix may need. Verified identical to the old rule for any non-colliding name, so nothing derived today moves. **Audit verdict — every component of a code is now pinned or guarded:** vendor prefix (frozen v11.11), karat (warned v11.12), category letters (frozen v11.10), weight + stones (already prompt a reprint, v10.14). Also re-verified: the cloud round-trip preserves the frozen fields, codes stay unique, no abbreviation can contain a digit.
- **Phantom karat warning fixed — pair rows by POSITION (v11.13):** found by auditing the owner's real backup (14 vendors / 82 categories / 1058 items). `saveVendor` paired each saved category row with its previous state using `_catKey`, which prefers the frozen letters — but **frozen letters are not unique per row on a legacy vendor**: the pre-v11.10 derivation (`buildNameToAbbr`) keys on `extractLetters(name)`, which **strips digits**, so «Διάφορα κοσμήματα 18k» and «Διάφορα κοσμήματα 14k» collapse to one key and share the letters `ΔΚ` (their *codes* still differ — the karat is in the code — which is why the earlier "no two categories share a code" check passed and this stayed hidden). `find()` therefore matched the 14k row against the 18k row and reported a **phantom "you changed the karat" warning (`ΚΑΛ18ΔΚ → ΚΑΛ14ΔΚ`) on every save of that vendor, with nothing edited** — harmless to data but alarming, and it blocked the save behind a scary danger dialog. Fixed by pairing old/new rows **by array index** (`oldCats[i]`): the form is built from `getVendorCategories(v)` in order, retired rows keep their slot (v11.12) and new rows are appended, so position is exact — and unlike any content-derived key it survives the very edits being detected (a changed karat or name). `_catKey` (still used to match an ITEM's category, e.g. `recalibrateCategoryItems`) now includes the karat (`'a:' + abbr + '|' + karat`) so it's unique per row; it must never be used to match a row across a karat edit. **Audit result on the real data: 607 tagged pieces checked, 0 that would fail to scan, 0 categories sharing a code, 0 vendor prefix collisions; the freeze is a no-op across all 82 categories, and renaming every category + vendor moves 0 codes. Verified the whole 14-vendor save pass: 0 phantom warnings, 0 codes moved, all pinned.** Two pre-existing data notes (not caused by any of this): one earring (`inv_1781809872180_82inh`, in stock) stores `vendorIdx` 8 (Butterflyfish) while its tag reads `KOY18S…` (Κούλης, 7) so its card can't price it though its tag scans correctly; and 2 items carry no `seqId`.
- **Wrong-vendor self-heal — `_itemVendor` (v11.14):** the audit of the owner's real backup proved the **`deleteVendor` reindex bug actually fired in production**, four weeks before it was fixed. Timeline from the data: two Κούλης emerald pieces created 2026-06-18 21:54/22:11 carry `vendorIdx: 8`; the first Butterflyfish piece (2026-06-19 15:51) also carries `vendorIdx: 8`; every later Κούλης piece (from 2026-06-23) carries `vendorIdx: 7`. So a vendor at index ≤7 was deleted on ~19 June, shifting Κούλης 8→7 and Butterflyfish 9→8. Only **2** items were stranded because the deletion happened the morning after Κούλης was added, while nothing else existed below the deleted slot — pure luck; today it would have moved hundreds. Both strays are in-stock and were mispriced: `KOY18K2.1-.22EME` (Κολιέ) resolved onto **Butterflyfish's** same-named «Κολιέ» (an other-type row with no rate → €0/g), and `KOY18S1-2x.22EME` (Σκουλαρίκια) resolved to **nothing** (Butterflyfish has no such category → `cats[2]` undefined → no labour at all). Fix: new helper **`_itemVendor(item)`** — items store `vendorName` alongside `vendorIdx` (609/1058 do), so when the two disagree the **name wins**, provided it matches exactly ONE vendor (same rule as `getItemCat`'s name-first category lookup; a *renamed* vendor makes the stored name match nothing, which correctly falls back to the index). Used by `getItemCat` and `calcInvItemPrice`. **Read-only — nothing is rewritten**, deliberately: a self-heal that silently edited records is exactly what went wrong with stone auto-pricing in v10.19. Verified on the real data: both strays now resolve to Κούλης €25/g, and **exactly 2 of 1058 items changed** — every other item resolves identically to the plain index lookup. NB the two records keep `vendorIdx: 8` on disk; the heal is at read time, so they will read correctly forever without a migration.
- **Group face = the original piece; twin sales don't strand stock (v11.15):** surfaced by the first real group whose members differ ONLY by `goldColor` (RG0110 — one Gold&White, one Rose Gold, added via "Add similar items" with the v11.06 gold-colour picker; same weight → **identical `qrCode`** `ALO14D2.6-ZIR` and identical `seqId`/ref code). (1) **Group representative** — the fold in `renderInvList` made "the first member met in sorted order" the rep, so with a newest-first sort the just-added Rose Gold became the group's face (photo/desc/badges). Now, after folding, the rep is reset to the member with the **earliest `dateAdded`** — the group's face is its original piece and is stable regardless of sort; the row keeps its sorted **position**, only the member it displays changes. (2) **Twin sale stranded a piece** — the Calc-scan mark-sold used `Object.entries(...).find(qrCode === qrNorm)`, i.e. the FIRST record of that QR *regardless of status*, then skipped it if already sold. So selling one twin marked record A; selling the second **found A again, saw `sold`, and did nothing** — leaving B in stock forever while the money was taken twice. Now it collects all non-bulk matches and prefers an **`in_stock`** one. **KNOWN LIMITATION (by design, needs an owner decision):** two pieces differing only in colour carry the *same printed tag*, so a scan **cannot** tell them apart — the price-bar/fullscreen photo (`cpbPhotoThumb`, resolved by `.find()` on `qrCode`) shows the first match, and the FIRST twin sale marks whichever record comes first, not necessarily the one in hand. Net stock counts stay correct; *which colour* is left may not. Selling the exact piece works today via the group detail sheet's per-piece Sell (`openInvGroup`). A proper fix = a variant chooser on scan when a QR matches several in-stock pieces with different `goldColor`/`color`, feeding the chosen record to the photo + sale + reserve paths.
- **Scan variant chooser — "which one are you holding?" (v11.16):** closes the v11.15 known limitation. Pieces in a similar-items group with the same weight get the **same `qrCode` AND the same ref code**, so a scan carries *no* information about which physical piece is in hand. Harmless while twins were identical (interchangeable) — but the v11.06 gold-colour picker made them visibly different, so the app was guessing: it took the first `.find()` match for the photo, the sale and the reservation. Now `_checkInvStatusForCalc` collects **all** records for the scanned QR, prefers an **`in_stock`** one (a sold twin first used to show a false "🏷️ Sold" chip for a piece on the shelf), and — when **>1 in-stock** match exists **and** they differ by `_variantKey(it)` = `goldColor|color` — populates `_scanVariants` and renders `#scanVariantPicker`: a photo + colour chip per candidate, **nothing pre-selected on purpose** (a wrong guess sells the wrong ring and strands its twin, so the deliberate tap is worth the second). `calculate()`'s photo IIFE returns early while a choice is pending (a wrong photo in front of a customer is worse than none). `_pickScanVariant(id)` sets `_scanChosenInvId` + `_lastScannedInvEntry` and paints the photo via `_applyScanVariantPhoto` (the fullscreen `#pfsPhoto` follows automatically — it reads `lastCalcResult.photoUrl`). The chosen id is then preferred by the **Calc-scan mark-sold** in `confirmSale` and by **`_resItemFromResult`** (reserve), so photo + sale + hold all target the same record. `_resetScanVariants()` runs on every new scan and on the calc reset. Identical twins and single pieces never show the picker. Verified: picker appears with both colours and no pre-selection; picking Rose paints the rose photo and the sale marks the ROSE record; identical twins / single piece / one-sold-one-in-stock stay silent; Greek labels correct; decorative `color` differences trigger it too, not just `goldColor`.
- **Variant chooser is a SHEET, not inline chips (v11.17):** the v11.16 inline chip row worked but cluttered the Calc at only 2 variants and could not scale — a group may hold every colour combination (8+ rings), which no chip row can show without squeezing the form off screen. Now `openScanVariantSheet()` renders a bottom sheet (same pattern/z-index as `openInvGroup`): title + "these N pieces share the SAME tag" explainer + a **scrollable vertical list**, one row per candidate (56px photo, colour label, weight, ✓ on the current pick). Tap a row → `_pickScanVariant` applies it and **closes the sheet**; the Calc then shows the right piece. It opens **automatically** on an ambiguous scan; `#scanVariantPicker` is now just a compact **one-line chip** ("✓ Ροζ Χρυσό — Αλλαγή →") that re-opens the sheet to change the pick. Backdrop-dismiss is disabled **while nothing is chosen** (a half-answered scan silently sells the wrong piece); `_resetScanVariants()` closes it on any new scan / calc reset. Measured at 375×812: sheet 82vh, 78px rows, **6 of 8 variants visible without scrolling**, no horizontal overflow. **Why not distinguish by ref code or record id instead (owner's suggestion):** the ref code is the *same* — `invAddSimilar` deliberately copies `seqId`/`refType` (one product, one code) — and the unique `inv_…` id is internal, never printed, so neither is on the tag for a scan to read. **Why not put the colour in the QR:** `color`/`goldColor` are editable in Edit Item, so the code would again derive from a mutable field — exactly the class of bug v11.10–v11.14 closed. The tag genuinely cannot identify the piece; asking is the honest answer.
- **Edit weight / vendor / category on ref-tagged pieces (v11.29):** the payoff of the ref-tag work — the printed tag carries only the reference code (`RG0110`), so the piece's weight, vendor and category no longer live on it and can finally be corrected without a reprint. Edit Item gains three fields (`#invEditWeight`, `#invEditVendorSelect`, `#invEditCatSelect`) shown **only** when `_invEditUnlocked(item)` — i.e. `tagRef && status==='in_stock' && metal!=='other' && !isBulk && !isCoin && !isMetal`. Anything else keeps the old read-only rows plus, for a lockable in-stock piece, one **"🆔 Print ref tag to unlock editing"** button (`_invEditUnlock` → closes the form → `invPrintTagFromCard`, where "Print as ref tag" converts it). **Sold pieces stay locked with no button** — their price is history and a restock prints a new tag, unlocking them at exactly the right moment. **There is deliberately no karat field:** the labor category is karat-specific and already decides the karat (`syncKaratToCat` enforces the same rule in the calc), so on save the category sets `item.metal`/`item.karat` from its `cat.karat` (`'18'` → gold/18, `'925s'` → silver/925, `'—'` → leave alone) — one source, never two that can disagree. **Both dropdowns are constrained**: vendors exclude `vendorType==='other'` (multiplier pricing, no karat) and categories are filtered to the piece's **own `rateType`** (`_invEditCatOptions`, via `#invEditCatSelect.dataset.rateType`) — a per-item category prices off an `itemMargin` derived from a Target Retail field this form only shows when the piece was already per-item, so crossing that boundary would price it off a margin nobody set; retired vendors/categories stay listed only if the piece is already in one. On save (`_idChanged`): `vendorIdx/vendorName/catIdx/catName` update, `_rebuildItemQR(item)` regenerates the stored descriptive code from the new identity **keeping its stone suffix** (`item.code` too, for the search haystack) — the code still prices the piece when its ref resolves there, and the existing stone block then rebuilds its suffix onto the new base. **Baselines are rescaled** by the ratio the correction itself caused (`initialRetail *= afterRetail/beforeRetail`, same for `initialMetalValue`), both readings taken at one instant so only the edit — never gold-price movement — is divided out; without this, correcting 4g→3g would show a fake −25% loss. Skipped when the per-item Target Retail block already set an explicit `initialRetail` (`_ieTargetSet`). Items with no stored `initialMetalValue` need no rescale — their May-2025 anchor derives from pure grams, which follows the weight. A blank/invalid weight or a missing vendor/category **refuses to save**. **Shared-ref split:** a correction can break a shared ref (five 0.2g clasps sharing `XX0532` are interchangeable until one becomes 0.3g), so `_refCheckSplitAfterEdit(id, item)` runs after a successful save and, on a `_refSharedWithDifferent` clash, offers to print a new tag — it **never mints a seqId on its own**, because a new number without a print would leave the piece's physical tag reading `XX0532` and resolving, silently, to a piece of the wrong weight; `_printAsRefTag` mints and prints atomically. New helper `_piecesWord(n)` fixes the Greek/English plural in both that warning and `_printAsRefTag`'s ("4 κομμάτια … ΔΙΑΦΕΡΟΥΝ", not "4 κομμάτι … ΔΙΑΦΕΡΕΙ"). Verified against the real 1072-item backup: all 15 ref-tagged pieces rebuild their stored code byte-identically (so a no-change edit is a true no-op), and gain % held to ≤0.002pp across weight, category (18K→14K) and vendor edits.
- **Ref-scan pin is sticky, not single-use (v11.30):** editing a ref-tagged piece's weight can make its stored descriptive `qrCode` collide with an *unrelated* item that already has that code — e.g. NK0616 edited 1.601→1.6 became `ALO14KL1.6`, which a **sold** necklace (NK0482) already carried. Scanning NK0616's ref tag then resolved correctly on the first pass but **flipped to the sold NK0482 on the next passive re-check** (a spot-price tick, a re-render, a language toggle — all re-call `_checkInvStatusForCalc` with the *descriptive* code and no pin), showing the wrong photo and a "Sold" chip. Root cause: `_refPinnedInvId` (set by `lookupCode`'s ref branch so the descriptive re-run resolves the exact piece) was **single-use** — nulled after one read in `_checkInvStatusForCalc` — while a ref-tagged piece is deliberately excluded from the descriptive pool (`_descPool` filters out `tagRef` items, since its physical tag prints the ref, not the code), so once the pin was gone its own code resolved to a *different* record. Fix: the pin is now **sticky and code-scoped**. New var `_refPinnedForCode` stores the descriptive code the pin maps to; `_pinnedEntry` is built only when `_refPinnedForCode === rawNorm`, so it survives repeated re-checks of the same code but never leaks onto a different one. `lookupCode(raw, opts)` clears the pin at the top of every fresh (non-`fromRef`) call — the ref branch's own recursive re-run passes `{fromRef:true}` to keep the pin it just set — and the calc reset clears it too. The single-use nulling in `_checkInvStatusForCalc` was removed. Verified on the real 1072-item backup with the exact collision reproduced: first scan and 3+ re-checks all stay on NK0616 (chip, `_lastScannedInvEntry`, and the SELL target `_scanChosenInvId`); a later different plain scan doesn't inherit the pin; a plain scan of `ALO14KL1.6` still resolves to sold NK0482; the ER0613/NOH14L2.8 pendant-vs-earrings case (v11.25) still resolves by ref with no wrong chooser; and the genuine in-stock twin chooser still opens. (The descriptive-code collision itself is left in place — it is harmless for scanning now that the ref pin holds, and collisions with sold pieces are common; only the ref tag is the reliable identity.)
- **Every printed tag is a reference-code tag; redundant button removed (v11.31):** the Stock-card Print Tag modal (`invPrintTagFromCard`) had two print actions — plain **Print** (printed the *descriptive* QR) and a green **🆔 Print as ref tag** (printed the ref-code QR, stamped `tagRef`, handled the shared-ref split). The owner flagged the second as redundant: if you print a tag at all, it should be the ref tag. The green button was removed and the main **Print** now routes through `_printAsRefTag` (via `_withRefSync` to fix a frozen-prefix mismatch first), so it prints the ref-code QR, stamps `item.tagRef`, handles the shared-ref split and saves. **Print QR Only** now prints with `useRef=true` (`data.refCode = itemRefCode(item)`) and stamps `tagRef` too; **Save QR as Image** exports the ref-code QR (`fullQR`/`greekCode` set to `itemRefCode(item)`) so a BarTender-printed tag is also a ref tag. A rare legacy item **with no `seqId`** (no ref code) falls back to the descriptive tag so it can still print. The modal preview now shows the ref code prominently with the internal descriptive code beneath it. This also fixes a latent inconsistency where the old plain Print put a *descriptive* QR onto a piece already marked `tagRef` (physical QR then disagreed with the app). The separate new-item label modal already printed ref tags by default (v11.24) — unchanged. Verified against the real backup: Print / Print-QR-Only / Save-Image all carry the ref code and stamp `tagRef`; a legacy no-seqId item falls back to descriptive; the green button is gone.
- **Duplicate-QR guard removed; temp tools + dead code cleaned (v11.32):** with the reference-code tag system, the descriptive `qrCode` no longer needs to be globally unique — every physical tag carries the unique reference code (`RG0624` vs `RG0625`), so two pieces of the same weight/vendor/category/stones share a descriptive code yet each scans to its own record (verified end-to-end against the real backup: chip, photo, and the SELL/`_scanChosenInvId` target all resolve to the scanned piece, incl. across the v11.30 re-check). So the old "Duplicate QR Code — adjust the weight for a unique code" flow (which forced `1.3 → 1.301`) is **gone**: (1) the `openLabelModal` pre-check now just assigns the ref code and opens the modal, (2) the belt-and-suspenders guard in `_saveToInventoryFromLabel` was removed, and (3) the `_showDupQrWarning` dialog function was deleted. The owner keeps the **exact** weight. (The `invRestock` dup-check is a separate flow and is unchanged.) **Also removed:** the two temporary Settings tools from the ref-tag rollout — the "🆔 Shared Codes" migration list (`#refFixSection` markup + `renderRefFixList`/`_refFixPrint`/`_refFixZoom`/`_refFixDesc` + its `applyRoleUI` line) and the "🧪 Tag Test" section (`#tagTestSection` + `_printTestTag` + its `applyRoleUI` line). **Dead-code sweep:** removed 7 verified-orphan functions (zero call sites, superseded or stubs) — `saveManualSale` (superseded by `spAddManualSale`/`spAddManualSale2`), `compressPhoto` (superseded by the inline resize in `_photoInputChanged`), `openPhotoPicker` (empty stub), `getStoneApplyLabelText`, `_resItemSnap`, `openQuoteWindowFallback`, `invUngroup` (superseded by `invDeleteGroup` since v10.43). Verified: app parses, console clean, no handler points to a removed function. **(v11.33) The three then-flagged orphans were resolved and removed** after review — **originally LEFT** (each represents a feature whose button is currently absent — flagged rather than silently deleted, pending an owner decision): `deleteUser` (team-member deletion), `openInvScanner`/`_invScanMode` (the old dedicated scan-into-inventory mode; the scan-to-inventory *flow* still works via `prepareInvConfirm` from an unknown-QR chip), and `resetCredentials`. The v10.19 `stoneAuditSection` (Stone Price Audit) also remains — a separate older temporary tool, not part of the ref-tag work.
- **Flagged orphans + Stone Price Audit removed (v11.33):** the three functions v11.32 deliberately left are now resolved and gone. `deleteUser` — removed; team-member removal is intentionally **deactivate** (`toggleUserActive`, the ⏸ button in `renderTeamList`), which disables access while keeping the member's sales history intact (a hard delete would orphan their sale records). `resetCredentials` — removed; obsolete JSONBin-era code (referenced dead `binIdOut`/`empBinId` DOM ids), no caller. `openInvScanner` + the `_invScanMode` flag — removed; the flag was only ever set by that dead function, so `handleScannedCode` always took the Calc-lookup branch anyway (the reads collapsed to their always-false form: `modeBtn` display, `_scanMode`, and the `if (_invScanMode)` branch). **Scan-to-inventory is unaffected** — it runs through `prepareInvConfirm` from the "add to inventory?" chip on an unknown-QR scan, a separate path. Also removed the v10.19 **Stone Price Audit** temporary tool in full: `#stoneAuditSection` markup, its `applyRoleUI` line, `runStonePriceAudit`/`stoneAuditFix`/`stoneAuditOpen`/`_stoneAuditCandidates`, and the `stoneAudit*` i18n keys (both languages). Verified: app parses, console clean, `handleScannedCode` still routes plain codes and `?code=` URLs to `lookupCode`, team list intact, 5 nav tabs. Orphan scan now clean (only the self-invoking `_prefillQuoteTitle` IIFE remains, which is not an orphan).
- **"+" (load to Calc) sends the reference code (v11.34):** the Stock card's **+** button (`invLoadToCalc`, also the group sheet's "➕ Calc") fed Calc the item's **descriptive** `qrCode`. Since v11.32 dropped the duplicate-code guard, several pieces legitimately share a descriptive code (same weight/vendor/category), so pressing + on one of four 1.3 g rings popped the "which one are you holding?" variant chooser — for a piece the app already knew exactly, because the button carries the record id. Fixed: `invLoadToCalc` now sends `itemRefCode(item)` when the item has a `seqId`, so + behaves exactly like scanning that piece's ref tag and resolves (photo, chip, and the SELL/`_scanChosenInvId` target) to the tapped record. Falls back to the descriptive code for a legacy piece with no `seqId`. Verified against the real 1072-item backup, reproducing the 4-shared-tag case: no chooser, resolves to the tapped piece, sell target correct. 622 of 1072 items take the new path; the rest are byte-identical — **coins are unchanged** (their descriptive code *is* their ref code, `GC0510`), **metal** has no + button, and the two **bulk chains** resolve by ref then route correctly into `_openBulkSellByQr` (verified), as do the 46 **other-type** items.
- **Roles Phase 1 — permission table, real Manager, Team screen (v11.35):** the roles area was the least-polished corner: `manager` existed in name only (every gate was `isOwnerOrManager()`, so a manager silently had full owner powers — the sole difference was one PIN check), and the Sync panel mixed "set up THIS device" with "manage OTHER people". Rebuilt per `TEAM_ROLES_DESIGN.md`. **(1) Permission table** — `PERMISSIONS` (10 names) + `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` (owner = all, hard-coded and never restrictable; manager; employee = none) + `can(perm)`; **43 scattered gates converted** from `isOwnerOrManager()` / `creds.role === 'employee'` to `can('…')`. Behaviour-neutral **by construction** for owner (every perm true) and employee (every perm false) — verified role-by-role in the browser; only Manager changes. Permissions live on the ROLE, never the person (the Shopify/Square/Loyverse pattern; per-person grants are the "role explosion" anti-pattern) — so making roles *editable* later is an edit to this one table, not a hunt through 60 checks. `isOwnerOrManager()` is kept but **only for identity/labelling** (sync status text, team sort), never for permission decisions. **(2) Manager is now real:** keeps see_cost / manage_stock / vendors / desk_admin / sales_admin / stocktake / export; **loses** `settings` (new `#taxMarginSection` + `#recoverySection` ids added to gate VAT/margin/currency and force-restore), `sync` (whole panel), `backup` (create AND restore — owner-only per owner's decision). The Settings *tab* stays visible to managers because export/stocktake/sales live inside it — `_canSeeSettingsTab()` shows the tab if any section within is permitted. `applyRoleUI` rewritten around a `show(id, visible, mode)` helper. **(3) Team moved to its own `#panel-team`** with a `nav-team` entry in the ☰ More menu (gated by `canManageTeam()`), out of `#panel-sync` — this was **required**, not cosmetic: Sync became owner-only, so leaving Team inside it would have locked managers out of the team management they're supposed to have. `showPanel('team')` calls `loadTeamSection()`; added to the desktop 560px-centred rule; new i18n `teamTitleShort`/`teamDesc`. **(4) Add Person → 3-way role picker** (`_roleChoices`/`_renderRoleOptions`) — stacked rows, each with a plain-language line about what that role can do, replacing the cramped 2-button Manager/Employee toggle. Filtered by **`canAssignRole(role)`**: an owner may assign any role, a **manager only `employee`**. That guard is applied in depth — the picker, `setUserRole`, `saveUser`, and the team list's **Edit / Deactivate / Share-link** buttons (a manager could otherwise have shared the *owner's* invite link, which mints an owner device: a real privilege escalation). `shareUserLink` also stopped collapsing any non-manager role to `employee`, which would have silently invited a co-owner as an employee now that Owner is choosable. Verified: owner/manager/employee UI matrices, all 10 panels render, console clean, employee vendor view unchanged (names + codes, no rates). **Owner lock-out guard:** `saveUser` refuses to demote an owner in two cases — **yourself** (your device would lose Sync/Settings/Backups on the next `syncIdentityFromCloud`, and Sync is where the owner role lives, so there would be no way back) and the **last remaining owner**. Demoting a *second* owner to manager works normally, and promoting back to owner is unrestricted. **Still open:** the Sync panel's "👑 Owner / 👤 Employee" device toggle (see design doc §5); per-role editable permissions remain deliberately unbuilt (Phase 2).
- **"melee" is internal-only, never customer-facing (v11.37):** `buildStoneDescription` records `melee` when a stone's qty is `'t'` (bought as a parcel priced by total weight rather than counted). That is a **purchasing** detail; a customer should just read the stone. It had already been stripped in `showroom.htm`, `item.htm`, `drawPromoCanvas` and `togglePriceFullscreen`, but was still printing on **quotes and certificates** (`buildQuoteHTML` / `buildCertificateHTML` pushed `<em>melee total</em>` / «Μελέ σύνολο») and in the **catalog / order sheet** (`buildSharePDF` printed the raw `item.stonesDesc`). Two helpers added next to `stonesDescDisplay`: **`stripMelee(text)`** removes the melee wording in either language *without* translating (the share PDF deliberately stays in English for jsPDF font safety), and **`stonesDescPublic(desc)`** = `stripMelee(stonesDescDisplay(desc))` for surfaces that should also translate. Quote/cert now emit nothing for a melee parcel (the `qty > 1` count is still shown for normal multi-stone entries); `buildSharePDF` wraps its `stonesDesc` in `stripMelee`. **The stored `stonesDesc` and every internal screen are unchanged** — the app relies on the melee marker for the QR round-trip (v10.12) and the owner still needs to see it. Verified in both languages: internal `buildStoneDescription` still yields `melee Zircon`, internal Greek display still `Μελέ Ζιρκόνιο`, while quote/certificate output contains no melee in EN or EL. *(Noted but NOT changed: quote/cert print the stone TYPE in English even in Greek mode — pre-existing, separate from this fix.)*
- **Greek stone names in customer documents + label modal cleanup (v11.38):** (1) **Translations** — `buildQuoteHTML` / `buildCertificateHTML` printed the raw `s.type`, so a Greek quote said "Zircon" instead of «Ζιρκόνιο». They receive an `isEl` flag rather than reading the global `lang`, so a language-explicit **`stoneLabelFor(type, isEl)`** was added and `stoneLabel(type)` now delegates to it. The **catalog / order sheet** (`buildSharePDF`) printed the raw English `stonesDesc` behind a stale "English stone names (safe)" comment — it actually registers **Noto Sans** and already prints Greek throughout (Χρυσός, ΚΑΤΑΛΟΓΟΣ, Greek descriptions), so it now uses `stonesDescPublic` (translated **and** melee-stripped). **`drawPromoCanvas` deliberately left in English** — the promo image prefers `descEn` first because it targets social media, and translating only the stones would produce a mixed-language image. Stone **shapes** (Round/Princess/Oval…) are still English — no curated Greek trade terms exist and guessing them would be worse than leaving them; flagged to the owner. (2) **Label modal** — the LINE 1 / LINE 2 / LINE 3 rows (and, for a stone-less item, the single Greek-code row) were removed from `_openLabelModalInner`: they existed for the old BarTender flow where those lines were typed onto the tag, and the tag now prints the reference code. Only **Full QR code** + **Reference code** remain, and the Copy button now copies just the full QR string. **`data.lines` is untouched** — `buildTSPL` still falls back to it for a legacy item with no ref code. Verified: printing is byte-identical (one TEXT line = the ref code, QR payload `RG0639`).
- **Greek stone-cut names + Baguette (v11.39):** owner supplied the cut names Greek jewellers and diamond dealers actually use, so `STONE_SHAPE_TRANSLATIONS` + `shapeLabelFor(shape, isEl)` / `shapeLabel(shape)` were added alongside the stone-name pair. Translated: Round→**Στρογγυλή κοπή**, Oval→**Οβάλ**, Pear→**Δάκρυ**, Marquise→**Μαρκίζ**, Heart→**Καρδιά**, Baguette→**Μπαγκέτα**, Emerald Cut→**Emerald**. **Princess / Cushion / Radiant / Asscher stay English on purpose** — international trade names, left untranslated in professional Greek catalogs (anything absent from the map passes through unchanged). **`Baguette` was added to `STONE_SHAPES`** — the owner's list had 11 cuts to the app's 10; that cut was simply missing. Wired into the three customer surfaces only: `buildQuoteHTML`, `buildCertificateHTML` (both take an explicit `isEl`) and `togglePriceFullscreen` (global `lang`); the catalog/order sheet gets it via `stonesDescDisplay`. **`buildStoneDescription` is deliberately untouched** — the stored `stonesDesc` stays English because it is parsed back out and round-trips through the QR. **Collision fixed:** "Emerald Cut" contains the stone name "Emerald", so `stonesDescDisplay` used to render a Diamond in an Emerald Cut as «Σμαράγδι Cut». It now parks shape matches behind `@@SHPn@@` placeholders *before* the stone pass and restores them after (longest key first in both passes). Verified: `Diamond 0.20ct Emerald Cut` → «Διαμάντι 0.20ct Emerald», while a real `Emerald 0.30ct` still → «Σμαράγδι 0.30ct», and carats are never corrupted.
- **VAT default corrected to 17% (v11.40):** the store is on Karpathos, which uses Greece's **reduced island VAT rate of 17%**, not the mainland 24%. Three places still assumed 24: (1) the `state` initializer `vat: 24` — only in effect on a fresh device until `applyCloudConfig` loads the owner's saved value, but wrong as a starting point; (2) the Settings VAT input's `placeholder="24"`, cosmetic but misleading; (3) **the real bug** — `buildMultiQuoteHTML` computed `const vatRate = parseFloat(cartItems[0].result.vat) || 24`, which both hardcoded 24 *and*, because `||` treats `0` as falsy, converted a **legitimate 0% VAT into 24%**. Now `Number.isFinite(_cqVat) ? _cqVat : (parseFloat(state.vat) || 0)` — an explicit finite check, falling back to the configured VAT rather than any literal. **Pricing and profit were never affected**: every other VAT read in the file (~20 sites) already used `parseFloat(state.vat) || 0`, which is safe (absent VAT → no VAT). Verified by driving `buildMultiQuoteHTML` itself: a €117 item yields 17% / €17.00; `vat: 0` now correctly renders 0% / €0.00 (was 24%); `undefined`/`null`/junk fall back to `state.vat`. **Rule going forward: never hardcode a VAT rate — always read `state.vat`.**
- **Gift certificates (v11.41):** a customer pays now; someone redeems for jewelry later. Third segment in the Desk tab (`deskShow('gift')` → `renderGiftCards`). Private array node **`/jewelprice_giftcards`** (+ `/jewelprice_giftcard_counter`, `localStorage 'jpro_giftcards'`, added to `BACKUP_PATHS`); **never published** (customer PII). Numbered **`G-0004`** via `gcNoFmt` — **the hyphen is load-bearing**: reference codes are 2 letters + 4 digits and `GC0510` is a *coin*, so `gcFindByCode` (`/^G-(\d{1,6})$/`) can never collide with one. Full design in `GIFT_CERTIFICATES_DESIGN.md`.
  - **Money model — counted at REDEMPTION, never at issue.** Issuing writes **nothing** to Sales (the money isn't earned yet); the sale is recorded at full value when the certificate is spent; an unused balance at expiry is booked as pure profit. Counting at both ends would double the revenue. Deliberately **no Sales-tab line for issued/outstanding** (owner's call — "Sales means sales"); the outstanding liability is summed at the top of the Desk → Gift Cards list instead.
  - **Balance is always derived** (`gcBalance` = amount − Σ `redemptions`), never stored. `_gcCheckExpiry` (from `autoSync`) self-heals a zero-balance `active` card to `redeemed`.
  - **Issue form** (`openGiftModal`, all roles): amount + buyer name/phone/email (required), optional `toName` + `message`, `payMethod` (cash/card/bank) **separate from** the receipt toggle — **card and bank force a receipt** (toggle switched off and disabled: those payments leave a bank trace). Expiry defaults to **1 year** (`GC_DEFAULT_TERM_DAYS`), freely editable. **Recipient contact is deliberately NOT collected at issue** (the buyer rarely knows it, and the expiry call goes to the buyer); `redeemerName/Phone/Email` are captured **at redemption**, when they are standing in the shop becoming a real customer.
  - **Redemption — the sale amount is NEVER reduced.** `openGiftPickerForSale` → `_pickGiftForSale` sets `window._saleGiftCard`; the dialog shows `G-0004 (Maria) −€100.00` and a **Cash due** line while `saleAmountInput` stays at the full price. That field *is* the sale record — deducting it would log a €160 piece as a €60 sale and destroy €100 of revenue and profit on every redemption. `confirmSale` re-derives the usable amount **from the card** (not the on-screen figure) so a stale dialog can't overdraw it, and spreads it across a cart **proportionally** so each record's VAT split is right (verified: cart parts sum to the same ex-VAT total as the single-item equivalent).
  - **Portion-split VAT/profit.** A part-paid sale arrived under two regimes months apart, so one `cashSale` flag cannot describe it. `addSaleRecord` gained an optional 15th `opts` arg (`{giftAmt, giftWasCash, giftCardId, giftCardNo}`) and computes `exVat = portion(gift, giftWasCash) + portion(rest, cashSale)`. **With no certificate `giftAmt` is 0 and it reduces exactly to the original formula** — verified across 384 cases (8 amounts × 6 costs × 2 cash × 4 VAT rates), all identical, and no gift fields stamped on ordinary sales. Worked example at 17%: €160 piece, €100 certificate taken as cash, €60 by card, cost €50 → exVat **€151.28**, profit **€101.28** (vs €86.75 all-receipt / €110.00 all-cash). Records carry `giftCardId/No/Amt/WasCash` only when used. New shared helper **`_saleExVat(s, vat)`** is the single place that knows the rule, used by the sale detail, the group detail total and `_toggleGroupCash` (which must not rewrite the certificate half settled months ago).
  - **Scanning:** `handleScannedCode` checks `gcFindByCode` **first**; scanning a certificate while the sale dialog is open selects it for that sale, otherwise it opens the card. Verified a coin ref (`GC0510`) is not hijacked.
  - **Custom orders too, not repairs** (a custom order is jewelry being made; a repair is labour on the customer's own item). Same picker in `_repairCollectOpen` gated on `r.type === 'custom'`; `_recordRepairIncome` gained the same `giftOpts` split.
  - **Expiry & breakage:** `_gcCheckExpiry` flags expired cards and raises **one** bell notification 30 days out (`GC_EXPIRY_WARN_DAYS`) via `_gcPushExpiryNotif` — a new `kind: 'gift_expiry'` in the existing `/jewelprice_notifications` system, rendered **green** in `openNotifPanel` with a View button. `notifCheckAndUpdateBadge` now splits `msgCount` from gift notifications: **messages stay red on the Stock tab**, gift expiries are **green** on Desk + the Gift segment (`_gcUpdateBadges`), and the bell is green only when nothing but expiries is waiting. The card is marked warned **only if the push actually landed**, so an offline device can't silently swallow the warning. Breakage (`_gcBookBreakage`) is **one explicit tap**, never automatic (the v10.19 silent-write lesson) — or `_gcExtend` gives more time; booking logs `giftBreakage: true` (profit = amount, cost null) and zeroes the balance so it can never be booked twice.
  - **Deleting a sale hands the balance back** (`_gcUnapplyForSale` from `deleteSaleRecord`), same principle as restoring a sold piece (v10.59); a cancelled card is never silently reactivated.
- **Greek all-caps tonos on printed documents (v11.41):** the v10.63 rule relies on the browser applying Greek casing to `text-transform:uppercase`, which it only does when the document declares its language — but **none of the generated documents set `lang`**, so uppercase Greek kept its accent. Two real customer-facing bugs: the **reservation form** (ΑΠΌΔΕΙΞΗ ΚΡΆΤΗΣΗΣ, ΥΠΟΓΡΑΦΉ ΠΕΛΆΤΗ, ΣΦΡΑΓΊΔΑ) and the **certificate of authenticity** (ΠΙΣΤΟΠΟΙΗΤΙΚΌ ΑΥΘΕΝΤΙΚΌΤΗΤΑΣ, ΥΠΟΓΡΑΦΉ). All five builders now set `lang` (`buildReservationHTML`, `buildQuoteHTML`, `buildMultiQuoteHTML`, `buildCertificateHTML`, the code reference card) plus the new gift certificate. Proven by measurement: with no `lang` the transformed text matches the *accented* reference width, with `lang="el"` the *unaccented* one. **NB when verifying this, measure a proportional font** — the signature label is DM Mono and monospace widths are identical either way, which reads as a false negative. **Rule: every generated document must set `lang`.**
- **Dates are LOCAL, never UTC (v11.42):** `todayStr()` was built with `toISOString().slice(0,10)`, which converts to **UTC** — and Karpathos is **UTC+3** in summer, so from local midnight until 03:00 it returned **yesterday**. Everything stamped with it in those hours was filed a day early: sale records (`addSaleRecord`), scrap gain, repair income, gift breakage, reservation payments — and a sale just after midnight on the 1st landed in the **previous month**, moving a month-end total. Now `todayStr()` returns the local calendar date via new helpers **`localDateStr(d)`** and **`dateStrInDays(n)`**; the four `Date.now() + N*864e5 → toISOString()` defaults (gift expiry ×2, reservation hold-until, repair ready-by) and the auto-backup daily gate use them too, as does the issue date printed on a gift certificate. **Rule: never derive a date string with `toISOString()`** — it is a UTC conversion, not a formatter. Existing records are left as they are (no migration; history isn't rewritten). Note the divergence window is local **00:00–03:00**, not the evening — UTC is *behind* Greece, so late-evening local times are still the same UTC day.
- **Never use a bare visible `<input type="date">` (v11.42):** it renders in the **device's** locale (a US-region iPhone shows `07/19/2027`), which CSS/JS cannot override. The app's pattern is an invisible native input (`position:absolute;inset:0;opacity:0`) over a styled sibling div showing `fmtDate(...)`, refreshed `onchange` — used by the sale date, reservation hold-until, repair ready-by, inventory date filters and now the gift certificate expiry.
