Still bare (pre-existing, owner-facing filters): `clientDateFrom`/`clientDateTo`, `ieDateFrom`/`ieDateTo`, `bulkSellDate`.
- **Gift certificate: cash default + Edit (v11.43):** the issue form had **two controls both labelled "Cash"** — the `payMethod` row (tender) and the `cashSale` toggle (receipt or not) — which could disagree silently. Picking **Cash** now arms the no-receipt toggle (`_setCashBtnState(toggle, true)` in `_gcSetPay`), card/bank still force it off and lock it, and the hint line spells out "Pressed = no receipt". This also fixes a trap where toggle-on → Card → back to Cash silently left it off. **Why it mattered:** a certificate stored as receipt makes `addSaleRecord` charge VAT on the whole sale — on a €175 sale with a €150 certificate and €74.88 cost, profit read €74.69 instead of €96.49 (the €21.80 gap is exactly the VAT on €150 that never went through a receipt). **New `openGiftEdit`/`_gcEditSave`** (owner/manager, ✏️ in the detail sheet) edits amount, buyer, To/message, payMethod, cash/receipt and expiry. The amount cannot drop below `gcRedeemed()`; raising it revives a `redeemed` card, lowering it can settle one. **Changing the cash flag re-derives any sale that already used the certificate** (`s.giftCardId === id` → update `giftCardWasCash`, recompute profit via `_saleExVat`) behind a `showConfirm` naming the count — otherwise the books would disagree with the card. `_gceSetPay(method, keep)` takes a `keep` flag so opening the form preserves the stored flag instead of applying the new-certificate default.
- **Gift certificate: picker scan button + landscape share PDF (v11.44):** two gaps found in real use. (1) The picker sheet said "or scan the certificate code" but had **no scan button**, and the scanner was unreachable anyway once the sale dialog was open. New `_gcScanForSale()` — it **must remove the picker sheet first**: the sheet is `z-index:999995` and `.qr-overlay` is `99999`, so the camera would otherwise open *behind* it. The sale dialog stays open underneath, which is exactly what `handleScannedCode` looks for to attach the scanned certificate to that sale. (2) **The shared PDF was clipped in half.** `shareQuoteAsPDF` was hardcoded portrait — a 794px-wide iframe, `orientation:'portrait'`, 210mm page — while the certificate is **A4 landscape** (297mm/1123px), so everything past 210mm was cut off, losing the stamp box and QR. It now takes an optional 5th arg `opts` with `{landscape:true}`, switching the iframe to 1123×794, the html2canvas width, and the jsPDF orientation/page size; **portrait remains the default so every existing caller is untouched**. Verified by measurement: under the old 794px pipeline the QR sat at x=1014 and the stamp at x=904 (both off-page); under the fix nothing is clipped. `shareGiftCert` passes the flag. **Rule: any A4-landscape document must pass `{landscape:true}` to `shareQuoteAsPDF`.**
- **Desk: hide finished work + delete a cancelled certificate + last date fields (v11.45):** three queued items.
  - **Archive finished records.** Over the years the Desk lists fill with completed work and the live records get buried. All three lists now show only **open** work by default. One shared helper `_deskIsFinished(kind, r)` — repairs: `collected`/`cancelled`; reservations: `completed`/`cancelled`/`expired`; gift: `redeemed`/`cancelled`, and `expired` **only when the balance is 0 or `breakageSaleId` is set** (an expired certificate still owing money must stay visible — it's waiting on a book-or-extend decision, exactly the thing you'd not want buried). Shared control `_deskFinishedToggleHtml(kind, hidden)` → `👁 Show finished (N)` with the withheld count, state in `localStorage 'jpro_showFinished_<kind>'` via `_deskShowFinished`/`_deskToggleFinished`. **A search always bypasses the filter** (same rule `renderInvList` uses for coins/metal) — searching a name or number is how you find something from last season, and a filter that swallowed it would look broken. Display-only: nothing archived, moved or rewritten.
  - **Delete a gift certificate** — `_gcCanDelete(g)` = `status === 'cancelled' && !redemptions.length`, so it appears only after the deliberate cancel step and never for one that was spent (redeemed cards are referenced by real sale records via `giftCardId`, which `_gcUnapplyForSale` and the sale detail read back — orphaning them would corrupt the books). `_gcDelete` is `showConfirm`-gated, owner/manager.
  - **The last three bare `<input type="date">`** now use the invisible-input-over-`fmtDate`-display pattern: the Clients date filter, the inventory-export range, and the bulk-chain sale date. Because setting `.value` in JS does **not** fire `onchange`, the programmatic writers needed explicit refreshes — new `_syncClientDateDisplays()` (called from `clearClientDateFilter` + `pickClientSeason`) and `_syncIeDateDisplays()` (from `pickInvExportSeason`). **No bare visible date input remains in the app.**
- **Returns, refunds, store credit & exchange (v11.46):** the piece comes back. Prompted by a real case — a €1500 necklace bought as a gift, returned days later, exchanged for nothing the daughter liked, and settled as €1500 of credit. The only prior tool was "Restore to Stock", which **deletes** the sale; that is the *mistake* path (its label was narrowed to say so) and was wrong for a real return. Full design + worked examples in `RETURNS_DESIGN.md`.
  - **Money model — a return is a NEW negative record dated the day it happens, never an edit or a delete.** Exactly what a real till does with a credit note: June keeps its €1500, July carries −€1500, the year nets to zero, and the history stays readable. Backdating (or deleting) would silently move a past month's totals and destroy the record that a sale ever happened.
  - **The return mirrors what the sale RECORDED, never a recomputation.** VAT has already changed once (24%→17%, v11.40) and gold moves daily, so recomputing would leave a residue. `processReturn` **spreads the original record** and negates its money fields — which also means every field (customer, vendor, employee, `invId`, `seqRef`, `bulkInvId`, `salePhotoUrl`) carries across and can never fall out of step with a field added to sales later.
  - **The exVat trick that makes partial refunds exact:** a refund below the sale price, or a piece that is NOT restocked, both need an ex-VAT figure — and the record does **not** store the VAT rate in force at the time. Since `profit = exVat − cost`, the original's exVat is recoverable as **`profit + cost`** (`_saleOrigExVat`) — from the record's own numbers, no VAT rate, no drift. Then `retExVat = −(origExVat × refund/origAmount)`, `retCost = restocked ? −orig.cost : null`, `retProfit = retExVat − (retCost||0)`. A full restocked return reduces to exact negation; a scrapped piece books the whole ex-VAT amount as a loss (net profit = the cost of the goods lost); an unknown cost stays `null`.
  - **Store credit IS a gift certificate** (`origin:'return'`, shared `G-` numbering) — reusing the v11.41 object inherits the picker, `gcFindByCode` scanning, proportional cart spreading, expiry sweep, breakage, backup and the printed document for free. It runs **two years** (`RETURN_CREDIT_TERM_DAYS = 730`, separate from the unchanged `GC_DEFAULT_TERM_DAYS = 365`): the shop is **seasonal** (Apr–Nov), so a customer given credit in August is realistically back the *following* season and a 1-year card could expire days before they walk in — and there is no hurry to be paid, since the money is already in hand while the credit stays a fixed number of euros against stock that reprices with gold. `gcTermDays(g)` picks the right term per card so **Extend** offers a card its own kind. **The load-bearing reason** is the split-regime problem it already solved: the credit carries `cashSale` from the original sale and is consumed as `giftCardWasCash` when spent months later, so money taken as cash in June is still treated as cash when it becomes a ring in August. A separate `C-` series was rejected (two scan resolvers, collision risk, no gain). The Desk "Outstanding" total sums gift + credit (genuinely one liability) with a split line beneath.
  - **Printed EXCHANGE CARD:** `buildGiftCertHTML` branches on `gcIsCredit(g)` for the title («Κάρτα Αλλαγής» / Exchange Card), the sub-line and the terms ("issued for a returned item"); `shareGiftCert` renames the file and keeps `{landscape:true}`. Greek all-caps tonos verified stripped by measurement (painted width matches the un-accented reference exactly).
  - **Exchange card on a GIFT (κάρτα αλλαγής, `X-0001`):** someone buys a piece as a present; the person who unwraps it may want to change it, without ever learning what was paid. The card **carries no value and is not a certificate** — it identifies THE SALE. `exchangeCardNo` is stamped on the sale record (counter `/jewelprice_exchangecard_counter`); `exNoFmt`/`exFindByCode` use the `X-` + hyphen form, which can collide with neither a reference code (2 letters + 4 digits) nor a gift certificate (`G-0004`). `handleScannedCode` resolves it **before** `lookupCode` and opens `showSaleDetail(id, true)` — the piece is on screen and ↩️ Return is right there, so the owner either swaps on the spot (🔄 Αλλαγή τώρα) or issues credit. **Value only comes into existence when the piece actually comes back.** Two entry points, both asked for: a **🎁 Gift toggle in the sale box** (`_saleWantsExCard`, captured into `_wantExCard` at the top of `confirmSale` because `closeSaleModal()` runs mid-function and resets it; the card is issued at the very END, since it needs the record's id/invId/seqRef) and a **🎁 button in the sale detail** for the customer who asks on the way out. `issueExchangeCard` is **idempotent** — a reprint of a lost card returns the same number, never a second card. `buildExchangeCardHTML` is A4 landscape in the certificate style, showing photo/description/metal/karat/type/ref and **never a price** (verified by asserting no currency figure appears in the output). `_exCardItem` resolves the piece from `invId`, else the QR. *(Note: `metalLabel` is only ever a LOCAL variable elsewhere in the file, never a global — referencing it here threw a ReferenceError swallowed by the try/catch, silently dropping the metal line. Uses `t(it.metal)`.)*
  - **An expired card revives when you give it a new date (v11.46 fix):** `_gcEditSave` revived only a `redeemed` card, and `_gcCheckExpiry` skips anything not `active` — so pushing out an **expired** card's expiry left it `expired`, and therefore unspendable (`gcIsSpendable` requires `active`). It now reactivates an expired card with a balance whose new date is in the future. Found by the owner asking "if he's abroad, can I just change the date?".
  - **Exchange = return + sale, not a new transaction type.** `_armCreditForNextSale` / `_consumePendingCredit` hand the fresh credit to the next sale dialog opened (hooked into `openSaleModalWithBonus`, the one funnel all four openers share). Deliberately narrow: **single-use and ignored after 30 minutes**, so it can never silently attach to an unrelated sale the next day; it is visible and removable in the dialog.
  - **Guards** (all derived, never a stored flag that can drift): a sale is returned iff a record has `returnOfSaleId === s.id`; a return can't be returned; a certificate-part-paid sale can only refund that portion as credit (cash was never paid for it); a return is **undone, not deleted** (`_undoReturn` re-marks the piece sold, removes the record, cancels the card) and is **refused once the credit has been spent**; the sale *underneath* a return can't be deleted either. Both guards live inside `deleteSaleRecord`, so every entry point is covered. `saleGroup` is dropped from a return (inheriting it would fold the pair into one €0 row); a multi-item return mints its own group so it collapses into ONE row and issues **ONE** card (`processReturnGroup`, proportional split with the remainder absorbed by the last line).
  - **`_saleExVat` made sign-safe:** its gift clamp `min(max(giftCardAmt,0), amt)` assumed a positive amount and, on `amount:-1500` with no certificate, returned `min(0,-1500) = -1500` — dumping the whole value into the gift portion under the wrong regime. Now clamps by magnitude and forces the gift portion to share the sale's sign. **Proven identical across 1024 cases** (8 amounts × 8 gift values × cash flags × 4 VAT rates), so no existing record moved.
  - **Shared `_restoreStockForSale`** extracted from `deleteSaleRecord` (discrete piece by exact `invId`, else a **sold-only** QR match; plus bulk grams) so return and delete are literally the same act. Verified against the real backup: **all 33 genuinely sold items restore the exact record in both the modern (`invId`) and legacy (QR-only) paths, including the 3 whose QR is shared with an in-stock twin, with zero collateral changes.**
  - **`_nextSaleId` (real bug found in testing):** sale ids are `Date.now()`, and returning a 3-item cart created **three records with the same id** — an id is what every lookup, delete and return keys on. Now steps to the next free number; applied to `addSaleRecord` too (returns `Date.now()` unless taken, so nothing else changes).
  - **UI:** `↩️ Return` in the sale detail (permission = `can('sales_admin')` **or** the employee who made that sale — same rule Edit uses); dialog with refund amount / method (💳 credit default · 💵 cash · 🏦 card) / date (invisible-input-over-`fmtDate` pattern) / reason / 📦 restock toggle, plus a notice spelling out the forced gift split and what "not back in stock" costs; `Return All` on a multi-item transaction with per-line "returned" marks; red negative rows with a `↩️ ΕΠΙΣΤΡΟΦΗ` badge; "Returned on …" banner on the original; `🎟️` + "Return credit" in the Desk gift list. `fmtAmt` now prints the sign before the symbol (**−€1,500.00**, identical for positives).
  - **Known/left alone:** the Delete button still shows on a returned sale and refuses with an explanatory toast (rather than being hidden); `renderSalesDashboard` was found to be **orphaned pre-existing dead code** (all its DOM ids were removed long ago, so it throws on every call) — flagged separately, not touched here.
- **A return credit says so, everywhere (v11.47):** the owner hit this in the shop — a credit from a return appeared in the sale-time picker as an ordinary "Gift Card", indistinguishable from a bought certificate. v11.46 had labelled it in the Desk list only, which is the one place it doesn't matter. Two shared helpers next to `gcIsCredit` — **`gcKindIcon(g)`** (🎟️ / 🎁) and **`gcKindLabel(g)`** («Πίστωση επιστροφής» / «Δωροεπιταγή») — are now used by **every** surface that names a card: the sale-time picker rows (plus a green tint on a credit) and its header («Δωροεπιταγή ή πίστωση»), the applied line in the sale dialog (`_updateSaleGiftDisplay`), the custom-order equivalent (`_repUpdateGiftDisplay`), the card detail header, and the `gcUseOnSale` button label. **Type toggle in Edit (`_gceSetKind`, persisted in `_gcEditSave`):** cards created before returns existed carry no `origin` and would read as gift certificates forever, so the owner can mark one himself — 🎁 Gift certificate / 🎟️ Return credit. Marking sets `origin:'return'` (which also switches `gcTermDays` to the 2-year term); un-marking **deletes** the key rather than storing `'purchase'`, so an ordinary certificate stays byte-identical to how it has always been stored. **Bug fixed in the same pass:** the v11.46 "🎁 Δώρο — κάρτα αλλαγής" toggle in the sale box carried `data-i18n="exCardOffer"` with no such key in either dictionary, so it rendered the raw key (`exCar…`) — spotted in a screenshot, not by reasoning. Added to both dicts, and a full sweep now confirms every `data-i18n` key in the markup resolves. *(That sweep also surfaced 9 keys present only in `T.en` — incl. the spot-price warning banner — which therefore show English in Greek mode. Pre-existing, flagged separately, not touched here. `qrFromGallery`/`qrClose` are deliberately absent from the dictionary and handled around L4093.)*
- **Scanning an exchange card mid-sale applies its credit (v11.48):** reported from the counter — the owner started a new sale, opened the certificate picker, scanned the customer's **κάρτα αλλαγής** (`X-0001`), and instead of the amount going onto the sale he was dropped on the old sale's detail sheet. The code was doing what v11.46 told it to: an exchange card identifies a SALE, so it called `showSaleDetail`. That is right when nothing else is happening and **wrong while a sale is being written** — the card holds no money itself, but the **credit its return produced does**. `handleScannedCode`'s exchange branch now checks the same "is a pick in progress?" condition the gift-certificate branch uses (`saleModal` visible, **or** `_gcPickTarget === 'repair'` for a custom-order collection) and, if so, follows the chain — `_saleReturnRecord(sale.id)` → `creditCardId` → `gcFindById` → `_pickGiftForSale` — so the credit lands on the sale being written. Three honest outcomes instead of a silent misfire: credit applied (toast `🎟️ G-0003`); credit exists but is spent/cancelled («δεν έχει υπόλοιπο»); or the piece **hasn't been returned yet**, which says so and tells the owner to close the sale, scan again and press Return. **Crucially it no longer navigates away in any of those cases** — hijacking the sale in progress was the actual complaint. Outside a sale the original behaviour is unchanged (opens the sale, piece on screen, ↩️ Return to hand). Verified by replaying the exact reported sequence: €210 sale, scan `X-0001` → `−€175.23` applied, `€34.77` cash due, sale detail never opens.
- **Sale box scrolls; credit naming polish (v11.49):** all reported from the counter with screenshots. (1) **`#saleModal` could not scroll** — its outer div was `align-items:center` with **no `overflow-y` and no `max-height` on the inner box**, so once the content exceeded the viewport it was clipped at BOTH ends with no way to reach the title or the Record button. Latent since long before returns (a certificate applied + the bonus preview was already enough); the v11.46 exchange-card toggle is simply what tipped it over. Fixed with the same `align-items:flex-start` + `overflow-y:auto` + inner `margin:auto` pattern every other sheet uses. Verified at a 630px-tall viewport: 901px of content, 271px of scroll, title reachable at the top and Record at the bottom. (2) **Desk tab** renamed `deskGift` → «Επιταγές & Πιστώσεις» / "Gift Cards & Credit"; measured at 375px, wraps to two lines in both languages with no clipping or row overflow. (3) **The sale-box exchange-card toggle** dropped its 🎁 and the word "Gift" (owner: it read as a near-duplicate of the "Gift certificate or credit" button directly above) — now plain «Κάρτα αλλαγής» / "Exchange card" with the 🔄 exchange icon this feature already uses; changed in the markup, in `_syncSaleExCardBtn`'s two innerHTML branches, and in both dictionaries. (4) A credit with **no customer name** showed a bare `—` as its title in the Desk list; it now falls back to `gcKindLabel(g)` (and the redundant subtitle is suppressed in that case), and the **Outstanding** line uses 🎟️ instead of 🎁 when the whole balance is return credit.
- **Printed stamp box (v11.50) — SIZES ARE NOT YET CONSISTENT, see below:** the owner reported the stamp box on the gift certificate / κάρτα αλλαγής was too small, and that the real shop stamp is **oblong (2:1), not square**. The app currently carries **three different stamp boxes**: the quote/certificate at **10×5 cm** (the original, built when the owner first supplied the real dimensions — hence the authoritative 2:1 ratio), the reservation form at **5×3 cm**, and the gift/exchange card at **4.4×2.6 cm**. The card's box was enlarged to **7×3.5 cm** — the largest exact-2:1 box the A4-landscape foot row can take. That row was already packed edge to edge (239.4mm of children in 239.4mm), so the width came out of `.sign-line` (70→50mm) and the gaps (10→6mm), and the extra height out of the flexible `.spacer`; verified to still fit ONE sheet with a long dedication present. **RESOLVED in v11.55 — see the stamp entry below; the size is 10x5 cm on all three.**
- **A credit is never anonymous (v11.51):** found by the owner on the first real test. Small sales are routinely recorded with NO customer details — normal practice — and `_gcIssueReturnCredit` copied `orig.customer`/`orig.email` from the sale, so returning such a sale produced an **anonymous credit**: nobody to warn before expiry, and no way to match a lost card to a person. A credit is a **debt the shop owes**; it must have a name on it. The return dialog now carries a **customer block** (name with Clients autocomplete via `_retClientAutocomplete`/`_retSelectClient`, country-code phone, email), pre-filled from the sale and topped up from the client book when the name is already known (the sale record stores no phone). `_retWillIssueCredit()` — true when the method is credit **or** a certificate portion forces one — drives a «— απαιτείται» marker and a hard block in `_returnDlgConfirm` if the name is blank; a **cash or card refund requires nothing**, since no debt is left behind. A name with neither phone nor email is allowed but warns that the expiry reminder has nowhere to go. Details flow `opts.customer` → `processReturn` → `_gcIssueReturnCredit` (new 4th arg, falling back to the sale's customer for any caller that omits it), are stamped on the RETURN record, and the customer is saved via `upsertClientFromSale`. **The original sale is deliberately not back-filled** — history is not rewritten. Verified end to end on a customer-less sale: anonymous save refused, cash path unaffected, card carries name/phone/email, client saved.
- **Searchable country picker on phone fields (v11.52):** a tourist says their country and the owner had to hunt it in a native `<select>` of **50 flags with no names** — `_countryOptions` rendered only `flag + dial`, even though `COUNTRY_DIAL` has carried a `n` (name) all along. Replaced with a bottom sheet: **search box + alphabetical list of country NAMES**, Greece pinned first (shop's own country and the default). `_cpFilter` matches the **name or the dial code** ("no", "norway" and "47" all land on Norway; a leading `+` is tolerated) and ranks name-prefix > name-contains > code-prefix. **The low-risk part:** the visible control is a `<button>` while the dial code lives in a **hidden `<input>` keeping the same id**, so `_readPhoneField`/`_setPhoneField` and all 13 call sites keep reading `.value` exactly as before — no caller changed. `_setPhoneField` branches on `tagName === 'SELECT'` so a legacy select would still work, and `_syncCountryBtn` keeps the button label in step. The two static `<select>`s in the markup (sale modal, client edit) were converted; `_countryOptions` now also includes the name, for the legacy path. Picking a country focuses the number field. Sheet is `z-index:1000000`, above every dialog that contains a phone field. Verified: search probes, Greece-first + alphabetical ordering, no stray selects, and a `_setPhoneField`→`_readPhoneField` round trip.
- **A sheet with a search field belongs at the TOP (v11.53):** the owner caught the v11.52 country picker breaking the moment he typed — it was a bottom sheet, so its results sat directly above the keyboard's landing zone and vanished behind it; dismissing the keyboard left the single result pinned to the very bottom edge. Re-anchored to `align-items:flex-start` with `border-radius:0 0 16px 16px`, `padding-top:env(safe-area-inset-top)` and `max-height:92dvh` (with a `92vh` fallback) so the box tracks the shrunken visual viewport while the keyboard is up. Verified at 393x400 (≈ a phone mid-typing): search visible, the Norway row at y=112, fully inside the viewport. The search input is now **autofocused** too, which only became safe once the sheet moved to the top — tap the flag and type. **The general rule the owner drew out of this — a bottom sheet is fine for a list you only scroll, and wrong as soon as it contains typing.** An audit of the other 30 bottom-anchored sheets found five with input fields (`openBulkRestockSheet`, `openAddCoinModal`, `openInvGroup`, `invShareToTeam`, one inline) — all left alone for now, because in those you type INTO the field (browsers scroll a focused input into view) rather than reading content that changes BELOW it, which is what made the picker fail.
- **Remaining typing sheets moved to the top (v11.54):** applying the v11.53 rule to the rest, at the owner's request. A re-audit of the 31 bottom-anchored overlays found the earlier count of five was too crude — `openAddCoinModal` is already a CENTRED modal, and one hit was not an overlay at all. The three genuine bottom sheets containing typing fields were converted to `align-items:flex-start` + `padding-top:env(safe-area-inset-top)`, bottom-rounded corners and `max-height:92dvh` (`92vh` fallback) + `overflow-y:auto`: **`openBulkRestockSheet`** (weight field — and it had **no max-height at all**, so on a short screen it could clip with no way to reach the buttons, the same latent bug as the sale modal in v11.49), **`openInvGroup`** (the sell-several stepper) and **`invShareToTeam`** (message field). Verified all three open at `top:0`, anchored flex-start and scrollable. **The rule now applied consistently: a bottom sheet is for a list you only scroll; anything with typing is anchored to the top.**
- **THE SHOP STAMP IS 10 x 5 cm (v11.55) — settled, do not guess again:** the owner confirmed the real stamp is the size of the ORIGINAL box on the quote/certificate, which he supplied when that document was first built. It is **oblong (2:1), not square**. All three documents now use `width:100mm; height:50mm`: the quote/certificate (already correct, untouched), the **reservation form** (was 5x3cm — its `.sign-line` was `width:62%`, leaving nowhere near enough beside it, so it is now a fixed 60mm; row measures 60+100 in 174mm, no overflow) and the **gift certificate / κάρτα αλλαγής** (was 4.4x2.6, briefly 7x3.5 in v11.50). The card's A4-landscape footer could not fit a 100mm box alongside signature + number/dates + QR, so **the number and dates moved out of the footer to their own centred `.meta-line` above it**; the footer is now signature 55mm | stamp 100mm | QR 22mm = 193mm inside 239.4mm. Verified with the longest realistic content (long recipient + full 80-char dedication): still ONE sheet, terms ending at 786 of 794px. **If a layout ever cannot fit the stamp, rearrange the layout — never shrink the box.**
- **Client sort is remembered (v11.55):** the Clients tab's sort `<select>` was plain static markup with no persistence, so it reset to name A→Z on every reload while Stock (`localStorage 'invSort'`) remembered its choice. Now `_clientSortChanged` writes `localStorage 'jpro_clientSort'` and `_restoreClientSort` restores it once per page load (guarded by a `dataset.restored` flag — the select is static markup and keeps its value between renders, only a reload resets it). An unknown/removed saved value falls back to the markup default rather than breaking.
- **Greek/English dictionaries fully in sync (v11.55):** 9 keys existed only in `T.en`, and since `t()` falls back `T[lang][key] || T['en'][key]`, they rendered in ENGLISH while the app was in Greek — including `spotWarningText`, the "cannot fetch live price" banner the owner actually sees, plus the heavy-weight tier fields and the whole secondary-metal section. Greek added for all 9; a sweep now reports **zero** keys present in one dictionary but not the other, in either direction. (`qrFromGallery`/`qrClose` remain deliberately outside the dictionary — handled directly near L4093.) A language pass over the whole returns/exchange flow — dialog, notices, credit sheet, sale detail, Desk list, printed card — confirmed every surface differs correctly between EL and EN.
- **Partly-spent cards announce themselves (v11.56):** the owner asked what happens when a €200 certificate is used for a €150 purchase — is a second card issued for the change, and is he warned if the customer comes back later waving the original €200 paper? Driving the real flow settled it: **no second card is ever issued.** `gcBalance` is derived (`amount − Σ redemptions`), so the SAME card keeps its number and carries its remaining balance for life — after the €150 sale there is still exactly one card, face €200, balance €50, status `active`. Re-scanning it applies the **balance**, never the face value, so the money was always right. **What was missing is that nothing said so.** The paper in the customer's hand still reads €200, and the only place showing «€50.00 από €200.00» was the Desk list — no help mid-sale. Now, when `gcRedeemed(g) > 0`: the scan toast reads `G-0002 — υπόλοιπο €50.00 από €200.00`, the applied box in the sale dialog gains an amber `#saleGiftPartial` line («Χρησιμοποιημένη — υπόλοιπο X από Y» plus how much was redeemed and the date of the last redemption), and the picker row shows «από €200.00» beneath the balance. An untouched card shows none of it — verified, along with both languages.
- **A part-spent card reprints as a STATEMENT (v11.57):** the owner's idea, and a better one than the v11.56 warning — throw the stale paper away, press Print, and hand over a card that explains itself. When `gcRedeemed(g) > 0`, `buildGiftCertHTML` now sets the **balance** as the big figure (not the face value), prints «αρχικό ποσό €200.00» beneath it, and adds a `.stmt` block listing each redemption — **date, what it bought, amount** — closed by a balance row. New helper **`_gcRedemptionLines(g)`** resolves the description: a redemption stores only `saleId`, so it looks the sale up and, when that sale has a `saleGroup`, joins all its siblings (a cart draws the certificate down once against its first record). `printGiftCert`/`shareGiftCert`/`openGiftCardDetail` became **async** and `await getSales()` first, or the descriptions would be blank. The **same statement is in the app**, in the Desk card detail. **An untouched gift certificate is byte-identical to before** — face value, dedication and "To" all intact.
  - **Fitting it on one sheet took measurement, not guesswork.** A4 landscape has a fixed budget and the 10x5cm stamp takes a large fixed bite; the first attempt spilled onto a second page at 4+ purchases (948px vs 794) and pushed the stamp off. Three changes, each measured: the **To/dedication is suppressed once a card is part-spent** (it is a working document now, and that is ~130px), the list is **capped at the 4 most recent** with a «…και N ακόμη παλαιότερες» line so height is bounded however many purchases there are, and a **`.stmt-mode` body class** trims the presentation half (amount 64pt→46pt, store name 26pt→21pt, tighter rule and terms) — scoped by that class so an unused certificate keeps its full grandeur. Verified at 1, 2, 3, 4, 8-with-dedication and 20 purchases: **all fit ONE sheet, stamp still 100x50mm and fully on the page.**
- **«Pearl» rendered as «Δάκρυl» in Greek (v11.58) — pre-existing, everywhere:** found while checking a real card. `stonesDescDisplay` replaced terms with `split(k).join(v)`, a raw SUBSTRING replace, and the SHAPE `Pear` matched inside the STONE `Pearl`. Sorting longest-first could never help: the two live in **different maps** and the shape pass runs first (it has to, so «Emerald Cut» is not read as the stone «Emerald»). So every pearl — plain, Akoya, South Sea, Freshwater, Tahitian — printed as «Δάκρυl» on **quotes, certificates, catalogues and order sheets**. New helper `_replaceWholeWord` does `\b`-anchored replacement, used by both passes and by the `melee` swap; a shape only claims a placeholder slot if it actually matched. Verified: Pearl→Μαργαριτάρι, Pear→Δάκρυ, «Emerald Cut» still parks correctly, **zero** stones or shapes fail to translate, English untouched.
- **Certificate statement: real item names, tappable (v11.58):** the v11.57 statement was built from the sale's `note`, which is wrong twice over — every member of a multi-item sale stores the SAME joined note (so listing the members repeated it, «A, B · A, B»), and that note falls back to the raw `qrCode` when an item has no description, printing `ORO14SK0.8-2x2.5PEA`. `_gcRedemptionLines` now builds each entry **per item from the inventory record**: reference code + type + karat + stones + description («ER0664 Σκουλαρίκια · 14K»), returning a structured `items[]` plus a flat `desc` for print. It also returns `saleId`, so each line in the Desk sheet is **tappable → `showSaleDetail`** (`_gcOpenRedemptionSale`). `openGiftCardDetail`/`printGiftCert`/`shareGiftCert` now await **inventory** as well as sales.
- **Stamp box → stamp LINE (v11.58):** the owner confirmed 10x5cm is exactly right but the dashed box "looks ugly" on paper. All three documents now keep the same **50mm of clear room** closed by a rule with «ΣΦΡΑΓΙΔΑ» beneath it, matching the signature line and sharing its baseline — it shows where to stamp without drawing a box. `.stamp-box` is now a wrapper with `.ln` (the 50mm room) and `.lbl`.
- **Frame overflow — and a measurement lesson (v11.58):** the owner's picture 3 showed the terms paragraph printing OUTSIDE the gold frame whenever a certificate carried a dedication. v11.57 had "verified" this fits by checking `document.body.scrollHeight` — **worthless here**, because `.inner` is `height:100%`, so content escapes the frame without ever growing the body. Measuring `terms.bottom` against `.inner.bottom` instead reproduced it immediately: **67px past the frame**. Fixed with a `.has-ded` body class (mirroring `stmt-mode`) that tightens only the dedication case, plus tighter `stmt-mode` values and a statement cap of **3** rows. Re-verified the right way — plain, dedication, long dedication, and statements of 1/2/3/4/12/40 purchases all sit inside the frame with 15–43px of headroom. **Never measure page fit with `body.scrollHeight` on these fixed-height documents.**
- **Exchange-card scan needed a warm sales cache (v11.59):** the owner reported that test 6 (scan `X-…`) "worked earlier but I cannot replicate it now" — a perfect description of a cold-cache bug. `gcFindByCode` resolves through `getGiftCards()`, which **self-loads from localStorage**, but `exFindByCode` read `(_salesCache || [])` **raw**, and that cache is null until something touches Sales in the session. So scanning an exchange card worked right after doing a return (cache warm) and silently found nothing on a fresh app open, falling through as an unknown item code. `handleScannedCode` is now async and `await getSales()` when the scanned value matches `/^X-\d{1,6}$/` and the cache is empty — gated on the pattern so an ordinary item scan never pays for it. Same family as the v10.72 stocktake scanner and v10.74 export sheet: **a resolver that assumes a cache someone else populated.**
- **Card codes can now be TYPED, not only scanned (v11.59):** found while answering the above — `handleCodeKey`/`scheduleLookup` call `lookupCode` directly, and `lookupCode` knew nothing about `G-`/`X-` codes, so only the camera could reach those resolvers. Typing a card number reported "code not found". `lookupCode` now delegates a `/^[GX]-\d{1,6}$/i` match to `handleScannedCode` (case-insensitive), which is the same fallback philosophy as the OCR text-scan for damaged tags. **Recursion guard:** `handleScannedCode`'s own fallback calls `lookupCode(code, {noSpecial:true})`, so an unknown card number cannot bounce between the two forever — verified.
- **A scanned card is HELD for the sale that follows (v11.60):** the owner described the real counter order — the customer hands the credit over first, he scans it, then she tries six or seven pieces before choosing. Scanning a card outside a sale merely opened its detail, so by the time he pressed SOLD it was gone and had to be picked again by hand (verified before changing anything). `handleScannedCode`'s no-sale-open branch now also calls `_armCreditForNextSale`, reusing the machinery built for 🔄 Αλλαγή τώρα, and still opens the card so its balance can be read aloud. **`_consumePendingCredit` no longer clears on the sale box merely OPENING** — she is still choosing and that box may open and close repeatedly; losing the card on the first open would mean re-scanning every time. It is now released on three events instead: a **recorded sale** (`confirmSale`), the **✕** beside it (`clearGiftFromSale` — a deliberate "not this sale"), or the **30-minute** staleness bound. `closeSaleModal` deliberately does NOT clear it. Verified end to end: scan → six open/cancel cycles → SOLD applies €150 of a €485 credit, €335 left, released after recording; and it does not leak into the next customer, does not return after the ✕, and is ignored at 31 minutes.
- **An unloaded card list answered "never spent" (v11.61) — THE class of bug to watch for:** reported from a live test with Maria; reproduced exactly against the real code, then fixed. **Root cause: `autoSync()` runs ONCE at boot ([L26374](jewelprice.htm)), is NOT awaited, and every pull inside it swallows its own errors — so the screen is usable while `pullGiftCardsFromCloud` is still queued behind six other calls, and `getGiftCards()` legitimately returns `[]`.** Every guard that asks "has this credit been spent?" then answers **no** and waves through the exact thing it exists to stop. Sequence that produced the reported mess: a €310 sale → returned as a €310 credit → €265.28 exchange sale against it (balance €44.72) → then, with the card list empty, (1) **`_undoReturn` allowed the return to be undone** although the credit was spent (`gcFindById` → null → the `gcRedeemed(g) > 0` check skipped), (2) **`deleteSaleRecord` deleted the exchange sale and `_gcUnapplyForSale` handed nothing back** (empty list matches nothing, returns silently), (3) the €310 sale then had no return guarding it and deleted too. The card reloaded from Firebase still carrying the €265.28 redemption; cancelling it left a **cancelled card with a live redemption — unspendable AND undeletable** (`_gcCanDelete` requires zero redemptions). Verified reproduction reproduced all of it: `sales left: []`, `status=cancelled redeemed=265.28 balance=44.72 canDelete=false`, piece correctly back `in_stock`. **Fix:** new **`ensureGiftCards()`** pulls if the once-per-boot pull hasn't landed and **returns whether the answer can be trusted** (true also when no cloud is configured — local IS the truth then); `_undoReturn` and `_gcUnapplyForSale` both call it, and `deleteSaleRecord` checks it **before** removing the sale (deleting first and discovering afterwards leaves the sale gone and the credit still spent). When it can't load, both **refuse with a plain bilingual message** instead of proceeding blind. Nuance: `_undoReturn` refuses on "card not found" only when the load **failed** — if the load succeeded and the card is genuinely gone there is nothing left to protect, so it proceeds (otherwise that return could never be undone). **Escape hatch for money already stranded:** `_gcOrphanRedemptions(g, sales)` finds redemptions whose `saleId` names a sale that no longer exists, surfaced as an amber block + one-tap **`_gcReleaseOrphans`** in the card detail (owner/manager, confirm-gated) — **surfaced, never repaired silently** (the v10.19 stone auto-pricing lesson: this is money). Two load-bearing guards inside it: a redemption with **no `saleId`** is legitimate (a custom order collected without being logged as income) and is never orphaned; and an **EMPTY sales array is never evidence** — `pullSalesFromCloud` returns `[]` on *any* failure, so treating that as "none of these sales exist" would orphan every redemption on every card and hand back money customers genuinely spent. `_gcReleaseOrphans` re-reads sales from the cloud and aborts on an empty result. Verified: offline → both actions refuse, nothing lost; online → undo blocked while spent, delete hands €265.28 back, undo then allowed, card ends deletable; a healthy part-spent card and a fresh card show no warning; all four safety cases return 0 orphans. **Not caused by two devices** — the owner confirmed everything happened on one phone. **Still open (found in the same pass, not yet fixed):** on a **cart** paid by a certificate the redemption links only to the FIRST record ([L10779](jewelprice.htm)), so deleting a single non-first line leaves the certificate drawn down — verified. And the whole-array `PUT` model (51 call sites across sales/gift cards/repairs/reservations/clients, no read-before-write) is last-writer-wins across devices; inventory is safe because `saveInventoryItem` writes per item.
- **A failed write reported as a successful restore (v11.62) — the same silent-failure family as v11.61:** the owner checked after the v11.61 deploy and found the exchange piece (`KYK925B15.2` / BR0207) still marked **sold** with its sale record gone, and "💰 View Sale" dead-ending on «No sale record found». Cause: **`_restoreStockForSale` never checked `saveInventoryItem`'s return value.** It flipped the local copy, fired «📦 Inventory restored», and moved on — so when the Firebase PUT failed on the same weak connection that broke the gift-card pull, the piece stayed sold in the cloud and came back sold on the next open, with its sale already deleted. (Note `saveInventoryItem` returns `res.ok`, or `false` on throw — it never throws, so the `try/catch` around it caught nothing.) **Fix:** the result is now checked; on failure the local cache is rolled back to `sold` (never leave a lie in the cache) and a plain bilingual message names the manual repair path (Stock → Restock → Restore). A no-match is also announced now (`⚠️ could not find the piece`) instead of returning in silence — that silence is how a piece stays sold forever. `invRestoreToStock` got the same treatment: a failed save rolls back and **returns before deleting the sale record**, so a restore that didn't persist can't also destroy the sale that proves it happened. **Escape hatch for the piece already stranded:** `invGoToSale(qrCode, invId)` now takes the record id — it prefers an exact **`invId`** match (pieces in a similar-items group or a coin batch share a QR, so the old QR-only match could land on a twin's sale) and, when a **sold** non-bulk piece has no sale record at all, offers «Επαναφορά σε απόθεμα;» right there instead of dead-ending on a toast. Both card call sites pass `'${id}'`. Verified: a failed inventory write leaves the piece `sold` + warns; a good connection restores it; the stuck-bracelet case restores and clears `dateSold`; and a piece **with** a live sale still navigates to sale `999002` with no prompt and stays sold. **The lesson, now three for three (v10.72 stocktake, v10.74 export, v11.61 gift cards, v11.62 stock): every load and every write in this app must be checked — an unchecked failure here does not look like an error, it looks like success.**
- **A return credit is a CREDIT, not an exchange card (v11.63):** the owner drew the distinction from the counter — the **κάρτα αλλαγής (`X-…`)** is the paper that goes WITH a gift: it carries no value and only identifies the sale. The moment the piece actually comes back, what the customer holds is **money**, and the paper he hands over is a **credit (`G-…`)**. v11.46 had titled the credit document «Κάρτα Αλλαγής» / "Exchange Card", so the two different documents shared a name. `buildGiftCertHTML` now titles a credit **«Πίστωση» / "Credit"** (new `_credTitle`, used by the `<title>` and `.doc-title`), the terms read «Η παρούσα **πίστωση** εκδόθηκε για επιστροφή κοσμήματος…» / "…present this **credit** when redeeming", the unspent sub-line drops "towards an **exchange**" for the plain «για κοσμήματα της επιλογής σας», and `shareGiftCert` names the file `credit-G-0012.pdf` (was `exchange-`). **`buildExchangeCardHTML` (the `X-…` document) is deliberately untouched** — verified still «Κάρτα Αλλαγής» and still showing no price. **The statement itself already worked for credits** (the `_spent > 0` block was never gated on `gcIsCredit`) — verified end-to-end for the owner's exact flow: a fresh credit prints €200 «για κοσμήματα της επιλογής σας»; after €100 is spent the reprint shows **€100 balance large, «αρχικό ποσό €200.00» beneath, and «ER0664 Σκουλαρίκια · 14K · Σκουλαρίκια καρφωτά −€100.00 / Υπόλοιπο €100.00»** — the same block the Desk card detail shows. A bought gift certificate is byte-identical to before (still «Δωροεπιταγή»). One-sheet fit re-verified the v11.58 way (`terms.bottom` vs `.inner.bottom`, **never `body.scrollHeight`**): 42px headroom with a 3-row statement, stamp still 100mm and fully on the page.
- **Cart + certificate: deleting one line gave money away (v11.63):** flagged in the v11.61 pass, fixed here. `confirmSale` draws the certificate down **once** for the whole transaction, linked to the FIRST record ([L10779](jewelprice.htm)), while **every** member carries `giftCardId`. So on a €100 + €200 cart paid by a €300 card: deleting the **second** line released **nothing**, and deleting the **first** released **all €300** while the €200 line stayed on the books — inventing €200. Fix: `_gcApplyRedemption` now also stores **`saleGroup`** on the redemption, and `_gcUnapplyForSale(saleId, rec, remaining)` takes the record being deleted plus the sales that survive it. It finds the redemption by exact `saleId`, else by `saleGroup`, else (a cart recorded before `saleGroup` was stored) by a **sibling's** id; then, if lines of that same transaction paid by that card are still on the books, it **subtracts only that line's `giftCardAmt`** and **re-points `r.saleId` at a surviving sibling** — without that, the remainder would read as money spent on a sale that no longer exists and `_gcOrphanRedemptions` (v11.61) would flag it. Only when no sibling survives (or no share is known — legacy records) is the redemption removed outright, **so a plain single-item sale behaves exactly as it always did**. Verified: second-line delete → redeemed €100 / balance €200; first-line delete → redeemed €200 / balance €100, link points at a live sale, not orphan-flagged; deleting the remaining line in either order → balance €300, zero redemptions; single-item regression fully released and back to `active`.
- **Multi-device safety — per-record cloud writes (v11.64) — THE most important structural change in the app:** with a third person joining the shop the owner asked whether concurrent use was safe. It was not. **Sales, gift cards, reservations, repairs and clients were each saved by PUTting the WHOLE list** (`pushSalesToCloud(sales)` → `PUT /jewelprice_sales.json`, ~50 such call sites), and a device only refreshes its copy at a **full app restart** (`autoSync()` runs once, at boot, and is not awaited). So a phone open since morning held a morning-old list, and its next save **erased every record the other phones had written since** — silently, no error. It survived two people only because phones suspend and reload constantly. (**Inventory was never affected**: `saveInventoryItem` has always written one item to its own path — that is precisely the model adopted here.) **Fix, underneath the call sites rather than through them:** each list keeps a **snapshot** (`_cloudSnap[path]`: Map of id → JSON as last seen in cloud, set by `_snapRecord` on every pull), and `_syncListToCloud(path, list, idOf)` diffs the list against it, writing **only genuinely changed records, one PUT/DELETE per record path**. The load-bearing property: **a record this device has never seen is not in its snapshot, so it is never written and never deleted** — another phone's work is invisible and therefore untouchable; and an unmodified record hashes identically and is skipped, so a stale copy can't clobber someone's edit either. All ~50 existing save sites are **unchanged**. `_migrateNodeToKeyed` converts each legacy array node to records keyed by id in ONE atomic PUT (owner/manager device, no-ops once converted, run from `autoSync` **before** anything pulls); every pull now reads both shapes via `_listFromNode`. **`_syncListToCloud` lazily establishes its snapshot with a single GET when the node was never pulled** (offline boot) — without that, one new sale on a 500-sale shop would have fired 500 PUTs and overwritten other phones' changes; **verified: 1 GET + 1 PUT, not 501**. **Record ids had to become globally unique** — this was found *by the test, not by reasoning*: ids were `Date.now()` checked only against the device's OWN list, so the first three-phone run minted **three identical ids** and two sales overwrote the third. Now `_stampId()` = `Date.now() * 1000 + _deviceSalt()` (a permanent per-device 100–999 in `localStorage 'jpro_device_salt'`), strictly increasing within a device (stepping by a full 1000 so the salt digits — and thus cross-device uniqueness — survive); used by sales (`_nextSaleId`), gift cards, clients, reservations and repairs. Verified 5000 ids on one device and 900 interleaved across three, all unique, still time-ordered. **`_refreshSharedLists()`** now re-pulls all five lists on `visibilitychange`→visible (throttled 20s, skipped while a sale/return/gift/repair dialog is open so nothing shifts under someone's hands) and repaints Sales/Desk/Clients if open. **Proven with a three-phone simulation against the real functions** (fake Firebase honouring per-path PUT/DELETE, one cache + snapshot per phone): three phones open at 09:00, none reopened, each records a sale → **old code kept 1 of 3 (FAIL, 4 records), new code keeps 3 of 3 (PASS, 6)**; interleaved sell/delete/sell → 7 records, every new sale present, the deleted one gone, untouched originals kept; a phone picking up mid-session sees all 7. Also re-verified the v11.63 cart-certificate logic and normal record/edit/delete through the new path. **Rollout was gated on the other two users being PAUSED** (`toggleUserActive` → `active:false` → `showDeactivatedScreen`, a full-screen opaque lock at z-index 999999 that blocks every write) — with their apps closed and paused, no old-version device could write during the format change. There is **no service worker**; GitHub Pages serves `max-age=600`, so a reopened phone picks up the new version within 10 minutes. **Known gap, deliberately not fixed here:** a sale recorded with **no internet** lives only in that phone's `localStorage` and is silently dropped by the next pull (`pullSalesFromCloud` overwrites `_salesCache`) — flagged to the owner as its own future job.
- **Offline sales are no longer lost — the outbox (v11.65):** the last failure of the v11.61–64 family. A sale recorded with no signal was written to `_salesCache` + `localStorage`, the push failed silently, and **the next successful `pullSalesFromCloud` overwrote the cache with the cloud's copy — erasing it**, having looked recorded the whole time. In a stone building with bad reception that is a real lost sale. **The outbox** (`localStorage 'jpro_outbox'`, entries `{path,id,op:'put'|'del',json,at}`) holds anything that failed to reach the cloud: `_syncListToCloud` queues on a failed/errored write and clears on a confirmed one, and `addSaleRecord` **queues the new record BEFORE attempting the push** — on a phone that booted with no signal there is no cloud baseline, so the diff can't even work out what changed, and without this the sale would be queued nowhere. **The load-bearing part is `_applyOutbox`**: every pull now sets the snapshot from the cloud and then lays pending work back on top (`_snapRecord` → `_applyOutbox` → cache), so a sync can never drop unsent records — and an offline DELETE isn't resurrected either. `outboxFlush()` runs on the `online` event, on foreground (`_refreshSharedLists`, before pulling) and at boot after `migrateSharedLists`; unsent work is visible as **«⏳ N σε αναμονή»** in the header sync indicator (`_outboxUpdateBadge`, which only clears its own message so a real sync error isn't papered over), plus a toast at record time and on successful flush. **`outboxFlush` REPLAYS THE STORED INTENT** (PUT this json / DELETE this id) rather than re-diffing the device's list — the test caught that the diff version, run after a restart with `_salesCache` still null, read "everything was deleted" and tried to **empty the node**. That also motivated a permanent safety net in `_syncListToCloud`: **it refuses to delete every record of a populated list** (`next.size === 0 && snap.size > 0` → abort), since "delete all" is never a real shop action, only what a stale/unloaded cache looks like. Verified: a pull landing before the retry keeps the sale (the original killer), a cold offline boot survives an app restart and syncs on reconnect without wiping anything, an offline delete stays deleted, the wipe guard refuses and leaves the node intact, and normal online sales queue nothing. The v11.63 cart-certificate and v11.64 three-phone tests were re-run and still pass.
- **Card balance on the sale, back-navigation, and deleting the LAST card (v11.66):** three counter-reported fixes.
  - **The sale box now says what the card holds.** A certificate/credit applied to a sale showed only the amount taken (`−€155.23`) — nothing said the card was worth €520 or that €364.77 would be left, which is exactly what the customer asks while the sale is being written. `_updateSaleGiftDisplay` now ALWAYS fills `#saleGiftPartial`: an untouched card reads «Υπόλοιπο κάρτας: €520.00 → μετά: €364.77» in muted text, and a part-spent one keeps the v11.56 amber warning with an extra «Μετά από αυτή την πώληση» line. The colour is set per case so an ordinary card no longer looks like a warning.
  - **Closing a sale opened from a card returns to the card.** `_gcOpenRedemptionSale(saleId, gcId)` stores `window._gcDetailReturnTo`, and `closeSaleDetailModal(navigating)` reopens that card — but **only on a plain close** (the ✕ or the backdrop). The eight internal callers that close the sheet in order to go somewhere (View item, Return, Edit, …) pass `true`, or the card would pop back on top of wherever they went.
  - **Deleting the last gift card didn't persist (the real bug).** The v11.65 wipe guard in `_syncListToCloud` refuses to empty a populated node — the right rule for a stale/unloaded cache, but it also caught the perfectly ordinary act of deleting the ONLY card: the DELETE was never sent, the record stayed in Firebase and walked back in on the next refresh, looking deleted until the app was reopened. `_syncListToCloud(path, list, idOf, opts)` now takes `{allowEmpty:true}`, threaded through all five push wrappers and set at the deliberate-delete sites only (`_gcDelete`, `deleteSaleRecord`, `_undoReturn`, `_resDelete`, `_repairDelete`, `deleteClient`) — each of which has just filtered a record out of a list it is holding, so an empty result there is genuine. **Without the flag the guard is unchanged**, so a not-yet-loaded cache still can't wipe a node. Verified by driving the real function: delete-last now sends the DELETE and returns ok; an empty list with no flag is still refused with the node intact; delete-one-of-two, plain add and no-change are byte-identical to before; and a failed delete still queues in the outbox and reports honestly.
- **Quote / certificate for a sale that already happened (v11.67):** a customer paid cash for a baptism cross + a by-the-gram chain, came back an hour later and asked for a paper so she could split the cost with the second godparent — and there was nothing to give her. A quote is naturally printed BEFORE a sale (Calc is the right home for it), but a **certificate is almost always wanted AFTER**, and it could only be made from Calc, i.e. before. The sale detail sheet (single and multi-item) now has a **🖨️ Print** button → `openSaleDocPicker(saleId, groupId)`, a small sheet offering **Quote** and **Certificate**, each with **🖨️ Print** and **📤 PDF** (`_saleDocPrint` / `_saleDocShare`, going through the existing `openQuoteWindow` / `shareQuoteAsPDF`).
  - **The existing builders are reused untouched** — `buildQuoteHTML`, `buildCertificateHTML` and `buildMultiQuoteHTML`. The documents are byte-for-byte the same layout as the Calc ones; only where the numbers come from changed. Nothing was forked, so the two paths can never drift.
  - **`_saleDocResult(rec)`** turns one sale record into the calc-shaped `result` the builders expect. The piece is resolved the way the return/restore path does it (`bulkInvId` → `invId` → QR, since twins in a group share a QR). Metal/purity are rendered in the calc's own wording (`Χρυσός · 14K (58.5%)`, `Ασήμι · 925 (92.5%)`), stones come from `_invEditParseStoredStones(item.stonesDesc)` (so melee never leaks — `qty === 't'` is already suppressed by the builders).
  - **The price is what was PAID, not a live recalculation** — `finalPrice = rec.amount`, and the ex-VAT figure comes from `_saleExVat(rec, vat)`, the one helper that knows the cash / receipt / gift-certificate-portion rule. A **cash sale sets `window._quoteIsCash`**, so the printed document shows no VAT split at all, matching how the money was actually taken. **Discount and list-price rows are deliberately omitted**: a "list price" would have to be computed from TODAY's gold, which is not what the customer was quoted — on a document written after the fact the price paid is the whole truth.
  - **Bulk chains**: the weight printed is `bulkSoldWeight` (the grams that left), not the batch's remaining weight, and **no photo** (the batch picture is not the piece she bought) — the owner's explicit requirement.
  - A **multi-item transaction** prints ONE document via `buildMultiQuoteHTML` with `extraDiscPct = 0` and `roundTarget = 0`, so the total is the exact sum of the recorded lines. The sale's **own date/time** and `employeeName` are used, not today's. Return records get no Print button.
  - **`#sdmActions` now wraps** (`flex-wrap` + a `min-width:92px` rule): Print was the sixth button in the single-sale row, and each is created with an inline `flex:1` (basis 0), so wrapping alone would never trigger — the min-width is what forces two tidy rows of three instead of six slivers. Verified at 375px: no horizontal overflow.
  - **Naming a line — `rec.note` is a trap (fixed in v11.68).** Every member of a multi-item sale stores the **same joined note**, so the first version's `_itemDesc(item) || rec.note` fallback made a piece with no description of its own inherit its neighbour's name: Φανή's baptism cross printed as **"Bulk chains"**. Exactly the trap the certificate statement hit in v11.58. The note is now used only for a sale that is genuinely ONE record (`!rec.saleGroup`), and the fallback is the piece's **type** via `invTypeLabel` — always right, bilingual, and it reads properly to a customer ("Σταυρός" / "Cross"). A **bulk chain prefers the type outright**, because its stored description is the owner's internal batch wording, not the piece she bought.
  - **The batch link is the LAST resort, not the first (v11.69) — and the bug underneath it.** The printout still showed Φανή's cross as «Αλυσίδα» with the chain's weight after the naming fix, because the record genuinely resolved to the batch: `_saleDocResult` checked `bulkInvId` first, and **the cross's record actually carries the chain's `bulkInvId`/`bulkSoldWeight`**. Root cause in `confirmSale`: `_firstBulkInfo` fell back to `lastCalcResult.isBulkChain` **even when a cart decided item 1**, so a cart whose first piece was not the chain still got stamped with the chain's batch link, sold weight and cost — and because that `if` wins over the `else if (_firstInvId)`, the record's **own `invId` was never stamped**, meaning **deleting or returning that sale would have returned grams to the batch instead of putting the piece back in stock**. Two fixes: (1) `confirmSale` only takes the `lastCalcResult` fallback when there is **no cart at all** (`!_csiFirst`; `_cartSaleItems` is nulled on all four single-sale paths, so this is exact) — future records are clean; (2) `_saleDocResult` resolves `invId` → `invQr` → `bulkInvId`, and decides `isBulk` from the **resolved piece** rather than a stray `bulkSoldWeight`, so the documents are right on the records already written. Verified against the exact polluted shape: line 1 «Σταυρός» with its own 3.10 g and its own photo, line 2 «Αλυσίδα» with the 5.80 g sold and no photo, total €1300.00 — and a clean bulk-only single sale still prints correctly.
- **Sale-link invariant + repair tool (v11.70):** the two halves of "make sure this doesn't happen again", after the v11.69 root-cause fix.
  - **The invariant** (`confirmSale`, right after `_firstBulkInfo` is decided): a by-the-gram batch link may only ever be written onto a record whose own piece IS that batch — `if (_firstBulkInfo && _firstInvId && _firstInvId !== _firstBulkInfo.invId) _firstBulkInfo = null;` (with a `console.warn`). v11.69 closed the one path that was found; this closes the **class**, including paths not yet traced. The piece always wins, because a wrong bulk link costs a record three things at once: its identity (the bulk branch wins over `else if (_firstInvId)`, so `invId` is never stamped and a delete/return hands grams back to a batch instead of restoring the piece), its cost, and therefore its profit. Verified against all three shapes: mismatched → dropped; a genuine batch sale (`_firstInvId === bulk.invId`) → kept; the Calc bulk path (no `_saleFromInvId`) → kept.
  - **The repair tool** — Settings → **"🔗 Έλεγχος Συνδέσεων Πωλήσεων" / "Sale Link Check"** (`#saleLinkFixSection`, gated on `can('sales_admin')`), for records already written. `_saleLinkBadRecords()` uses a fingerprint that **cannot false-positive on a genuine bulk line**: the record points at a real batch, `invId !== bulkInvId`, and its OWN identity (`invId`, else `invQr`) resolves to a real piece that is neither that batch nor bulk itself. `scanSaleLinks()` lists each hit with **what it says now vs what it should be** (piece, cost, profit) and repairs are **per-row and confirm-gated** — the v10.19 silent-write lesson: this is money, so it is surfaced, never fixed automatically. `_saleLinkFixedValues` re-derives cost from the piece (`purchaseCost`, else `calcInvItemPrice().baseCost`) and profit from the **recorded** ex-VAT via `_saleExVat`, so the arithmetic stays the app's own; the UI says plainly that cost is recomputed at today's metal price. `fixSaleLink` snapshots the record first and **rolls back on a failed write** (the v11.62 lesson), reporting honestly instead of leaving a lie in the cache. Verified on a realistic four-record set: only the polluted record is flagged (two genuine bulk lines and an ordinary sale untouched), a failed write restores every field exactly, a successful one sets `invId`/`seqRef`/cost/profit and leaves the others alone, the re-scan comes back clean, the document then prints «Σταυρός» + «Αλυσίδα» correctly, and a second repair is a no-op. **NO LONGER TEMPORARY — do NOT delete this section.** The owner ran it on 21/07/2026: it found the one polluted record (Φανή's €653.94 cross line, cost €421.73 → €453.45, profit €232.21 → €200.49) and the scan then reported clean, so the *bulk-link* half has done its job and will not fire again (v11.69/v11.70 closed the cause). But v11.78 added a **second, permanent** finding to the same button — "paid for, but still listed in stock" — which is an ongoing safety net, not a migration. Keep the section, `scanSaleLinks`, `fixSaleLink` and the `slf*` keys. `_saleLinkBadRecords`/`_saleLinkFixedValues` (the bulk-link half) are now dormant and could be dropped on their own, but they cost nothing and document a real historical failure.
  - Verified by rebuilding the owner's actual transaction and driving the real functions: total **€1300.00** with lines €653.94 + €646.06, VAT hidden (cash), cross photo present / chain photo absent, 14.20 g for the chain and 3.40 g for the cross, customer + sale date, both ref codes, the handmade note, the certificate's authenticity title and 10 cm stamp — and a receipt-flagged single sale correctly splitting €653.94 into €558.92 + €95.02 at 17%.
- **"No photo" filter + sort by sale date (v11.71):** two small Stock-tab additions. (1) **📷 No photo** — a new pill in the Status filter group (`statuses` gains `'nophoto'`), for finding the pieces whose photo was never uploaded without scrolling 700 items. Like `hidden`/`grouped`/`reserved` it is a **cross-cutting flag, not a real status** — it requires `!item.photoUrl` while the in_stock/sold pills still apply (so it defaults to in-stock, and ticking Sold too narrows to sold pieces with no photo), and it is excluded from `_realStatus`. **Coins and raw metal are never flagged** (`item.isCoin || item.isMetal`) — they carry no photo by design, so they are not "missing" one. The four label expressions for the Status pills (filter sheet + the chip summary line) were collapsed into one shared helper **`_statusPillLabel(v)`** rather than adding a fifth ternary to each. (2) **Sort by sale date** — `sold_new` / `sold_old` in `INV_SORT_OPTIONS`, carrying a `soldOnly: true` flag: `openInvSortSheet` lists them **only when the Sold status filter is on**, per the owner's request that they not clutter the normal sort list — *or* while one of them is already the active sort, so it can still be changed after the filter is cleared. The comparator **skips the in-stock-first status grouping** for these two sorts (grouping by status is meaningless when sorting by sale date) and puts a piece with no `dateSold` **last in both directions**. i18n: `invFilterNoPhoto`, `invSortSoldNewest`, `invSortSoldOldest`. Verified by driving `renderInvList`/`openInvSortSheet` on a fixture: no-photo returns exactly the photo-less pieces (coin excluded), the sold-date orders are correct both ways, unsold pieces sort last in a mixed list, and the existing default/newest orders are unchanged.
- **Raw metal: remove grams + clean weight decimals (v11.72):** two fixes to the metal-stock lots. (1) **`− Remove`** — a third button on the metal card (`openMetalModal('take',{editId})`), for the everyday act of taking grams off the pile to make a piece. The modal reuses the add/edit one with `window._metalMode === 'take'`: the metal toggle, karat select and custom-‰ box are **hidden** (they stay in the DOM because `_metalCurrentSpec` reads them, and are prefilled from the lot), the price-paid field is absent, and the weight field asks how much to remove. It removes at the **pile's own average fineness** (`metalPure/metalGross`), not the karat preset — a pile built from several buys sits slightly off the preset, and removing at the preset would leave the remainder's purity quietly wrong. The preview shows what will be left; taking more than the pile holds is refused both in the preview and in `_metalConfirm`. Baselines (`initialMelt`/`initialRetail`/`purchaseCost`) are **scaled by the pure-grams ratio**, the same rule edit mode already used, so the remaining pile's cost and gain % stay true. Emptying the pile **confirms first**, then deletes the record rather than leaving a 0 g lot. **Nothing is written to Sales** — the metal becomes a piece that gets priced on its own, so booking income here would double-count it. A failed write **rolls the cache back** and says so (the v11.62 lesson). The metal card's action row gains `flex-wrap:wrap` + `min-width:120px` per button so the four actions lay out **2 + 2** — with `flex:1` alone the Greek labels can't shrink past their own words and Delete was clipped off the card edge (**caught by a screenshot, not by measurement** — a per-button `scrollWidth > clientWidth` check reports no clipping when it is the ROW that overflows; measure each button's `right` against the row's `right`). Scoped to the metal card; every other card still has three actions on one row. (2) **Weight decimals** — `metalGross`/`metalPure` were accumulated with raw `+`, so two lots summed to `53.300000000000004` and that noise showed in the Edit weight box. New helper **`_gRound(n)`** (3 decimals, finer than any jeweller's scale) is applied wherever grams are **stored or prefilled**: `_metalCurrentSpec`'s weight/pure, the add-mode accumulation, the edit prefill and the new-total preview. Verified by driving the real add/take flows: the exact reported drift (52.1 + 1.2) now stores 53.3, a 3.2 g removal leaves 50.10 g with cost scaled 1150 → 1080.96, an over-take is blocked, a failed write leaves the lot untouched, and a mixed-fineness pile removes and remains at its own 600‰.
- **Per-item scan raced the inventory load and showed the METAL price (v11.73) — the most dangerous bug found so far:** the owner scanned a per-item silver necklace and got **€6.21 instead of €80.01**, then a correct €80.01 on the second scan, and asked whether it was a one-off. It was not — it is a **reproducible race**, verified by driving the real `lookupCode` path. A **per-item** category takes BOTH its labour and its margin from the scanned piece's own inventory record (`calculate()`: `laborCost = _lastScannedInvEntry?.laborCost || 0`, `margin = _lastScannedInvEntry?.itemMargin || 0`). `lookupCode` fired `_checkInvStatusForCalc(raw)` **without awaiting it** and then ran `calculate()` on a **fixed 900 ms timer** — while `_checkInvStatusForCalc` does `await loadInventory()`, which re-downloads the **entire** inventory (1000+ items, `cache:'no-store'`) on **every** scan. Slower than 900 ms → both fall back to 0 → the price is the bare metal value + VAT. **Measured threshold, exactly at the timer:** load 50 ms → €80.01, 600 ms → €80.01, **1000 ms → €6.22, 2500 ms → €6.22** — and, because nothing re-ran afterwards, the wrong figure **stayed on screen in front of the customer**. Per-gram pieces were never affected (their rate comes from the vendor table, not the record) — verified identical at every speed. **Fix:** `_invStatusReady` holds the in-flight lookup promise (captured at all three `lookupCode` call sites) and the two `setTimeout(() => calculate(), 300|900)` became `_calcAfterInvReady()`, which awaits it first — so the price waits for the record however slow the connection (verified correct at 50 ms → 5 s). Plus a **self-healing net**: `_checkInvStatusForCalc` re-runs `calculate()` when it resolves a record while a per-item price is showing (`_isPerItemCatSelected()`), so any *other* path — the language-toggle re-check at L4094, a re-render — repairs a stale figure instead of leaving it. **And an honest failure mode:** when the record genuinely cannot be read (offline, or a tag belonging to a deleted piece) the price is still metal-only, so `_syncPerItemWarn()` shows a red **"this is NOT the selling price"** line under the amount. It is keyed on the **code box being non-empty**, because creating a NEW per-item piece from scratch has no code and legitimately shows the metal figure until a Target Retail is entered — verified that flow is unchanged and never warns. *(Bug found in the fix itself while testing: `_isPerItemCatSelected` first read `getElementById('vendor')` — the real id is **`vendorSelect`** — which would have silently disabled the whole self-healing net.)* **The general lesson, now the fifth of its kind (v10.72, v10.74, v11.59, v11.61, v11.62): a fixed timer is not a substitute for awaiting the data you are about to price with.**
- **Scan speed, a cache-wipe bug, and the Other-type equivalent (v11.74) — the follow-up audit to v11.73:** the owner asked whether Other-type items carry the same danger, and for the scan slowness to be looked at. **Answer on Other-type: no catastrophic race.** Its price comes from the QR's own embedded cost × the vendor multiplier — both available synchronously — and the one inventory-dependent part (a manually-set **`fixedRetail`** that overrides the formula) is already computed **inside** the awaited block. Verified: €120 manual price returns correctly at 50 ms, 1000 ms and 3000 ms. **But one real gap:** when the stock cannot be read at all, the override is silently missed and the **formula price shows instead** (€100 where the owner set €120) — a plausible number with nothing to signal it. Closed with the same honest-warning treatment, in different wording because the figure *is* usable, just unverified. To tell "couldn't look" from "genuinely not in stock" (`loadInventory` returns `{}` for both) a **`_invLoadFailed`** flag was added to `loadInventory`; `_otherPriceUnverified` drives the note. The v11.73 `#perItemWarn` element was renamed **`#calcPriceWarn`** / `_syncPriceWarn()` as it now covers both cases.
  - **A cache-wipe bug found while reading the hot path:** `_checkInvStatusForCalc` did `_inventoryCache = await loadInventory()`, assigning the result **straight into the cache** — and `loadInventory` returns `{}` on failure. So **one scan taken while the signal dropped replaced the entire in-memory stock with an empty object**, and every lookup after it missed until a good reload — which for a per-item piece means the €6.22 metal-only price even though the app had the record a second earlier. `_invEnsureForScan()` now keeps the last good cache on failure. Verified: after a failed load the cache still holds its items and the price stays €80.01 (previously it collapsed).
  - **Scan speed.** That same line re-downloaded **all 1000+ items** (`cache:'no-store'`) on **every single scan** — the reason the v11.73 race was so easy to hit. `_invEnsureForScan()` now serves a copy from memory when it is younger than **`INV_SCAN_TTL_MS` (60 s)**, awaits a real load otherwise, and runs a background refresh (throttled by **`INV_BG_MIN_MS`, 15 s**) that re-resolves the scanned code when the data actually changed — reusing the v11.73 self-heal so an edit made on another device lands on screen rather than being missed. **Measured with a 1.2 s download: first scan 1245 ms → subsequent scans 51 ms, and four scans cost ONE network fetch instead of four.** Staleness proven bounded: inside 15 s → 0 fetches and the cached price; past 15 s → instant cached price then **self-heals** to the new one; past 60 s → awaits a fresh load. No loop is possible — `_invBgRefreshing` stays true across the re-resolve (confirmed by the fetch counts).
  - Full matrix re-verified after the change: per-item correct at 50 ms/900 ms/1000 ms/2500 ms and warned offline; **per-gram byte-identical at every speed including offline** (its rate comes from the vendor table, never the record); Other-type correct with and without an override. *(Testing note: a harness that injects `state.vendors` must also call `populateVendorSelect()` — without it the vendor `<option>`s don't exist, `vendorSelect.value` silently stays `''`, and the multiplier falls back to 1. That produced a phantom failure that was purely the test's fault.)*
- **Edit all in a group (v11.75):** the owner had 8 identical grouped necklaces with a typo in the description and a missing vendor code, and editing them one by one "defeats the whole purpose of having them in a group." The group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) now has an **"✏️ Edit all (N)"** button (owner/manager, `can('see_cost')`, hidden for coins/metal and for a group of one) → **`invEditGroup(gid)`**, a focused form pre-filled from the group's face piece (earliest `dateAdded`). **`_invEditGroupSave(gid)`** writes the entered values to **every in-stock member** and saves each via `saveInventoryItem` (public-feed sync happens inside it). It edits the fields that are genuinely SHARED across a similar-items group: **description (EL/EN + derived `description`), vendor code, handmade, target audience (`gender` + `alsoGenders`), colour (`color`), gold colour (`goldColor`, only shown for a gold group), hide-from-showroom.** It deliberately does **not** touch weight, vendor, category, karat, price, the QR / reference code, `seqId`, baselines, stones or the photo — identity and pricing stay per piece. **Colour and gold colour are opt-in** (added at the owner's request): a group is legitimately split by colour in the showroom (v10.28 / v11.06), so bulk-setting it must never happen by accident. The colour select carries a **`__keep__` "keep each piece's colour"** option that is the **default whenever the members differ**, and pre-selects the shared value when they all match; save applies colour only when it is not `__keep__` (blank option → plain/`null`). So opening the form to fix a description leaves a red+blue set exactly as it was, while an explicit pick sets them all. A per-piece **snapshot + rollback** on a failed write (the v11.62 lesson) keeps the cache honest, and the toast reports `saved/failed` counts. Verified end-to-end by driving the real functions: all 8 pieces took the corrected description/vendor code/handmade while a deliberately-divergent member's weight (3.9 g), QR, `initialRetail` and `seqId` were left untouched; a **mixed red/blue/plain group left on "keep" preserved every colour** while the description applied to all, an explicit "green" set all three, a uniform group re-set to plain went to `null`; gold colour behaved identically (mixed→keep preserves gold/rose, explicit white sets all, absent on a silver group); a forced 4th-write failure rolled that one piece back while the other 7 kept the change; EL + EN render; console clean.
- **Sold-date sort tie fix (v11.76):** the v11.71 "Sold date" sort compares `item.dateSold` (a timestamp), but `confirmSale` set it to `new Date(date).getTime()` where `date` is a date-**only** string from `saleDateInput` — so every piece sold on the same calendar day got the **identical midnight timestamp**, tied, and could not be ordered (the owner's "last thing sold showing 5th" report). Two of the three mark-sold paths used that midnight value; the multi-item "rest" loop already used `Date.now()`, an inconsistency. `confirmSale` now computes one **`soldTs`** near the top — `Date.now()` when the sale is dated **today** (captures the real clock time so same-day sales order correctly), else `new Date(date).getTime()` (midnight, the best a backdated sale can know) — and all three paths (`_saleFromInvId` at ~L11884, the QR-match at ~L11947, the cart "rest" at ~L11993) use it. Display-only sort; no schema change. **Not retroactive** — pieces already sold before the update keep their stored midnight timestamp (so a batch sold earlier today still ties among itself, but new sales carry real times and sort above them under newest-first).
- **Sold-date sort restored-state fix (v11.77):** `_invSort` persists in `localStorage 'invSort'` across a reload, but `_invFilters` does **not** (it resets to the default in-stock view every load). So a `sold_new`/`sold_old` sort restored while the Sold filter was gone, leaving the list sorting in-stock pieces — which have no `dateSold` — by sold date, and `openInvSortSheet` still offering/checking the sold-date option (its `_soldOn` gate is true whenever a sold sort is *active*). Fixed at the restore line (just after `let _invSort = ...`): if the restored sort's `INV_SORT_OPTIONS` entry is `soldOnly` and `_invFilters.status` doesn't include `'sold'`, fall back to `'newest'`. The subsequent `setInvSort(_invSort)` at boot persists the correction. Keeps the invariant that the sold-date sort only applies alongside the Sold filter.
- **A sale links to the PIECE, not to a code string (v11.78) — the €380 cross:** the owner sold a stone-set cross from the Calc; the sale was recorded, but the piece stayed **in stock**, "View Item" said «Δεν βρέθηκε στο απόθεμα», and the printed certificate came out with **no photo, no metal, no weight** — only the ref code. Root cause, confirmed against the real backup: the sale identified the piece by a **re-derived code string** (`lastCalcResult.labelCode` = `buildLabelStrings().fullQR`) and matched inventory on it.
