Four pieces created **25/06/2026 — eleven days before the v10.13 separator fix (06/07/2026)** — carry the malformed leading-`+` stone suffix (a carat-less Diamond emits nothing, so the old index-based loop let it consume the leading `-`): stored `KAL18S1.23+4xEME`, while the calc regenerates the **corrected** `KAL18S1.23-4xEME`. Same physical piece, two spellings, so the match found **nothing** — no mark-sold, and a sale record pointing at no item, which is why both the "View Item" jump and `_saleDocResult` came up empty. The other 3 (`KAL18S1.3+4xSAP`, `+5xSAP`, `+4xRUB`) are still in stock and were the same accident waiting to happen. Three layers of fix:
  - **The identity is the record id.** New `_calcScanInvId()` returns the record the scan already resolved (`_scanChosenInvId` → the v11.16 chooser answer / the v11.30 ref pin, else the new `_lastScannedInvId` set beside `_lastScannedInvEntry`). `confirmSale` computes it **once, up front** (before anything flips a status) and uses it to stamp `invId` on the record and to pick the piece to mark sold — so a Calc sale now carries the exact link that a sale from a Stock card already did. **66 of 76 sales in the backup had no `invId` at all**; that whole class is closed. Guarded so a stale resolution can never be pinned on the wrong piece: the code box must still hold the string that produced it (`_qrKey`-compared, so the corrected spelling still counts), and the piece must still be `in_stock` and non-bulk. Null for a cart, a sale opened from a stock card, or an unresolved scan — every one of those paths is byte-identical to before. `invQr` now also stores the piece's **own stored code** rather than the regenerated one.
  - **One spelling of a code, everywhere.** New **`_qrKey(code)`** = trim + uppercase, and — only when there is no `-` anywhere — turns the first `+` into `-`. Applied at every code↔record resolution site: `_checkInvStatusForCalc`'s photo fallback in `calculate()`, the `confirmSale` mark-sold, `_restoreStockForSale` (delete/return), the sale-detail item info, the "View Item" button, `_saleDocResult` (quote/certificate), `invGoToSale`, and the sale-link scan's index. **Verified by driving the real function over all 662 distinct stored codes: a no-op for every one except those 4, no two different codes collapse to one key, and none of the 4 corrected forms collides with a code that already exists.** This is what makes the sale record **already written** resolve — no data migration, and the physical `+` tags on those 4 pieces still scan. (The malformed codes are deliberately left in the DB, exactly as v10.13 decided.)
  - **The check the owner asked for.** Settings → **"🔗 Έλεγχος Συνδέσεων Πωλήσεων" / "Sale Link Check"** (the v11.70 tool) gained a second, more important finding: **"paid for, but still listed in stock."** `_saleUnsoldRecords` counts **per code, never per record** — identical pieces in a similar-items group and every coin in a batch legitimately share one code, so five twins with three sales and three sold pieces is correct and must not be flagged; only a code with **more sales than sold pieces** has a jewel that was paid for and never moved. Returned sales, bulk lines, scrap/repair/gift records and sales whose piece was deleted are all excluded. `_fixSaleUnsold` marks the piece sold (dated from the **sale**, not from today) and stamps `invId` + the piece's own `invQr`; **the money is never touched** — amount, cost and profit were computed correctly at sale time, only the jewel failed to follow. Confirm-gated, and rollback-checked in both directions (the v11.62 lesson): a failed inventory write restores the status and reports it; a failed sales write leaves the piece sold and says exactly that rather than claiming success. The "cost is re-derived" footnote now shows only for the batch-link repair, which is the only kind that touches money.
  - **Verified end to end by driving the real functions**, using the reported record's exact shape: the old strict comparison returns **NOT FOUND** while `_saleDocResult` now yields «Σταυρός» + photo + «Χρυσός · 18K (75.0%)» + 1.23 g + Diamond/4× Emerald + €380.00 with VAT hidden (cash); "View Item" resolves; the scan-id guards all hold (stale code box, already-sold piece, bulk batch, nothing scanned, empty box → all null); the check flags exactly the one sale out of ten realistic records and correctly stays silent on twins, coins, returns, bulk and deleted pieces; and all three write outcomes behave.
- **The ✕ goes back one step, not all the way out (v11.79):** in a multi-item transaction, tapping a line opened it via `showSaleDetail(m.id, true)` **in the same sheet**, so the ✕ closed everything and dumped the owner on the Sales list instead of returning him to the transaction he was reading. v11.66 had already built the mechanism for exactly this (`_gcDetailReturnTo`, sale → gift card) but nothing else used it. Generalised into a small **return stack**, unwound innermost-first in `closeSaleDetailModal`: (1) `_sdmReturnToGroup` — one line of a transaction → back to the transaction, set by the new `_sdmOpenMember(groupSaleId, memberId)`; (2) `_gcDetailReturnTo` — sale → gift card (unchanged); (3) `_sdmReturnToClient` — a sale opened from a client's purchase history → back to that history, via the new `_sdmOpenFromClient(saleId)` (the row used to `.remove()` the overlay and jump, landing the owner on the Clients list — the same complaint one level out). Each target is set **after** `await showSaleDetail(...)` returns, because `showSaleDetail` clears `_sdmReturnToGroup` at the top so a sale opened fresh (list, search, scan) can never inherit a stale target; `_showSaleGroupDetail` clears it too, so arriving back at the transaction means the next ✕ really closes. **Every target is cleared whatever the reason for closing**, while only a plain close (`navigating` falsy — the ✕ or the backdrop) acts on one; the ~9 internal callers that close the sheet in order to go somewhere already pass `navigating = true`, so View Item / Return / Edit never bounce back. **The client name rides on `overlay.dataset.client`, never inside the row's `onclick` string** — it is user-typed and a quote or apostrophe would break the attribute (verified with `Φανή Ο'Κουλουριώτη "η μικρή"`). Verified by driving the real functions: the reported case returns to the transaction with the sheet still open and closes on the second ✕; the full client-history → transaction → line chain unwinds exactly one step per ✕; the gift-card level still works; navigating away clears all three; and a plain sale from the list closes with nothing reopened and no target left set. The three cases found and deferred here — the Stale Stock report's "Open", the group sheet's per-piece Edit, and the notification panel → gift card — were done next, in **v11.80** (see below). The sale document picker (Quote/Certificate) correctly needs no return — printing is terminal, and the group sheet's Sell / Print Tag / Calc / Share / Delete are left alone on purpose: each hands off to its own flow whose natural end is the Stock list.
- **The remaining three back-steps (v11.80):** the three cases v11.79 found and deliberately deferred, now done. All follow the same return-target pattern (set the target **after** the opener runs, because the opener clears it; clear it on every close; act on it only for a plain close).
  - **Edit Item** gained `_invEditReturn` (`{kind:'group', id}` | `{kind:'stale'}` | null) and a single **`closeInvEdit(navigating)`** — it previously had **no close function at all**, removing `#invEditOverlay` from five separate places, which is why v11.79 deferred it. The two ways in that have somewhere to go back to: **`_invEditFromGroup(gid, id)`** (the group sheet's per-piece ✏️ Edit — the button now carries the `gid`, which is in lexical scope inside `openInvGroup`'s `members.map`) and **`_staleOpen(id)`** (the Stale Stock report row). `invEdit(id)` clears the target on its first line, so a form opened straight from a Stock card never bounces. `_invEditUnlock`, the reset-initial-price action and `invAddSimilar` pass `navigating = true` (they are going somewhere else); Cancel and a successful Save return.
  - **The one real ordering trap:** the stone-reprint prompt (`_promptStoneTagReprint`) is **z-index 99997**, but the Stale Stock report is **99998** — so reopening the report on top of it would hide the very question being asked. The save path therefore passes `navigating = !!(_stoneTagStale && _newFullQRForStones)`, closing to the Stock list whenever a prompt is coming. (The group sheet at 99996 would have been safe, and `showConfirm` at 999998 is above everything, so only the report was at risk — worth re-checking the z-index if another list is ever added here.)
  - **Gift card detail** gained `_gcDetailReturnToNotif` + **`closeGiftCardDetail(navigating)`**, with `_gcOpenFromNotif(gcId)` for the 🔔 panel's "View" button (expiry warnings arrive several at a time, so losing the list after each one meant reopening the bell to reach the next). The **seven** action paths that dismiss the card on their way elsewhere (`_gcEditSave`, `_gcExtend`, `_gcBookBreakage`, `_gcDelete`, `_gcReleaseOrphans`, `_gcCancel`, `_gcOpenRedemptionSale`) now call `closeGiftCardDetail(true)` so the target can never be consumed later; the raw `.remove()` inside `openGiftCardDetail` (clearing a previous card) is deliberately left alone — routing it through the closer would reopen the bell.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: group → Edit → ✕ returns to the group (and the button carries the right `gid`); report → Edit → ✕ returns to the report **with the chosen threshold intact** (`_staleThreshold` is module-level, so it survives); notification → card → ✕ reopens the bell while a card opened from the Desk list does not; every `navigating` path closes without bouncing and leaves no target set; and the v11.79 sale flows (line → transaction, client history → transaction → line) still pass. Console clean.
- **Find Jewelry — the lost-tag finder (v11.81):** the owner cleaned the window displays, brought every piece out and back, and lost the tags off several open rings and bracelets; recovering their identity meant walking the whole inventory. New **`#panel-find`** (☰ More menu, `nav-find`, **all roles** — an employee loses a tag too): pick what you can see about the piece (type / metal / karat / audience), type the weight off the scale, get a photo grid of candidates each with its ref code and a **🏷️ print-tag** button (`invPrintTagFromCard`) and **Open** (`_jumpToInvCard`).
  - **It never writes a weight, or anything else.** The stored weight drives the metal value, the retail price and the gain % on every card, so an automatic "correction" there is the same class of silent write that v10.19 had to be cleaned up after. The owner asked for this explicitly and it is the load-bearing constraint of the whole feature. (An earlier proposal to *learn* the true weight on a confirmed match was rejected by him and is not built. If it is ever revisited it must be a **separate field** that never feeds pricing.)
  - **Two stages, deliberately separate so a result says how confident it is.** Stage 1 = exact weight; stage 2 = **±`FIND_WIDE` (0.2 g)**, reached by an explicit "Search wider" button that **carries the count of what it would add** (`+N`), so widening never leads into an empty screen. Typing a new weight always resets to stage 1.
  - **There is deliberately NO "add the stone weight back" stage.** Measured against the real 600-piece stock: 357 pieces have no stones, 91 have every carat recorded, and **152 are blind** (92 of them zircon) — and it is exactly those that never had a carat entered, because the owner only records carats for precious stones. Any allowance for them is a guess, and an uncapped variant was measured to hand back ~11 ranked candidates versus the ~13 his existing Stock stone filter already gives — better, not transformative. So when both stages come up empty the panel **says so** and offers **`_findOpenStock()`**, which copies the current narrowing into `_invFilters` and jumps to Stock (karat is mapped back through the pool because `f.karats` is compared against the RAW stored value, a string on some records and a number on others). A search that quietly returns a list not containing your ring is worse than one that admits it found nothing.
  - **The filters are the load-bearing part, not decoration** — measured: weight ALONE gives a median of **8** exact hits and **34** within ±0.2 g (many pieces sit on round weights; 25 at exactly 1.0 g). With type + metal + karat + audience the median drops to **4**, and 0 of 600 pieces are ever missed. Pills are built from what is actually in stock, and the gender pill test mirrors `_findFilterOk` exactly (unisex spans Men and Women only — never Children) so no pill can be offered that returns nothing.
  - Pool = in-stock gold/silver jewelry; **coins, raw metal and bulk chains are excluded** (no tag to lose; a bulk chain's weight is a remaining batch, not a piece). An **"Include sold"** pill adds sold pieces, marked `ΠΩΛΗΘΗΚΕ`/`SOLD`, for a mis-marked or returned piece. Opens via `_invEnsureForScan()` (v11.74's safe loader — keeps the last good cache when the network is down instead of reporting an empty stock). i18n `find*`; `#panel-find` added to the desktop 560px-centred rule.
- **A scanned REF code can be ambiguous too — ask (v11.81):** the owner created an evil-eye earring (`ER0701`, Aquamarine), added a second piece to the group via **Add similar items** choosing a different `color`, then hand-edited that second record's stone to Turquoise. Scanning **either** tag always showed the aquamarine. Root cause: `invAddSimilar` deliberately copies `seqId`/`refType` (one product, one number), so both pieces print **`ER0701`** — and `lookupCode`'s ref branch resolved it with **`Object.entries(...).find()`**, i.e. whichever record came first in the cache. It then pinned that piece (`_refPinnedInvId`), and because `_checkInvStatusForCalc` treats a pin as "the ref named this piece" (`_wasPinned`), the v11.16/v11.17 variant chooser was **skipped entirely**. The chooser only ever covered a shared *descriptive* code; a shared *ref* code was assumed to name one piece.
  - **Fix:** the ref branch now collects **all** matches, prefers **in-stock**, and — when more than one in-stock piece is behind that number **and they genuinely differ** — opens the same chooser sheet. New **`_refVariantKey(it)`** = `_variantKey` (goldColor|color) **+ the descriptive `qrCode`**, because at the ref level the members can differ by **stones or weight**, which live in the code and not in the colour. `_variantLabel` appends the stones **only when they are what distinguishes the variants** (`Γαλάζιο · Μελέ Ακουαμαρίνα` vs `Τιρκουάζ · Μελέ Τυρκουάζ`), so the ordinary colour-only case stays short. The sheet's explainer dropped "(same weight)" — no longer true here.
  - **Nothing is looked up until the answer arrives.** Unlike the descriptive case (one code → one price, only the photo moves), ref variants can price **differently**, so the ref branch `return`s at the chooser and `_pickScanVariant` then calls the new **`_refResolvePiece(entry)`** (extracted from the old inline pin+re-run) to pin the chosen record and re-run `lookupCode` **by its own descriptive code** — a provisional price for the wrong piece in front of a customer is worse than a blank one.
  - **The pick has to survive its own re-lookup.** `_checkInvStatusForCalc` calls `_resetScanVariants()` at the top — right for a fresh scan, wrong for the re-run that *answers* the question, which would have wiped the "✓ Τιρκουάζ — Αλλαγή →" chip the instant it was set. New **`_refVariantCarry = { ref, list }`** is restored inside the `_wasPinned` branch when the pinned piece belongs to it; cleared on any fresh `lookupCode` (alongside `_refPinnedInvId`) and on the calc reset.
  - **The descriptive path is untouched**, and deliberately so: the re-lookup is gated on `_refVariantCarry` containing the picked id (`fromRef`), **not** on the code box differing — an empty box would otherwise have triggered it. Verified: picking there still changes only the photo, leaves the code box alone, and sets the same sell target as before.
  - **Also fixed by the same change:** a ref whose pieces are part sold now resolves to the **in-stock** one instead of whichever came first (the old `.find()` could land on the sold twin).
  - Verified by driving the real functions against the 1,129-record backup **with the owner's real 14-vendor config loaded** (without it `AMA…` cannot parse, `_checkInvStatusForCalc` never runs, and every sell-target assertion is a false negative): the reported pair asks with nothing pre-selected, picking Turquoise sets code `AMA925S1.5-tTUR` and sell target `inv_TUR`, changing the mind moves both to the aquamarine, identical twins stay silent, one-sold resolves silently to the in-stock piece, an ordinary single-piece ref is unchanged, and the original shared-descriptive-code chooser still asks and still only swaps the photo. **Testing note:** `_inventoryCache` is a top-level `let`, so it lives in the global *lexical* scope and is **not** `window._inventoryCache` — assigning the window property leaves the app reading its own object and produces convincing but meaningless results.
- **Stone selector in "Add similar items" + a group of one is not a group (v11.82):** the two follow-ups to the v11.81 scan fix, both from the same real case.
  - **Stone selector** (`#invAddSimStone` in `invAddSimilar`) — the flow cloned the source's stones, so making the turquoise twin of an aquamarine earring meant adding it and then hand-editing it, which is what produced the shared-ref ambiguity in the first place. Only the stone **TYPE** is swappable: qty/melee, carat, clarity, colour and shape are carried over, because this is the same piece with a different stone, not a different piece. Offered **only when the source has exactly one stone** (`_srcStones.length === 1` via `_invEditParseStoredStones`) — with several, "change the stone" has no unambiguous meaning, so they are inherited and a one-line note says so. The swap feeds both the **code** (`_buildStoneQRSuffix` → `AMA925S1.5-tAQU` → `AMA925S1.5-tTUR`) and the **description** (`buildStoneDescription`), so the two can never disagree.
    - **`_newStones()` returns null when the stone is unchanged**, and `buildQR` then falls back to the source's **original stored suffix verbatim** rather than a regenerated one — so leaving the stone alone is byte-identical to the pre-v11.82 behaviour, legacy suffix formatting and all. Verified: swap → `-tTUR`, swap back → exactly `-tAQU`.
    - `buildQR(w, nsOverride)` takes the stones the save path captured **once** up front (`_swapped`), so a select touched during the photo upload can't give two pieces of one batch different codes.
    - `stoneCost` is deliberately **inherited**: there is no price for the new stone and the owner's pieces here are per-item priced (the target retail drives the price, not the stone). The retail/metal baselines are re-derived per piece as before.
  - **A group of one is not a group.** Once the other members sell, the last piece rendered as a `🔗 1 in stock` card whose every action sat a tap deeper. `renderInvList` now **unwraps** any folded row with a single member back into a plain `[id, item]` row. **Display-time only** — the `groupId` stays on the record, so adding pieces later re-forms the group by itself and ticking **Sold** still shows the whole batch together; nothing is rewritten in the database for a cosmetic change (the v10.19 lesson, and the owner's own standing instruction not to auto-rewrite stored fields).
    - The **"🔗 Grouped" status pill** was made to agree with that: a per-render `_grpLive` Map counts in-stock members per `groupId` and the filter now requires `> 1`, so filtering by Grouped can never list a card that isn't drawn as a group.
  - Verified by driving the real functions with the owner's 14-vendor config: the selector pre-selects the source's stone in Greek (Ακουαμαρίνα), swapping writes `AMA925S1.5-tTUR` + `melee Turquoise` on all N of a batch with the melee marker, ref code and groupId intact and the **source untouched**; leaving it alone reproduces the source's code and description exactly. For the fold: 2 in stock → one group row of 2; sell one → the survivor renders as an ordinary card; tick Sold → regroups to 2; the Grouped pill returns 0 for the lone survivor and 1 for a real pair.
- **One printed code, one identifiable piece (v11.83) — the RIGHT fix for the shared-ref problem:** the owner pushed back on v11.81, correctly: making the app **ask** "which one are you holding?" is an admission it cannot identify the piece, not a solution. His rule — *share a reference code only while the pieces are absolutely the same; any difference in weight, stone or colour earns its own number* — is right, and he had **already proposed it in v11.17**, where it was dismissed with the circular argument that "the ref code is the same because `invAddSimilar` copies it".
  - **The principle was already in the codebase.** `_printAsRefTag` has long refused to print a shared code onto a diverged piece — *"One tag cannot identify two different pieces. Give this one its own code ER0702 and print it?"* — minting a fresh `seqId` via `_refSharedWithDifferent`/`_refIdentityKey`. **`invAddSimilar` simply never went through it**: it prints its own labels (`printLabel` inside `addBatch`) and copied `seqId`/`refType` unconditionally. That bypass is the whole bug.
  - **Fix:** `addBatch` now builds a `_probe` of the piece it is about to create (weight, regenerated `qrCode`, `color`, `goldColor`) and compares `_refIdentityKey(_probe)` with the source's. Differ → **one new `seqId` for the batch** (`getNextSeqId`, which recycles freed numbers) and `delete newItem.refType` so the fresh number takes its prefix from the piece's own type. Identical → shares the source's number exactly as before, so "12 identical rings, one code, N copies of one label" is untouched. The number is minted **once per batch**, not per piece: the N pieces of a batch are identical to each other; only the source is being diverged from. **Both cases keep the shared `groupId`** — they are still one product family, just individually identifiable.
  - **`_refIdentityKey` now includes the descriptive code** (`_qrKey(qrCode)`, which encodes weight AND stones) alongside weight/colour/goldColor. It previously compared `weight | color | goldColor` only, so after the v11.82 stone selector two pieces differing **only by stone** were judged interchangeable — by the very guard that exists to stop that. This also makes `_printAsRefTag` and `_refCheckSplitAfterEdit` stricter in the same direction; identical pieces hash identically, so no new nagging.
  - **The sheet says which it will be, before you commit** (`#invAddSimRefNote`, updated by `updatePreview` from the *same* identity test the save path uses, so it can never promise one thing and do another): «🔗 ίδιο κομμάτι — ίδιος κωδικός ER0701» vs «🆔 διαφορετικό κομμάτι — θα πάρει δικό του κωδικό».
  - **Rejected: `ER0701-1` / `ER0701-2` suffixes** (owner's suggestion). Verified against the code, not argued: the OCR text-scan runs Tesseract with the whitelist `BCEGHKLNOPRTWX0123456789` — **no hyphen** — and `extractRefCode` strips every non-alphanumeric then matches a 6-char token, so `ER0701-2` reads as a clean, confident **`ER0701`**. That is *worse* than the bug being fixed: the app would hand over the wrong variant silently, with no chooser, precisely on the faded tags OCR exists for. Also: `2 letters + 4 digits` is hard-coded in ~8 places including `showroom.htm` and `item.htm`, and the hyphen is deliberately reserved so `G-0004` (gift) and `X-0001` (exchange) can never collide with a ref code. A printed *non-code* hint line (`≡ ER0701`) on the label remains a safe future option — the scanner only ever reads the code token.
  - **Nothing is renumbered retroactively** — physical tags already in the shop keep their codes. The v11.81 chooser stays as the safety net for pieces that already share a number, and printing a tag for one of them offers the split. Verified: identical batch → shares ER0701 and consumes no number; different stone/colour/weight → ER0900/0901/0902, `refType` cleared, group preserved; a 3-piece identical batch → one code, one number; **scanning the diverged pair now resolves each tag straight to its own record with NO chooser**; a still-shared legacy pair still asks; the clash guard fires for the legacy pair and stays silent for true twins.
- **Photo match — find a piece by photographing it (v11.84):** the weight finder cannot reach a stone-set piece, and the owner produced the decisive example: a byzantine ring stored at **12.7 g** that the scale reads at **13.2 g**, because he deducts the stone weight when adding it. No sane tolerance spans that. But those are precisely the *visually distinctive* pieces, so a picture is the right sensor exactly where grams are the wrong one. A **📷 camera button in the Find Jewelry tab** ranks the stock by visual similarity to a photo taken now.
  - **Everything runs on the device.** `transformers.js` (v3, jsdelivr) + **`Xenova/dinov2-small`**, lazy-loaded on first use and cached in Cache Storage (`transformers-cache`) — the same pattern as the OCR scanner. **No photo ever leaves the phone.** First use needs internet: **42.4 MB** (WebGPU fp16) or **23.3 MB** (wasm q8 fallback) plus ~4 MB runtime, once.
  - **Model chosen by measurement, not assumption.** DINOv2 is built for *instance retrieval* ("find THIS object again") rather than text matching. Tested on **140 real stock photos** with a deliberately hard query — rotated 8°, pulled back 22%, composited onto a wooden surface, 10% dimmer — it returned **99% top-1, 100% top-3, worst rank 2**. A cheap colour-grid descriptor was tried FIRST and rejected: it scored a flattering 96% on an easier test but its confidence margin was **0.011**, because your photos are a small object on a big white background and it was mostly measuring *the background* — it collapsed to 71% once the crop widened. DINOv2's margin on the same task is **0.177**, sixteen times larger. That margin, not the headline accuracy, is why one shipped and the other did not.
  - **Storage:** `/jewelprice_imgvec/<itemId>` = `{v: base64(int8[384]), p: photoUrl, at}` — a private node holding only image fingerprints (no prices, no customer data; never public). int8 quantisation measured **free**: top-1 unchanged, average score drift 0.00012, **~320 KB for the whole 642-piece stock**. `p` is the photo URL, so a piece is re-indexed only when its **photo** changes and the pass is cheap to repeat.
  - **The performance trap worth remembering:** the obvious implementation — `canvas.convertToBlob()` → `RawImage.fromBlob()` — encodes each frame to JPEG and decodes it again, costing **a full second per photo, nine times the inference itself**. Feeding `getImageData` pixels straight into `new RawImage(rgb, w, h, 3)` took it from 1.6 s to **109 ms** (WebGPU) / 619 ms (wasm) — the difference between a 17-minute index and a **70-second** one.
  - **Indexing** (`visIndexStock`, owner/manager) is resumable by construction: it only ever works on `_visPending` (never indexed, or photo changed), so an interrupted run resumes; writes go out in PATCH batches of 40 rather than 642 separate PUTs. The Find panel shows the pending count and a progress bar with a Stop button — self-service, not a hidden background job.
  - **Filters and photo compose.** `visMatch(canvas, ids)` ranks only what the panel's filters already narrowed to (the owner's own instinct: he filtered by stones and got 18). Comparing against 18 is far more reliable than 642. **The weight box is deliberately IGNORED** in a photo search — it is the very field that excludes the right answer for a stone-set piece.
  - The match bar is a **relative** confidence cue, never dressed up as a probability; the ranking is the answer. Verified end to end on real stock: 80 pieces indexed 80/80 on WebGPU, and a rotated/re-lit/re-backgrounded shot of the byzantine ring returned it **first at 93%**, ahead of another emerald-and-diamond ring at 84%, in **116 ms**. **Real-world accuracy is still unmeasured** — synthetic perturbations flatter, which is exactly how the colour descriptor fooled its own test. It needs the owner photographing real pieces and counting.
- **AI photo search — the catalogue goes in the prompt, not the pixels (v11.86):** the v11.84 on-device model was measured on the owner's REAL counter photo and its scores crowded: his byzantine ring came back **62%** against a wrong ring at **63%** — the right answer was in the list, but its position was luck. Comparing pictures to pictures throws away the one thing the shop already knows perfectly: **what stones each piece has**. So `_findAiSearch` sends the photo plus the ENTIRE in-stock catalogue as text — `REF | type | metal karat | colours | stones | description`, one line each — and asks a vision model which entry it is looking at. Measured: **600 pieces compact to ~9,700 tokens**, comfortably inside a 1M-context model, at roughly **half a euro cent per search**. No shortlist, no filters required — the owner's own request ("I take the picture and the AI immediately finds the right one").
  - **Grounded, not generative.** Every returned `ref` is resolved against the real piece list and **silently dropped if it does not exist**, so a hallucinated code can never render as stock (verified: `ZZ9999` discarded, the real match kept). The model is also told to return fewer results rather than a confident wrong one, and its `seen` line ("a wide gold ring with a green and a red stone") is displayed — a misread is then obvious at a glance instead of leaving the owner puzzled by the list.
  - **Robust parsing:** clean JSON, ```-fenced JSON, and prose-wrapped JSON all parse; garbage throws. `models: [...]` lets OpenRouter fall through `gemini-2.5-flash → gemini-3.1-flash-lite → gpt-5-mini`. The query photo is capped at **768px/JPEG 0.85** — it is the only part that costs real tokens. `temperature: 0`.
  - **The on-device model stays as the fallback**, unchanged, for no key / no internet / an AI error (a failure logs and falls through, it never dead-ends). Filters still narrow the catalogue that gets sent when they are set, but are no longer needed.
  - **Not built (superseded):** a Stone filter for the Find panel, which the owner asked for while the local model was the only path. The AI now reads the stones itself, so the filter's value is marginal — reinstate only if he asks.
  - **The on-device model was REMOVED in v11.88** — everything in the v11.84 entry above (transformers.js, `Xenova/dinov2-small`, `_vis*`, `visIndexStock`, `visMatch`, the index UI and the `/jewelprice_imgvec` node) is gone from the code. It existed only as the no-internet fallback, and the owner confirmed the shop always has internet (wifi, and unlimited 5G behind it). Against that it cost a **42 MB** download per device, an index that had to be rebuilt as stock changed, ~250 lines, and it was the part that **measured badly on a real photo** (62% on the correct ring against 63% on a wrong one). A fallback that returns a confident-looking wrong answer is worse than an honest "needs internet", so the photo button is now purely AI and says so when no key is set (`findNeedKey`). **~14 KB removed.** The v11.84 entry is kept because the measurements in it are why the AI design was chosen. *The orphaned `/jewelprice_imgvec` Firebase node was left in place — inert, ~320 KB of regenerable fingerprints, referenced by nothing.*
- **`aiDesc` — the catalogue had nothing to match on (v11.89):** after 6/6, the owner hit a clean failure: **every silver piece**, including an unmistakable pendant with a gold turtle on a silver rim. The AI's own `seen` line was perfect — *"A round pendant with a silver-colored outer rim and a gold-colored center featuring a turtle design, hanging from a brown braided cord"* — and it still found nothing. **Vision was never the weak part; the TEXT was empty.** That piece's catalogue line read `Μενταγιόν | silver 925 | no stones` and no more; the word "turtle" appears nowhere in the data. Measured on the real backup: **30% of in-stock silver and 14% of gold carry NEITHER a description NOR stones**, which is exactly why silver failed as a *group* and why plain gold rings surprisingly worked — it was never plain-vs-fancy, it was how much text a piece has. (Filtering would not have rescued it: a shorter list still has no turtle in it.)
  - **Fix:** `buildSearchDescriptions()` describes every in-stock photo once into a **new `aiDesc` field** (+ `aiDescPhoto`), which `_findCatalogueLines` appends. The owner's `descEl`/`descEn`/`description` are **never touched** — his standing instruction — and the write is a **PATCH of exactly two keys**, not a whole-record PUT, so a stale cached copy cannot clobber a concurrent edit and the public feed is not re-synced 650 times (`aiDesc` is deliberately NOT in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`).
  - **Batching, and the guard that matters:** photos go **by URL** (already public on ImgBB, a URL costs a few tokens where an inlined image costs thousands), `AI_DESC_BATCH = 4` per request. A batch whose reply doesn't have exactly one line per photo is **refused** (`count-mismatch`) and redone one photo at a time — misaligned lines would silently attach the wrong description to the wrong piece and poison the very index this builds. Verified: 4-photo batch failing → 4 single calls → 4 saved, 0 lost. Resumable (only `_visPending`-style work is queued), **blind pieces first** so value lands early if stopped, and a failed write leaves **no phantom description in the cache**.
  - **The prompt was the bug, twice (v11.95 / v11.96) — the most instructive failure of the feature.** After v11.92 a square silver pendant with dolphins on a brass coin still failed, and the owner pushed back correctly: *"it read the dolphins on the scan, so why not when it built the description?"* **Auditing his own fresh backup answered it:** across 698 in-stock pieces the v2 descriptions were **87% keyword lists**, **33% byte-identical to another piece**, median **7 words** where 30 were asked for. His pendant held `"dolphins, round, square, two-tone, textured, polished"` — naming *both* round and square — and five gold dolphin pieces shared the identical line `"dolphin, free-form, gold, polished"`. The search was not broken; it was refusing to guess between six indistinguishable entries. **Cause: the v2 prompt was written as a checklist** ("give the shape: square, round, oval… the finish: polished, matte…") and the model returned those menu words as a list instead of describing anything. v3 demands a natural SENTENCE and shows worked good/bad examples.
    - **Then the near-miss that matters more.** The v3 "good example" was written *from the owner's own dolphin pendant*, and his first single-piece test returned it **word for word** — indistinguishable from the model actually looking. Run across the stock it would have stamped "three dolphins in a square silver frame" onto 698 unrelated pieces: a *convincing* corruption, far worse than the keyword lists. **Rule: an example in a prompt must never describe a real record**, or you cannot tell recitation from observation. v4 uses invented pieces (a lion signet, a chain with a ladybird), states in the prompt that they are not in the shop, and **`_echoesExample()` rejects any reply reproducing one** (exact, case-insensitive, or a lifted 7-word run) so the batch throws and retries rather than saving. The word "dolphins" was removed from the prompt entirely — including from the *bad* example — so a correct answer can only come from the photo. Confirmed: the owner's retest returned "textured dark grey border… central gold-toned disc depicting two dolphins swimming in a spiral" — its own wording, a detail ("spiral") never given to it, and a correct hedge ("gold-toned", not gold).
    - **`_descQuality()`** now grades every rebuild for duplicates and keyword-list shape and warns instead of showing a tick. v2 wrote 658 unusable lines and **nothing surfaced it** — it was found only by photographing a pendant and getting nothing back. Any generated-content pass should measure its own output.
  - **Perception and retrieval are two jobs (v12.00) — the fix that actually worked.** With every description rebuilt correctly, the dolphin pendant *still* failed: its stored line read "two dolphins swimming in a spiral", the photo was read as "two dolphins swimming in a spiral", and the model answered **"nothing matches"**. The descriptions were never the last problem — **asking one model to both LOOK at a photograph and scan 658 catalogue lines for the best match is two jobs**, and it is only reliable at the first. `_findTextRank(seen, pieces)` now searches the catalogue **in code**, using the model's own reading of the photo: a small IDF-weighted scorer over `aiDesc + description + stones + type`, so a word appearing in six pieces ("dolphins") outweighs one appearing in hundreds ("gold"). Requires ≥2 shared terms and a score near the best, so weak noise never pads the answer. Its hits are merged after the model's own picks, which means **a piece whose description matches can no longer be missed**, deterministically. Verified on the owner's live data — and against the *old* v2 keyword descriptions, so it does not even depend on the good ones: the pendant returns **first** (7 shared terms), its horse-motif sibling second, a ring query returns rings, an absurd query returns almost nothing, and generic words ("gold polished") return nothing at all.
  - **Also v11.97–11.99, all self-inflicted and worth remembering:** (1) the v11.96 anti-parrot guard rejected any line sharing a lifted 7-word run with an example — but a genuine chain *is* described in the words of a chain example, so 42 chains and signet rings failed every run; it now judges whole-line similarity. (2) That failure was **invisible for several rounds** because the runner drew its explanation and `_findRefreshDescBox` wiped it a moment later in its `finally`; the panel now renders it. (3) The final cause was **`ai-http-402` — the OpenRouter key's spend limit** — so `_descErrorLabel()` translates codes into plain language ("OpenRouter credit used up"). *A status code is a correct answer to the wrong audience.*
  - **Automatic on save (v11.90):** the owner asked for it — *"I already have so many things to do, I don't need to remember any others."* `_autoDescribe(id, item)` is hooked into **`saveInventoryItem`**, the one place every create/edit path already funnels through, so no individual flow needed touching. **Fire-and-forget on purpose**: the record is already written and the owner has moved on, so it must never delay a save nor surface an error on finished work — measured, the save returns in **1 ms** and the description lands behind it. A serial queue (`_autoDescQ`) keeps concurrent saves from firing parallel requests. Guards, all verified: a **sold** piece is skipped; re-saving an unchanged piece costs **nothing** (`aiDescPhoto === photoUrl`); changing the **photo** re-describes; coins/metal/bulk skipped; a failure is **silent** and leaves no phantom description, the piece simply reappearing in the manual button's count. **Twin de-duplication:** "Add similar items" creates N pieces sharing ONE photo, so the drain copies an existing twin's line instead of paying N times — verified 3 pieces → **1** AI call.
  - **v2 of the recipe — describe from the HI-RES photo (v11.92):** a square silver pendant with two dolphins engraved on a gold coin insert failed, and the owner asked exactly the right question: *"it read the dolphins correctly on the scan, so why not when it built the description?"* **Measured, not guessed:** the small `photoUrl` is **270×480 / 8 KB**, so that gold disc lands on roughly **89 px** — enough to report "a round gold insert", not enough to resolve two stylised dolphins. His own query photo shows the same disc at ~450 px, which is why the live scan read them. The AI was never at fault in either direction; it had been handed a thumbnail. Fixes: describe from **`photoUrlHi || photoUrl`** (99% of pieces have a distinct hi-res at 675×1200), raise the limit **14 → 30 words**, make naming the **SUBJECT** the first instruction and tell it to look **INSIDE any central disc/coin/medallion** — that is where the motif usually is and it is small — and drop to **`AI_DESC_BATCH = 2`** for more attention per image. **`AI_DESC_VERSION`** gates all of it: a stored line from an older recipe counts as pending again, so pressing the button re-describes the whole stock, and future recipe changes only need the number bumped. Cost check from the owner: the v1 scan of ~640 pieces cost **$0.20**, so none of this is expensive — v2 is expected around €0.60–1.
  - **It has to survive the screen going off, and finish (v11.93):** the owner started the v2 rebuild and reported *"when my screen is off the scanning stops, I'm still at 48, this will take all night"* — both true, and both fatal to a job whose whole premise is "start it and walk away". (1) A phone browser suspends the page when the screen sleeps, so the run simply halted: `_keepAwake()` takes a **`navigator.wakeLock`** for the duration and re-takes it on `visibilitychange`, since the browser drops the lock whenever the page is hidden. (2) The loop was strictly serial — one request at a time on a network-bound job. **`AI_DESC_WORKERS = 4`** now pull from a shared cursor (`next`, incremented without an await in between, so no two workers can claim the same batch). Quality is untouched: still 2 photos per request, just four requests in flight. Verified: peak concurrency 4, every piece written exactly once, Stop still halts promptly with all four running and loses nothing already saved, wake lock released in a `finally`. **Testing note:** stubbing *all* `firebaseio` calls to return `{}` wipes `_inventoryCache` through `_invEnsureForScan` and makes every batch fail for reasons that have nothing to do with the code under test — stub only the PATCH, and pin `loadInventory`.
  - **Tap a result to enlarge it (v11.91):** confirming a match off a 64px thumbnail is guesswork, and being sure is the entire job. `_findThumb(it, size)` is shared by both result cards and opens the existing **`showPhotoFullscreen`** overlay (the same one the Stock cards use — not a second pattern) on **`photoUrlHi || photoUrl`**: the list still loads the small file, the enlargement shows the detail being checked. The owner's own photo in the results header is tappable too, so the two can be compared. Handles a missing photo, a null item and an apostrophe in the URL.
  - **Filters already narrowed the AI search** since v11.86 (`_findAllPieces().filter(_findFilterOk)` is what gets sent) — the owner asked; he simply had everything on *All*.
- **Real-world result: 6/6.** The owner photographed six pieces on his counter and the AI identified every one. This is the measurement that matters — synthetic perturbations of a stored photo flatter badly (that is exactly how the colour-grid descriptor fooled its own test at 96%), so nothing was trusted until he ran real photos. Six is a small sample and does not establish a 100% rate, but it is decisive against the on-device model, which put the correct ring second on the very first real photo. **Where it should still be expected to fail: plain pieces with no stones** (a plain band has little to distinguish it in the catalogue text, and many entries look alike) — which is precisely where the WEIGHT search is exact, because a stone-less piece has nothing deducted from its stored weight. The two halves of the Find tab cover each other completely: photo for stone-set and distinctive pieces, weight for plain ones.
  - **Confirmed in the shop (v11.87):** the owner photographed the byzantine ring on his counter and it returned **RG0075, CONFIDENT, first**, with the reason *"features melee Diamond, Sapphire, Emerald, and Ruby, matching the visible stones and metal colors in the photo"*, having read the photo as *"a wide gold and white gold ring with multiple colored stones (green, red, blue) and small diamonds"* across all 658 pieces. The same photo scored 62% against a wrong ring's 63% under the on-device model. **The result list now shows only the confident piece(s)**; the runners-up fold behind a `Show the others (N)` toggle (`_findToggleOthers`) — they are the model hedging, useful when it is wrong and noise when it is right. If nothing came back `high`, the best guess still shows, because a collapsed panel with nothing above it reads as "no result". The `photos indexed` line is hidden whenever an AI key is set.
- **No `<input type="password">` anywhere in the app (v12.02) — and it must stay that way:** the owner reported the browser's "Save password?" prompt firing **on every tab change**, and that accepting it left stray numbers (e.g. `5.8`, plainly an old weight) autofilled into fields. Both symptoms are one cause. The app changes tabs with **`history.pushState`** (`_jpTrackPanel`, the v10.22 back-gesture guard), which Chrome treats as a navigation — and `loadSyncForm` **prefills** the Sync panel's password fields with the real stored values, so at every tab switch Chrome saw a filled password form on a "navigating" page and offered to save it. Having saved a credential for the origin, it then autofilled its heuristically-guessed username field with junk. **There is no password login in this app at all** — the only real password in the project is the showroom's, which lives in `showroom.htm`. The three fields were never credentials: `dbSecretInput`, `ownerPinInput` and `aiKeyInput` are stored settings that merely want masking. They are now `type="text"` with a **`.masked-field`** class (`-webkit-text-security: disc`, Chrome/Edge/Safari and Firefox 127+) plus `autocomplete="off"` and the third-party manager opt-outs (`data-lpignore`, `data-1p-ignore`, `data-bwignore`, `data-form-type="other"`). **Chrome's password manager keys strictly on `type="password"`**, so with none present it neither offers to save nor autofills — the whole surface is gone rather than argued with. Visually identical (verified: all three render as dots, computed `-webkit-text-security: disc`, layout unchanged); `.value` read/write is unchanged, so `loadSyncForm` and `ownerSaveAndPush` needed no edits, and `maxlength="8"` on the PIN still applies. `#weight` also got `autocomplete="off"` as cheap insurance. `pinHiddenInput` (the owner-PIN overlay) was already `type="tel"` and is untouched. **Rule: never introduce an `<input type="password">` into `jewelprice.htm` — mask with `.masked-field` instead.** Note the owner must also delete any already-saved entry for the site from his browser's password manager; the app cannot remove it.
- **Recovery Card + persistent storage (v12.03) — surviving a cleared browser:** the follow-up to v12.02. Deleting a saved browser password is harmless, but the neighbouring **"clear site data"** button wipes `localStorage`, and **no page can prevent that**. Two complementary answers, because they address different failure modes.
  - **What is actually at risk is narrow, and worth stating plainly:** the shop's records live in Firebase and are never in danger — only *this device's ability to reach them*. Of the four secrets in the Sync tab, three (Owner PIN, Telegram token, OpenRouter key) are mirrored into the Firebase config by `pushToCloud` and restored by `applyCloudConfig`, so they come back by themselves. **The Database Secret cannot**: it is what unlocks the cloud, so it can never be restored *from* the cloud. It exists only in `jpro_creds`.
  - **Recovery Card** (`openRecoveryCard` / `closeRecoveryCard` / `_recCopy` / `_recPrint`, button `#recoveryCardBtnEl` in the Sync panel, i18n `recoveryCardBtn`): shows the Firebase URL + Database Secret with **Copy** and **Print**, to be printed once and kept in the safe. Top-anchored and scrollable (the v11.53 rule — verified reachable at a 420px-tall viewport, where the 488px card scrolls 100px with the title at the top and Close reachable at the bottom). Refuses politely when there is no secret yet rather than opening an empty card. `_recPrint` builds a plain black-on-white A4 page and goes through `openQuoteWindow(html, false, null)`; it declares `lang` (the v11.41 rule).
  - **Deliberately NO QR code.** Every QR in this app is rendered by **`api.qrserver.com`**, so a QR of this card would hand a master write-credential to a third-party server — the same objection that keeps the secret out of the JSON backups that sync to Google Drive. There is no local QR *encoder* in the file (the inlined jsQR is a decoder), and inlining one for convenience is not worth the weight. Copy and print only.
  - **`_requestPersistentStorage()`** (boot, after `_jpInitBackGuard`): calls `navigator.storage.persist()`, which the app had never asked for. This does **not** stop a manual clear — it stops the browser **evicting storage on its own initiative** when the device runs low on space, which is the more dangerous version because there is no prompt and no warning, and it would take the **offline outbox** (`jpro_outbox`, unsent sales, v11.65) with it. Skips the call when `persisted()` is already true; fire-and-forget, so an unsupported browser or a refusal is harmless.
- **Owner-controlled showroom gate (v12.04):** the showroom's access code was hardcoded in `showroom.htm` (`SHOWROOM_PASSWORD = 'lekkas2025'`), so changing it meant editing and redeploying the site. It is now set from **Sync → «Κωδικός Showroom»** (`#srGateToggle` / `#srGatePassInput` / `saveShowroomGate`), with an on/off toggle so the showroom can be opened to everyone and closed again.
  - **Be honest about what the gate is:** the showroom is a public page and the check runs in the customer's browser, so it is a **doorman, not a lock** — it keeps casual visitors and search engines out, nothing more. What actually protects the business is that `/jewelprice_public` carries no prices, costs or vendors. This was equally true of the hardcoded version (which was plainly visible in the public file).
  - **Two places on purpose.** The **hash** (SHA-256, `_sha256Hex`) goes to the world-readable **`/jewelprice_showroom_config`** — `showroom.htm` has no credentials, so it must be able to read the gate. The **readable code** goes to the app's **private** config (`showroomPass` in `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`) so the owner can look it up on any device to tell a customer. A world-readable node therefore never carries the code itself — an improvement on the old hardcoded plaintext. `showroomGate` ('on'/'off') is checked with `!== undefined`, not truthiness, or `'off'` would never survive the round trip.
  - **The one thing that can silently fail is the Firebase rule**, so `saveShowroomGate` **reads the node back the way the showroom does — with no auth token** — and, if that read fails, refuses to report success: it says so and opens `_srShowRulesHelp()`, a sheet with the exact rules JSON (`_srRulesSnippet()`, the single source of truth, mirrored in the security section above) and a Copy button. A dedicated sheet rather than `showConfirm` because that renders with `textContent` and would collapse the JSON to one line — and this text exists to be copied.
  - **`showroom.htm` fails CLOSED.** `_loadShowroomGate()` runs at parse time into `_srGateReady`, which `checkPassword` awaits so a fast typist cannot beat the fetch. A 401 (rule not added), a missing node, or being offline all leave `_srGate` null → the legacy `SHOWROOM_PASSWORD` still works and the door stays shut; **only an explicit `enabled: false` opens it** (`_srUnlock`). Once a hash IS configured the legacy code is **rejected** — it is visible in the public file, so honouring it would be a published backdoor; if `crypto.subtle` ever fails the check fails closed for the same reason. Verified across all five boot paths plus wrong/legacy/new/case-varied codes.
- **`_autoDescribe` never ran for a NEW piece (v12.08):** the owner turned on the Photo Search Check and found every recently added piece showing "none; this piece cannot be found from a photo", while older pieces had descriptions — so the v11.90 promise ("no need to remember, it happens on save") had silently never worked for new items. **Cause: a cache ordering race.** `saveInventoryItem` calls `_autoDescribe(id, item)` inside its `res.ok` branch, but `_autoDescDrain` — and `_findDescribeBatch` / `_findSaveDesc` under it — all resolve the piece **by id out of `_inventoryCache`**, and a newly created item is cached by its caller only **after** `saveInventoryItem` returns (`_saveToInventoryFromLabel` line ~18385). The drain's first cache read happens before its first `await`, so it read `undefined` and hit `continue` — skipped in total silence, exactly as the fire-and-forget design intends for a real failure. An EDIT of an existing piece was already in the cache, which is why only new pieces were affected. (`invAddSimilar` caches before saving, so that path always worked — which is why it was never noticed there.)
  - **Fix:** `saveInventoryItem` seeds `_inventoryCache[id] = item` inside the success branch, before `_autoDescribe`. The PUT succeeded, so the piece genuinely exists — caching it there is simply the cache telling the truth, and it is what every other creation path already does manually. Guarded on `_inventoryCache` being populated (never creates it from null, which would make `renderInventory` think stock was loaded) and on the key being absent (never overwrites a fresher copy).
  - **Verified** by driving the real `saveInventoryItem`: a brand-new piece now makes exactly 1 AI call and PATCHes `aiDesc`/`aiDescPhoto`/`aiDescV:4` onto the record; the twin de-duplication still copies a sibling's line with **0** AI calls; and sold / no-photo / coin pieces still make 0 calls. *(Testing note: `_findDescribeBatch` expects strict `{"d":[…]}` JSON — a stub returning a numbered list throws `bad-json` and the drain swallows it, which looks exactly like the bug.)*
  - **Pieces added before this fix have no `aiDesc`** and are not retried automatically; they reappear in the manual button's count (Settings → Photo Search → build missing descriptions), which is the designed catch-up.
- **Three print paths were still emitting DESCRIPTIVE-code QRs (v12.07) — the "which one are you holding?" that would not go away:** the owner had two earrings correctly carrying **different** ref codes (`ER0712` pink / `ER0714` white, exactly what v11.83 was built to produce) and printed tags whose **text** read those codes — yet scanning still opened the variant chooser. Nothing had been reverted. **v11.31 ("every printed tag is a reference-code tag") converted `invPrintTagFromCard` but missed three other places that call `printLabel` directly**, and `printLabel`'s QR payload is `(useRef && data.refCode) ? data.refCode : data.fullQR` — so with `useRef` omitted the tag printed `ER0712` in text while its **QR carried the shared `AMA925S4.2`**. The scan therefore resolved by descriptive code, found two in-stock pieces differing by colour, and asked. The ref codes were right all along; the QR was not.
  - Fixed by routing all three through **`_printAsRefTag`** (which sets `data.refCode`, passes `useRef: true`, stamps `tagRef`, and handles a shared-ref split): **`invAddSimilar`'s `addBatch`**, the stone-change reprint in **`_promptStoneTagReprint`**, and the **restock** print. Each falls back to the old `printLabel` for a legacy piece with no `seqId` (no ref code to print).
  - **`_printAsRefTag` gained `copies` and `alsoIds`.** An "add similar" batch of N identical pieces prints **ONE** label with N copies, so without `alsoIds` only the last piece would have been stamped `tagRef` while N−1 identically-tagged siblings were left unmarked. `addBatch` now tracks `createdIds` and passes the rest. No spurious split prompt fires for a batch: its members share both the ref *and* `_refIdentityKey`, and `_refSharedWithDifferent` only flags a *different* identity.
  - **Verified** against a reconstruction of the owner's exact pair (same `qrCode`, seqIds 712/714): the print job's `qrData` is now `ER0712` where it used to be `AMA925S4.2`; a 3-piece batch emits `PRINT 1,3` with `qrData: ER0720` and stamps all three; no confirm dialog appears.
  - **Physical tags already printed carry the wrong QR** and must be reprinted (Stock → 🏷️ TAG, which has always used the correct path). Nothing in the data needs fixing — the records were always right.
- **NEVER add a retry loop to `uploadToImgBB` (v12.05 added one, v12.06 took it back out) — read this before "improving" photo upload:** the owner added a piece, got «Αποτυχία αποστολής φωτογραφίας», and the piece saved with no photo. v12.05 reacted by adding 4 attempts with backoff. **That was the wrong call and it probably made things worse.** A jewel uploads TWO images (small + hi-res) **in parallel**, so 4 tries turned one jewel into **up to 8 requests in ~5 seconds** — and those extra requests only ever fire when ImgBB is *already refusing*, i.e. exactly when hammering it is most likely to deepen a rate limit (failed attempts still count). Uploads then failed across **two different wifi IPs, 5G, both devices, and ImgBB's own website**, and stayed failing. Reverted to **one attempt** in v12.06 (verified: 1 request on failure with no delay, 2 per whole jewel, down from 8).
  - **The owner's rule, and he is right:** the single-attempt version uploaded **1770 photos without a single hiccup**. A one-off failure is not evidence that a working path needs re-engineering, and a "fix" that multiplies load during an outage is worse than the outage. **A long track record beats a single incident.** His suspicion that the change was involved was also *methodologically* correct — "it broke right after a change" is the strongest signal in debugging and must be ruled out by measurement, not by reassurance. (Ruled out here by `curl` with no app/browser/Firebase, and by the same code working on 5G while failing on wifi.) It was coincidence for the FIRST failure only; the sustained block that followed was partly self-inflicted by the retry and by ~20 rapid diagnostic uploads.
  - **Kept from v12.05:** surfacing ImgBB's own words (`message (code)` / `HTTP nnn` / "no connection"). It costs **no extra request** and is what turned an unexplainable "Photo upload failed" into the diagnosable `Internal upload error (111)`. Discarding `json.error` was a real flaw; the retry was not the fix.
  - **If a photo ever genuinely needs rescuing after a failure, the answer is a QUEUE** that retries minutes later (the offline sales outbox pattern, v11.65) plus a circuit breaker — never a tight loop at the call site. Proposed and not built; the owner chose to revert first.
  - **What the incident was NOT:** not v12.02–v12.04 (none touch this path — proven by `curl` with no app/browser/Firebase involved, and by the same code working on 5G while failing on wifi); not the Firebase rules (verified from the open internet: `jewelprice_public` and `jewelprice_showroom_config` 200, and `inventory`/`sales`/`clients`/`jewelprice`/`giftcards`/`backups` all **401**); not a size or storage limit (a **1 KB** image failed, and the "64 MB" figure on ImgBB's homepage is per-file); not a revoked key (an invalid key returns a **different** error, `"Invalid API v1 key." code 100`); and not the existing photos, which all still load fine.
  - **Diagnostics have a cost.** ~20 rapid test uploads during the investigation are themselves a plausible trigger for the sustained block. When debugging a rate-limited third party, test **once**, not in bursts.
  - **Finding the casualties:** the v11.71 **"📷 No photo"** status filter lists in-stock pieces with no `photoUrl` (coins and raw metal correctly never flagged). Fix by Edit → re-add the photo.
  - **`IMGBB_KEY` moved to the private config (v12.09)** — see the entry below. The constant is gone; `_imgbbKey()` reads `localStorage 'jpro_imgbb_key'`, set in Sync.
- **Photo migration ImgBB → Cloudinary (v12.11):** Settings → **"Μεταφορά Φωτογραφιών στο Cloudinary"** (`#photoMigSection`, `can('manage_stock')`) copies every photo still hosted elsewhere. Prompted by the ImgBB account restriction: uploads were already blocked while the old images **still served**, so there was a window to get them somewhere safe before that changed — if the account were suspended, all 1770 photos would vanish from the app *and* the public showroom at once.
  - **Cloudinary fetches each image by URL** (`file` = the existing `https://i.ibb.co/…`), so no image data passes through the phone. Verified against the real account: a genuine inventory photo came across byte-identical (9465 bytes, 362×480) with the original untouched.
  - **Safety, in order:** nothing is ever deleted from ImgBB; a record is PATCHed **only after** its new URL comes back, so a failure leaves the piece on its working ImgBB link and no item can end up with a broken photo; the write is a **PATCH of just the photo fields**, so it cannot clobber a concurrent edit from another device; and it is **resumable** — anything already on `res.cloudinary.com` is skipped, so re-running costs nothing.
  - **THE TRAP IT AVOIDS:** changing `photoUrl` makes `aiDescPhoto !== photoUrl`, which is the app's "this photo changed, re-describe it" signal — migrating ~700 pieces would have silently queued ~700 AI calls for descriptions that were already correct. A piece whose description matched its OLD photo has **`aiDescPhoto` carried over** in the same PATCH.
  - `_photoMigPending()` returns **null**, never `[]`, when the inventory can't be read — an empty list would read as "all done" (the v11.61/v11.74 lesson). Three workers share a cursor claimed with no `await` between read and increment; `_keepAwake` holds the screen (v11.93).
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a fixture: only the pieces needing it are picked; an item whose small and hi URLs are identical is uploaded **once** (3 uploads for 2 items, not 4); `aiDescPhoto` carried; already-migrated and photo-less items untouched; a re-run finds 0 and uploads 0; and on a forced upload failure **0 records were written**, the item still pointed at ImgBB, and the reason was captured for the UI.
- **Cloudinary as the photo host, switchable (v12.10):** after ImgBB cut the shop's paid account off mid-season (see v12.09) — and a **brand-new key on that account failed identically**, confirming account-level — photos can now go to **Cloudinary** instead. Sync → Cloudinary: **cloud name** + **unsigned upload preset**, a **Test & Save** that proves it with a real upload, and a **"New photos go to"** switch (ImgBB / Cloudinary).
  - **`uploadToCloudinary`** POSTs to `https://api.cloudinary.com/v1_1/<cloud>/image/upload` with `file` = the **base64 data URI directly** (Cloudinary accepts one, so no Blob conversion), `upload_preset`, and a sanitised **`tags`** value. *Tags, deliberately, not `public_id`* — tags are always permitted on an unsigned upload, so the call can never be rejected because of a preset setting, and the photo is still findable by its ref code in the media library; Cloudinary names the file. Verified against the live API that the endpoint shape is right and that errors come back as `{"error":{"message":…}}`, which is what the parser reads.
  - **`_uploadOnePhoto(dataUrl, name)`** is the single dispatcher, so `uploadInventoryPhoto` — and therefore every one of the existing save paths — is untouched by the switch. `_photoProvider()` can never return a service that isn't configured (selecting Cloudinary without a cloud+preset falls back to ImgBB), and defaults to whichever actually works.
  - **Existing photos are never migrated.** Each item stores its own absolute `photoUrl`/`photoUrlHi`, so the 1770 `i.ibb.co` URLs keep working untouched and only NEW uploads go to Cloudinary. That is what makes this reversible in one tap and is why it was safe to ship in the middle of a bad day.
  - **The unsigned preset is semi-public by nature** — it is the browser-upload equivalent of ImgBB's key, and there is no way around that without a server. It lives in the private config (never in this file), and the protection is on the **preset** in Cloudinary: restrict it to a folder, to images, and to a max size; if abused, delete it and make another, which is a settings change with no deploy.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: correct endpoint/preset/tag/data-URI; Cloudinary's own error surfaced with a null result; a whole jewel = 2 requests both landing on Cloudinary; switching back to ImgBB routes to `api.imgbb.com`; provider defaults and the unconfigured-fallback all hold.
- **ImgBB refused only the OWNER'S KEY — and the photo key is now owner-settable (v12.09):** the "down for maintenance" message the owner kept seeing was **not a general outage and not his IP**. Proven with three requests from one connection in the same second: **no key** → `"Invalid API v1 key." (100)`, **a wrong key** → the same, but **his key** → `"Imgbb is currently down for maintenance." (100)`. The API was up and validating keys normally for his IP; it only refused once it recognised *his account*. Nothing online reported an outage because there wasn't one. (This also retired the IP theory for good — a blocked IP is refused whatever key it carries. His IP had already changed twice.)
  - **`IMGBB_KEY` is gone from the file.** It lived here as `atob('…')` — an *encoding*, not encryption — so the key was readable and spendable by anyone who opened this public page. Now `_imgbbKey()` reads `localStorage 'jpro_imgbb_key'`, entered in **Sync → Photo Upload Key**, carried to the other devices through the private config (`imgbbKey` in `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`). Same pattern as `aiKey` and the Telegram token. **RULE (restated): never put a credential in `jewelprice.htm`.**
  - **`saveImgbbKey` proves the key by actually uploading a 1×1 pixel with it.** A format check would have been useless here — the owner's key was *perfectly valid* and still refused every upload, which is precisely the failure the app has to be able to tell him about. Whatever ImgBB answers is shown **verbatim**, and a key that cannot upload is **not stored** (storing it would just move the failure to the next jewel, where it costs a photo instead of two seconds).
  - **Why it matters operationally:** the key is the one thing that had to be changed by editing and redeploying the app. Now the owner swaps keys himself in seconds — which is exactly what a service that cuts off an account without warning demands. `uploadToImgBB` with no key set makes **zero** requests and says where to set it.
  - **After deploying v12.09 photo upload does nothing until a key is entered** (there is no fallback by design — a fallback would put the key back in the public file). The old key was `ba97b8e1b964eadcdcfaa185edb859a0` if it is ever needed again.
  - Verified: old constant gone and the key absent from the file (plain and base64); no key → 0 requests + a clear message; bad format rejected; a key ImgBB refuses is **not** stored and its reason quoted; a working key is stored and used by the next upload.
- **Silver plating — the silver counterpart of gold colour (v12.12, platinum added v12.13):** a 925 piece is often gold, rose-gold or platinum plated, and the app could only describe gold colours, so a plated piece read as plain «Ασήμι 925» on the showroom card. New optional field **`silverPlating`** (blank/null = plain silver, the vast majority), backed by **`SILVER_PLATING`** with helpers `silverPlatingLabel` (short, for badges/dropdowns), `silverPlatingOptionsHtml` and **`metalWithPlating(item, isEl)`** (the full customer phrase). Offered in **the same places as gold colour** and shown only when `metal === 'silver'`: **label creation** (`#labelPlatingWrap`/`#labelPlatingSelect`, reset to plain on each open — a leftover plating on the next piece would be a silent mislabel), **Edit Item** (`#invEditPlatingSelect`), **"Add similar items"** (`#invAddSimPlating`), and the group **"Edit all"** (`#invEditGroupPlating`, with the same opt-in `__keep__` default as colour/gold colour so a description-only edit can't flatten a mixed batch). Added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`.
  - **Wording follows the jewellery trade, not the data model:** the plating is an ADJECTIVE in front of the base metal — «Επιχρυσωμένο Ασήμι 925» / "Gold Plated Silver 925" — the form Pandora/Missoma/Monica Vinader use. (The technical term for gold on sterling is **vermeil**; deliberately not used, no walk-in tourist knows it.) A **two-tone** piece is not "gold plated" as a whole, so those read as a SUFFIX instead — «Ασήμι 925 & Επιχρυσωμένο» — which is what the entry's `mode: 'pre' | 'post'` selects. `gold_rose` carries a separate `pre_el` because the short Greek in front of the metal would read «Επιχρυσωμένο & Ροζ **Ασήμι**», i.e. *pink silver*.
  - **Platinum plating (v12.13)** — added by the owner's own term, «Επιπλατινωμένο» / "Platinum Plated", plus the **gold + platinum** two-tone (a common look: yellow-plated detail against a white body). Its Stock badge takes the **white-gold** colours, not the gold ones — it is a white finish. Note the trade reality, not changed because the owner's wording is what customers read: most "platinum plated" silver is in fact **rhodium** plated (`επιροδιωμένο`). The remaining permutations (silver + platinum, rose + platinum) are one table row each if he asks; silver + platinum was left out because bare 925 and a platinum finish look nearly identical, so the distinction would not be visible on a card.
  - **The "925K" bug went with it:** the showroom appended `K` to every karat, so silver printed "SILVER 925**K**". 925 is a millesimal fineness, not karats — `metalWithPlating` adds `K` only for gold.
  - **It is part of the piece's IDENTITY**, so it joins `_refIdentityKey` (a plated twin of a plain piece is a *different* piece and earns its own reference code via v11.83 — verified) and `_variantKey`/`_variantLabel` (the scan chooser can tell two platings apart). It also splits the **showroom** grid card, the lightbox in-stock count and `_navFacetResultCount` by `groupId + color + goldColor + silverPlating`, exactly like gold colour.
  - Surfaced in the app on the Stock card (`_platingBadge`, gold or rose tint), the grouped card (`_grpPlating` + the colour breakdown, which now buckets a plain-colour silver piece by its plating), the customer-facing fullscreen price view, and the shared-ref clash listings. Also fed to the **AI photo-search catalogue** (`_findCatalogueLines`, in English) — the highest-value spot: a gold-plated silver piece READS as gold in a photo while the record says silver, so without it the model is handed the wrong colour. Ported to `showroom.htm` and `item.htm`.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: all 7 options in both languages; each of the three forms shows/hides correctly per metal, resets per item and saves (`silver_rose`, blank → `null`, gold pieces forced `null` with `goldColor` untouched); "Add similar" note and save mint a new ref code on a plating change but still share one on an identical clone, and **gold sources are byte-identical to before**; "Edit all" keeps a mixed group mixed on a description-only save; showroom splits 3 plain + 2 plated into two cards with counts 3 and 2; Greek all-caps tonos stripping confirmed **by measurement with a proportional font** (`.card-tag` is DM Mono, whose identical widths make the usual probe read as a false negative — the `lang="en"` control proves the test can detect the difference).
  - **Filter pills in both places**, mirroring the gold-colour group: the Stock filter sheet (`platingGroup`, `_invFilters.platings`) and the showroom nav drawer (`opts.platings`, `activeFilters.platings`). Only platings **actually in stock** are offered, plus a **"Απλό ασήμι / Plain silver"** pill, and the whole group is **absent** unless there is a real choice to make (`length > 1` in the showroom; app hides it when every silver piece is plain) — so a shop with no plated stock sees nothing new. Selecting any plating implies silver, so gold pieces drop out. **Plain silver is stored as `null`/`''`, which the two hosts handle differently:** the showroom's `toggleDraftPill` is happy with an empty value, but the app's shared pill handler bails on `if (!grp || !val)`, so there the pill carries the sentinel **`PLATING_PLAIN`** (`'__plain__'`) and `applyFilters` maps it back. The showroom's ported `SILVER_PLATING` has no blank row at all (it needs no dropdown), so `getFilterOptions` prepends `''` by hand — without it "plain + gold plated" would be ONE option and the group would never render.
- **Automatic price rounding on a scanned piece (v12.14):** a piece is priced to the cent (€239.42), which is the right number for the owner and the wrong one to read out to a customer. When a REAL tagged piece is looked up the customer-facing figure is now rounded to a jeweller's price — 80, 85, 90, 155, 160, 1205. `autoRoundPrice(price)` is plain nearest-step rounding (`Math.round(price/step)*step`), which reproduces the owner's spec exactly: 82.49 → 80, 82.50 → 85. It returns **null** (meaning "change nothing") when the step is 0, the price is already round, or the price is below the floor.
  - **Display only.** `_autoRoundScannedPrice(finalPrice)` runs at the end of `calculate()` and simply calls the existing **`applyRoundedPrice`** — the same function the manual rounding box has always used — so the rounded figure flows through the price bar, the fullscreen customer view, the cart, the quote and the sale exactly as a hand-typed rounding does. **Nothing is written to the inventory record**: the Stock card, `initialRetail`, the gain % and the CSV export all keep the exact price.
  - **It also FILLS the rounding box** (value + the ✕ state) rather than acting invisibly, so it is obvious it happened and **one tap on the ✕ brings the cents back** — reusing a control the owner already knows instead of inventing a second one.
  - **Trigger = the code box is non-empty**, the same signal `_syncPriceWarn` uses. That covers scan, typed code and the Stock card's **+** (v11.34 sends the ref code) — all "a real piece is in hand". Creating a NEW piece from scratch has no code and keeps its exact figure, because that number is what the owner is about to price the piece at. Because the check lives inside `calculate()`, the rounding survives every re-calculate (language toggle, the per-item self-heal at ~L15996, a spot tick) instead of being wiped by them.
  - **€20 floor** (`state.autoRoundMin`), chosen by the owner: nearest-5 moves a price by up to €2.50, which is nothing on a €239 ring and +56% on a €3.20 clasp. Below the floor the exact price stands.
  - **Self-service, per the owner's standing preference** — he asked to "test it for a few days and revert if I don't like it", so instead of a temporary experiment there is a permanent control: **Settings → Tax & Margin** has a step select (Off / €1 / €5 / €10, default **€5**) and the floor (default **€20**). `autoRoundStep`/`autoRoundMin` are in `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`, so a change reaches every device. Reverting is picking "Off"; no code change, no deploy.
  - **Defaults are read through `_autoRoundStep()`/`_autoRoundMin()`, never off `state` directly** — an existing device's saved `jpro_state` predates these keys, so a bare `state.autoRoundStep` would be `undefined` (verified: the helpers fall back to 5/€20 and such a device rounds correctly).
  - **`applyRoundedPrice` fixed in two ways while it was being made automatic:** rounding UP was stored as a **negative discount** (−0.24%), which would have printed on a quote — the discount is now clamped at 0; and the **ex-VAT line under the price** (`#preVatDisplay`) was never updated by a rounding, so it disagreed with the figure above it (both `applyRoundedPrice` and `resetRoundedPrice` now keep it in step). Both bugs pre-date this change and also affected the manual rounding box.
  - **Other-type items need nothing** — `calculateOtherPrice` has always rounded to the nearest 5 (`Math.round(cost * mult / 5) * 5`), which is what validated the choice of step. **Bulk chains are untouched** (they route to `_openBulkSellByQr`, which has its own exact rounding-target field), as is the Other-type `fixedRetail` override (a price the owner set deliberately must not be second-guessed).
- **Photo per piece in the group sheet (v12.15):** the group detail sheet (`openInvGroup`) listed each piece by ref code, weight, colour and price but showed **no photo**, so the pieces of a mixed group (a red and a light-blue Fish necklace) could not be told apart by eye. Each non-coin piece row now leads with a **56px thumbnail that opens full-screen on tap**, reusing **`_findThumb(it, 56)`** (built for the Find results) → `showPhotoFullscreen(photoUrlHi || photoUrl)` — the small file in the list, the hi-res on the enlargement, and the same overlay the Stock cards use rather than a second pattern. Verified: the enlargement opens above the sheet (z-index) and dismissing it leaves the sheet open underneath.
  - **One thumbnail per DISTINCT piece**, keyed on **`_refVariantKey(it)`** (colour + gold colour + plating + descriptive `qrCode`) — the app's own "are these two interchangeable?" test — tracked by a `_seenVariants` Set while the rows are built. Pieces that genuinely differ each show their own picture; a batch of identical pieces shows it **once, on the first row** — the owner's own rule, and it stops a 12-piece batch repeating the same image twelve times. **NOT keyed on the photo URL** (the obvious first attempt, shipped and immediately wrong): identical pieces added separately each get their **own ImgBB upload — same picture, different URL** — so a URL de-dup showed every one (the reported ER0698 case, 6 identical earrings all showing the thumbnail). A piece with no photo simply has no thumbnail (no grey placeholder — `_findThumb` is only called when `photoUrl` exists), and its row's text starts at the left edge.
  - **Coins are excluded** — the coin branch returns earlier and bullion carries no photo. Layout verified at 375px and 1280px: no horizontal overflow, the info block is a flex child with `min-width:0` so the descriptive code still wraps.
- **Give away as a gift (v12.16):** the owner routinely hands a small, often slow-moving piece to a good customer's child as goodwill. That is neither a sale (no money in) nor shrinkage — it is a **marketing/goodwill expense** — but before this there was no way to record it, so a given-away piece stayed "in stock" and would have shown up as **missing** in the end-of-season stocktake (v10.70). `invGiveaway(id)` (all roles — an employee can hand a gift over at the counter, the owner's explicit choice) opens a small top-anchored overlay (item + optional free-text note e.g. "Maria's daughter" + the cost it will book) and `_invGiveawayConfirm(id)`:
  - **removes the piece from stock** — sets `status:'sold'`, `dateSold:Date.now()`, and a new `giveaway:true` flag on the item, checking `saveInventoryItem` **landed** before booking anything and rolling the cache back on a failed write (the v11.62 lesson);
