  - **books the cost as a loss** — a **€0 sale record** (`amount:0`), `cost` = the piece's cost via `_invGiveawayCost(item)` (`purchaseCost` if set, else `calcInvItemPrice(item).baseCost`), `profit = −cost`, flagged **`giveaway:true`** and linked by **`invId`** (+ `invQr`/`seqRef`) so **deleting the record restores the exact piece** via the shared `_restoreStockForSale` (v10.59), and built the robust way (`_nextSaleId` + `_outboxAdd` before the push) so an offline gift is never lost (v11.65). Because the amount is €0 it adds **nothing to revenue**, while `_calcSalesProfit` includes the negative `profit` so the **period profit reflects what the gift cost** — giving a running "goodwill spend" figure that doubles as a dead-stock clear-out signal.
  - **Surfaced transparently, never mistaken for a €0 sale:** a **🎁 gift icon** on the in-stock single card's top row (next to Reserve, same `!isSold && !isBulk && !isCoin && !isMetal` gate) and a **🎁 Gift** button in the group detail per-piece action row (non-coin/non-metal); a gold **🎁 GIFT** badge on the sales-list row (`giveaway` is passed through the `_groupSalesForDisplay` single-record shape, which only copies named fields); and a **🎁 Gift — given away, no payment taken** meta line in the sale detail. Reserved pieces are refused (steer to the reservation instead). Verified in-browser: functions defined, overlay builds with the note field + confirm wiring + cost shown, cost pulled from `purchaseCost`.
- **Vendor category wipe — production data-loss incident & surgical recovery (v12.17, guard later superseded by v12.18):** the Edit Vendor form's METAL/OTHER buttons were a *mode switch*, not "add another type". `saveVendor` rebuilt the vendor from ONLY the visible tab's rows — so switching an existing metal vendor to OTHER and pressing Save silently discarded **all** its metal categories, orphaning every item that used them (`getItemCat` then resolved to nothing). This actually happened: the owner tried to add a bronze "Other" category to the silver vendor **Amaya** (14 vendors, ~1000+ items) and lost its 4 silver categories (Σκουλαρίκια/Κολιέ/Βραχιόλια/Δαχτυλίδια). **Recovery was surgical and touched no stock item:** the daily cloud backups at `/jewelprice_backups/<ms>` were read with the DB secret (from the owner's Recovery Card); the 10:39 snapshot still had Amaya intact; a diff confirmed **only** `vendors[12]` differed from live; that one vendor object was PUT back to `/jewelprice/vendors/12` (a full safety copy of the live config saved first, to `SAFETY_live_config_before_fix.json`). On the owner's next app open `autoSync` → `applyCloudConfig` pulls it and the items reconnect by category name/idx. **General recovery pattern to reuse:** read `/jewelprice_backups.json?shallow=true` to list snapshot timestamps, pull `/<ms>/config/vendors.json` from a good one, diff against live `/jewelprice/vendors.json`, and PUT back only the differing element — never a full-node restore (that would revert unrelated same-day work). v12.17 first added a destructive-switch confirm guard (`_dispatchVendorSave`); **that guard was REMOVED in v12.18** once the real fix landed (a vendor can now hold both kinds, so switching views no longer discards anything).
- **Unified vendors — one vendor holds BOTH metal and other categories (v12.18):** the proper fix for the v12.17 trap, and a feature the owner asked for. A vendor can now carry metal categories (gold/silver, karat + rate/flat/per-item) **and** "other" categories (bronze, steel… priced by ×multiplier) at the same time; in Calc the **metal button** chooses which set the category dropdown shows (Gold/Silver → metal categories, Other → other categories). Full design in `UNIFIED_VENDOR_DESIGN.md`.
  - **The load-bearing discriminator: `catIsOther(cat)` = `cat.multiplier !== undefined`** (a category's kind is read from the category, NEVER from the vendor). Verified against the live backup: every other category has a multiplier and no metal category does — **0 exceptions across 14 vendors** — so for every EXISTING vendor `catIsOther(cat)` is *exactly equivalent* to the old `vendor.vendorType==='other'`, and every swap is provably identical on current data.
  - **Data model (Design A, no migration):** categories of both kinds live in the ONE `laborCategories` array, so `catIdx` stays a single-array index and **no existing item/record changes**. `discount` applies to the other side. `vendorType:'other'` is written ONLY for a *pure* other vendor (no metal cats), so legacy pure-other vendors round-trip byte-identically; a metal or mixed vendor carries no `vendorType`. New helpers `catIsOther`/`vendorHasMetal`/`vendorHasOther`/`vendorIsMixed`.
  - **Untouched (proven-safe by omission):** `calcInvItemPrice`, `getItemCat`, `catAbbrs`, `vendorAbbr` — the pricing/code core. An other item is still identified by `item.metal==='other'` (→ `fixedRetail`); a metal item by its metal category. The save-from-label path was already `isOther = (metal==='other')`, so item creation needed no change.
  - **Changed (each equivalence-preserving on existing data):** `buildCodeMap`/`buildLabelStrings` use `catIsOther(cat)` not `vendor.vendorType`; `updateWorkTypeOptions` filters the dropdown by the calc `metal` for a MIXED vendor only (pure vendors unfiltered), always keeping the ORIGINAL index as the option value; `setMetal` refreshes the dropdown; the scan handler sets the metal mode BEFORE building the category list; `calcOtherRetail`/`calculateOtherPrice` accept a mixed vendor (kind from the selected category, not the vendor); `_invEditVendorOptions`→`vendorHasMetal`, `_invEditCatOptions` excludes other cats; the bulk sheet lists `vendorHasMetal` vendors and metal cats only (also **fixed a latent pre-existing bug** where the bulk vendor `<option>` used the *filtered* index but was read back as the *real* index — wrong for any vendor after Butterflyfish); the workType-change karat sync skips other cats; `renderVendors` shows `×mult` for other cats.
  - **`editVendor`/`saveVendor` load & save BOTH sections.** Each existing category row carries `dataset.origIdx`; on save the array is rebuilt by placing every existing row back at its own index and appending new rows — so positions never shift **in either direction** (pure-metal + other → metal cats keep 0..k-1; pure-other **Butterflyfish** + metal → its other cats keep 0,1 and the new metal cat appends at 2 — a naive "metal-first" rebuild would have shifted them). The Metal/Other buttons are now a *view switch*; both sections always save. A hint line (`vendorTypeHint`) says so. The add-vendor MODAL still creates pure vendors (mixed is made by editing).
  - **Verified in the browser against the real 14-vendor config:** `buildCodeMap` is **byte-identical, 170/170 entries, 0 added/0 removed** before vs after; metal categories are value-identical through a save round-trip; codes are stable through a save (freezing adds `abbr` but the codes don't move — Butterflyfish BUT/BUTR/BUTK/BUTΚ unchanged); a mixed Amaya prices silver items and a bronze ×3 item, `getItemCat` resolves both, no code collisions; the calc dropdown filters correctly per metal; console clean. **Nothing about existing stock changed.**
- **Material field for "other" items (v12.19):** "other"-type items (fashion pieces priced by ×multiplier — brass/steel/plated, or silver-based fashion jewelry) carry no metal+karat, so their only material info used to live in the free-text description — not shown as a field and not filterable. New optional field **`otherMaterial`** (language-neutral key, blank = unspecified), backed by **`OTHER_MATERIALS`** + helpers `otherMaterialLabel(value,isEl)` / `otherMaterialOptionsHtml(selected)` (mirrors `SILVER_PLATING`/`ITEM_COLORS` exactly). List seeded from the shop's REAL usage (audited the live 47 other-items): gold-plated brass (most common), silver-plated brass, rose-gold-plated brass, plated brass, brass, stainless steel, **silver + gold-plated silver** (many "other" pieces are silver-based semi-precious fashion jewelry), bronze, copper. Surfaced as a dropdown **only when `metal === 'other'`** in **label creation** (`#labelMaterialWrap`/`#labelMaterialSelect`, shown/reset in `_openLabelModalInner`, saved in the label item builder next to `silverPlating`) and **Edit Item** (`#invEditMaterialSelect`, in the `isOtherEdit` section right above the existing simple stone chip-picker, saved in `invEdit`). Displayed: the in-app card's meta line (`buildCard`, other branch — was blank, now the material label), the **showroom** card tag + lightbox detail "Material" row (`showroom.htm`, ported `OTHER_MATERIALS`/`otherMaterialLabel`), and **`item.htm`** (ported `OTHER_MATERIALS_L` map + a Material row). Added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` and carried by `publicProjection`. i18n `materialLabel`. **Existing items are unchanged** — no `otherMaterial` → label is `''`, exactly the old blank (verified: `otherMaterialLabel(undefined)===''`, legacy other items still project, all three files parse clean with no console errors, helper works EL/EN in all three). **Stones for other items already existed** — the Edit form's simple chip picker (stone TYPE only, no carat/clarity/shape, `#invEditStonePicker`/`#invEditStoneChips` → `stonesDesc`) feeds the showroom stone filter; v12.19 didn't change it. Two follow-ups were done in **v12.20** (below). **Still deferred:** the material field on the **scan-to-inventory** form; adding "Cubic Zirconia"/"Fossil Coral" to `STONE_TYPES` (an audit of all stones actually used showed **every one is already covered**; those two are 2-use near-variants of the existing Zircon/Coral, so skipped to avoid the abbrev/translation round-trip).
- **Material feature follow-ups — showroom filter + stones-at-creation (v12.20):** (1) **Showroom Material filter** — a new `materials` facet in `showroom.htm`, mirroring the `platings` facet end to end: `getFilterOptions().materials` (distinct `otherMaterial` present on other items, in `OTHER_MATERIALS` order, blank not offered), a pill group in `renderDrawer` (only when `>1` present), the `applyFilters` check (`item.metal==='other'` AND material in the set — so non-other and material-less pieces drop out), plus `materials:[]` threaded through **every** `activeFilters`/`draftFilters` object (init, `openFilterDrawer` clone, `navApplyFacets`, `clearAllFilters`, `clearDraftFilters`, the commit) and the active-filter count + summary chip. `applyFilters` uses `(activeFilters.materials||[]).length` defensively. Verified in-browser: gold-plated-brass filter → exactly the 2 GPB items, steel → the 1 steel item, material-less/non-other excluded, drawer renders, console clean. (2) **Stone chip-picker on the first add screen** — the label-creation modal (`#labelStonesWrap`/`#labelStonePicker`/`#labelStoneChips`/`#labelStoneAddBtn`, shown only when `metal==='other'`) mirrors the Edit-Item chip picker (stone TYPE only, no carat/shape), wired in `_openLabelModalInner` (fresh `window._labelStones=[]` per open, `.onclick=` assignment so re-opens never stack handlers), and `_saveToInventoryFromLabel` uses those chips for `stonesDesc` when `metal==='other'` (metal items keep the calc's carat-based stone rows — verified the override is `other`-only). i18n `stonesOptional`. Verified: add/remove chips update the array, saved `stonesDesc` reflects it, metal path unchanged, no console errors.
- **Fullscreen price view — material not "Other", no 0g weight (v12.21):** the customer-facing enlarged price overlay (`togglePriceFullscreen` → `#pfsDetails`) showed a **"Metal: Other"** row and a **"Weight: 0.00g"** row for "other"-type pieces — both ugly now that material exists. Fixed: `_isOtherPfs = (lastCalcResult.metal==='other' || lastCalcResult.isOtherType)`; for those the metal row is replaced by a **"Material"** row (`t('materialLabel')` → «Υλικό»/"Material") showing `otherMaterialLabel(...)`, sourced from `lastCalcResult.otherMaterial` (added to the scan/fixedRetail `lastCalcResult` builder) with a fallback to `_lastScannedInvEntry.otherMaterial`; if no material is set, **no row at all** (never the bare "Other"). The **weight row is skipped entirely** for other items (and for any weight `≤ 0`). Gold/silver pieces are unchanged (Metal + Weight rows intact — verified: gold shows "Metal: Gold · 18K" + "Weight: 3.20g"). Verified EL+EN, console clean.
- **Total Cost card in the Stock summary (v12.22):** the inventory summary header had **Pieces / Total Retail / Metal Value** cards but no way to see what the shown pieces COST — the owner wanted to filter a batch (e.g. vendor + today's date-added) and reconcile the total against the vendor's invoice. A **fourth "Κόστος / Total Cost" stat card** was added (`invStatCard3`/`#invStatCost`, i18n `invTotalCost`), computed in `renderInvSummary` over the same **in-stock** `inStock` loop as retail/metal, using the **exact per-item cost rule as the CSV export**: `_invOtherCost(item)` for `metal==='other'`, else `purchaseCost` when stored, else `calcInvItemPrice(item).baseCost` (metal + labour + stones). It respects the current filters (the summary already receives the filtered rows) and has a tappable gold/silver/other breakdown in its expand (`invStatCostGold/Silver/Other`), mirroring the retail card; `costGold+costSilver+costOther === totalCost` by construction. **Owner/manager only** — hidden for employees like the retail/metal value cards (`_statCostCard` display + `_isEmpSummary` on the value). `toggleInvStat` now covers cards `[0,1,2,3]`. Display-only; no data written, no per-item behaviour change. **(v12.23) The card is HIDDEN by default** — it made four cards too tight/small on the phone. It's now opt-in via a **"💰 Εμφάνιση κόστους / Show cost" toggle pill in the Filter sheet** (`showCostGroup`, owner/manager only, i18n `invShowCostLbl`/`invShowCostPill`), backed by `let _showCostCard` persisted in `localStorage 'jpro_showCost'`. Default OFF → the summary stays the comfortable **3-column** layout; only when ON does `.inv-summary` get the `.show-cost` class (→ `repeat(4,1fr)`, tighter gap/padding, value font 22→20 / 17→16px) and the cost card show. It's a **view preference, not a filter**: it lives outside `_invFilters` (so "Clear filters" doesn't reset it and it doesn't count toward the filter badge), and the toggle handler flips the card's `display` + the container class **directly** (instant, no `renderInvList()`) — the cost totals are computed on every summary render regardless, so revealing the card just shows the already-current numbers without prematurely applying the sheet's not-yet-Applied pill/date changes. `renderInvSummary` reads `_showCostCard` to set the card visibility + container class on every render.
- **Summary cards open full-screen (v12.24):** each Stock summary stat card (`toggleInvStat`) is now a **three-state cycle** — 1st tap **expands** (the existing gold/silver/other breakdown), 2nd tap opens a **big readable full-screen view** (`openStatFullscreen`), and tapping the overlay returns the card to its original collapsed size (`closeStatFullscreen`). The overlay (`#statFullscreenOverlay`, mirrors the customer-price `#priceFullscreenOverlay` style/z-index) reads the values **straight from the already-rendered card DOM** (label, `.inv-stat-val`, `.inv-stat-gain` innerHTML for the coloured gain %, `#invStatPureGold` line, and the `.inv-stat-row` breakdown rows filtered to non-`—`) so it always matches the card and needs no recompute. Hidden on any tab change in `showPanel` (same as the price overlay). Owner-only cards stay owner-only (they're already hidden from employees). Display-only. **(v12.25) Overlay re-parented to `<body>` on open** — `#panel-inventory` is `position:fixed; z-index:100` and lives **outside** `.app` (`position:relative; z-index:1`), so the overlay nested in `.app` was trapped in that stacking context and painted **behind** the Stock panel however high its z-index (the classic "hidden in the back" symptom, same root cause as the ☰ More-menu fix in v11.06). `openStatFullscreen` now does `document.body.appendChild(ov)` (idempotent) so its z-index applies at the root and it covers everything.
- **Quotes for custom orders (v12.26):** a customer walked in asking what two custom wedding rings would cost. There was no way to record that — a custom order **required a deposit** at creation, and the only "send a message" button was the ready-for-pickup notify — so the owner had to type the whole quote into SMS by hand. A custom ticket now models the real sequence: **quote → deposit → order**.
  - **New status `'quote'`, entered by leaving the deposit blank.** No extra control: on the custom tab the deposit is now optional (its label says so, with a hint line spelling out what blank means) and the estimate is optional too (a custom piece is often priced later; a **repair keeps its required estimate**, since the item is in your hand — `_repairSetType` swaps that label). `_repairSave` sets `status: isQuote ? 'quote' : 'in_progress'` and, for a quote, opens the detail straight away so the next step is in front of you. **A custom ticket saved WITH a deposit behaves exactly as before.**
  - **`_repairQuoteOpen`/`_repairQuoteSave`** — fill in or correct the price when the workshop answers: description, price (low–high), the **deposit you ask for** (`depositAsked`, quote-only) and the ready-by date.
  - **Sending it reuses the existing three channels.** `_repairNotify` branches on the status — a quote sends `_repQuoteMessage(r)` (description, price, deposit asked, ready-by, sign-off), otherwise the unchanged "ready for pickup" text — so WhatsApp / SMS / Email, the contact fallback to the linked client, and the sent-stamp all come for free. The stamp is **`quoteSentAt` for a quote, `notifiedAt` otherwise**, so "quote sent" and "ready sent" are two different facts. Sending with no price is refused and opens the price form instead.
  - **`_grVocative(name)`** — Greek addresses a person in the vocative, so «Γεια σας **Μανώλη**», not «Μανώλης». Only touches a single all-Greek first word ending -ης/-ας/-ος; a foreign or unrecognised name is left exactly as typed (verified: Μανώλης→Μανώλη, Κώστας→Κώστα, Γιώργος→Γιώργο, Μαρία/Ελένη unchanged, "John Smith" untouched, no-op in English). Used by the quote **and** the ready message.
  - **`_repairDepositOpen`/`_repairDepositConfirm`** — the customer accepted and paid: amount (prefilled from `depositAsked`) + method (cash/card/**bank** — a transfer is normal for a big order), sets `deposit`/`depositMethod`/`dateDeposit` and flips the status to `in_progress`. From that moment the ticket is the ordinary custom order, with Mark ready / Collect back. **No income is booked here** — the whole price is booked at collection, as it always was.
  - **Printed form:** `buildRepairPDF` titles a quote **ΠΡΟΣΦΟΡΑ / QUOTE**, labels the amount "Τιμή προσφοράς", prints the requested deposit and carries quote-specific terms (approximate price, the order starts on the deposit, which is non-refundable); the file is `quote-R-0042.pdf`. Order and repair forms are byte-identical to before (verified by capturing every string drawn into the PDF).
  - The list sorts quotes first, shows a violet **📝 Προσφορά** chip, and a quote is cancellable like any ticket. Verified end to end in the browser against the owner's real case: quote saved with no deposit, price filled in, WhatsApp URL carrying the full Greek message, deposit → in_progress with the collect suggestion still €1100, and repairs/custom-with-deposit unchanged.
- **THE CART RULE — a cart line owns a RECORD, not a code (v12.26):** the owner put PE0550 and NK0044 in the cart and pressed ΚΡΑΤΗΣΗ; the printed hold showed **NK0044 twice** — both ref codes and both photos — while descriptions and prices were correct, and only NK0044 was actually flagged reserved, so **PE0550 stayed live on the public showroom** while the customer believed it was held.
  - **Cause:** `_resItemFromResult` preferred the **global `_scanChosenInvId`** ("the piece the calc resolved last", set on essentially every successful lookup — `_checkInvStatusForCalc` pins it for a ref match or a single in-stock match, not just the variant chooser). `reserveCartItems` mapped that function over the cart, so **every line came back as the same record**. Descriptions/prices came from the line's own `result`, which is why only the identity fields were wrong. `_resDialogSave`'s `itemIds` then held `[NK, NK]` and `_resMarkItems` flagged one piece.
  - **The rule now:** the record id is captured by `confirmAddToCart` at the moment the line is added (`invId: _calcScanInvId()` — the same guarded resolver the sale uses, read **before** the code box is cleared), and **nothing downstream re-derives the piece from a code string**. `_resItemFromResult(name, result, entry)` now does **no lookup at all** — the caller passes the resolved `[id, item]`.
  - **`_cartLineInv(ci, used)`** = pinned id → else a code/ref fallback for a legacy or hand-typed line, both skipping ids in `used`. **`_resolveCart(cart)`** runs that over the whole cart with one `used` set and returns `{lines, missing}`. The code-string fallback alone is not safe: since v11.32 two pieces may legitimately share a descriptive code, so `.find()` can return a twin — or the same record twice.
  - **A line that cannot be placed is NAMED, never dropped in silence** (`_cartMissingOk` → a confirm listing them, "continue with the other N?"). The reserve path used to `.filter()` them away silently — which is precisely how the owner ended up with a 2-item hold that held one piece.
  - Used by all three cart consumers, so they can no longer disagree: **reserve** (`reserveCartItems`), **sell** (`markCartAsSold` — its own duplicate code-match loop was deleted), and the **multi-quote photos** (which had the same `.find(qrCode === labelCode)` bug and could print one photo twice). `openReserveDialog` also dedupes by `invId` as a last line of defence. Bulk-chain lines are keyed by `bulkInvId` and deliberately **not** deduped — two cuts of one chain in a cart are legitimate.
  - **Two more holes closed in the same pass:** (1) creating a hold **from the cart** left the cart full, so the next tap on "Sell all" would sell the pieces just put on hold, leaving the reservation dangling — `_resDlgSource` is now remembered and `_resDialogSave` empties the cart when the hold came from it; (2) `markCartAsSold` never checked `reservationId`, so a held piece could be sold from the cart with no word (the single-item path has warned since v10.80) — `_cartReservedOk` now names the held pieces and points at Desk → «Ολοκλήρωση & πώληση», which is the path that also closes the hold. The legitimate completion path is unaffected (`_resComplete` sets `_cartSaleItems` and calls `openMultiSaleModal` directly).
  - **Verified by driving the real functions** with the owner's two pieces and `_scanChosenInvId` deliberately pinned to NK0044: the hold now stores two distinct `itemIds`, two ref codes, two photos, €560, and **both** pieces get `reservationId` (both leave the showroom); the printed form carries one ref + one photo per line. Also: two legacy no-`invId` lines sharing one code resolve to two **different** twins; three lines for two pieces name the third; the same piece added twice names the second; a piece sold since it was added is named; a stale code box yields no pin; bulk cuts keep their own weights; and both languages read correctly.
- **The cart survives a reload / the OS killing the app (v12.27):** the owner asked for it once the identity fix made it safe. The cart was memory-only (the `sessionStorage 'jpro_quoteCart'` key in `clearQuoteCart` was vestigial — nothing ever wrote it, and the boot comment already claimed to restore from it), so a reload, or Android killing a backgrounded PWA mid-customer, lost a half-built sale with no warning.
  - **`localStorage`, not `sessionStorage`** — the app kill this exists to survive is exactly what ends a session.
  - **One save point:** `_saveQuoteCart()` is called at the end of **`updateCartUI()`**, which every cart mutation already calls (all three push sites, `removeFromCart`, `clearQuoteCart`, and the sale/hold clears) — so a future caller cannot forget it. An empty cart **removes** the key rather than storing `[]`.
  - **Bounded on purpose:** `_restoreQuoteCart()` restores only a cart from the **same local calendar day** and **< 12 h** old (`CART_MAX_AGE_MS`); anything older is deleted, not resurrected — its prices were quoted at that moment's gold. It announces itself with a toast naming the time it was built (`Καλάθι από 12:06 · 2 είδη`, 24-hour, correct singular/plural), because a cart that silently reappears reads as a bug.
  - **Quota-safe:** a bulk-chain line can carry the photo taken at sale time as a **data URL**. A failed `setItem` is retried once with `photoDataUrl` stripped (the in-memory copy keeps it, so the current session's quote is unaffected) and, failing that, the key is removed — a full localStorage must never take the outbox or the credentials down with it.
  - Restored lines keep their pinned **`invId`**, so a cart rebuilt after a restart still reserves/sells the exact pieces. Verified by driving the real functions: two pieces added through `confirmAddToCart`, memory and all scan globals wiped, restore → reserve produces two distinct `itemIds` / refs / photos; plus yesterday's cart dropped, a 13-hour-old cart dropped, a 3-hour-old cart restored, corrupt JSON and price-less lines ignored, Clear wiping both cart and key, and the quota fallback keeping `bulkInvId`.
- **Deleting a category identifies the ROW, not the name (v12.28):** the owner spotted **two identical «Μενταγιόν» rows** on the Kyklos vendor (both 925s, per-item, same frozen abbr `Μ`/`M` → the same code `ΚΥΚ925Μ`) and asked whether deleting one would break anything. It would have: `confirmDeleteLaborRow` found the pieces to move by **`item.catName === catName`**, which v11.10 (duplicate names allowed) turned into a trap — deleting either row swept up **both** rows' pieces (4 of them in his real data, not 1), and the destination list filtered `c.name !== catName`, so the twin — the one correct target — was the only category NOT offered. The only way through was to dump the pendants into an unrelated category.
  - The row is now identified by its **position** (`dataset.origIdx`, stamped by `editVendor`), and membership is decided by **`getItemCat(item) === thisCat`** — the same resolver that prices the piece, so the two can never disagree. Vendor matching uses `_itemVendor` (v11.14), so a piece with a stale `vendorIdx` but a matching name is still counted.
  - Every other live row is a destination, **including a same-name twin**; options are labelled with `catOptionLabel` and get a ` — #N` suffix when two render identically, since picking the wrong one silently reprices pieces. The select now carries the real category index (it used to carry a position in the filtered list).
  - The move also **checks that each `saveInventoryItem` landed** and rolls back exactly the ones that didn't (v11.62 lesson) — a failed write no longer reports success and retires the row anyway.
  - **Verified against the owner's real backup** (14 vendors, 1129 items, Kyklos = `vendorIdx 11`): the old code matched **4** pieces, the new code matches **1** (row 0's own); the twin appears as a destination; after moving that piece to row 4 and saving, `buildCodeMap` is **byte-identical** (every printed tag still scans), all **40** Kyklos pieces price **unchanged**, all 8 slots are kept with row 0 marked `deleted: true`, no phantom karat warning fires, and the retired row disappears from the Calc dropdown while the vendor card shows it as «αποσύρθηκε».
  - **Keeping a duplicate is harmless** — both rows produce the same code and both are per-item, so pieces on either price identically. `_catKey` is ambiguous across such twins (same `abbr|karat`), which only matters for `recalibrateCategoryItems` on a **rate** change; per-item rows have no rate, so it cannot fire here.
- **The second metal reaches the showroom (v12.29):** a piece can be made of two metals (`secondaryMetal`/`secondaryKarat`/`secondaryWeight` — e.g. RG0761, a 925 silver ring with a 14K gold face, 9.5 g + 1.3 g). The app card has always shown it («925 + 14K», `Silver · 925 · 9.50g · Gold 14K · 1.30g`) but the three fields were **not in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`**, so the showroom read it as plain silver and half the piece went unmentioned to the customer.
  - Added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (they are the same kind of fact as the primary metal + weight, which are already public; no cost/vendor information — `secondaryMetalCost` is deliberately NOT included, and a projection check confirms nothing else leaks).
  - **`showroom.htm`**: shared `secondMetalLabel(item)` / `secondMetalWeight(item)` (empty string when there is no second metal or its weight is 0). The grid card gets a **second tag** beside the first («ΑΣΗΜΙ 925» + «ΧΡΥΣΟΣ 14K»); the lightbox detail and the compare view show **Metal: `Silver 925 + Gold 14K`** and **Weight: `9.5 g + 1.3 g`** — both metals in the same order, so the pairing is obvious without extra rows. Silver purity keeps its millesimal form (no `K`); gold keeps `K`.
  - **`item.htm`** appends ` + Gold · 14K · 1.3g` to its single combined metal row. While there, the **missing `K` on gold** was fixed (`_kSuffix`): that page had always printed `Gold · 18` where the showroom said `Gold 18K`.
  - **No migration needed** — `republishPublicShowroom()` diffs the whole feed against the projection and PUTs when they differ, so the next time the owner opens the Stock tab every existing piece gains the fields. Verified: the live public feed today has **0** items carrying `secondaryMetal`, and the real RG0761 is there without it.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: card tags and detail rows correct in EL and EN, plain pieces byte-identical (no stray `+`), a zero/absent second weight renders nothing, gold-primary + silver-secondary reads correctly, and Greek all-caps tonos still stripped (ΑΣΗΜΙ/ΧΡΥΣΟΣ/ΜΕΤΑΛΛΟ/ΒΑΡΟΣ).
  - **Left alone:** the showroom's metal FILTER still keys on the primary metal only, so this ring appears under Silver and not under Gold. Changing that would put mostly-silver pieces into Gold results — the owner's call.
- **Piece count per vendor category (v12.30):** the owner's follow-up to v12.28 — "so I never have to press delete to find out what's in a category". Shared **`catPieceCounts(vendor)`** returns in-stock counts index-aligned with the vendor's categories, deciding membership with **`getItemCat`** — the same resolver the delete flow and the pricing use, so the badge and the delete dialog can never disagree. It returns **null** (not zeros) when the inventory isn't loaded, so a caller can tell "none" from "don't know yet" and shows nothing rather than a confident 0.
  - **Vendor edit form:** a blue `.cat-count` badge before each row's ✕ (`_fillCatCounts`), rendered only for rows with a `dataset.origIdx` — a brand-new vendor's rows in the add-vendor modal are untouched, and a category with 0 pieces shows an invisible placeholder rather than a "0". `editVendor` fills it immediately when the cache is warm and otherwise loads the stock in the background and fills in when it lands (guarding against a blanked cache and against the form having been closed meanwhile). The badge is a **sibling** of the ✕, so `confirmDeleteLaborRow(this)`'s `btn.parentElement` is still the row, and `saveVendor` selects `.labor-row`/`.other-cat-row` explicitly so an extra child changes nothing.
  - **Vendor card:** the expanded code list gains `· N κομμάτια / N pieces` (correct singular), which is what tells two same-name rows apart at a glance.
  - Verified against the real backup: Kyklos reads 1 / 12 / 13 / 3 / 3 / 1 / 2 / 1 — matching the delete dialog exactly — Butterflyfish's "other" categories count too, no row overflows, EL + EN, and simulating the owner's post-cleanup state shows the emptied row with **no badge** and the receiving row with **4**.
  - The **"other"-type** row's ✕ was found to be `this.parentElement.remove()` — not the retire path — and was fixed in **v12.31** (below).
- **"Other"-type categories delete like every other category (v12.31):** the ✕ on an `other-cat-row` (×multiplier) called `this.parentElement.remove()`, so `saveVendor` found the slot empty and restored the stored category from `_origCats[_i]` — the very fallback that protects the numbering — and **the row came back after save**. Proven by driving the real functions: DOM-remove Butterflyfish's «Earrings», save, and both categories are still there, unchanged. No data was ever corrupted; the button simply did nothing.
  - Both row kinds now go through `confirmDeleteLaborRow`, which takes the name from `.labor-name` **or** the row's first text input. The **add-vendor MODAL**'s `addOtherCatRow` deliberately keeps the plain `.remove()` — that vendor does not exist yet, so nothing can reference its rows.
  - **Destinations are now restricted to the same kind** (`catIsOther(c) === catIsOther(_myCat)`): a ×multiplier category and a per-gram metal one price by different models, so moving a piece across that line is never right. Verified on a deliberately MIXED vendor — the metal row's list excludes the ×2.5 row and vice versa.
  - After a move the cache is **repainted rather than dropped** (`_fillCatCounts(vendor)` instead of `_inventoryCache = null`): it holds the very objects just mutated (and rolled back on a failed write), so it is already correct, and nulling it only forced a re-download and blanked the new v12.30 counts. Verified live: 24 + 16 → the retired row empties and the destination reads 40.
  - Verified end to end on the real backup: «Earrings» warns with its own 24 pieces, offers only «Κολιέ», moves all 24 (`catIdx 1`), retires the row, and after save the vendor reads `#1 Earrings [RETIRED]` / `#2 Κολιέ` — **both slots kept**, `buildCodeMap` byte-identical, `vendorType:'other'` preserved, and reopening the form keeps the retired row hidden. Deleting the **last** live category of its kind while it still holds pieces is refused with «Προσθέστε άλλη κατηγορία πρώτα» and leaves the row untouched.
- **Manufacturing wastage («φύρα») per vendor (v12.32):** a supplier does not necessarily sell the metal at spot. Καλλίνικος bills 14K gold at **spot +10%** to cover what is lost in casting/filing/polishing, so €66.90/g became €73.59/g and a 1.4 g pendant cost **€127.40** where the app said **€117** — the owner's report, reproduced exactly. Other vendors fold the same money into a higher labour rate instead, which is why this is a property of the **VENDOR**, never a global setting. New optional field **`vendor.wastage`** (percent), edited in the vendor form's metal section (`#v_wastage`, label «Φύρα Μετάλλου (%)»), shown on the vendor card, plus an optional **per-CATEGORY override `cat.wastage`**. Read through `vendorWastagePct(v)` → `catWastagePct(v, cat)` → **`wastageFactor(v, cat)`**; `catHasWastageOverride(cat)` says whether a row carries its own figure.
  - **Blank on a category = inherit the vendor's; an explicit `0` means "this category has none" even when the vendor does** — so `catWastagePct` tests `>= 0`, not truthiness, and `saveVendor` must **store a 0** rather than drop it. That is the whole point of the override (a supplier who charges wastage on cast pieces but not on chains).
  - **The override lives in the category-name overlay (`#catWastageWrap`), not in the labor row.** Measured: the row already carries 8 controls and ~330px of fixed columns, so at 375px the name input is **22px wide and the row already overflows** — verified identical on the pre-change version, i.e. pre-existing, and precisely why the name field opens an overlay to be edited at all. Another input there would have made it worse. The value is held on `row.dataset.wastage` until the vendor is saved, seeded by `editVendor`, read by `saveVendor`, and hidden for a per-item row. `_markCatWastageRow` marks an overriding row with an inset gold bar + title; the authoritative view is the vendor card, which shows a gold **`φN%`** badge **only on rows that override** (otherwise all 14 rows would parrot the vendor's figure).
  - **`#catNameOverlay` was re-anchored to the top** (`align-items:flex-start`, `max-height:92dvh`, `overflow-y:auto`) per the v11.53 rule: the name field autofocuses, so the keyboard is up on open, and the centred box put the new field and the OK button behind it. Measured at 375×812: buttons at y=314, inside a ~380px keyboard-up viewport (was 542). This was already true before the extra field; the field made it unmissable.
  - **It multiplies the METAL only** — verified against the real invoice: 10% on metal lands on €127.40 at the invoice-day spot (€67.27/g), while charging wastage on the labour too would give €129.78 and on the whole price €129.20. Labour is charged on the true weight.
  - **It never applies to a `peritem` category** (nor to other-type/coins/raw metal): there the owner types the supplier's invoice total, which *already contains* the wastage — applying it again would double-count and push a piece he deliberately priced at €300 to €309. `wastageFactor` returns 1 for those, so the `itemMargin` derivation in the label-save and `invEdit` paths needs no change and the target retail stays exactly honoured.
  - Applied at the four vendor-priced sites: `calculate()` (primary + secondary metal), `calcInvItemPrice()` (same), and `_bulkSoldCost()` for by-the-gram chains. **`metalValue` deliberately stays RAW** — it is the real worth of the gold in the piece, not what the supplier charged, so the Stock header's "Metal Value" total, the melt floor and the Stale-Stock report are unaffected.
  - **The Calc breakdown gets its own «Φύρα 10%» / "Wastage 10%" row** (i18n `rWastage`) between the metal and labour lines, and the metal row shows the **spot** figure, so the rows still add up to the subtotal and the whole thing reads straight against the supplier's invoice. `calcInvItemPrice` also returns `wastageAmt`.
  - **Setting it reprices existing stock**, so `saveVendor` detects a change (`wastageLines`, at either level) and routes it through the **existing rate-change prompt** — "price increase" vs "mistake fix". Comparison is on the **effective** figure per category, so raising the vendor 0→10% while a category holds its own 0% correctly reports no change for that category; and a category that merely inherits is **not listed** (the vendor line already covers it, and listing all 14 would bury the one row that actually differs). Setting it for the first time is nearly always a correction (the supplier was always charging it; the app just didn't know), and the fix path calls `recalibrateCategoryItems(idx, **null**)` — a new "whole vendor" mode that re-baselines `initialRetail` on every per-gram/flat piece so the gain % doesn't show a fake jump. That function now matches the vendor via `_itemVendor` (v11.14) instead of the raw index, consistent with `catPieceCounts`.
  - **`wastage` is only written when > 0**, so a vendor that charges metal at spot round-trips byte-identically. **Verified against the owner's real 1129-item backup: with no wastage configured, all 1129 items price to a byte-identical hash before vs after the change** (A/B against the pre-change file). Setting 10% on Καλλίνικος moves **101 in-stock pieces by 5.3–8.2%** (labour + margin dilute the 10%) and **zero** items of any other vendor and **zero** per-item rows. Secondary metal carries the same factor; a plain open+save of a vendor still adds no key and raises no prompt.
  - Per-category verification: all **8** inheritance cases pass (vendor-only, category-only, explicit 0 over a vendor 10, per-item and other-type forced to 0); pricing, the bulk-chain cost and the secondary metal all follow the category figure; the overlay round-trips set → save → reopen → clear, storing the 0 and removing the key on blank; the prompt names a category-only change precisely and stays silent on a plain re-save; EL + EN strings correct; console clean.
- **"Labor charged" filter — per gram / flat / per item (v12.33):** a Stock filter group (`invFilterRateType`, `_invFilters.rateTypes`) for the three ways a metal category charges labour, so the owner can isolate e.g. every per-item piece (the ones the v12.32 wastage deliberately does not touch). Shared helpers next to `catOptionLabel`: **`RATE_TYPES`**, **`rateTypeLabel(rt)`** (labelled `€/γρ.` · `€ Σταθερή` · `📦 Ανά τεμάχιο`, the same shorthand as the vendor form's rate-type toggle) and **`itemRateType(item)`**, which resolves through **`getItemCat`** — the same lookup that prices the piece, so the filter can never disagree with the price on the card.
  - Returns **null** for anything with no metal category (coins, raw metal, other-type `×multiplier`), which therefore drops out when any pill is selected. Verified against the real backup that this hides nothing legitimate: of 489 in-stock pieces with no rate type, **448 are coins, 40 other-type, 1 the raw-metal lot, and 0 are orphans** — no metal piece fails to resolve its category.
  - **Owner/manager only** (`can('see_cost')`), consistent with the gain and time-in-stock filters, and the group is **omitted unless at least two kinds are actually in stock**, so a shop using only per-gram never sees it. Uses the generic `pillGroup` + array-group click handler, so no new toggle logic.
  - Verified on the real 1129-item backup: the owner's stock is **469 per-gram / 101 per-item / 32 flat** in stock; selecting €/g returns exactly 469 pieces and per-item returns 96 rows in which **every** row is genuinely per-item; badge, summary chip and Clear filters all include it; employee sees nothing; EL + EN correct; v12.32 pricing hash unchanged.
- **The price bar belongs to the Calc tab (v12.34):** the owner switched language while on Desk → Repairs and the Calc's floating customer price bar appeared over the repair tickets. Cause: `setLang` re-runs `calculate()` whenever the result card is still `show` (so the breakdown re-renders in the new language), and `calculate()` — plus the Other-type path and the scanned `fixedRetail` path — set `customerPriceBar.style.display = 'block'` **unconditionally**, with no idea which tab was on screen. `showPanel` has always hidden the bar when leaving Calc, so the invariant existed; the three show-sites simply didn't respect it. All three now go through **`_showPriceBar()`**, which returns early unless `#panel-calc` is `.active`. Nothing is lost by skipping it — `showPanel('calc')` re-shows the bar on the way back (it keys off `lastCalcResult`). **The same class of bug fires without any language change** — a spot-price tick or the per-item self-heal (v11.73) can re-run `calculate()` while the owner is on another tab — so the fix is at the show-site, not in `setLang`.
  - **Two related leaks closed in the same pass:** (1) the **minimized restore pill** (`#cpbRestorePill`, `position:fixed`) was never hidden by `showPanel`, so a minimized price bar left its pill floating over every other tab; (2) returning to Calc then showed the **full bar AND the pill at once**, because `showPanel` re-showed the bar while `togglePriceBar`'s only notion of "minimized" was `bar.style.display === 'none'` — which `showPanel` itself sets on every tab change. A sticky **`_priceBarMinimized`** flag (mirroring `_invBarMinimized`, v11.07) is now the source of truth: `showPanel` shows the bar or the pill (never both, neither off-Calc), `_showPriceBar` won't pop a minimized bar back up, and `clearCalc` resets it.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: language toggle on Desk leaves both hidden; `_showPriceBar()` off-tab is a no-op and on Calc still shows the bar; minimize → tab away → return restores the pill, not the bar; `clearCalc` clears the flag and both elements. Console clean.
- **Reserved is DERIVED, not a flag you must never lose (v12.35) — the reservation-bug class closed:** the owner made a multi-item hold; two pieces showed 🔒 and the third (NK0044) sat in the reservation's item list while its Stock card read plain "IN STOCK" — so it was still on the public showroom and an employee could have sold a piece a customer was waiting for. His complaint was the right one: *"every time we fix something something else breaks and eventually I'll miss something."* The cause is structural, not a typo. **`item.reservationId` was a COPY of a fact that lives in the reservation record, and every consumer trusted the copy.** A copy can be lost four ways, and all four existed:
  - **The reconcile could destroy a live hold.** `_resReconcileReservedFlags` only ever CLEARED, off `getReservations()` — and an empty or out-of-date list is indistinguishable from "no hold exists". `_resLoaded` stays true for the whole session after the FIRST successful pull, `_refreshSharedLists` is throttled to 20 s and skipped while a dialog is open, and `pullReservationsFromCloud` returns silently on `!r.ok`. So a phone whose list was minutes old, rendering Stock against fresher inventory, would delete the flag **and PUT it to the cloud** — silently releasing someone's hold and republishing the piece.
  - **`_resMarkItems` never checked its writes.** `saveInventoryItem` returns `res.ok` (it never throws, so the `try/catch` caught nothing), and a missing cache entry was a bare `continue`. A hold that was never applied reported success — the v11.62 family again.
  - **Nothing ever repaired a lost flag**, in either direction.
  - **The Calc SOLD button had NO reservation guard at all** — the busiest path in the shop. The scan chip said "📦 In stock" for a held piece and `openSaleModal` → `_openSingleSaleModal` went straight to the sale. The Stock card has warned since v10.80; the counter never did.
  - **The fix: the LIST is the truth and the flag is only a cache of it.** New `_resHoldFor(id)` (walks the active reservations, honouring `itemIds` **and** `items[].invId`) and `_resActiveByItem()` (one Map per render, rebuilt in `openInvGroup` too since that sheet opens long after a render). Every decision that costs money asks those: `invMarkSold`, `invGiveaway`, `_cartReservedOk`, `openReserveDialog`'s already-held check, `invReserve`, and **`publicProjection(item, id)`** — so a piece whose flag was lost never reappears online. Display follows through `_isHeld(id, item)` (records first, flag as fallback, never for a sold piece), used by the card badge, the card + group-sheet lock icons, the grouped card and the 🔒 Reserved filter — so the badge is right **on the render**, without waiting for a repair write.
  - **`_resReconcileReservedFlags` is now bidirectional and refuses to guess.** It SETS a missing flag (always safe — over-protecting costs a stale badge, under-protecting costs the piece) and CLEARS only off a list it can prove is current: `_resLoadedAt` is stamped by `pullReservationsFromCloud` **only** (a local edit does not make the list current w.r.t. other devices), the reconcile pulls when the list is older than `RES_STALE_MS` (60 s), and if that pull fails it leaves every flag alone. Throttled to once per 15 s. A piece already `sold` is never re-held.
  - **`_resMarkItems` returns `{ok, failed}`**, rolls the cache back on a failed write, and reports a missing record instead of skipping it; `_resDialogSave` names the pieces it could not mark in a one-button `showConfirm` (new `hideCancel` option on `showConfirm` — a message to acknowledge, not a choice) and says Stock will repair them. **Deliberately no retry loop** — the `track-record-beats-one-incident` memory; the self-heal is the retry.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions** (40 assertions): the reported case (held, flag missing) reads as held, blocks the sale from Stock **and** from the Calc SOLD button naming `H-0011`, is hidden from the showroom, and is repaired to the right reservation on the next Stock render; a **stale list releases nothing** while a fresh list still releases a cancelled hold; a failed write returns `ok:false` with the cache rolled back; the scan chip reads «🔒 Κρατημένο — Maria» / "🔒 Reserved — Maria" in both languages while free and sold pieces are unchanged; and every free-piece path (Stock sell, Calc SOLD, gift, cart, nothing-scanned) behaves exactly as before. **Regression against the owner's real 20/07 backup — 1129 items, 4 reservations: 0 changes, 0 cloud writes, cache byte-identical**, with the 3 live holds still held and the completed hold not re-holding its sold piece. (The 1129 items were reconstructed by class: the decision depends only on status + flag + membership, and the three classes present — 1088 in-stock free, 38 sold, 3 held — were *measured* from the real file, not assumed.)
  - **Which of the four defects actually hit NK0044 was not determined** — that needs the live database, which this session had no read access to. The fix does not depend on knowing: the flag is now derived, so every one of the four is repaired rather than merely made less likely. NK0044's 🔒 comes back the first time the owner opens Stock online.
- **Sort by RESERVATION date, with the time (v12.36):** the owner reserved several pieces, filtered Stock by 🔒 Reserved, sorted "Newest first" and got what looked like a random order. Cause: `newest`/`oldest` sort by **`dateAdded`** — when a hold was made is a different fact, living on the reservation record, and a piece added in April can be reserved this afternoon. New sort pair **`res_new`/`res_old`** ("Ημ. κράτησης / Reserved date"), gated on the **Reserved** status filter exactly as `sold_new`/`sold_old` are gated on Sold (`reservedOnly` flag, mirroring `soldOnly`: hidden from `openInvSortSheet` unless the filter is on *or* it is the active sort, and reset to `newest` at boot if the restored sort has no filter to go with it — the v11.77 fix, generalised). The date comes from the hold's **`dateCreated`** (a full `Date.now()` ms stamp, so **the time is included** — the last piece reserved today sorts first), resolved through **`_isHeld(id, item)`** so it reads the reservation RECORDS, not the `item.reservationId` flag (v12.35: the list is the truth). Memoized per render (`_rd`, beside `_px`/`_gn`) so ~700 pieces don't each walk the reservations; a piece with no hold sorts **last in both directions**, never first. The comparator's destructuring changed from `([,a],[,b])` to `([ida,a],[idb,b])` — the id is needed to look a hold up.
  - **Date-added now catches the legacy pieces too:** `newest`/`oldest`/`stock_first`/`sold_first` went through `a.dateAdded||0`, so a record with no stored `dateAdded` sorted as date 0 — dumped to the bottom of the list. They now use the memoized **`_ad` → `_invItemDate(id, item)`**, which falls back to the ms timestamp inside the `inv_<ms>_…` id. Verified: a piece with `dateAdded` deleted lands at its id's date in both directions.
  - **`#invSortSheet` gained `max-height:92vh/92dvh` + `overflow-y:auto`** — it had neither, and the option count grows with the filters (14 base → 18 with both Sold and Reserved on). Measured at 375×620 with all 18 listed: scrollable, title reachable at the top, last row reachable at the bottom; without it the last row sat ~230px below the fold with no way to reach it (the v11.49/v11.54 latent-clipping pattern). Stays **bottom**-anchored — it is a list you only scroll, no typing (v11.53 rule).
  - Verified by driving `renderInvList` on a fixture where the reservation order is deliberately the OPPOSITE of the add order: `res_new` → the April piece reserved at 18:00 today first, then 09:00 today, then yesterday's; `res_old` exactly reversed; un-held pieces last in both; sheet gating correct with the filter on and off; boot fallback confirmed by reload; EL + EN labels; no clipping or horizontal overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **Desk lists are sortable (v12.37):** the owner's follow-up to v12.36 — "I don't have a date sorting at all in desk. Maybe I should have." Correct: all three Desk lists had ONE fixed order hard-coded in their render, and **not one of the three cards showed a date**, so there was nothing to sort by and nothing to read. Each segment now has its own `<select>` (`resSortSelect` / `repSortSelect` / `gcSortSelect`, `.desk-sort`), following the **Clients** pattern (`_clientSortChanged`/`_restoreClientSort`, v11.55) rather than the Stock bottom sheet — these lists are short and the select is already the established control for a list this size.
  - **Four modes, one comparator.** `newest` / `oldest` (by `dateCreated`), **`due_soon`** (the date it comes due) and `name_asc`. The mode keys are shared by all three lists so `_deskCmp(mode, a, b, due, name)` is written once, with per-segment accessors — reservations `holdUntil`/`clientName`, repairs `datePromised`/`clientName`, gift `expiresAt`/`buyerName` — and each segment labels `due_soon` in its own words («Λήγει η κράτηση πρώτα» / «Παράδοση: πιο κοντινή» / «Λήγει πρώτα»). `due_soon` is the operationally useful one: what expires next, which job is due next. Name sorting passes the `el` locale so Greek collates properly.
  - **`_deskCmpDue` puts a MISSING date LAST.** These fields are `YYYY-MM-DD` strings and a plain `localeCompare` sorts `''` **first** — so a repair ticket with no promised date would have headed the list of what is due soonest, the exact opposite of the truth. `datePromised` is genuinely optional, so this is a live case, not a theoretical one.
  - **The status rank stays the PRIMARY key in every mode** (active/quote first, finished last). Finished work is hidden by default (v11.45) so the grouping is invisible day to day; when finished records ARE shown — or a search reaches them — today's active hold must not end up buried under last season's completed ones. It also makes every default byte-identical to the old fixed order: **verified by running the old and new comparators side by side on mixed fixtures — reservations, repairs and gift all produce the identical sequence.** `DESK_SORT_DEFAULT` is `newest`/`newest`/**`due_soon`** — gift's old order was expiry-soonest, so its default had to be that, not `newest`.
  - **One deliberate difference:** a gift card with **no `expiresAt`** moves from first to last among the actives. The app cannot create one (the issue form defaults a date, `_gcEditSave` requires one), and an undated card is not "expiring soonest" — so last is right.
  - **`_deskDateLine(created, due, dueWord, isOpen)`** adds a compact mono line to all three cards — `📅 24/07/2026 · ⏳ έως 30/09/2026` — shipped *with* the sort because an order you can change by date, on cards that show no date, just looks like the list jumped about. The ⏳ half is dropped once the record is finished (`_deskIsFinished`), since a collected ticket's ready-by date is history. All dates through `fmtDate`, wrapped in `new Date(...)` for the ms `dateCreated` (**`fmtDate` takes a string or a Date — a raw ms number throws**, since a `Number` has no `toLocaleDateString`).
  - Verified by driving the three real render functions: all 4 modes × 3 lists in the right order (including time-of-day within one day, and the no-due-date ticket last); the completed hold stays below the active one in all four modes even though it was created later, expires sooner *and* sorts earlier by name; the date line correct on all three cards and the ⏳ correctly absent on a finished one; a search still reaches a finished record; persistence across a reload per segment, a bogus saved value falling back, and a fresh device getting the old defaults; the select driven by a real `change` event and surviving a segment round-trip; EL + EN labels; no clipping or horizontal overflow at 375px; readable in both light and dark (it uses the theme variables); console clean.
- **A long badge label collapsed the code column (v12.38):** an inventory card printed its descriptive code **vertically, one character per line** (`P S I 9 2 5 K 0 1 4 4`). Cause: **every badge is `white-space:nowrap; flex-shrink:0` inside a `flex-shrink:0` column**, so a long label has nothing to stop it — «Silver & Gold Plated» made the badge column **137px**, the code column's `flex:1;min-width:0` collapsed to **21px**, and its `word-break:break-all` then broke the code at every character. **The owner had already asked for the fix when the gold-colour badge was introduced** ("make it two lines"): `_goldColorBadge` carried a bespoke `' &<br>'` split — and the v12.12 plating badge, which copied that badge's styling, **did not carry the split**. A per-label patch that the next label didn't inherit.
  - Shared **`badgeWrapLabel(lbl)`** (next to `goldColorLabel`) breaks after every separator — `,\s+` → `,<br>` and `\s+&\s+` → `' &<br>'` — so each part gets its own line. Applied at **all four** badge sites (single-card gold + plating, grouped-card gold + plating; the grouped card had never had the split for gold colour either). Verified over every multi-part label in `GOLD_COLORS` and `SILVER_PLATING` in both languages, with no stray leading/trailing `<br>`. One behaviour change: a 3-part label now breaks into 3 lines rather than 2 (`Gold, White & Rose Gold` → `Gold, / White & / Rose Gold`) — narrower, and consistent with the 2-part case.
  - **Structural cap so the class of bug cannot recur:** `.inv-badge-col { max-width:140px }` + `.inv-badge-col .inv-card-badge { white-space:normal }`, applied to the badge column in all three cards (single, grouped, metal-stock). The split keeps labels well under the cap; the cap is the net for whatever label is added next.
  - Measured at a 400px card, before → after: badge column **137 → 88px**, code column **21 → 70px**. **70px/2 lines is the ordinary baseline** — a plain piece with no plating badge measures 88px/2 lines — so the card is back to normal, not merely less broken. Worst case («Ασήμι, Επιχρυσωμένο & Ροζ», 3 lines, 112px) leaves the code 46px/3 lines, never the 11-line collapse. Row overflow **0** in every case; console clean.
- **Card quick actions in a 2×2 grid; top row aligned (v12.39):** the owner's follow-up to v12.38 — "put the lock and gift under the + and share, it gives the code more room and the space below is going to waste." He was right on both counts. The four 28px buttons in **one row cost 127px** of the card's top row, and the space beneath them (the top row is as tall as the 64px photo) was empty. New **`.inv-card-btns`** (`display:grid; grid-template-columns:repeat(2,28px)`) costs **61px** and fills the height the photo already occupies; a card with only two actions (sold, coin, group) simply fills one row. Applied to **both** the single and grouped cards — the group card's 2–3 actions (numista / share / lock) use the same class, per the [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]] rule.
  - **Also top-aligned the right-hand block** (`align-items:center` → `flex-start` on the photo+badge container in both cards), so the code, the button grid, the photo and the badge column all start on the same line — the owner's "make it completely aligned". Verified: all four elements report an identical `getBoundingClientRect().top`. It also makes the card shorter, since the top row no longer centres a short photo against a tall badge stack.
  - **Measured by A/B on the identical real card** (driving `renderInvList` with a fixture, toggling `.inv-card-btns{display:flex}` to restore the old single row) at a 400px viewport / 340px card inner: code column **22px → 88px**, **6 lines → 2**. Note this is *after* the v12.38 badge fix — that fix alone still left only 22px at this width, so the grid is doing the heavy lifting on a real phone. A typical piece (1–2 badges) now fits its code on **one line**; a heavily-badged piece (in-stock + handmade + colour + plating + gender) takes two; a 19-char stone code takes three. Row overflow **0** in every case; console clean.
- **Matching sets (v12.40):** the owner sells full matching SETS — a necklace, bracelet, ring and earrings that go together (and often the same set in several stones, e.g. amethyst + moonstone). A set is **not** a group: a group collapses IDENTICAL interchangeable pieces into one card, while a set links DIFFERENT pieces, each with its own ref code, weight and price, **sold separately**. So it is a separate, independent field — a piece can be in both a group and a set at once (the owner's grid: group the pendants across stones, group the earrings, then run stone-specific sets down). Design validated against Baymard's "buy the outfit" pattern + Pandora/Tiffany "Jewelry Sets" collections.
  - **Data:** `setId` + bilingual `setNameEl`/`setNameEn`, stored on each member (no central node, exactly like `groupId`). All three added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (carried by `publicProjection`'s field loop automatically). `setId` uses `'set_' + _stampId()` (globally unique across devices, v11.64). **Additive — no migration; existing pieces have no set fields and are untouched.**
  - **App (Stock):** create via select-mode **"🎀 Σετ / Set"** button (`invMakeSet`, owner/manager via `applyRoleUI` `#invSetBtn`) → an EL/EN name dialog (`_openSetNameDialog`, top-anchored per the typing-sheet rule); ≥2 pieces, non-bulk/coin/metal; reuses an existing `setId` in the selection so a piece folds into an existing set. A tappable **`🎀 Σετ` badge** on the single card (`_setBadge`) and grouped card (`_grpSet`, takes the first member's set since a group's stone variants can sit in different sets) opens `openInvSet(setId)` — a sheet listing in-stock members (thumbnail/ref/type/desc/price + Open/Edit/Remove), with owner **Rename** (`_invSetRename`), **Remove** (`_invSetRemove`, which releases the lone survivor too since a set of one is nothing) and **Disband** (`_invSetDisband`, clears all incl. sold). All writes are rollback-checked (v11.62). A **"🎀 Σετ" Status filter** pill (cross-cutting flag like grouped/reserved: `renderInvList` requires `item.setId`, excluded from `_realStatus`); set names are in the search haystack. Set-bar row 2 is now 5 buttons (Share/Group/Set/Reserve/Sell), gap tightened to fit; role-hiding keeps flex reflow.
  - **Showroom (`showroom.htm`):** each set member shows individually (findable under its own type/metal) with a tappable **«🎀 [set name]» `.set-tag`** that opens the **set carousel** — the existing lightbox extended with ‹ › arrows (`lbSetIds`/`lbSetIdx`/`lbSetId`, `enterSetMode`/`lbSetStep`), a "Piece 2/4 · name" banner, and a "View the whole set" button on any set member in normal view. `_setMembers(setId)` dedups by the visual key so grouped twins show once. A **"🎀 Sets" filter** (`setsOnly`, mirrors the `handmade` toggle end-to-end: `getFilterOptions.hasSets`, drawer pill `toggleDraftSets`, `applyFilters`, count + summary + clear + commit + nav reset). **Group cards now also split by STONE** (`_stoneSig` added to the grid collapse key, the nav facet count and the lightbox in-stock count) so an amethyst vs moonstone piece of one design shows as two cards — each cleanly part of its own set.
  - **Pairing pieces that live inside GROUPS (v12.41):** the owner groups by stone (both pendants together, both earrings together), so to build a *stone-specific* set (amethyst pendant + amethyst earrings) he must reach individual members of two different collapsed group cards — which select-mode can't do (a group card's checkbox selects all members). New **set picker** reachable from a **"🎀 Σετ" button on each piece row in the group detail sheet** (`openInvGroup`, owner/manager, non-coin/metal): `openSetPicker(itemId)` lists existing sets (`_allSets()`, deduped, with counts) + "➕ New set", and shows/removes the piece's current set. `_setPickExisting` (copies the target set's name), `_setPickNew` (`_openSetNameDialog` → mints), `_setDetach` (releases the lone survivor too) — all rollback-checked, then `renderInvList()` + `openInvSet()` for feedback. The group row's `sub` now also shows the **stone** (`stonesDescDisplay`) so amethyst vs moonstone rows are distinguishable. Flow: pendant group → amethyst pendant → 🎀 → New set; earrings group → amethyst earrings → 🎀 → pick that set. Ungrouped pieces still use select-mode Make Set. Picker at z 99997 (between the group sheet 99996 and the name dialog 99998).
  - **Set photo + set-box showroom view + word tag (v12.42):** owner feedback on the first live use. (1) **Set photo** — a per-set photo of all the pieces together (necklace + earrings in one shot), stored as `setPhotoUrl`/`setPhotoUrlHi` on each member (added to `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`), set via a **📷 Set photo** row in `openInvSet` → `openSetPhotoDialog` (reuses `renderPhotoPicker` + `uploadInventoryPhoto`, writes to all members, rollback-checked) / `_setPhotoRemove`. If none, the showroom falls back to the **first piece added** (earliest `dateAdded`). (2) **Sets-filter shows ONE box per set** — `renderGrid` gets an early `activeFilters.setsOnly` branch that folds `filtered` by `setId` into set boxes (set photo or first piece; set name; the piece **types** it holds — "Necklace · Earrings"; a "N pieces" count), each opening the carousel via `enterSetMode`. Individual pieces are collapsed away in that view (normal browse still shows them individually with the tag). New `.set-card`/`.card-set-pieces`/`.card-set-count` CSS. (3) **No bow in the showroom** — every customer-facing 🎀 removed: the card tag now reads **"Set [name]"** / «Σετ [name]» (word, not icon), and the lightbox banner/button, the filter summary and the drawer pill all use words. App-side badges keep the 🎀.
  - **Multi-set group chooser + carousel order + member strip (v12.43):** three owner-reported follow-ups. (1) **A grouped card's Set badge could only reach ONE set** — the group holds amethyst pieces (in set A) and moonstone pieces (in set B), but `_grpSet` opened the first member's set. It now calls `_openGroupSets(rep.groupId)`: distinct setIds among in-stock members → `openInvSet` directly if one, else `openSetChooser` (a small overlay listing each set by name + count). (2) **Carousel started at 2/2** — `_setMembers` returned `allItems` order, so the earliest piece wasn't index 0. It's now sorted by `dateAdded`, `enterSetMode` defaults to index 0 when no focus key, and the **set box passes no focus key** (`enterSetMode(setId)`) so it opens at piece 1. (3) **Member strip** — the carousel now shows a horizontal `.lb-set-strip` of every piece (thumbnail + type, current one ringed) → `lbSetJump(i)` opens that piece, so a customer can view the necklace/earrings individually. **Escaping lesson:** a Greek/English ternary had `'group\\'s'` — the `\\'` is an escaped backslash + a string-closing quote, which broke the ENTIRE script (all functions undefined, and a SyntaxError that didn't surface in the console capture); rephrased to avoid the apostrophe. Always avoid apostrophes in single-quoted strings inside a template literal, or the whole `<script>` can silently fail to parse.
  - **Verified end-to-end in a browser** driving the real functions: stone-split gives separate cards, `set_A` resolves to the amethyst necklace + amethyst earrings (not the moonstone ones), the Sets filter narrows to set pieces, the carousel counter/banner/wrap-around work, the nav hides on a normal open and the "View the set" button shows only on set members; app-side create/sheet/rename/disband/name-dialog/photo-dialog all build and close cleanly; the group-picker pairing flow reproduces the owner's exact case (amethyst pendant + amethyst earrings paired, moonstone untouched, name carried, detach cleans up the lone survivor); the multi-set group opens a chooser listing both sets while a single-set group opens directly; the set box opens the carousel at "piece 1/2" (earliest piece) with a strip listing Necklace + Earrings and `lbSetJump` moving both banner and strip; the sets-filter view renders one box per set ("Set / name / Necklace · Earrings / N pieces"), the moonstone set using its own set photo while the amethyst set falls back to its first-added piece, the tag reads "Set Limpet Shell" with zero bows in the grid. Clean console on both files.
  - **Sets in Browse nav + add-set-to-cart + "inquire about the set" (v12.44):** three follow-ups (owner picked all). (1) **Showroom "Sets" in the Browse menu** — `renderNavMenuTypeLevel` prepends a **"Σετ / Sets (N)"** entry (N = distinct in-stock sets) that reuses the existing two-level nav: `navMenuSelectType('__sets__')` → `renderNavMenuFacetLevel` (special-cased: title "Sets", base = `allItems.filter(i=>i.setId)`, so the metal/gender facets are computed over set pieces) → Apply → `navApplyFacets('__sets__')` sets `setsOnly:true` + clears `types`. `_navFacetResultCount('__sets__')` returns the count of **distinct sets** and the Apply button reads "Show N Sets". (2) **App "Add set to cart"** — `invAddSetToCart(setId)` (all roles) pushes every in-stock member into the existing **quote cart** as `{name, result:{finalPrice, retail, metalLabel, weight, photoUrl…}, invId:id}` (pinned by record id per the [[cart-lines-own-a-record]] rule; de-duped against what's already in the cart), then shows `#cartPanel`. **No new sell/reserve/remove machinery** — the cart's existing Sell-all / Reserve / per-line ✕ do it all, which is why this is one button. Button + a one-line hint sit at the top of `openInvSet`. (3) **Showroom "Inquire about the whole set"** — `selectWholeSet()` (button in the set carousel, `.lb-set-inquire`) adds every `lbSetIds` key to the `selected` set, updates the selection bar and closes the lightbox, so the customer lands on the standard "N selected · Send Inquiry" bar. Verified in a browser: the nav Sets entry → facet level → 2 boxes; the inquire button selects both pieces + closes + shows the sel bar; the cart gets both members with pinned ids and correct prices, opens the panel, and re-adding doesn't duplicate. Clean console on both files.
- **Other-type sales lost all their profit — `calcInvItemPrice` had no `baseCost` for them (v12.45) — a real financial corruption, found in production:** the owner sold a 3-item cash cart (NK0044 «Love Othos» necklace + PE0550 + NK0719) and the combined profit read **€168.38** when NK0044 alone should have been ~€200. Traced to the stored records: NK0044 (`qrCode: BUTK-2618-150`, an **other-type** Butterflyfish piece) had **no `cost` and no `profit` at all** — in Firebase, `cost:null`/`profit:null` drop the keys — so it contributed **€0** profit to the group total (168.38 = 0 + 122.38 + 46, exactly). **Root cause, singular:** `calcInvItemPrice(item)` returns `{retail, metalValue, cur, isFixed}` for an other-type item with **NO `baseCost` field**, because an other-type piece keeps its cost in the QR (`gross × vendor-discount`, via `_invOtherCost`), not in `purchaseCost`. Every sale/reservation/giveaway/set path that derives cost as `calcInvItemPrice(item).baseCost` therefore recorded **null cost** for other-type pieces — and NK0044 was the **first other-type item ever sold** (Butterflyfish is the only "other" vendor), so this was the first time it bit. Correct cost = 150 × (1 − 20%) = **€120**, profit = 320 − 120 = **€200**, correct sale-total profit = **€368.38**. A backup audit found **one more** older victim (a 18/06 single €75 other-type sale, cost €24 lost). All 30 other null-cost records are legitimate **manual sales** (owner typed a revenue figure, no linked piece) and are untouched. **The whole-app arithmetic was otherwise clean** — every record with a cost had `profit == exVat − cost` (0 mismatches across 96 records), so no record carried a *wrong* cost, only missing ones. **Fixes:** (1) **source** — `calcInvItemPrice`'s other-type branch now returns `baseCost: _invOtherCost(item)`, fixing the whole class at once (sales, reservations `_resItemFromResult`/`_resItemFromInv`, sets `invAddSetToCart`, giveaways `_invGiveawayCost`, totals, `_saleLinkFixedValues`); (2) **single source of truth** — new **`_invSaleCost(item)`** (other → `_invOtherCost`; else `purchaseCost>0 ? purchaseCost : calcInvItemPrice.baseCost`; null when underivable — mirrors the CSV export's proven rule), and `confirmSale`'s first-record path AND the rest-of-cart loop both route through it (removing the drift-prone duplicated cost logic — the first record used to lean on stale `lastCalcResult.baseCost`, which belongs to whatever was last in the calc, not the cart item); (3) **safety net** — the permanent Settings → **"Sale Link Check"** tool (`scanSaleLinks`) gained a third finding kind **`missingcost`** (`_saleMissingCostRecords`): an item-linked normal sale (amount > 0, not scrap/repair/giveaway/return/bulk) whose stored cost is null but whose piece has a derivable cost — one-tap `fixSaleLink` records the correct cost + profit (rollback-checked, v11.62; the sale AMOUNT never changes). **The owner must run Sale Link Check once after this deploys** to repair NK0044 (€120/€200) and the 18/06 straggler (€24/€40.10). Verified in the live page: `calcInvItemPrice(NK0044).baseCost === 120`, `_invSaleCost === 120`, per-item purchaseCost path === 35, detector flags exactly the 2 victims and 0 of the 30 manual sales, no console/syntax errors.
- **Printed tag — the ref code sits two dots lower (v12.46 → v12.47):** the owner photographed a `PE0805` tag whose first character was losing a sliver to the label's die-cut edge, and asked for the text to move down by the smallest possible step, with the QR left exactly where it is. New constant **`TAG_TEXT_NUDGE`** (next to `TAG_QR_Y`) is added to `buildTSPL`'s `topMargin`, so the TEXT lines print N printer dots lower (**1 dot = 0.125 mm at 203 dpi** — the smallest step the TTP-244 can make). Shipped at 1 in v12.46; the owner's test print still caught the edge, so it is **2** from v12.47 (`TEXT 228,43`, was 41). **The QR is untouched** — it is positioned by `TAG_QR_Y` (27) alone, and `printLabel`'s job header still mirrors that constant, so the Python print helper cannot drift from it. To nudge further, change this one number rather than editing the margin expression. Verified by driving the real `buildTSPL`: ref tag → `TEXT 228,43` with `QRCODE 395,27` unchanged; a legacy descriptive tag moves the same amount; `qrOnly` mode still emits no text and the same QR. Multi-line legacy tags stay inside the label (3 lines → 29/43/57, bottom edge 69 of 96).
- **Stone colour families — one grouping system for every stone list (v12.48):** the shop kept hitting the edges of a flat 42-stone list — **Mother of Pearl was missing entirely**, and stones that come in several well-known colours (Topaz, Quartz, Sapphire, Diamond, Tourmaline…) had only their base name, so a blue topaz and an imperial topaz were indistinguishable in the data. **42 varieties added (84 stones total)** and, more importantly, the *pearl-only* subcategory UI was generalised into a **family system used by every stone list in the app and the showroom** — the [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]] rule: the next family costs one table row, not new code in six places.
  - **`STONE_FAMILIES`** (parent → varieties) is the single source of truth, duplicated identically in `jewelprice.htm` and `showroom.htm`. **`STONE_TYPES` is now DERIVED** from `STONE_ORDER` + the families (`flatMap`), so the flat list every other consumer reads can never fall behind a family edit — plus `STONE_FAMILY_OF` / `stoneFamilyOf` / `stoneFamilyMembers`, `STONE_FAMILY_LABELS` (the «… (all)» header, written out per family because Greek plurals and their agreement with «όλα/όλες/όλοι» cannot be derived), and **`STONE_TYPES_LONGEST`** — the longest-first order that EVERY "does this text start with a stone name?" matcher must use, or «Blue Topaz» is read as plain «Topaz». Two matchers (`_invEditParseStoredStones`, the legacy stonesDesc migrator) were iterating the raw array and are now on it.
  - **The 12 families:** Diamond (Black, Champagne, Yellow, Pink, Salt and Pepper), Sapphire (Pink, Yellow, White, Green, Padparadscha), Pearl (the 7 existing + **Mother of Pearl**), Topaz (Blue, London Blue, Swiss Blue, White, Imperial, Mystic), Quartz (Rose, Smoky, Rutilated, Lemon, Clear), Tourmaline (Pink, Green, Watermelon, Black, Paraiba), Garnet (Rhodolite, Tsavorite, Spessartite), Opal (White, Black, Fire, Boulder), Moonstone (Rainbow, Peach), Coral (Red, Pink, Black), Agate (Blue Lace, Moss), Spinel (Black). **Amethyst is deliberately left alone** — the owner's own call, it is purple and nothing else. Ruby/Emerald are single-colour by definition. **The Greek names were settled by looking at what Greek shops actually write, not by translating** (owner's rule: a real Greek term if one is in use, otherwise the English word Greek sites use). Greek where it is genuinely established: **«Αχάτης Βρυώδης»** (Moss Agate — handra.gr, metaphysicshop.gr), **«Χαλαζίας Καπνίας»** (Smoky Quartz — the fixed Greek term, handra.gr), **«Γρανάτης Σπεσσαρτίνη»** (the Greek mineral name is σπεσσαρτίνη, not σπεσσαρτίτης), **«Διαμάντι Σαμπανιζέ»** (monopetro.com — «σαμπανιζέ», never «σαμπάνια»), «Γρανάτης Ροδολίτης/Τσαβορίτης», «Φίλντισι» (Mother of Pearl — universal on Greek jewellers). English kept where Greek shops themselves keep it: **«Φεγγαρόλιθος Rainbow»** (kymajewelry.com writes «Φεγγαρόπετρα Rainbow»), **«Χαλαζίας Lemon»** (handra.gr lists it in English), London/Swiss Blue Topaz (kasparianjewels, lilalo, ochrablue, agora1866 all write it in English), **Salt & Pepper** (auraelume.gr), Blue Lace Agate (no Greek term exists), plus the proper/trade names Padparadscha, Paraiba, Imperial, Mystic, Boulder. Same rule `STONE_SHAPE_TRANSLATIONS` already follows. Note the base **«Τουρμαλίνης»** is kept masculine to match the app's existing term even though Greek stone shops write the feminine «Τουρμαλίνη» — changing it would move the display of existing stock.
  - **Fancy-colour diamonds are their own TYPE, not the `color` field** — that field is the D–M colourless grade, a different axis. `stoneToLabelPart` branches on `stone.type === 'Diamond'` exactly, so a Black Diamond takes the ordinary abbreviation path (`.30BLD`) instead of the clarity+colour form, and carat-less it still encodes (a carat-less *white* Diamond deliberately emits nothing).
  - **Every stone must be encodable, and no two may share a code.** 42 new 3-letter abbreviations (three, not four: the vendor prefix is already in every QR and a 4th letter costs space the stone suffix may need — v11.12). A load-time **`_assertStoneAbbrevsUnique()`** now guards the whole map: two codes for one stone, a stone with no code, or a **digit in a code** (which `parseCodeInput` would swallow into the weight) each log an error. Naming varieties *adjective-first* also means a base name is never a prefix of a variety, so prefix matching stays unambiguous.
  - **`COLORED_FULL` / `COLORED_FULL_EARLY` are now DERIVED** from `STONE_TYPES` (minus Diamond) instead of two hand-kept lists that had already fallen behind — a stone written out in full in a code is understood the moment it is added.
  - **The three divergent per-carat price tables became ONE** (`COLORED_PER_CT` + **`stonePerCaratGuide(type)`**, which falls back to the stone's FAMILY before the generic default). So «Blue Topaz» is priced as Topaz automatically, and only varieties genuinely worth different money carry their own row (fancy diamonds, Padparadscha, Paraiba, Tsavorite, Spessartite, Black/Fire Opal, Mother of Pearl). This is the ✨ Estimate button only — a guide the owner taps and can override; nothing stored moves.
  - **UI:** every stone `<select>` (calc stone row, label-creation chip picker, Edit Item stone row + other-type chip picker, "Add similar items" stone swap) now uses the shared **`stoneOptionsHtml(selected)`** with native **`<optgroup>`**s — no new JS, works on iOS.
