Both **filter sheets** (app `openInvFilterSheet`, showroom `renderDrawer`) render a generic collapsible family: the row body expands/collapses, the circle selects/clears the whole family, and everything is scoped by **`data-fam`** so families never affect each other (`inv-fs-fam-all`/`inv-fs-fam-sub` + `_invSyncFamHeaders`; `sw-fam-all`/`sw-fam-sub` + `toggleDraftFamilyAll`/`_swSyncFamilyHeaders`). A family with only ONE variety in stock renders as an ordinary row — collapsing one item is noise.
  - **Verified against the owner's real 25/07 backup (1286 items): all 754 distinct stored `qrCode`s and all 176 stored `stonesDesc` strings parse byte-identically before and after, and render byte-identically in both languages.** Nothing in the existing stock moved. Plus 501 synthetic round-trips (every stone × carat/no-carat × qty 1/N/melee) encode and parse back to the same stone, real diamond codes (`.30VS1H`, `8x.10SI2G`, `t.80SI2G`) still resolve, and both filter sheets were driven for real (expand, select-all, partial-untick header sync, clear, and the resulting item match — «Μαργαριτάρια (όλα)» returns exactly the pearl pieces including Mother of Pearl). The only intended difference: Agate/Onyx/Crystal and the pearl varieties now get real guide prices instead of the €160/ct generic default.
- **Add pieces to a set by scanning (v12.49):** the owner's own framing of the problem — "I have a set with a necklace and earrings, then someday I receive the ring and the bracelet, and I have to scroll the inventory to find the set." A set has **no code of its own and doesn't need one: any MEMBER's tag identifies it.** So the set sheet (`openInvSet`) gains **"➕ Προσθήκη κομματιών / Add pieces"** (`openSetAddPieces`, owner/manager) — open the set, scan the new piece's tag, repeat. The typed field beside it is **not an iPhone fallback**: it is how this is done on the laptop, and it covers a tag too scratched to scan.
  - **Why from the SET and not from the new piece:** the piece-side entry (the v12.41 `openSetPicker`) already exists and stays. Working from the set means several arrivals go in one after another without reopening anything, and it matches how the owner describes it out loud.
  - **Own camera + decode loop** (`_setAddStartCamera`/`_setAddDecodeLoop`, modelled on the stocktake scanner, 250 ms poll + 1300 ms cooldown), never the Calc scanner — the panel sits above the set sheet at z 99997 and must not disturb the counter's scan state. `canDecodeQr()` false (iOS/Safari) or a denied camera → the video is hidden and the typed field is focused, with the reason on screen.
  - **New shared resolver `resolveCodeToInvEntry(raw)`** — raw scanned/typed value → `{id, item, code, matches}`, by descriptive `qrCode` then printed ref code, preferring an in-stock piece, handling an `item.htm?qr=` URL. It returns the **id**, which `stkResolve` never did, because a caller that intends to WRITE needs the record id, not a code string ([[link-records-by-id-not-string]]); and it returns **every** match, because identical grouped pieces and coin batches share a code. It compares through **`_qrKey`** per the v11.78 rule (verified on the 25/07 backup: of 754 distinct stored codes only the 4 legacy `+`-form ones normalise and **none** collide). **`stkResolve` now delegates to it** — verified byte-equivalent on every probe except (a) a miss returning `null` instead of `undefined` (its only caller, `stkOnScan`, tests truthiness throughout) and (b) the intended `+`-code improvement.
  - **Every outcome is named, because a scan that silently does nothing looks like a broken scanner:** unknown code, sold piece, coin / raw metal / bulk chain (each says which), already in this set, or ✅ with the ref + type + description + colour + stones of what was added, plus a running "ADDED (N)" tray with a ✕ to undo a wrong pick on the spot.
  - **A piece already in ANOTHER set is confirmed before it moves**, naming both sets — and if the old set drops to one member that survivor is released, the same rule `_invSetRemove` follows (a set of one is nothing).
  - **Shared codes:** where several in-stock pieces carry the code and they genuinely differ (`_refVariantKey` — colour, plating, stones, weight), the panel **asks which one you are holding** with a photo + description per candidate, rather than guessing ([[one-code-one-piece]]). Where they are interchangeable twins it takes one that isn't already committed to another set, so scanning the same code twice adds the second twin instead of reporting "already in the set". Measured on the real backup: 25 in-stock ref codes are shared today and **0 of them have differing members**, so the chooser is the rare path, not the normal one.
  - Every write is **rollback-checked** ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]], v11.62): a failed `saveInventoryItem` restores the cached record and says the save failed, instead of leaving a piece on screen that isn't in the set.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions** on a fixture: unknown/sold/coin/already-in-set messages; add by ref code, by descriptive QR, by `?qr=` URL and by typed lowercase + Enter; the variant chooser (2 buttons, nothing pre-selected) and picking from it; interchangeable twins taking a different record on each scan and reporting "already in this set" on the third; the cross-set move confirming, moving, and releasing the lone survivor; a declined confirm writing nothing and resuming the camera; a failed write rolling back with the old set name intact; undo releasing the piece and clearing the row; Done stopping the camera and reopening the set with the new count; the button + hint present in the sheet. EL and EN, dark and light, top-anchored + scrollable per the [[sheets-with-typing-go-on-top]] rule, no overflow at 375px, console clean.
- **Edit a reservation (v12.50):** the owner made a hold with the wrong "hold until" date and had no way to correct it — the detail sheet only offered Print / Share / add-payment / Complete / Cancel, so fixing a typo meant cancelling the hold (releasing the pieces, losing the deposit history) and making it again. A **✏️ button next to the ✕** in `openReservationDetail` opens `openResEdit(id)`.
  - **What it edits:** hold-until date (the reported need, via the invisible-native-input-over-`fmtDate` pattern, never a bare visible `<input type="date">` — v11.42), customer name / phone / email, agreed price, the 💵 cash-or-receipt toggle (shared `_cashToggleHtml`/`_isCashBtn`), and a free-text **note** (the `notes` field has existed in the record shape since v10.80 and nothing ever wrote or showed it; the detail sheet now renders it under the email).
  - **The ITEMS are deliberately NOT editable here.** Changing them means moving reserved flags between pieces and re-deriving the agreed total; the honest way is Cancel + reserve again, and the form says so in one line.
  - **Who may edit:** `_resCanEdit(r)` = owner/manager (`can('desk_admin')`) **or the person who created the hold** — the same rule the sale detail uses for Edit/Return (v11.46). An employee who mistypes a date at the counter has to be able to fix it. A **finished** hold (`completed`/`cancelled`) is locked in every case: that is history.
  - **Guards:** a blank name is refused; the agreed price is refused if it would fall **below what the customer has already paid** (mirrors the gift-card "can't drop below redeemed" rule) — equality is allowed; a blank/junk price is refused. Every refusal writes to `#resEdStatus` and writes nothing.
  - **Reviving an expired hold** (`_resOfferRevive`) — the case that makes the date editable at all: a hold expires, the customer phones to say they are coming next week. `_resCheckExpiry` released the pieces back to stock and the showroom when it expired, so pushing the date forward must put them back on hold. Setting a **future** date on an `expired` record raises a confirm; accepting sets `status='active'`, clears `dateCompleted` and `_resMarkItems`-es the pieces. **Availability is worked out BEFORE the status flips** — `_resHoldFor` only looks at *active* reservations, so this one must not yet be one of them. A piece that is sold, gone, or now held by someone else is **named, never silently dropped**: it stays listed in the record (the history stays true) but is not re-flagged, and the owner is told to check Stock and adjust the agreed price. A **failed inventory write** joins the same warning list, so a hold is never reported as fully reactivated when it isn't ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]]). Declining leaves it `expired` with the new date saved (re-saving asks again).
  - Verified by driving the real functions with the cloud stubbed: the reported case (wrong date → corrected, items/payments/price untouched, 0 inventory writes); the full permission matrix incl. employee-is-creator; all four validation guards; a combined name+phone+email+price+cash edit; and six revive cases — clean revive (flag set, success toast), piece sold (0 writes, named), piece held by another hold (the other hold intact), a still-past date (no question asked), declining (stays expired), an **active** hold never asking, and a failed write reported instead of a false success. EL + EN, top-anchored + scrollable, no overflow at 375×812, console clean.
- **"Open" / "View →" landed on the Stock tab but not the piece (v12.51) — a fixed timer instead of awaiting the data, again:** the owner reported that the AI photo-match's **Open**, and the Calc chip's **View →**, sometimes took him to the Stock tab without jumping to the item — and that pressing it again worked. Reproduced by driving the real functions in a browser: **`_jumpToInvCard` fired on a blind `setTimeout(…, 250)`** while `showPanel('inventory')` → `renderInventory()` was still downloading the stock. At 250 ms there are no cards, `renderInvList()` draws the loading spinner and leaves `_invSortedRows` empty, `_invEnsureRendered` returns false, and the jump gives up **in silence**; the list appears a moment later, scrolled to the top. The second press works because the cache is now warm — the exact signature the owner described. Measured with a 900 ms load: card found `false`, scrollTop 0. Same family as [[await-data-before-pricing]] (v11.73) — **a timer is not a substitute for awaiting the data**.
  - **Fix:** `renderInventory()` is now a thin wrapper that stores its promise in **`_invReady`** (body moved to `_renderInventoryNow`), and `_jumpToInvCard` is `async` and **awaits it** before focusing. Verified at 900 ms and 3 s loads: the card lands centred on the first press.
  - **The widening was wrong twice.** (1) It cleared `_invFilters.status` on **every** failed jump — so a jump that failed because the list had not loaded silently wiped the owner's filter for nothing (confirmed: `status` went `['in_stock'] → []`). It now widens **only** when the piece is genuinely in `_inventoryCache`, and otherwise says so («Δεν φορτώθηκε το απόθεμα — δοκιμάστε ξανά»). (2) It set `status = []` to reveal a sold piece — but **an empty status filter is not "show everything"**: `renderInvList` hides sold items by default when no in_stock/sold pill is active, so a sold piece was *still* not found. Both widening steps now set `['in_stock','sold']` explicitly. A last resort clears the search box + `clearInvFilters()` (also `['in_stock','sold']`) when a search term or another filter is what is hiding it. The filter sheet rebuilds its pills from `_invFilters` on open, so the pills stay in step.
  - **A background redraw could steal a landed jump.** `renderInventory()` refreshes from the cloud behind the list and redraws when anything changed (`_autoDescribe` writes make that common), rebuilding the DOM and resetting the scroll. The jump is now held as `_invJumpTarget` for `INV_JUMP_HOLD_MS` (6 s) and **re-applied** after such a redraw (`_invReapplyJump`, `quiet` — never widens filters, never toasts).
  - **A failed background refresh wiped the whole in-memory stock.** `loadInventory()` returns `{}` on ANY failure, and the background branch treated `{}` as valid fresh data → `_inventoryCache = {}` → "no items in stock" from a passing network blip. Now guarded by `_invLoadFailed`, exactly as `_invEnsureForScan` has been since v11.74 — **that fix never reached this path** (verified: cache 300 → 0 before, 300 → 300 after).
  - **`scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'})` is an animation and can simply be dropped** (reduced-motion, a page not being painted — it was a no-op in the test browser while the instant form worked). A 450 ms belt re-issues an instant `scrollIntoView` if nothing moved and the card is still off screen.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a 300-piece fixture: cold + 900 ms load, cold + 3 s load, warm cache, a **sold** piece, a piece hidden by a search term + metal filter, a piece not in the data (filters untouched + honest message), a **failed** refresh, a redraw landing under the jump, and a **grouped member** (whose card is the group representative's) — all land the card visible in the panel, console clean. Fixes every caller at once: the Calc chip, the AI photo-match results, the Stale Stock report and the set sheet.
- **Printed tag — the whole print moved 7 dots lower, the ref code 6 dots right (v12.52 + v12.53 + v12.58 + v12.59):** the tag printer was dropped and the label roll re-inserted, after which it fed high (owner's photo of an `NK0853` tag, then an `R0878` one). Both vertical constants moved down together, a few dots at a time with a test print in between: **`TAG_QR_Y` 27 → 29 → 31 → 34** and **`TAG_TEXT_NUDGE` 2 → 4 → 6 → 9** (1 dot = 0.125 mm at 203 dpi, the smallest step the TTP-244 can make). The ref code and the QR shift by the same amount, so their positions relative to each other never change. **The QR's X is never touched** (`QRCODE 395` — the owner's explicit constraint); the TEXT's X is its own constant **`TAG_TEXT_X`** (228 → 231 in v12.58 → 234 in v12.59), so a horizontal nudge of the printed code cannot move the QR — v12.59 was exactly that request ("QR stays where it is, code 3 more steps right") and the QRCODE line came out byte-identical. Verified each time by driving the real `buildTSPL`: now `TEXT 234,50` + `QRCODE 395,34`; `qrOnly` emits the same moved QR and no text; a legacy 3-line descriptive tag moves the same amount (`234`, 36/50/64, bottom edge 76 of 96) and the QR's bottom edge is 76 of 96 — still inside the label, with ~7 dots of headroom for a further nudge. `printLabel`'s job header still mirrors `TAG_QR_Y`, so the Python print helper cannot drift from it. **To nudge vertically again, change ONLY the two Y numbers, by the same amount; to move the code left/right, change ONLY `TAG_TEXT_X`.**
- **Handmade is saved at creation and healed everywhere (v12.54):** the owner found a handmade-category ring (RG0861) showing no «Χειροποίητο» tag in the showroom. Two related defects, both the [[derived-not-copied-flags]] pattern: the app's ✋ badge is DERIVED live (`item.handmade || getItemCat(item)?.handmade`) while the showroom reads only the SAVED field. (1) **Creation saved the wrong value** — the label modal's ✋ toggle was synced from `calcHandmadeActive` only, and the scan-to-inventory builder read `calcHandmadeActive || false`, so a piece priced with a handmade *category* (without pressing the calc ✋) was born `handmade:false`. Both now OR in the selected/matched category's `handmade` — the same rule `calculate()` has always used for `lastCalcResult.handmade`. (2) **The self-heal never reached grouped pieces** — the persist-and-resync heal lived only in `buildCard`; `buildGroupCard` derived the badge but wrote nothing, so a grouped piece stayed wrong in the public feed forever. The group card now heals each in-stock member exactly like the single card (verified by driving `renderInvList` on a fixture: both stuck members written `handmade:true`, the sold member untouched, badge intact, idempotent on re-render). **Damage audit against the 25/07 backup + live public feed:** 338 category-handmade items were already correct (the single-card heal working), RG0861 self-healed before the fix, and exactly 2 in-stock pieces were stuck — PE0553/PE0618, the two grouped «Αρχαίος Έλληνας πολεμιστής» pendants — which repair themselves on the next Stock render under this fix. 2 sold items remain `false` (harmless: sold pieces never publish; the heal deliberately skips sold, matching the single card). **(v12.55) The card-render heals were not enough on their own** — a heal only fires when the card is actually drawn, and with ~200 newer items above it the pendants' group card sat several scroll-pages deep, unrendered (PE0553 had healed only because a search once rendered it as a single card). The real fix is the [[derived-not-copied-flags]] rule applied at the source: **`publicProjection` now derives `out.handmade = !!(item.handmade || getItemCat(item)?.handmade)`**, so the once-per-session `republishPublicShowroom()` diff repairs the ENTIRE feed in one pass on the first Stock open — no card render needed. Verified by driving the real `publicProjection`: stored-false + handmade category → true; plain category stays false; explicit true kept; sold still null; a ghost vendor falls back without crashing. The v12.54 creation-path and heal fixes are kept (they keep the stored field right for exports/audits), but the showroom no longer depends on them.
- **Promo image names the metal (v12.57):** the Instagram/Facebook promo (`drawPromoCanvas`) showed type, name and stone names but never said what the piece is MADE of — the owner asked for the metal, and for the handmade mark. Built first as boxed chips mirroring the showroom card (`SILVER 925` · `GOLD 14K` · **`HANDMADE`**) and **rejected by the owner on sight: framed pills read as UI, and this image goes on Instagram.** Shipped instead as a second quiet text line under the stones, in exactly the stones' own treatment (letterspaced DM Mono small caps, `P.sub`, ` · ` separators) — `PERIDOT · DIAMOND` / `SILVER 925 · GOLD 14K`. **Handmade was dropped entirely** at his request (the footer already says «Handcrafted with passion», and the promo is not a spec sheet). `_promoMetals(item)` returns the parts: `metalWithPlating(item, false)` (so a plated piece reads `GOLD PLATED SILVER 925`) plus the **second metal** when `secondaryWeight > 0`, using the same rule as `showroom.htm`'s `secondMetalLabel`; an **other**-type piece shows `otherMaterialLabel` instead. **English only**, like the rest of the promo image (it prefers `descEn` and targets social media — a half-translated image reads worse than an English one). Layout: with a metal line the photo card gives up 40px (story) / 46px (post) and the text block moves up, so the **footer stays byte-identical**; with no metal to name the ORIGINAL metrics are used unchanged, so a metal-less piece renders exactly as before with no empty gap.
  - **Pre-existing overflow bug found and fixed in the same pass:** the stones line shrank its FONT when too long but left the letterspacing at a fixed 12px — and on a long line the spacing is most of the width. Measured: a 5-stone piece (`PEARL · DIAMOND · SAPPHIRE · EMERALD · RUBY`) painted **1020px on a 1080px canvas**, and the worst metal phrase painted 1014px — both running off the edges (reproduced visually, then measured). New shared **`_promoFitMono(ctx, txt, maxW, fs, ls, minFs)`** shrinks size AND spacing together and measures the REAL painted width (setting `ctx.letterSpacing` before `measureText` and subtracting the trailing spacing canvas adds after the last glyph), and **both** lines now go through it. Verified: the two worst cases land at 847px and 852px against the 880px limit, while every ordinary line still resolves to the original 30px/12 — so existing promos are unchanged. [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]].
- **Edit a repair / custom-order ticket (v12.57):** the owner put the wrong ready-by date on a repair ticket (R-0006) and, with no way to correct it, printed the claim form and fixed it **by hand** — the same gap `openResEdit` closed for reservations in v12.50. A **✏️ button next to the ✕** in `openRepairDetail` opens `openRepairEdit(id)`.
  - **What it edits:** ready-by date (the reported need, via the invisible-native-input-over-`fmtDate` pattern — never a bare visible `<input type="date">`, v11.42), customer name / phone / email, **photo** (`renderPhotoPicker` with `existingUrl`), description, estimate low/high, a free-text **note**, plus the two money fields that belong to the ticket's current shape: a **paid deposit** (amount + method, only rendered when `deposit > 0`) and a **quote's `depositAsked`** (only while `status === 'quote'`). `r.notes` had existed in the record shape since v10.89 with nothing writing or showing it — exactly like `notes` on a reservation before v12.50 — so the detail sheet now renders it too.
  - **The TYPE and the STATUS are deliberately NOT editable**, and the form says so in one line. The status is moved by the buttons that also do the work behind it (Mark ready, Deposit → order, Collect, Cancel), and changing repair↔custom mid-ticket would rewrite what the customer already signed for. A **paid deposit can be corrected but not removed** — handing money back is a cancellation, not a typo fix — so a blank/0 there is refused with that sentence rather than silently nulling it.
  - **Who may edit:** `_repCanEdit(r)` = owner/manager (`can('desk_admin')`) **or the person who opened the ticket** (`createdBy`), the same rule `_resCanEdit` and the sale detail use; a **finished** ticket (`_deskIsFinished('repairs', r)` — collected/cancelled) is locked for everyone, because that is history. Guarded so a blank name on both sides cannot unlock a ticket.
  - **A repair keeps its required estimate; a custom order does not** — the same asymmetry `_repairSave`/`_repairSetType` already encode (a repair is priced with the item in your hand; a custom order is often quoted later). The existing quote-specific form (`_repairQuoteOpen`) is untouched and still the workflow step for "fill in the price, then send it".
  - **A failed photo upload never wipes the photo already on the ticket** ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]], the v11.62 lesson): the new URL is only stamped on when the upload genuinely returns one, and the toast says «✓ Αποθηκεύτηκε — η φωτογραφία δεν ανέβηκε» instead of reporting a clean save. Own pending vars (`_repEdPendingPhoto`) so the create modal's cannot leak in. `upsertClientFromSale` runs on save, so a corrected number reaches the Clients book.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a fixture of the owner's actual R-0006: the reported case (date 05/08 → 12/08 + a note) saves with `status`/`type`/`ticketNo`/`dateCreated`/photo untouched and the detail reopening with the new date and note; blank name, missing estimate on a repair and a removed paid deposit each refuse with the form still open and **0** cloud pushes; a custom order saves with no estimate; a quote's `depositAsked` round-trips; a collected/cancelled ticket shows no pencil and refuses `openRepairEdit`; the full permission matrix (owner / creator-employee / other-employee / no-name / both-blank / creator-but-collected); all four photo outcomes (fail → old kept + honest toast, throw → same, success → replaced + pending cleared, untouched → **0** upload calls). EL + EN, top-anchored + scrollable, nothing clipped at 375×812, console clean. *(Testing note: `creds` and `lang` are top-level **lexical** bindings, not `window.creds`/`window.lang` — assigning the window property leaves the app reading its own value and produces convincing false results, the same trap as `_inventoryCache` in v11.81. Also `innerText` returns CSS-**uppercased** label text, so a case-sensitive string probe reads as a false negative.)*
- **Showroom price bands (v12.60):** the showroom shows no prices and must not — a customer who can read an exact figure off a public page can price the whole stock. But "roughly what does this cost?" is the first question anyone asks, so each published piece now carries a coarse **`priceBand`** and the filter drawer gains a **Price** group of fixed pills: `€0–200 · €201–400 · €401–600 · €601–800 · €801–1000 · €1001–1500 · €1501–2000 · €2001–3000 · €3001–4000 · €4001–5000 · €5001+`. **Fixed buckets on purpose, at the owner's instruction — never a slider and never a min/max box**, because either lets a viewer narrow the window until it names the price; a bucket can only ever say "somewhere in this €200 range".
  - **The published value is the band's own LABEL (`'201-400'`), not an index.** `PRICE_BANDS` is duplicated in `jewelprice.htm` and `showroom.htm` (the established pattern for `SILVER_PLATING`/`ITEM_COLORS`/`STONE_FAMILIES`), and an **index would silently mislabel every piece if the two copies ever drifted**, while a self-describing key can only fail to match. `priceBandLabel` builds the display text from the band's own bounds, so a label can never disagree with the key it came from; an unknown key (a feed written by a newer app) is shown as-is at the end of the list rather than dropped.
  - **Derived at publish time in `publicProjection`, exactly like `handmade` (v12.55)** — from `calcInvItemPrice(item).retail` put through **`autoRoundPrice`**, so a piece can never land in a band the owner would not quote it at. It follows the gold price by itself: the once-per-session `republishPublicShowroom()` diff re-bands the whole feed when a piece crosses a boundary. **Nothing is stored on the item and `priceBand` is NOT in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS`** (it is computed, not copied).
  - **Omitted, never guessed, when the price cannot be computed** — no spot price loaded yet, or an other-type piece with no `fixedRetail` (`calcInvItemPrice` returns null in both cases). Such a piece still publishes, it just doesn't match any price filter, and `applyFilters` drops a band-less piece while a price filter is on: we cannot claim it falls in a range we never worked out.
  - The group renders only when the stock spans **more than one** band (same rule as platings/materials), so it is simply absent on a feed with no bands — which is what today's live feed looks like until the owner opens the Stock tab once.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions against the owner's real 25/07 backup (1286 items, 14 vendors, VAT 17, round step €5/floor €20):** all **770** published pieces get a band, **0** land outside their band's bounds, **0** expose a price figure anywhere in the projection, and the published object gains exactly one new key. No-spot and no-`fixedRetail` cases both omit the band and still publish. Showroom side driven end to end with that feed: options in table order, one band → 87/87, two bands → 288/288, price + gold → all match, a band-less piece excluded under a price filter and shown without one, badge/summary/count/Clear-All correct, real pill clicks + Apply + reopen-preselected + untick, EL («Τιμή», «278 τεμ.») and EN, 11 pills with no overflow at 375px, and the live band-less feed correctly renders no Price group. Console clean on all three files.
- **Tapping a phone number offers all four channels (v12.61):** a client's phone number went straight to the dialer (`_callPhone`), while a Desk repair ticket has offered WhatsApp / SMS / Email since v10.89 — so the owner could call a client from the Clients tab but not message one. Shared **`openContactSheet(phone, email, name)`** now opens a small top-anchored sheet with **📞 Call · 💬 WhatsApp · ✉️ SMS · 📧 Email**, each button only rendered when the record actually has that detail (no phone → Email alone; neither → the sheet refuses with a toast rather than opening empty). No pre-filled message, unlike `_repairNotify` — there is no ticket here to write about. Wired into **all three** places a phone number is tapped (the [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]] rule, not just the Clients tab the owner reported): the **client card**, the **reservation detail** header and the **gift-card detail** row. `_callPhone` is kept — the sheet's Call button uses it in spirit (`tel:`), and nothing else changed.
  - **The record is looked up by id (`contactFromRecord(kind, id)`), never passed through the attribute** — a client called `Φανή O'Brien "η μικρή"` would otherwise break the `onclick` string, exactly the trap `showClientPurchases` documents with `overlay.dataset.client`. The phone/email likewise ride on `overlay.dataset`, so `_contactVia` reads them back instead of having them interpolated into four more onclick strings.
  - **WhatsApp needs digits only** (`replace(/[^0-9]/g,'')` → `wa.me/306941234567`) while `tel:`/`sms:` keep the leading `+` — the same split `_repairNotify` uses. A number stored WITHOUT a country code is passed as-is and WhatsApp resolves it against the viewer's own country; the app's phone fields all carry the country-code picker (v11.52), so this is the rare path.
  - z-index **999997** — above every detail overlay (99998) and the purchase-history sheet (999995), below `showConfirm` (999998) and the country picker (1000000).
  - Verified by driving the real functions: a real tap on a real client card opens it (48px buttons, all inside a 375×812 viewport, no horizontal overflow, correct in dark AND light); the four URLs are `tel:+30…`, `https://wa.me/30…`, `sms:+30…`, `mailto:…`; a name with an apostrophe and quotes renders intact; phone-only → 3 buttons, email-only → 1, neither → no sheet, missing record → no sheet; reservation and gift-card taps resolve their own `clientPhone`/`buyerPhone` + name; EL and EN labels.
- **Lava Stone & Abalone (v12.61):** two stones the shop stocks that the list was missing, added the full round trip — `STONE_ORDER` (alphabetical: Abalone before Agate, Lava Stone between Lapis Lazuli and Malachite, so `STONE_TYPES` derives them), `STONE_TRANSLATIONS` («Λάβα» / «Αμπαλόνε»), `STONE_ABBREV_MAP` (**`LAV`** / **`ABA`**, both 3-letter and previously unused), `COLORED_PER_CT` (`Lava Stone:[8,4,2,1]`, `Abalone:[18,9,5,2]` — organic bead materials, so without their own rows the ✨ Estimate button would have guessed the €160/ct gemstone default), plus `showroom.htm`'s `STONE_ORDER`, `STONE_TRANSLATIONS` and BOTH plural maps (EN `Lava Stones` / `Abalone`; EL «Λάβα» / «Αμπαλόνε», both mass nouns like Τυρκουάζ). Greek names follow the owner's rule — the term Greek shops actually write: **«Λάβα»** is the established word, **«Αμπαλόνε»** is the transliteration Greek jewellers use for abalone (they also write it in Latin script; say the word and it will be corrected if he prefers `Abalone`). Verified: the load-time `_assertStoneAbbrevsUnique()` passes (0 duplicate codes, 0 missing, 0 digits, 85 codes), 86 stones total, and every encode→parse round trip is exact — `LAV`/`tLAV`/`4x2.5LAV` and `ABA`/`tABA`/`2x1.2ABA` all parse back to the right stone, qty and carat; `buildStoneDescription` → `stonesDescDisplay` → `_invEditParseStoredStones` round-trips («8× Λάβα · Αμπαλόνε»); both appear in the `<select>` as standalone options (no family); no stone anywhere is missing a translation or a plural.
- **Stone list doubled — 86 → 149 (v12.62):** the owner asked for **Prehnite** and, so the list stops needing a patch every few weeks, for the popular stones and colour varieties a jewellery shop realistically stocks. **25 new stones**, each a full round trip (Greek name, unique 3-letter tag code, guide price, plural in both languages for the showroom card line): Prehnite, Amber, Aventurine, Bloodstone, Chalcedony, Chrome Diopside, Chrysoprase, Fluorite, Howlite, Iolite, Jasper, Kunzite, Kyanite, Larimar, Marcasite, Moldavite, Obsidian, Pyrite, Rhodochrosite, Rhodonite, Selenite, Sodalite, Sunstone, Tiger Eye, Unakite. **8 became new families** (Amber, Aventurine, Chalcedony, Jasper, Obsidian, Tiger Eye + the previously flat Ruby and Onyx) and **38 varieties** were added to existing ones — Blue/Grey Diamond, Star Ruby, Purple/Star Sapphire, Dendritic/Botswana/Fire Agate, White/Fossil Coral, Almandine/Demantoid Garnet, Blue/Grey Moonstone, Black/Green Onyx, Ethiopian/Pink Opal, Milky/Strawberry Quartz, Red/Blue Spinel, Pink Topaz, Rubellite/Indicolite Tourmaline. **Amethyst is still deliberately left alone** (owner's call — it is purple and nothing else).
  - **No new code — the v12.48 family system absorbed all of it.** The tables are the only thing that changed: `STONE_FAMILIES` / `STONE_FAMILY_LABELS` / `STONE_ORDER` / `STONE_TRANSLATIONS` / `STONE_ABBREV_MAP` / `COLORED_PER_CT` in `jewelprice.htm`, mirrored (minus the codes and prices, which the showroom does not need) into `showroom.htm` along with both plural maps. `STONE_TYPES`, `COLORED_FULL`, `STONE_FAMILY_OF` and `STONE_TYPES_LONGEST` are all derived, so nothing else could fall stale. That is exactly what the v12.48 generalisation was for.
  - **`Tiger Eye`, not `Tiger's Eye`** — the apostrophe would have to survive HTML attributes and `onclick` strings, which is the trap that silently broke an entire `<script>` in v12.43. "Tiger Eye" is a normal trade spelling, so the risk was simply designed out.
  - **The Greek names came from the OWNER, not from translation** — he researched what Greek jewellery sites actually write and supplied all 51, which corrected a first pass that had guessed several wrong (Αιματόλιθος→**Αιματόπετρα**, Χρυσόπρασος→**Χρυσοπράσιος**, Σεληνίτης→**Σελενίτης**, Λαριμάρ→**Λάριμαρ**, Αβεντουρίνης→**Αβεντουρίνη** (feminine), Μάτι Τίγρη→**Μάτι Τίγρης**, «Ρουμπίνι/Ζαφείρι Αστέρι»→**Αστεροειδές**, Δεμαντοειδής→**Αδαμαντοειδής**, Αχάτης Botswana→**Αχάτης Μποτσουάνα**). **Ask him rather than translate.** They are stored in the app's own Title Case so they sit consistently beside the ~100 existing names in one dropdown; the words are his verbatim.
  - **One Greek word per family (v12.63).** Four of the owner's names used a different base word from the one the app already shows on existing pieces — Moonstone (**Φεγγαρόλιθος** vs Φεγγαρόπετρα), Opal (**Οπάλιο** vs Οπάλιος), Topaz (**Τοπάζιο** vs Τοπάζι), Tourmaline (**Τουρμαλίνης** vs Τουρμαλίνη) — so «Φεγγαρόλιθος Rainbow» and «Μπλε Φεγγαρόπετρα» would have sat in one list. Both forms are current in Greek retail, so this was **flagged rather than silently fixed** (aligning the other way would change the displayed name of existing stock); he chose the app's existing word. The 7 affected varieties are now Μπλε/Γκρι Φεγγαρόλιθος, Οπάλιο Αιθιοπίας, Ροζ Οπάλιο, Ροζ Τοπάζιο, Τουρμαλίνης Ρουμπελίτης/Ιντικολίτης — **his spelling of the variety word itself is kept** (Ιντικολίτης, not the first pass's Ινδικολίτης); only the family's base word reverted. A guard in the verification asserts every member of those four families contains the family's stem, in both the singular and the showroom plural. **Rule for any future stone: match the base word the family already uses.**
  - **Guide prices matter more here than for gemstones.** Most of these are bead materials, and a stone with no `COLORED_PER_CT` row inherits the **generic €160/ct default** — absurd for howlite or pyrite — so every new stone got a row (€6–140/ct), plus real rows for the varieties genuinely worth different money (Blue Diamond, Star Ruby/Sapphire, Demantoid, Ethiopian Opal, Red/Blue Spinel, Rubellite, Indicolite). **One pre-existing stone changed: Coral, €160 → €90/ct** (it had no row and its new varieties would have inherited the default). Estimate-button only — tap-only, never auto-applied.
  - **Verified by driving the real code against the owner's 25/07 backup (1286 items):** all **754** distinct stored `qrCode`s and all **176** stored `stonesDesc` strings parse and render **byte-identically before vs after** (930 probe lines, identical hash), so nothing in the existing stock moved. Plus **891** synthetic encode→parse round trips (every stone × carat/no-carat × qty 1/N/melee) all exact; the load-time `_assertStoneAbbrevsUnique()` silent (148 codes, 0 duplicate, 0 missing, 0 containing a digit); no stone missing a Greek name, a plural, or a family label in either file; the picker renders 20 `<optgroup>`s / 149 options in both languages; the filter sheet collapses a family only when 2+ of its varieties are in stock (a lone Star Sapphire or Ocean Jasper stays an ordinary row) and a real click on «Κεχριμπάρια (όλα)» selects both amber varieties and commits them to `_invFilters.stones`; and the showroom's substring-based `stonesDescDisplay` was checked for **every ordered pair** of stone names — 0 cases where a shorter name shadows a longer one.
- **A quote history entry stores the PIECES, not just a summary (v12.64):** the owner printed a 2-item quote (a by-the-gram chain + a cross, €1150), the customer came back without it, and the reprint produced a different document — ONE merged item titled «Bulk chains · 14K · 3.9g + Cross», the two weights added into 7.90 g, and **`€undefined/oz · €NaN/g`** where the metal price goes plus `€NaN` on both VAT lines. Cause: a history entry held only a summary (`title`, joined `metal` string, total `weight`, `finalPrice`, `isMultiItem`, `itemCount`) — the cart itself was never saved — and **`reprintQuote` always called `buildQuoteHTML`**, the single-item builder, whatever the entry was. A multi entry also carried no `preVat`/`vat`/`vatAmount`/`spotPrice` at all, which is where the NaNs came from.
  - **Fix:** every quote now also stores a slim **`items[]`** built by the shared **`_quoteHistItem(name, r, photo, invId)`** — exactly the fields `buildMultiQuoteHTML`/`buildQuoteHTML` read (labelCode, seqRef, metalLabel, weight, secondary metal, handmade, stones, discount/discountAmt/retail, finalPrice, vat/preVat/vatAmount, spotPrice, isBulkChain) — plus `extraDiscPct`/`roundTarget` on a multi entry so the totals reproduce exactly. `reprintQuote` branches on `items.length >= 2` → the **same multi-item document**, else the single builder. All four save sites (print × share, multi × single) go through the one helper so they cannot drift; **the single path gained `stones`, `labelCode`/`seqRef` and `retail`, which `printQuote` never stored** (so a reprint used to lose the stone block and the item QR).
  - **Photos are stored only as http(s) URLs.** A data URL (the photo captured at bulk-sale time) would be copied into `localStorage` **and** into the 200-entry cloud array. At reprint `_quoteReprintPhotos` falls back to the piece's current inventory photo — by the line's pinned **`invId`** ([[cart-lines-own-a-record]]) then by `_qrKey(labelCode)` — but **never for a bulk chain**, whose batch picture is not the piece that was sold (the same rule v11.67 set for printed sale documents). Measured: 200 two-item quotes ≈ **221 KB**.
  - **Quotes already saved cannot be rebuilt** — the items were never written. `_quoteReprintResult(e, src)` derives the missing VAT figures from the stored total (exact: 1150 / 1.17 → €982.91 + 17%) so the document is correct rather than full of NaN, and the owner gets a **toast** saying only the summary was saved. Deliberately no note on the document itself — it goes to a customer.
  - **Pre-existing bug fixed in passing:** `buildQuoteHTML`'s metal-spot row was unguarded (`${cur}${r.spotPrice}/oz`), and a **by-the-gram chain sets `spotPrice: null`** — so every single-item quote for a bulk chain printed **`€null/oz · €NaN/g`**. The row is now omitted when there is no finite spot price.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions** with the owner's exact quote rebuilt from the printed sheet: the reprint yields 2 item blocks, both names, €550.00 + €600.00, both discounts (−17.07% / −13.68%), grand €1150.00, `Ref. CR0490`, the cross's photo, the customer name and the stone — with **no NaN, no undefined, no €null** anywhere. Also: the legacy summary-only entry prints cleanly with derived VAT + the warning toast; a single-item quote round-trips its stones/ref/photo/handmade note; a 10% + rounding-to-€265 multi quote reprints subtotal €300 → −11.7% (−€35) → €265; and the bulk-chain single quote no longer prints a spot row. Console clean.
- **Orders get their own Desk section; three ticket types (v12.65):** the owner sold four Greek meander rings he did not stock — researched them, quoted €2000, took a €200 deposit — and had nowhere to file it but the **custom order** flow, which lives under **Repairs**. Two complaints, both right: an order of a piece that already exists is not a *custom* order, and jewellery being sold should not sit under a wrench. (Half of it was already true in the code: the Greek label for `custom` was «Παραγγελία» — the app was calling it an order while filing it as a repair.)
  - **Three types, one record shape.** `repIsOrder(r)` / `repKind(r)` are the only new vocabulary: **repair** = work on the customer's OWN item (it is in your hand, no deposit); **order** = a piece that exists, ordered from a vendor; **custom** = made from scratch. The last two share a lifecycle, a deposit rule and one list; a repair is a different job and gets its own back. `/jewelprice_repairs` is unchanged — **no migration**: existing `custom` tickets simply appear under Orders.
  - **Desk is a 2x2 grid** (🔒 Κρατήσεις · 📦 Παραγγελίες / 🔧 Επισκευές · 🎁 Επιταγές), **not four across one row** — measured at 375px, «Επιταγές & Πιστώσεις» wraps to two lines on its own. `grid-auto-rows:1fr` keeps all four the same height.
  - **`renderRepairs()` now fills BOTH lists** (`_renderTicketList('orders'|'repairs')`), so all ~15 existing call sites stayed correct with zero edits whichever kind was just saved. Each list has its own search box, sort select and show-finished state (`_deskIsFinished`/`_deskSortValue`/`DESK_SORT_IDS` gained an `orders` key with the same rules as `repairs`).
  - **The status names what actually happened, per type:** an ORDER is «Παραγγέλθηκε» then «Έφτασε»; a repair or custom piece is «Σε εξέλιξη» then «Έτοιμο». Carried into the detail button (📥 Έφτασε), the WhatsApp/SMS/email message («έφτασε και σας περιμένει» vs «είναι έτοιμη για παραλαβή»), the card's date line («άφιξη» vs «έτοιμο») and the PDF title (ΠΑΡΑΓΓΕΛΙΑ / ΕΙΔΙΚΗ ΚΑΤΑΣΚΕΥΗ / ΔΕΛΤΙΟ ΕΠΙΣΚΕΥΗΣ — all-caps Greek written with **no tonos**, since canvas/PDF text is not touched by CSS `text-transform`).
  - **The printed ticket number is frozen** ([[printed-codes-are-frozen]]): orders take their own `O-` series from a new `/jewelprice_order_counter`, and the letter is **STORED** as `ticketPrefix` rather than derived from the type — R-0007 is already on a customer's paper and must never silently become O-0007. `repNoFmt` falls back to `'R'` for every legacy ticket.
  - **Order ↔ custom is editable; repair is not.** `_repEdSetType` allows only that pair (same lifecycle, same deposit rule, same document — only the wording differs), and `_repairEditSave` re-checks `repIsOrder(r)` so forcing the dataset cannot reclassify a repair. This is what let the owner correct R-0007, which was filed as `custom` because nothing else existed.
  - The **new-ticket modal is contextual**: from Repairs there is no type toggle at all (mixing them is what made the old list confusing); from Orders it offers 📦/✨ with a one-line explanation. Deposit block and the optional-vs-required estimate follow the type. The gift-certificate picker at collection now covers **both** order kinds (was `custom` only) — an ordered ring can be paid with a certificate just as a made one can.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a fixture of the owner's two live tickets plus a repair and a new order: the lists split correctly and search in one does not touch the other; legacy tickets keep `R-`, new orders get `O-0001`; both modals; R-0007 flips `custom`→`order` keeping its number, deposit and status while a repair refuses even a forced flip; all four PDF titles + terms; all three notify messages (with the Greek vocative); gift picker true for an order and false for a repair; EL + EN; 375px with nothing clipped and no horizontal overflow; light and dark; console clean.
  - **Arrival: link → hold → sell (Part B, same version).** The pieces do not exist when the ticket is written, so the ticket carries **`invIds`** — a list of RECORD IDS, never a code string (a code is a description and several pieces legitimately share one).
    - **Link:** `openOrderAddPieces` is a top-anchored sheet (typing → the v11.53 rule) with a camera + decode loop and a typed fallback, resolving through the shared **`resolveCodeToInvEntry`** (v12.49). Every outcome is NAMED — unknown code, already sold, held by someone else, already on this order, bulk chain, raw metal — because a scan that silently does nothing is indistinguishable from a broken scanner, and this one writes to a customer's order. A code naming several pieces (grouped twins, a coin batch) takes the first that is in stock and uncommitted, so scanning the same code twice links the second twin. It loads the stock if the cache is cold (the v10.72/v10.74 lesson) and refuses to link a piece any hold already names.
    - **Hold: derived, never copied.** `_ordHoldFor(id)` / `_ordActiveByItem()` read the ticket's own `invIds` while its status is `in_progress`/`ready` — **no flag is written on the item**, so cancelling, collecting or deleting the ticket releases the pieces by itself and there is nothing to lose ([[derived-not-copied-flags]] applied at the source rather than adding a second losable copy beside `reservationId`).
    - **ONE question for every guard.** New **`_holdFor(id)`** returns a normalised `{kind,id,clientName,no}` from a reservation OR an order, and `_holdOpen(h)` opens the right sheet. **10 call sites converted** — Calc SOLD, `invMarkSold`, `invGiveaway`, `invReserve`, `openReserveDialog`, `_cartReservedOk`, the scan chip and **`publicProjection`** (so an ordered piece leaves the showroom) — instead of a second half-check beside each. The warnings name the right document and button in both languages. The display path (`_isHeld`, badge/filter/sort) gained `_ordHeldMap`, kept **separate from `_resActiveByItem`** because that one feeds the `reservationId` reconcile, which must never write a ticket id into a field meaning "reservation".
    - **Sell:** `_ordCompleteSale` hands the linked in-stock pieces to the ordinary multi-item sale at the agreed price, split **proportionally to each piece's live retail** with the remainder on the last line (so the parts always add back to the agreed total exactly, and each record's VAT split and profit are right); `confirmSale` then calls **`_ordFinalizeCollect`**, which closes the ticket and books **NO income of its own** — the sale records already carry the money, and doing both would count the order twice. Collect falls back to the old dialog when nothing is linked.
    - **Race fixed while testing:** `getUserMedia` resolves long after the sheet may have been closed and reopened, and the element ids are the same — a stale rejection wrote its message (in the OLD language) into the sheet that replaced it and could leave a camera running for a sheet that was gone. A `_ordAddSeq` session id now invalidates any callback in flight.
    - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's actual case (4 meander rings, €2000, €200 deposit): 4 tags linked, all 4 held under O-0001 and off the showroom, «Παραλαβή & πώληση» → **4 sale records 600+500+500+400 = €2000.00 in ONE transaction, each carrying its own `invId`, all 4 pieces sold, ticket collected at €2000, zero `repairIncome` records, hold released**. Plus every refusal (bulk, raw metal, reservation-held, sold, duplicate, unknown) writing nothing; an uneven €1897.35 splitting exactly; the repair path showing no pieces block; the full reservation flow unchanged (`_resComplete`, `invReserve` on a reserved piece, both hold warnings); EL + EN; 375px top-anchored and scrollable with no overflow; console clean.

- **Consignment stock — goods held but not paid for (v12.65):** the owner's supplier Butterflyfish sends 13 necklaces and 6 pairs of earrings that he does not pay for; at the end of the season he pays for what sold and hands the rest back. He does this occasionally with other vendors too. Before this there was nothing but **Delete**, which threw the record away — so a piece that came back next season had to be re-added from scratch, losing its ref code, photo, weight and price history.
  - **Two separate ideas, deliberately.** **`consignment: true`** is a flag saying WHOSE the goods are; **`status: 'returned'`** says WHERE the piece is. A piece can be consignment and in stock, consignment and sold, consignment and returned, or an ordinary owned piece returned to a vendor.
  - **A third status value is safe by construction.** All 63 status checks in the file ask `=== 'in_stock'` or `=== 'sold'`, so a returned piece drops out of `publicProjection` (showroom), the stocktake's expected list and every card action **without touching any of them**. Only three places needed work: `renderInvSummary`'s `inStock` filter (written as a second `!==` so a legacy record with no status counts exactly as before), the list filter's `isSoldItem ? 'sold' : 'in_stock'` two-way (now three-way, and the no-pill default hides returned as well as sold), and `buildCard`.
  - **The flag deliberately STAYS on a sold piece** — it is what the settlement report reads to work out what is still owed. Paying stamps **`consignSettledAt`**, so a settled piece drops out of the report and cannot be paid for twice. `consignOwed(item)` = consignment && sold && not settled.
  - **The card** gains a 🤝 badge (with «οφείλεται» once sold-unsettled) and, for a returned piece, a slate `ΣΤΟΝ ΠΡΟΜΗΘΕΥΤΗ` badge plus a single **↩️ back-to-stock** action. New `_gone = isSold || isRet` gates everything a piece off the shelf must not offer (sell/reserve/gift) or trigger (the three background self-heals) — `isSold` is kept only where it genuinely means *sold* (View Sale, restock).
  - **Filters:** `'returned'` is a real status pill; **`'consign'` is cross-cutting** like hidden/grouped/set (it narrows whichever real status is active, so "Consignment" alone means in-stock consignment and "Consignment + Sold" is what you owe).
  - **Bulk marking** via a new **⋯** button in the select bar's row 1 — row 2 already carries five actions and will not take a sixth, and these are occasional. The sheet **only offers what the selection can actually do**, so a tap is never a silent no-op. `_consignApply(ids, mutate)` is the ONE writer behind all four actions, checked and **rolled back on a failed write** (v11.62); a per-item toggle in Edit Item covers fixing a single piece, and stores the key only when true so an ordinary piece stays byte-identical.
  - **Settlement report** (Settings → 🤝, `can('see_cost')`): vendor list with what you owe / still here / returned, then per vendor three sections with Settle, Return-all and Bring-back-all. Cost goes through **`_invSaleCost`** — never a bespoke `purchaseCost || baseCost`, because an other-type piece keeps its cost in the QR and would report €0 ([[other-type-cost-in-qr]], v12.45). **That is not theoretical: the consignment vendor in this shop IS the other-type vendor.** Vendors are grouped via `_itemVendor` so a piece with a drifted `vendorIdx` (v11.14) still counts against the right one.
  - **Bring-back leaves the price baseline alone** (the owner's explicit choice), so the gain % keeps measuring real metal-price movement since the piece first arrived rather than resetting each season.
  - **Totals & the accountant:** the Stock Cost card gains a 🤝 row (shown only when there IS consignment stock) so "what I have spent" and "what I am holding" are not the same number; the CSV export gains a **Consignment** column plus `On consignment (pieces)` / `Consignment cost` / `Own goods cost` summary lines — **marked but still listed**, the owner's choice, so his accountant decides. The export's "in stock only" filter now also excludes returned pieces.
  - **Select-bar layout:** the ⋯ button pushed row 1 over the edge at 375px in Greek. Fixed with `min-width:0` on the count, `gap:8→6`, and the two labelled utility buttons going **icon-only** — the bar is capped at 360px on EVERY screen, so a viewport media query was the wrong trigger and the count (the one thing that must stay legible) was being squeezed to a single character.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's exact case (13 + 6 from Butterflyfish, an other-type vendor at 20% supplier discount): cost €120/€64 per piece; sell 4 + 2 → **you owe €608.00**; 13 returned in one action → default view drops to the one owned piece, cost total €2,104 → €160, consignment row €1,944 → hidden, showroom excludes them; report shows 608.00, Settle clears it and stamps 6 pieces; next season Bring-back restores all 13 with the flag intact, `dateReturned` cleared, baseline untouched and the showroom publishing again. Plus a forced write failure rolling back exactly the one piece; the Edit Item toggle; the CSV column, row values and split summary with returned pieces excluded; the ⋯ sheet offering only applicable actions with correct Greek/English plurals; employee cannot see the settlement section; no clipping at 375px in either language; console clean.

- **Consignment totals on screen (v12.66):** the owner filtered Stock to «Παρακαταθήκη + Πουλημένα», saw his three sold Butterflyfish pieces and asked for what he owes on each and in total — plus sales and profit. He had found a real gap: `renderInvSummary` values only in-stock goods, so with the Sold filter on the three header cards read **"—"** exactly when the numbers matter most.
  - **`#invConsignBar`** sits above the list and is scoped to the CURRENT filtered view — it answers "what am I looking at", which is what the filters were used to ask. Built in `renderInvList` from the same `filtered` rows `renderInvSummary` gets, so the two can never describe different sets of pieces. Owner/manager only (`can('see_cost')`); hidden when the view holds no consignment pieces.
  - **It only shows cells that mean something:** with sold pieces in view → **Οφείλετε / Πωλήσεις / Κέρδος** (plus «Στο ράφι» when the view also holds unsold ones); with none → just «Αξία στο ράφι». A prominent "you owe €0.00" on an ordinary stock view is noise, not information.
  - **Money is read back, never recomputed.** The sale AMOUNT and PROFIT live on the sale record, and `rec.profit` already knows the VAT / cash / gift-certificate rules — so the bar sums those rather than deriving its own ([[other-type-cost-in-qr]] generalised: one source for money). Owed comes from `_invSaleCost`, which is why an other-type piece (cost in the QR) reports correctly.
  - **`_consignMatchSale`** links a piece to its sale by `invId` first (v11.78), then by `_qrKey`, and **consumes** the matched record so two pieces sharing a descriptive code cannot both claim the same sale. `_consignSalePool` excludes **returns** (a negative mirror of a sale, v11.46 — counting one would inflate sales), scrap gain, repair income and gift breakage, but **keeps giveaways**: the piece left for nothing and the vendor is still owed.
  - **A short total says it is short** — any sold piece whose sale record cannot be found is counted in `unmatched` and surfaced as «⚠️ N χωρίς εγγραφή πώλησης», rather than silently under-reporting. Settled amounts show as a separate note so they never inflate what is owed.
  - **Per-card line** (`_consignCardLine`): «🤝 Οφείλετε €104.00» once sold, «Εξοφλήθηκε» after settling, «Θα οφείλετε» while on the shelf.
  - Sales are normally already in memory from the boot sync; if not, `renderConsignBar` loads them **once** (`_consignSalesTried`) and repaints — never in a loop.
  - Verified against the owner's three real cards (BUTK-9055-130 / BUTR-8398-48 / BUTR-3384-36 at Butterflyfish's 20%): owed €104.00 + €38.40 + €28.80 = **€171.20**, sales **€580.00**, profit **€324.53** (= 580/1.17 − 171.20). Plus a return record correctly ignored, an owned piece excluded from the consignment totals, a settled piece moved to its own note, a sold piece with no sale record flagged, the in-stock-only view showing just the shelf value, EL + EN, 375px with no clipping, and the employee seeing nothing.

- **Consignment bar is opt-in (v12.67):** the owner liked the totals but not seeing «ΑΞΙΑ ΣΤΟ ΡΑΦΙ €1188.80» sitting over his ordinary stock view, and asked for a toggle in the filter. Mirrors the **Total Cost card** pattern exactly ([[consistent-ui-patterns]], v12.23) rather than inventing a second kind of control: `_showConsignBar` in `localStorage 'jpro_showConsign'`, default **OFF**, with a **«🤝 Σύνολα παρακαταθήκης»** pill in the SAME filter-sheet group as «💰 Εμφάνιση κόστους», owner/manager only.
  - **Filtering BY consignment shows it regardless of the toggle** (`_consignBarVisible()` = `_showConsignBar || status.includes('consign')`) — that IS the question being asked, so it must not need a second tap. This is the owner's own stated rule.
  - **A view preference, not a filter:** it lives outside `_invFilters`, so it does not count towards the filter badge and «Clear filters» does not reset it; the handler flips the bar's `display` **directly** rather than re-rendering, so it cannot prematurely apply the sheet's other not-yet-Applied pills.
  - **The content is built even while the bar is hidden** (`dataset.has`), so flipping the toggle reveals real numbers instantly instead of an empty box waiting for the next render.
  - Verified by driving the real sheet: pill present beside the cost toggle and absent for an employee; off by default with the bar hidden on a plain stock view; shown when filtering by consignment with the toggle still off; tap on → visible + `jpro_showConsign=1` with `_invFilters` untouched; tap off → hidden; preference survives «Clear filters»; and a shop with no consignment stock never sees the bar either way.

- **Mixed payment — part cash, part card (v12.68):** the owner sold a 3-item cart for €250 and took **€200 in cash (no receipt) and €50 by card**; he reports the same on single pieces (a €700 ring settled €500 cash + €200 card). A single `cashSale` flag cannot describe one sale settled two ways, so answering it with the plain CASH toggle was wrong **in both directions** — pressed, the €50 that genuinely went through the till pays no VAT; unpressed, VAT is charged on €200 that never did. On his €250 cart at 17% the two wrong answers are €250 and €213.68 ex-VAT against the true **€242.74**, i.e. **€36.32 of profit** misstated either way.
  - **The shape already existed.** A sale part-paid by a gift certificate has arrived under two regimes since v11.41, and `_saleExVat` is the one place that knows it. Mixed payment is the same idea one step further, so it reuses that machinery rather than adding a parallel one: new field **`cashAmt`** = how much of the amount was taken as cash; the remainder of what is settled now has VAT extracted. `_saleExVatRaw` (unrounded, new) does the arithmetic and `_saleExVat` rounds it — **`addSaleRecord`, `confirmSaleEdit` and `_recordRepairIncome` now all take their ex-VAT figure from it** instead of each re-deriving the formula, so a stored profit can no longer disagree with the sale detail that displays it.
  - **`cashAmt` is stored ONLY when the sale is genuinely mixed** (`_splitResolve`): all cash canonicalises to `{cashSale:true}` with no `cashAmt`, all card to `{cashSale:false}` with none, so an ordinary record is **byte-identical** to one written before this existed. Proven, not asserted: **648 combinations** of amount / certificate portion / cash flag / VAT rate (including negative return amounts) return the identical value before and after the change.
  - **A cart splits proportionally, exactly like the certificate** — €250 with €200 cash across €100/€100/€50 lines gives 80/80/40, so every record keeps its own true VAT split and profit and the parts still sum to the whole. (Per-line rounding can leave the summed ex-VAT a cent from the single-sale equivalent; that is inherent to one-record-per-piece and already true of the certificate spread.)
  - **It composes**: certificate portion, then cash, then card — `_splitResolve` is handed the amount **less** what the certificate covers, so cash + card + certificate can never exceed the sale. `processReturn` mirrors it: `cashAmt` is scaled by the refund fraction and **negated**, and `_saleExVatRaw` clamps it by magnitude and forces it to share the sale's sign for the same reason the certificate portion does — a partial refund of a mixed sale must unwind under both regimes, not take the whole reversal out of one.
  - **UI: ONE control, three dialogs.** `_splitHtml`/`_splitOpen`/`_splitSync`/`_splitValue`/`_splitSet` + the `_SPLIT_HOSTS` table, mounted into the **sale dialog**, the **sale edit dialog** (both from static wrap divs at boot) and the **order/repair collection dialog** (rebuilt with its overlay, so it always starts closed) — so the three cannot drift. Closed by default; opening it **hides the plain CASH toggle**, which could only give a second, contradictory answer to the same question. You type the CASH figure only and the receipt half is computed and shown; it is clamped so a typo cannot invent money, and it follows the amount and the certificate as either changes. `data-i18n` spans inside, so a language switch relabels it (keys `splitPayBtn`/`splitCashPart`/`splitRestPart`, both dictionaries).
  - **Nothing silently inherits a split**: `closeSaleModal` clears it (a leftover would quietly halve the next customer's VAT), the collection overlay is rebuilt per ticket, and a mixed sale reopens in **Edit** showing its cash part so a mistyped split is corrected rather than deleted and re-recorded.
  - **Display**: the sale detail badges **`💵/💳 ΜΙΚΤΗ / SPLIT`** (neither `💵 CASH` nor blank would be true) and spells it out — «💵 €200.00 μετρητά + 🧾 €50.00 με απόδειξη»; when a certificate is also involved the certificate row stops repeating the rest under one regime word, since the payment row now breaks it down. The multi-item view sums the split into the same line and keeps the whole-transaction CASH toggle as an **override** (unpressed while split; pressing it means "all of it was cash" and deletes `cashAmt` from every member). The **printed** quote/certificate for a mixed sale makes **no VAT claim at all** (same treatment as cash): only part of it went through the till, so a full-amount VAT line would be false.
  - **Fixed in passing:** `confirmSaleEdit` computed `(cash ? amt : amt/(1+vat))` on its own and **ignored a gift-certificate portion entirely**, so editing such a sale silently rewrote its profit under one regime; it now goes through `_saleExVatRaw` like everything else. And `_recordRepairIncome` minted its id with a bare `Date.now()` — the collision v11.64 fixed elsewhere — now `_nextSaleId`.
  - **The whole-transaction control was rebuilt as ONE button (replaces `_toggleGroupCash`, v10.4).** A cart's split lives spread across its lines (€200 over €100/€100/€50 is stored as 80/80/40), so it could not be corrected at all except by editing every record and dividing the cash by hand — and a plain CASH toggle standing beside a `ΜΙΚΤΗ` badge simply contradicted it. `#sdmGroupPayBtn` («✏️ Πληρωμή») now opens **`_openGroupSplit`**, which offers all three answers — 💵 all cash / 🧾 all on receipt / 💵💳 split with the amount — and `_saveGroupSplit` re-spreads a split proportionally (remainder on the last line) and re-derives every member's profit through `_saleExVat`. A whole-transaction answer is **uniform by construction** (`_splitResolve(..., uniform)`, not each record's own old flag), or a line that happened to be wholly cash would stay cash while the rest went on receipt — mixed again with no split to show for it. The badge now carries the state for everyone, since the button no longer doubles as the indicator. `_toggleGroupCash` was removed (its button is gone; no other caller). Two layout problems drove the shape and were **caught by a screenshot, not by measurement**: three controls plus the amount wrapped the header at 375px, and the long payment value wrapped under its own label and collided with it — hence the single button and `_splitLabel(s, cur, compact)` (`💵 €200.00 + 🧾 €50.00`) for narrow label/value rows.
  - **`_saleCashPart(s)` — never sum `cashAmt` to total a transaction's cash.** A record is only *split* when it is PARTLY cash, so one that was wholly cash carries **no `cashAmt` at all** — which happens naturally to a cart line whose proportional share exactly covers its payable (e.g. a €200 line with a €100 certificate in a €300 transaction taking €150 cash). Summing `cashAmt` therefore under-reported the transaction's cash: the detail row read «💵 €50 + 🧾 €150» when the truth was the reverse, and opening the payment editor prefilled €50 — so saving would have **destroyed €100 of cash treatment**. `_saleCashPart` returns the split amount when split, else the whole payable or 0, and is used by `_splitLabel` and by both group sites. Found by a test, not by reading.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions in a browser (127 assertions):** the reported transaction records 3 records / €250 / cash parts summing to €200 / correct per-line profit / one `saleGroup` / all three pieces sold; the €700 single case; certificate + split on one record; full and partial returns netting to zero profit; canonicalisation of every degenerate split; the dialog driven by real clicks and typing (clamping, the receipt half tracking amount and certificate, EL/EN labels, no overflow at 375px, readable in light and dark); the reset after recording; edit prefill → correct → clear; the group override in both directions; the collection dialog booking €2000 as €1500 + €500/1.17 − €900; every plain cash / plain card / no-cost path byte-identical to before; and the transaction editor across all three answers (prefill from the real cash, re-spread and re-derive, uneven thirds summing exactly, clamping, certificate + split, permission gating, EL/EN, 375px).
- **THE SHELF RULE — a scanned code must never price off a piece that is gone (v12.69):** the owner was away, his mother worked the shift, and a pair of Amaya earrings worth **€80 was scanned, shown at €50, and sold at €50**. Reproduced exactly against his real 1589-item backup, then fixed and re-verified there. Two independent defects compounded:
  - **The trigger — `tagRef` was a lie on 42 tags.** Pre-v12.07 `invAddSimilar`'s `addBatch` called `printLabel(data, el, false, qty)` with no `useRef`, so the label printed the **ref code as TEXT and the DESCRIPTIVE code in the QR** — while `Object.assign({}, source, …)` copied **`tagRef: true`** from the source piece. v12.07 fixed the printing but nothing could fix the tags already on the shelf: 42 pieces (40 still in stock, seq 663/670/673/694/696/697/698/699/700) carry a descriptive QR on a record that claims to be a ref tag.
  - **The multiplier — `_descPool` excluded `tagRef` pieces FIRST, over the whole match set.** `const _descPool = _matches.filter(([, it]) => !it.tagRef); const _pool = _descPool.length ? _descPool : _matches;` — so one **unconverted SOLD** record beat every in-stock piece sharing its code. `AMA925S2.7` matched 5 records: 4 in-stock ER0699 (€80, all `tagRef`) and one sold **ER0462 (€50, no `tagRef`)** → `_pool` was the sold one alone. Amaya's category is **per-item**, so `calculate()` takes `laborCost`/`itemMargin` straight off `_lastScannedInvEntry` — the price shown was a **sold piece's price from a month earlier**. Same for `AMA925S3`: ER0698 €90 → the sold ER0594 at €70, whose `vendorCode` **`S000111`** is exactly what the owner reported seeing.
  - **The fix — `_descCodePool(code, entries)` / `_descCodeItem(code)`** next to `_qrKey`, now the ONE answer to "which piece did this descriptive code come from?": **(1) a piece ON THE SHELF outranks one that is not** — a sold piece's tag left with it, and for a per-item category its price is history; **(2) only WITHIN the shelf** does the ref-tag exclusion apply, yielding entirely if every shelf candidate is converted. Nothing in stock at all → all matches, so scanning a genuinely sold piece still says «Πουλήθηκε». **This makes the app correct for BOTH kinds of tag, so no reprint is needed to be safe** — the 42 mis-printed tags now scan correctly.
  - Applied at 5 sites (the [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]] rule, so the next copy inherits it): `_checkInvStatusForCalc` (**the price**), the other-type **`fixedRetail`** override, the calc's **`stoneCost`** lookup, the calc **photo** fallback, and `_showSaleGroupDetail`'s **`memberDesc`** — which took `m.invId` into account not at all and named a line by a plain `.find()`, which is why the deleted €250 sale showed «Tama with beads» (the SOLD ER0462's description) on a line whose ref read **ER0699**. The name and the ref were being read from two different pieces.
  - **Why the sale still recorded the right PIECE at the wrong PRICE:** `_calcScanInvId()` refuses a non-`in_stock` record, so the cart line got **no pinned `invId`**; `_cartLineInv`'s fallback requires `in_stock`, so it landed on a real ER0699 record. Hence `seqRef: ER0699` and `cost: €27` were correct while the price came from ER0462. The stock count and the cost were never wrong — only the money.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions against the real backup:** across all **954 distinct codes / 1589 items**, exactly **8 codes change and every one moves from a SOLD piece to an IN-STOCK piece** (Amaya ×2 — the money cases; ΑΛΟΡΟ ×6 — per-gram, so the price was already right and only the chip and the SELL target were wrong). **946 codes are byte-identical.** Of **1512** in-stock non-bulk tagged pieces, **0** now resolve to a piece off the shelf. `AMA925S2.7` → €80.00 «Στο απόθεμα» ER0699 (was €50 «Πουλήθηκε» ER0462); `AMA925S3` → €90.00 ER0698. The ref-tag scans (`ER0699`/`ER0698`), the sold-only code, bulk chains, unknown codes and the legacy `+`-form codes are all unchanged. **No variant chooser is lost anywhere; exactly one is gained** — `ALO14KL1.6`, where a necklace (NK0616, blue) and a **cross** (CR0810, white) genuinely share a code and the app previously priced off a sold piece in silence rather than asking.
  - **Deliberately NOT done:** the false `tagRef` is not cleared, and `addBatch` still inherits it via `Object.assign`. We cannot know which of the 42 tags have since been reprinted, and silently rewriting a stored field is the v10.19 lesson. The residual risk is narrow — `_invEditUnlocked` gates weight/vendor/category editing on `tagRef`, so editing the **weight** of one of those 40 pieces would leave its descriptive tag stale; reprinting from Stock → 🏷️ TAG (which always prints a ref QR) fixes that piece for good.
- **Edit a whole multi-item transaction (v12.70):** a single sale has always had an ✏️ Edit form; a cart sale had **none** — so a wrong **date**, the field that decides which day and which month the money belongs to, could not be corrected at all (the owner's report, with screenshots). `openGroupEdit(groupId)` / `_saveGroupEdit(groupId)` add an ✏️ Edit button to `_showSaleGroupDetail`, in the same overlay style as the v12.68 payment editor (z 999997, top-anchored per [[sheets-with-typing-go-on-top]]).
  - **It edits exactly the facts a cart's records genuinely SHARE** — `confirmSale` writes the same `date`, `note`, `vendor` and `employee`/`employeeName` onto every line — so they are edited once and written to all members. Deliberately **not** here: the price of one piece (tap the piece; it opens on its own with the form it already has) and the **payment**, which has its own button beside the total — two controls for one fact is how a half-answered dialog happens ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]).
  - **Changing the TOTAL re-spreads it proportionally with the remainder on the last line**, exactly as `confirmSale` spreads a cart in the first place, and each line's profit is re-derived from the record itself via `_saleExVat` — never a bespoke formula, so the stored profit and the detail sheet can never disagree ([[other-type-cost-in-qr]] generalised: one source for money).
  - **The cash figure is kept, not scaled.** It is a statement of fact about money that changed hands, so a corrected total takes the difference off the **card** side (cash clamped to the new payable, then re-spread). `_splitResolve` canonicalises the degenerate ends, so an **all-cash** transaction stays `cashSale:true` with no `cashAmt` and an **all-receipt** one stays untouched — verified byte-identical in both cases.
  - **Two guards, both explained rather than silent.** A **gift certificate** already drawn down by the transaction sets a floor on the total (a sale worth less than the card paid for it would leave that money paying for nothing) — refused with the amount named. A **returned** line's negative record mirrors what this one stored, so the total input is **locked read-only up front** (with the reason under it) instead of refusing after it has been typed; date/vendor/note/salesperson stay fully editable on such a group. The save-time check is kept behind the lock.
  - **Shared `_fillSaleEmployeeSelect` / `_readSaleEmployee`** extracted from `openSaleEditModal`/`confirmSaleEdit` and used by both forms, so the two can never disagree about who is offered (an inactive member is kept when the sale already names them, or editing anything else would silently reassign the sale). [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]
  - **Permission = the existing `canDel` rule** (owner/manager, or the employee who made that sale); a **return group** shows only Undo, matching a single return record.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's exact €250 / 3-item split transaction (profit €125.50, matching his screenshot): a date-only save moves all three dates with amounts/cash/profit **byte-identical**; 250→240 keeps €200 cash, sums to exactly 240 and yields ex-VAT €234.20 against the hand-computed €234.19 (a 1-cent per-line rounding, inherent to one-record-per-piece); all-cash and all-receipt totals re-spread correctly; both guards refuse and write nothing; blank amount refused; Cancel writes nothing; EL + EN; the full permission matrix; the single-sale Edit still round-trips after the refactor; no overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **SPLIT badge / Payment button alignment (v12.70):** the sale-detail header row was `align-items:baseline`, so `#sdmCashBadge` sat on the 28px amount's own baseline while `#sdmGroupPayBtn` used `align-self:center` — the pair that reads as one control (what was paid / change it) was visibly out of line. Row is now `align-items:center`, the button's `align-self` removed, and the badge given the button's padding/radius so they match in height. Measured identical top/bottom/height. CSS only.
- **A stale cache wiped 11 pieces off the public showroom (v12.71) — the LAST whole-node PUT in the app:** the owner shared 19 pieces from the showroom to WhatsApp at 20:05; the customer opened the link and saw **8**. Reproduced against the live public feed: it held **NK1109–NK1116 and nothing above** — a clean cut at `dateAdded` 19:27:48, with NK1117–NK1121 and ER1122–ER1127 (added between 19:28 and 20:05, and visible in the showroom when he selected them) simply gone. Not a link bug and not a sharing bug: `?items=` carried all 19 tokens correctly and `applyFilters` matched every piece that was actually published.
  - **Cause: `republishPublicShowroom()` did a whole-node `PUT` of `/jewelprice_public` built from `_inventoryCache`** — and it was called from **`renderInvList`**, which on the Stock tab's instant path (v10.23) draws from a cache that can be a whole session old (`renderInventory` renders from memory and refreshes *behind* the list; `_invEnsureForScan` also fills that cache and serves it for 60 s). So a device whose snapshot stopped at NK1116 opened Stock, and the PUT **deleted every piece it had never heard of**. `_publicShowroomSynced` is once-per-session, so the fresh data that landed a second later never republished. This is exactly the last-writer-wins disease [[v11.64]] fixed for sales / gift cards / reservations / repairs / clients — the public feed was the one node still writing the whole array. (Inventory itself was never at risk: `saveInventoryItem` writes one item to its own path.)
  - **Fix, two rules, both load-bearing.** (1) **`republishPublicShowroom(freshInv)` now REFUSES to run without a snapshot the caller has just loaded** — no argument, no writes — and the call moved out of `renderInvList` into `_renderInventoryNow`, which hands it the inventory it has just fetched (both the instant-path background refresh and the cold first open; skipped entirely when `_invLoadFailed`). (2) **It PATCHes a per-id diff instead of PUTting the node**, so an id it does not know about is never touched — the structural guard, per [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]: even a future stale caller cannot wipe pieces it never saw. Plus: an **unreadable feed** re-arms and writes nothing (there is nothing to diff against, so it cannot know what to delete); **"publish nothing" is refused** while the feed is populated (that is what an unloaded inventory looks like — the v11.65 outbox guard); a **failed PATCH** re-arms for the next Stock open; and an already-in-step feed still costs one read and **zero** writes.
  - **No repair tool is needed** — the 11 pieces are untouched in the private inventory, so the first Stock open on the updated app republishes them by itself.
  - Verified by driving the real functions in a browser: the reported shape (19 in stock, feed truncated at 8) emits **one PATCH adding exactly the 11 missing pieces and deleting nothing**; a call with no snapshot makes **zero** requests; a genuinely stale feed entry is still cleaned up (one `null` key, self-healing a failed `syncPublicItem` DELETE); unreadable feed, empty-desired and failed-write all write nothing and re-arm; an in-step feed writes nothing; **no path PUTs the node**. Also proven at the source: the old code's `desired`, built from the stale cache, contains neither new piece; and `_renderInventoryNow` passes the **fresh** snapshot (not the stale cache) on both paths, and nothing at all when the load failed.
- **The customer, added afterwards (v12.72):** a €140 sale was recorded with no customer name — the owner knew perfectly well who it was, it just never got typed at the counter — and there was no way to add it later. Both edit forms now carry the SAME customer block: **name** (with autocomplete from the Clients book), **phone** and **email**.
  - **Built once in `_saleCustBlockHtml(p, o)`** and mounted into both hosts, so the two can never drift ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]): the single-sale form gets it through a static `#saleEditCustWrap` (rebuilt on every open, exactly like the v12.68 `#saleEditSplitWrap`), and `openGroupEdit` renders it inline. `p` is the host's prefix (`saleEd` / `grpEd`) so the two sets of ids never collide, and the shared `_saleCustAuto` / `_saleCustPick` / `_saleCustHide` / `_saleCustRead` / `_saleCustFromBook` all key off it.
  - **The sale record stores the NAME and the EMAIL only** — that is its existing shape (`addSaleRecord`) — while the **phone** is used to fill in the Clients book, which is where a contact belongs. So the block **prefills the phone from the client book** by name (`_saleCustFromBook`, the same trick the return dialog has used since v11.51), and on save `upsertClientFromSale` writes anything new back, exactly as it would have done had the name been typed while recording the sale.
  - A multi-item transaction writes the name to **every member**, since `confirmSale` already writes one customer onto all its lines. Clearing the field clears the customer — this is an edit form, so a blank is deliberate.
