  - **Why it matters beyond the record:** `_showSaleGroupDetail`'s printed quote/certificate takes its customer from `s0.customer`, so a name added afterwards now appears on a document printed after the sale (the v11.67 case — the customer who comes back an hour later wanting a paper).
  - Verified by driving the real functions: the reported €140 sale takes «Φανή O'Brien» with amount/profit/cash/vendor/date byte-identical; a real click on a suggestion fills name+phone+email (apostrophe in the name round-trips); a customer not in the book is created with the typed phone; reopening prefills the name and pulls the phone from the book; an unknown name shows alone; a cleared field clears it; the 3-line cart writes the name to all three lines with amounts/profit untouched and reopens prefilled; the country picker still opens above both hosts (z 1000000); EL + EN labels and placeholders; no overflow at 375px (every field aligned at the same right edge); console clean.
- **Sales breakdown — tap the total (v12.73):** the Sales hero showed «€4,410.00 · 6 sales · Profit €2151.39» and nothing else; the owner asked to tap it and see where the money came from — total / cost / profit per sale, with totals. `openSalesBreakdown()` opens a sheet for **exactly the period the hero is showing**, at any level (day / month / year).
  - **Every figure is READ BACK from the sale records, never recomputed.** The stored `profit` already knows the cash / receipt / gift-certificate / split-payment rules (`_saleExVat` is the one place that knows them), so a breakdown deriving its own arithmetic would eventually disagree with the sale detail sitting beside it — the same single-source rule as [[other-type-cost-in-qr]].
  - **One row per TRANSACTION**, folded by `_groupSalesForDisplay` so a multi-item sale is one row exactly as it is in the list below — matching what the "6 sales" count already means. Row = `Total · Cost · Profit · %`, tap to open the sale.
  - **Four tiles** (Sales / Cost / Profit / Margin), then **"How it was paid"** — the split that actually decides the VAT. `_sbPayParts(s)` derives it exactly as `_saleExVatRaw` does (a certificate portion follows the regime it was settled under months ago; what is handed over now may itself be part cash / part card), signed so a return nets against the sale it reverses. **Proven, not asserted: `Σ cash + Σ receipt/(1+vat) === Σ _saleExVat(s)` to the cent** on both a real-shaped day and an all-edge-cases fixture.
  - **`unknown` counts what is missing from the PROFIT, not from the cost.** A scrap gain or an expired gift certificate legitimately has no cost and its profit is still exact, so keying the warning on a null `cost` cried wolf on three of them in testing. Only a record with no stored `profit` is genuinely absent, and it is named («1 πώληση δεν έχει καταγεγραμμένο κόστος») rather than quietly under-reported — a short total has to say it is short.
  - **Margin is measured over the sales whose profit is known** (`tProfit / knownAmt`), in the tiles, the totals row AND the CSV. Dividing by the whole revenue reads low, because a sale with no recorded cost adds to the bottom of the fraction and nothing to the top.
  - **Owner/manager only** (`can('see_cost')`) — an employee's hero is not tappable and `openSalesBreakdown` refuses. `_sbSetSource(null,…)` also **disarms it in the vendor/employee views**, which never write the hero and would otherwise open a breakdown for a stale set.
  - Re-parented to `<body>` on open (the Sales panel is `position:fixed; z-index:100` outside `.app`, so a nested overlay paints behind it — the v11.06 ☰ More-menu fix); closed by `showPanel`; a **fourth return-stack level** (`_sdmReturnToBreakdown`, cleared in `showSaleDetail` and on every close, acted on only for a plain ✕) hands the owner back to the breakdown instead of the Sales list. **CSV export** in the app's standard convention (comma-delimited, decimal point, UTF-8 BOM — verified `EF BB BF`).
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a reconstruction of the owner's day: hero €4,410.00 / 6 sales, rows and totals reconciling (cost 2265.45, profit 1400.79 = the hero's own figure), ex-VAT 4016.24 both ways; every special kind labelled and netting correctly (return +500/−500 → 0, giveaway €0 revenue with −€60 profit and margin "—", scrap gain, repair income, gift breakage, certificate-paid sale); employee locked out; vendor view disarmed; empty period; all four back-step directions; a customer name containing an apostrophe and quotes; EL + EN (caught a hardcoded «ΦΠΑ» in the English VAT line); light + dark; no horizontal overflow at 375px, top-anchored and fully reachable at 375×500; console clean.
- **A blended margin describes neither trade — segment it (v12.74):** the owner's first real day through the breakdown showed **48.4%**, and he was right that it was misleading. One British Sovereign — **€950.00 revenue, €888.41 cost, €61.59 profit, 6%** — was dragging a genuinely **59.0%** jewelry day down, because bullion is a small dealer spread on someone else's gold while a ring is retail margin on your own work. Averaging the two percentages describes neither.
  - **The money totals stay WHOLE and the margin is what changes.** Sales / Cost / Profit still cover everything (€4,710.00 / €2,386.89 / €2,279.53), because that money went through the till and the sheet has to agree with the hero above it. Only the **MARGIN tile** is measured on jewelry alone, captioned «μόνο κοσμήματα» / "jewelry only". *(Net-basis revenue — counting only the €61.59 spread as a sale — was offered and rejected: it is real accounting for a dealer, but it would make the breakdown disagree with the hero and understate his turnover.)*
  - **The same distortion runs the other way**, so this is one rule, not a coin-shaped special case ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]): a scrap gain or an expired gift certificate is pure profit with no cost, which pushes a margin **up** just as falsely. `SB_SEGMENTS` covers **jewelry / coin / repair / scrap / card / gift**, and a **"By activity"** section lists each with its own revenue, profit and %. It is drawn **only when more than one activity is present**, so an ordinary jewelry day shows nothing extra, and the lines add back to the totals — a complete split, not a list of exceptions.
  - **`_sbIsCoinRec` never guesses from the note.** It reads the exact piece via `invId` when the stock is loaded (`openSalesBreakdown` awaits `_invEnsureForScan()` first — the v11.74 loader that keeps the last good cache on a network failure), else the **`GC####` reference code stamped on the record at sale time**, else a `_qrKey` match. So the split still works with no stock loaded and for a piece since deleted; it degrades rather than disappearing.
  - **A transaction is coins only if EVERY line is** (`recs.every`): a jewelry cart that happens to include a sovereign would otherwise move the whole cart out of the jewelry margin, which is far worse than one coin staying in it. A **returned** coin follows the coin segment automatically, since the return copies the original's `invId`/`seqRef`.
  - **Fallback:** a period holding nothing but other activity (a day of only coins) shows the blended margin with **no** "jewelry only" caption, rather than a dash.
  - **The rows were also stripped** at the owner's request — customer, vendor and salesperson are gone (one tap away in the sale, and still in the CSV) because they crowded out the numbers that are the point of the screen. The piece's **reference code** (`NK0801`) stands in where there is no note, so no row is left blank and unidentifiable. The CSV gained **Activity** and **Ref** columns and keeps the three dropped ones, and its TOTAL margin uses the same jewelry-only rule as the screen.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's real day rebuilt from his screenshot: margin 48.4% → **59.0%** with the totals unchanged and still equal to the hero; activity lines «💍 Κοσμήματα €3,760.00 · €2,218.12 (59.0%)» / «🪙 Νομίσματα €950.00 · €61.59 (6.5%)»; coin identified by `invId`, by `seqRef` with an **empty** inventory cache, and by QR; a mixed cart staying jewelry; a coin batch reading as coins; a returned coin following the coin; all six activities at once with no overflow or label/value collision at 375px (longest Greek label wraps cleanly); a jewelry-only day showing no section and no caption; a coins-only day falling back to blended; CSV columns and total margin matching the screen; EL + EN; light + dark; console clean.
- **An id is not a date — client sorting and dates (v12.75):** the Clients tab's "Newest first" was visibly wrong and every card showed a date in the year **58,500**. `getClientDate(c)` falls back to the id when a client has no `dateAdded`, and that fallback assumed the id is `String(Date.now())` — true until **v11.64**, which changed record ids to **`_stampId()` = `Date.now() * 1000 + device salt`** (~1.78e15). Read as milliseconds that is the year 58,518, and since it is a thousand times any real timestamp such a client sorted **above every correctly-dated one** regardless of when they were actually added. Confirmed on the real 25/07 backup: Φανή Κουλουριώτη (id `1784544012833322`, added 20/07) sat at the top, above a customer added 24/07. It also poisoned the **date-range filter** and the **season chips**, which offered a "58519" season.
  - **Three fixes, at three depths.** (1) `getClientDate` **decodes** a stamped id (`n > 1e14 → Math.floor(n/1000)`), so every existing client reads correctly with **no migration** — the boundary is unambiguous, since a plain ms timestamp does not reach 1e14 until the year 5138. (2) The two creation paths that wrote no `dateAdded` now do: `autoSaveClientFromQuote` and the "+ Add" form (which also mints a **`_stampId()`** id now, for cross-device uniqueness per v11.64, instead of a bare `Date.now()`). (3) **`saveClientEdit` was rebuilding the client from the form fields alone**, so every edit silently dropped `dateAdded`, `addedBy` and `addedByName` — it now **merges onto the stored record**, and an edited legacy client is healed to its *decoded* date rather than to today.
  - **`getClientDate` was the only place in the file making that assumption** (verified by sweeping every `1e12`–`1e15` literal). Inventory is unaffected — its ids are `inv_<ms>_…` and were never stamped.
  - Verified by driving the real `renderClients` on the owner's actual client list: dates now read 05/08 · 24/07 · 23/07 · 21/07 · 20/07 ×3 · 17/07 · 14/07 ×2 · 12/06, strictly descending, with "Oldest first" the exact reverse; the 20/07 victim now sits among the other 20/07 clients instead of above the 24/07 one; a plain-id client is byte-identical to before; `dateAdded` still wins; junk/legacy ids still yield 0; the date range 19–21/07 now finds her; the season chips show only **2026**; an edit keeps `dateAdded`/`notes`/`addedByName`; and a brand-new client gets a stamped id, a `dateAdded` and reads as today. Console clean.
- **A gift is recorded under the client's name; the client screen shows the WHOLE history (v12.76):** the owner gave a piece to a customer and found the "Give as a gift" dialog had no customer field at all — only a free-text note, into which he typed the name by hand. So the gift never reached her record, and her client screen (a €240 sale) said nothing about it. His framing is the point of the feature: *a tourist he has not seen in three years walks in, he searches the name and sees everything — what they bought, what he made or repaired for them, and whether he ever gave them a gift.*
  - **The gift dialog gets the SHARED customer block** (`_saleCustBlockHtml('gift', …)`, v12.72) — name with Clients autocomplete, phone, email — not a bespoke name input ([[consistent-ui-patterns]] / [[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]). It is read **before** the overlay is removed, stamped on the record as `customer`/`email`, and pushed through **`upsertClientFromSale`** so a phone typed here is not lost and a name that is new becomes a client. Optional on purpose (a piece handed to a child at the counter has no name), and the note is now genuinely a note (`π.χ. για την κόρη της`). The focus moves to the name field.
  - **`showClientPurchases` is now a whole history**, in sections: **Purchases · Gifts · Orders · Repairs · Reservations · Gift Cards & Credit**. Sales are matched on `customer`, Desk records on `clientName` (gift cards on `buyerName` **or** `redeemerName`) — the name is the only key those records share.
  - **The money and the rest are deliberately separate sections.** A €0 gift folded in among the purchases would misstate both the count and the total ("2 purchases · €240"), and a repair is not a sale. Purchases still collapse a cart through `_groupSalesForDisplay`, and a return keeps its `↩️ ΕΠΙΣΤΡΟΦΗ` marker.
  - **A summary chip line** (`🛍 1 · €240.00 · 🎁 1 δώρο · 📦 1 · 🔧 1 · 🔒 1 · 🎟️ 1`) answers "have I seen this person before?" before any scrolling.
  - **An empty section means none, not "never loaded".** The Desk lists are pulled once at boot, so the sheet awaits `ensureGiftCards()` and pulls reservations/repairs when this device has not seen them (new `_repLoaded` flag mirroring `_resLoaded`/`_gcLoaded`) — the v11.61 lesson, applied to a read-only screen.
  - **The client sheet is z 999995, ABOVE the repair (99997) / reservation (99998) / gift-card detail overlays**, so tapping one of those rows must close it first or the detail paints behind. `_clientOpenRecord(kind, id)` does that and sets `_clientBackTo`; each of the three openers **clears** the target on its first line and only a **plain** ✕ consumes it (`_clientCloseDetail` / `closeGiftCardDetail`), so a detail reached any other way never bounces back — the same one-step-back rule as v11.79/v11.80. Sales keep their existing `_sdmReturnToClient`.
  - **The piece behind a sale row is resolved by `invId` first**, then by the code via `_descCodeItem` — the old code used a bare `.find()` on `qrCode`, and since v11.32 several pieces legitimately share a descriptive code ([[link-records-by-id-not-string]]).
  - Verified by driving the real functions: the reported case (gift recorded under «Κέλλυ Βούρτση» and appearing in her history); a new name creating a client with the typed phone; a blank name adding nothing; a **failed inventory write booking nothing and rolling the piece back**; autocomplete pick filling name/phone/email; all six sections rendering in EL and EN with correct counts, statuses and balances; all four return paths (sale, repair, reservation, gift card) closing one step at a time and a detail opened elsewhere not bouncing; a name containing an apostrophe, quotes and `<b>` round-tripping through `dataset.client` with no HTML injected; a gift whose piece was deleted falling back to the note without the doubled "🎁 Δώρο —" prefix; the empty state; 375px with no horizontal overflow, the gift dialog top-anchored (fits in 551px, fields aligned, confirm reachable); console clean.
  - **Known, pre-existing, not changed:** the shared client autocomplete matches **accent-sensitively**, so typing «Κελλυ» without the tonos finds «Κέλλυ». It affects the sale-edit, return and repair forms the same way; flagged rather than changed under a gift ticket.
- **A per-item twin pays for its extra METAL, not a bigger margin (v12.77):** the owner added an evil-eye necklace at **6.20 g / €65.00** (per-item category, purchase cost €15) and cloned it via "Add similar items" at **8.30 g** — the app priced it **€80.17**, where the extra 2.10 g of silver is only **€3.50** of metal. Not a bug in the arithmetic: a per-item category has no €/g rate, so the typed target retail is stored as (labour €) + (**margin %**), and `addBatch`'s `Object.assign({}, source, {weight: w})` copied that margin onto a heavier piece — scaling the *whole* price by weight (base cost €15 → €18.50, ×3.7037 ×1.17 = €80.17). Wrong for this category by definition: **the vendor charges ONE price per piece**, so the labour and the margin are per PIECE and only the metal is per gram.
  - **Fix:** a clone whose weight differs gets its own target — **the source's price today + its extra metal at cost** (`_applyPerItemWeightPrice` in `invAddSimilar`, run in `addBatch` before the `initialRetail` re-baseline). The metal difference is `newP.metalValue − srcP.metalValue`, both read at one instant so only the weight — never a spot move — is priced; per-item categories force wastage to 1, and the secondary metal is copied unchanged, so that difference is exactly the extra silver.
  - **Stored as a normal per-item piece, not a special case.** New shared helper **`_perItemSetFromTarget(item, targetRetail)`** (next to `calcInvItemPrice`) is the ONE place that turns a target retail into `laborCost` + `itemMargin` — the identical arithmetic the label form and Edit Item already did inline, extracted so a third caller cannot drift ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]). Verified it reproduces the label form byte-for-byte (labour €4.6644, margin 270.3704%). So the 8.30 g piece stores labour €1.16 / margin 290.32% / base cost still €15 / `initialRetail` €68.50 — indistinguishable from one created by hand at a €68.50 target, and it still floats with metal afterwards (silver +10% → €68.50 → €74.82).
  - **Deliberately narrow.** Per-gram categories are untouched (there the rate IS per gram — the owner's own words), and an **identical-weight** batch is skipped entirely (`Math.abs(newW − srcW) < 0.0001`) so "12 identical rings, one code, N copies of one label" stays byte-identical to before. `other`-type and a missing spot price fall through to the old copy-the-margin behaviour rather than guessing.
  - **The sheet shows the price before you commit** (`#invAddSimPriceNote`, filled from the SAME function the save path calls, so it cannot promise one thing and do another): `💶 €68.50 = €65.00 + €3.50 metal`, with a **minus** for a lighter piece (`€65.00 − €3.67 metal`, not `+ €-3.67`).
  - **`purchaseCost` is deliberately still copied** — the owner confirmed the vendor charged €15 for both pieces, which is the whole reason the price should not scale.
  - Verified by driving the real sheet with every write stubbed: the reported case €80.17 → **€68.50**; source untouched; own `seqId` minted and `refType` dropped (the weight differs, so [[one-code-one-piece]] still applies); same weight → labour/margin unchanged; lighter → €61.33; per-gram → no repricing and no breakdown line; a per-item piece with **no** purchase cost (cost defaults to the metal, labour clamps to 0) still lands exactly on the target; gold 2.0 g €200 → 3.0 g €256.42; offline (no spot) saves with the old margin and shows no price line; EL + EN; console clean.
  - **Known, flagged, NOT changed:** `invRestock` copies `laborCost`/`itemMargin` the same way when it re-adds a piece at a different weight, so it has the same amplification (it also inherits the source's `initialRetail` rather than re-baselining). Left alone because a restock may legitimately arrive at a new vendor price — the owner's call whether to apply the same rule there.
- **Money owed on a sale — sell now, get paid later (v12.78):** the ATM was broken, the customers were old and trusted, so the owner handed over two reserved pieces (H-0022 €620, H-0021 €1350) and will be paid Monday. He needed the sales marked complete while still seeing that the money is outstanding. The app already models *"this sale arrived under several regimes at once"* — a gift-certificate portion settled months ago (v11.41) plus part cash / part card today (v12.68) — and `_saleExVatRaw` is the one place that knows the rule. A credit sale is the same idea with a **time** axis, so it slots into that machinery rather than beside it.
  - **`owedAmt` is a TIMING fact, not a payment regime**, and the two are orthogonal. How the sale will be settled (cash / receipt / split) is declared when it is recorded and is what decides the VAT and therefore the profit — exactly as before — while `owedAmt` records how much has not physically arrived. **`_saleExVatRaw` therefore needed no change at all**, which is the load-bearing property: proven by driving the real `addSaleRecord` across 24 amount/cost/cash/certificate combinations (no `owed*` key written, record shape byte-identical) and by recording €620 with and without a debt (profit €320 both ways, only the owed keys differ).
  - **The money counts on the day the pieces LEFT** (the owner's explicit choice): revenue and profit are booked then, and a payment later only clears the debt — never rewriting a past day, the same rule returns follow (v11.46). The counterweight is the debt bar.
  - Record shape, written **only when a debt exists** so an ordinary sale is byte-identical: `owedAmt` (still outstanding, counts down), `owedOrig` (so "€300 of €620" can be shown), `owedDue`, `owedLog` (`[{amt,date,method,at}]`), plus `owedWarned` / `owedWrittenOff`. A cart's debt is **spread across its lines in proportion**, exactly like a certificate and a cash split, so every record is self-describing and deleting one line cannot strand a balance on another (verified: €200 over €100/€100/€50 → 80/80/40, and a €120 payment leaves 32/32/16).
  - **The control is separate from the split control**, deliberately — the split answers *under what regime*, this answers *has it arrived*. `_OWED_*` mirrors `_SPLIT_*` (`_owedHtml`/`_owedMount`/`_owedOpen`/`_owedClear`/`_owedSync`/`_owedValue`/`_owedDueValue`/`_owedSet`), mounted from static wraps into the **sale** and **sale-edit** dialogs at boot so the two cannot drift. It reuses **`_splitPayable`**, so a debt can never exceed the amount less what a certificate already covered. Opens prefilled with the **whole payable** (nothing paid yet is the common case). `OWED_DEFAULT_DAYS = 7`. **`_owedClear` resets the due DATE as well as the amount** — the control lives in the DOM for the whole session, and without that "pay me by Monday" would still be sitting there for the next customer three weeks later (found in testing).
  - **A debt is never anonymous** — a customer name is required in both the sale and the sale-edit dialog, the same rule a return credit follows (v11.51). Without a name you have a number and nobody to ask.
  - **Surfaces:** an **always-visible red bar** at the top of Sales — deliberately **NOT scoped to the period on screen**, unlike the consignment bar (v12.66), because a debt is outstanding now whoever's day you are looking at and one that only appeared when you navigated back to the right day would be useless. Absent entirely when nothing is owed, louder when something is overdue. Plus a **count badge on the Sales nav tab** (visible from anywhere), a red `🕐 ΟΦΕΙΛΕΤΑΙ €X` tag on the sales row, an owed row + `💰 Καταγραφή πληρωμής` button in both the single and the whole-transaction detail, and a **"Δεν έχει εισπραχθεί ακόμα"** line in the sales breakdown — kept **out** of the cash/receipt split there, which is a VAT-regime breakdown whose parts must keep reconciling to the net revenue (re-verified: `Σcash + Σreceipt/(1+vat) === Σ_saleExVat`).
  - **Employees see only debts from sales they made** (`_owedVisibleSales`), the same scoping `renderSalesPanel` already applies — so whoever made the credit sale can take the money without the whole book being visible.
  - **Payments** (`openOwedPayment` / `_owedApplyPayment`): amount / method (cash·card·bank) / date, partial allowed, spread across a transaction's records in proportion with the last absorbing the rounding. **Write-off** (`_owedWriteOff`) books the loss as a **NEW €0-revenue, negative-profit record** dated today — the giveaway shape (v12.16) and the never-rewrite-history rule — so the day's takings are untouched and profit falls by exactly the ex-VAT value never received.
  - **A return cancels the DEBT, it does not hand money back.** `processReturn` reduces the debt in proportion to the refund, which makes what remains exactly what the customer actually paid (`refund = (paid + owed) × r`, so taking `owed × r` off leaves `paid × r`), and `creditAmt` is clamped to that — otherwise the app would issue store credit for money it never received. The return record **strips the owed fields** (it is a spread of the original) or it would show up as a second copy of the same debt; `debtCleared` is stamped so **`_undoReturn` puts the debt back**. Verified: a fully-unpaid €620 sale returned issues **no credit**, hands back nothing and nets to zero; 600-paid/400-owed returns €600; a half refund of that returns €300 and halves the debt; and **a fully-paid sale is byte-identical to before**.
  - **Overdue** (`_owedCheckOverdue`, from `autoSync` after the sales pull): one `debt_overdue` bell notification, red, marked warned **only if the push actually landed** and re-armed when the due date is pushed back. It is excluded from `msgCount` so it never puts a red *message* badge on Stock.
  - **Contrast:** the debt colour is a theme token **`--debt`** (`#ff8a80` dark / `#9B2314` light), not a literal — the pale salmon the app uses elsewhere measures **1.69:1** on the light theme's ivory. Now 5.88 / 6.37 against 7.10 / 7.61 in dark.
  - Guards: `_saveGroupEdit` refuses a total below what is still owed; `openReturnDialog` spells out what the refund will actually do before you press Record; `_retWillIssueCredit` stops demanding a customer name for a credit it is not going to create.
  - *(Testing notes: `openSaleEditModal` is **async** — reading its DOM without awaiting gives convincing false failures. `processReturn` returns `{rec, card, creditAmt, giftForced}`, not the record. And `sed -i` in Git Bash **strips every CR** and rewrote the whole CRLF file as LF — a 39,395-line diff for an 11-line change; use the Edit tool, not sed, on this file.)*
- **List price + discount in the sale detail (v12.79):** the owner sold a €811.91 pendant for €690 and the sheet said nothing about what it had been on the shelf for — the discount he gave was invisible. The breakdown (owner/manager, above "Amount excl. VAT") now reads **«Τιμή Καταλόγου €811.91 · Έκπτωση −€121.91 (−15.0%)»**, reusing the Calc breakdown's existing `rListPrice`/`rDiscount` keys rather than inventing labels ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]).
  - **ONE source: `listPrice`, stamped on the record at sale time** (`confirmSale`, first record and every cart line) and frozen there — the price that was actually on the card the day the customer paid. **There is deliberately NO fallback to pricing the linked piece now.** A first version had one, labelled «(σήμερα)» / "(today)"; the owner asked the right question — *"if the item is sold the list price should stay exactly as when it was sold, is this the case?"* — and it was not: verified by moving the gold price +20%, a stored figure held at €811.91 while a derived one drifted €811.90 → €933.16. So a derived figure would keep moving after the piece had left the shop and report a discount that was never given. **Sales recorded before v12.79 therefore show no list price at all** — it was never written down, and the app keeps **no gold-price history** (verified: no such node exists) to reconstruct it from. Better a missing row than a plausible wrong one.
  - **`_saleListPriceRows`** is shared by the single and the transaction view so the two can never look different ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]). The helper touches no inventory at all, so the sheet needs no stock load for it (verified with the cache emptied).
  - **Silent rather than wrong:** a **return** (its amount is a negative mirror — and `processReturn` now also `delete rec.listPrice`, since a spread of the original would otherwise carry the asking price onto a record that never asked anything), a **giveaway** (nothing was paid, so "100% discount" is nonsense), a **bulk chain** (the batch's retail covers its whole remaining weight, not the grams sold — never stored), and a **premium** (a sale above the asking price is a rounding). A **transaction** shows the summed asking price only when EVERY line carries one — a partial sum would look like the total and silently under-state it.
  - `listPrice` is written **only when a piece is behind the sale**, so a manual sale's record is byte-identical to before (verified: the key is absent), and it **survives every later edit** — `confirmSaleEdit` spreads the record and `_saveGroupEdit` mutates fields in place (verified: correcting an amount, a date and a transaction total all keep it and just re-derive the discount). Discount colour is `var(--gold-light)`, a theme token — measured 12.12:1 dark / 4.86:1 light.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's exact sale: €811.91 / −€121.91 (−15.0%) above his unchanged €690.00 / €365.36 / €324.64 (47.0%); frozen against a +20% gold jump and with the inventory cache emptied; his own pre-v12.79 sale showing no row; EL + EN; every silent case incl. the return record coming out with the key deleted; a 2-piece transaction summing €1250.77 (−20.0%) and staying silent when one line lacks it; `confirmSale` storing it on a single sale and on both cart lines (proportional split untouched), never on the bulk line, and not at all on a manual sale; employee sees no breakdown; no overflow at 375px. The one console error on load is the **pre-existing** orphaned `renderSalesDashboard` (v11.46) — confirmed identical on the pre-change file.
- **Materials — stock that goes INTO a job (v12.80, refined v12.81):** a customer walked in and asked the owner to turn a pendant he had **in stock** (PE0543) into a ring; he wrote the custom order and took a deposit, but the pendant stayed listed as ordinary free stock — sellable, and on the public showroom. The v12.65 `invIds` link was the wrong shape for it: that models a piece that **ARRIVES from a vendor and is sold as it is**, and it refuses bulk chains outright, which the owner says are common in custom work.
  - **The distinction, which is the whole design:** a **PRODUCT** arrives and is sold at the agreed price (`_ordCompleteSale`); a **MATERIAL** is already yours and is **CONSUMED**. So a material is held exactly like a product but collecting does not sell it — it leaves stock and its cost becomes part of what the job cost you. New field **`materials: [{invId, grams, bulk, done}]`**, a RECORD ID and never a code string ([[link-records-by-id-not-string]]).
  - **Allowed on EVERY ticket kind, not just orders** — a repair on the customer's own necklace may still take 3 g of your chain, and that metal is a real cost of the job. Not on a **quote**: nothing is committed until the customer has, so a quote holds no stock.
  - **The hold is derived, never a second flag** ([[derived-not-copied-flags]]). `_ordHeldIds(r)` unions the products with the materials and feeds the existing `_ordActiveByItem`/`_ordHoldFor` → `_holdFor` → `_isHeld`, so the 🔒 badge, the Reserved filter, `publicProjection` (off the showroom — verified true→false) and all ten sale/gift/reserve guards were inherited with **no new call sites**. Cancelling the ticket releases everything by itself, with zero writes.
  - **A by-the-gram batch is a QUANTITY, not a piece, so it is NEVER held** — holding it would block selling a chain you still have 42 g of. Instead the grams are shown as promised on the batch card (`_ordBulkPromised`, live tickets only) and come off at collection. `_ordGramsAsk` asks for them **when the chain is attached** (the owner's choice) so the card can warn before the last of it is sold; it refuses more than the batch has, counting what other jobs already claimed.
  - **Cost uses the sale path's single source of truth** — `_invSaleCost` for a piece (an other-type piece keeps its cost in the QR, so `purchaseCost||baseCost` would report €0, [[other-type-cost-in-qr]]) and `_bulkSoldCost` for grams. A line whose cost cannot be worked out is counted as **unknown and said so**, never silently as zero.
  - **The collect box does the arithmetic**: `🧩 Materials €776.98` + `Your cost ___` = `Total cost`, recomputed at confirm rather than read off the screen. With no materials the dialog is **byte-identical to before** (no box, label still plain "Cost", `finalCost` = exactly what was typed) — verified. Stores `materialsCost`/`extraCost` alongside `finalCost`.
  - **`_ordConsumeMaterials` is idempotent via `done`** — re-running must not deduct the same grams twice, which is unrecoverable — and every write is **checked and rolled back on failure** ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]]), reporting the count rather than leaving a lie in the cache. A consumed piece carries **`usedInOrder`**, so its card reads «🧩 Χρησιμοποιήθηκε O-0001» and its sold-card button opens the job instead of `invGoToSale`'s dead end + wrong "restore to stock?" prompt (v11.62).
  - **Mixed products + materials** (an ordered ring plus 5 g of your chain): `_ordFinalizeCollect(tid, total, firstRec)` adds the materials cost to the first sale record of the transaction and re-derives its profit through `_saleExVat` — left off, the profit would read high by exactly what that stock was worth. Verified: cost 300 → 658.50, profit 500 → 141.50, chain 50 g → 45 g.
  - **The attach sheet is the SAME sheet in two modes** (`openOrderAddPieces(id, mode)`), so the camera loop, code resolution and list rendering are not forked. Material mode allows bulk, refuses raw metal by name (it has its own "− Remove", which keeps the pile's average fineness right), and turns the code box into a **search box** — a chain batch's tag is not something you scan at the counter, so it is found by code or description and tapped. Every outcome is named: unknown code, not in stock, already on this job, held by another ticket.
  - `_holdFor` gained **`openLabel`** so the confirm button names the actual ticket («Άνοιγμα · Ειδική κατασκευή», not "Open the order") now that a repair can hold stock too.
  - **The cost is what the stock cost YOU, and the selling price is shown beside it (v12.81).** The owner's first live use raised it: the collect box read €195.56 for a pendant whose card says €457.42, and he asked for the retail figure — *"I did pay the vendor to create it, it's not just metal"*. Both are defensible and they answer different questions, so it was put to him with the numbers: costing it at retail answers "was this job worth more than just selling the pendant?", but the €261.86 margin already earned on it would then be **counted as profit nowhere**, leaving the month lighter than the money actually taken (and retail carries VAT, which every other cost in the app excludes). He chose to keep the true cost and see the selling price too. `_ordMaterialsCost` now also returns **`retail`** — `calcInvItemPrice().retail` for a piece, and `retail / weight × grams` for a cut of chain, **the same per-gram the bulk-sell modal uses** (both verified equal to the figure on screen) — shown as a dim «Θα πουλιόνταν προς €X» / "Would have sold for €X" line under the cost in the ticket block and the collect box, with one line saying why. It is **never booked as a cost**; an unpriceable piece reports null rather than a fabricated number.
  - **There is deliberately NO "un-collect".** Deleting the job's income record does **not** restore a consumed piece (verified: it stays `sold`) — the income record carries no `invId` for it, and there is no un-collect for any ticket kind. Put to the owner with the option of wiring it; his answer: *"if the sale completes I don't see the reason why it will come back, so let's just leave it as it is and I'll manually restock."* The escape hatch for a mistaken collection is Stock → filter **Sold** → **Restock**. Do not build an auto-restore thinking this was an oversight.
  - Verified end to end by driving the real functions on the owner's exact case: pendant €180 + 8 g chain €596.98 = €776.98 materials, + €120 goldsmith = €896.98 cost, €1050 cash → **profit €153.02**; pendant sold + `usedInOrder`, chain 50 g → 42 g, hold released, promised grams back to 0. Plus attaching writing **0** stock records, idempotent re-consume, rollback on a forced write failure, cancel releasing with 0 writes, the sale/giveaway guards naming the ticket, the search + grams overlay driven by real clicks (including the over-grams refusal), EL + EN, light + dark, no overflow at 375px, console clean.

- **Gold traded in as part payment (v12.82):** a customer bought earrings and handed over **1.2 g of old 14K** towards the price. The app could record part cash / part card (v12.68) and part certificate (v11.41) but had no way to take metal, so the only options were to type a fake discount or lose the trade entirely. Researched first at the owner's request: a straight cash buy-out of scrap runs **60–80% of melt** (pawn shops ~50%), but a trade-in **against a purchase** is consistently **85–95%**, because the shop also earns retail margin on the piece going out — so his own instinct of 90% was right, and 10% is the default.
  - **It is a PAYMENT PORTION, never a discount.** A €200 piece part-paid with €76.54 of gold stays a **€200 sale**; reducing it would destroy the revenue and the record that the piece ever sold for €200 — the same rule the gift certificate follows. So it slots in as a **third portion** beside the certificate and the money: `amount = certificate + tradeIn + cash + card`.
  - **`_saleExVatRaw` gained one clause and nothing else.** The trade-in is clamped and signed exactly as the certificate portion is (a return carries a negative `tradeInAmt`), and added under the **cash regime** — no bank trace, so no VAT, which is the owner's decision and matches how the split's cash half already behaves. **Proven byte-identical for every record with no trade-in: 7,168 combinations** (8 amounts × 8 certificate values × 7 cash splits × 2 gift regimes × 2 cash flags × 4 VAT rates), **0 differing, delta exactly 0**.
  - **Two things happen and they reconcile.** The sale records the portion; the metal enters the raw-metal stock as a genuine purchase whose price paid IS the credit given. On a €200 sale of an €80 piece taking €76.54 of gold the app books **€120.00 sale profit + €5.95 scrap gain = €125.95**, against a true (€123.46 cash + €82.49 recoverable) − €80 = **€125.95**.
  - **`_metalAddToLot(spec, paid, noteLbl)` was EXTRACTED from `_metalConfirm`** so the trade-in and the **Add Metal** button cannot drift ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]) — merge into the existing lot of that metal+karat else create one, then book the buy-cheap gain. It now **checks the write and rolls back** (Add Metal previously reported success unconditionally) and returns the gain record's id so the gain can be unbooked later.
  - **The control** (`_TI_*`, mirroring `_SPLIT_*`/`_OWED_*`, mounted into the sale and sale-edit dialogs from static wraps at boot): **karat dropdown** — the owner's request was "just gold and the list of carats", so gold karats come first with silver in a second `<optgroup>` and the metal is implied by the choice, needing no second control — plus weight, live melt, an editable deduction %, an **editable credit** (a hand-typed figure is marked `manual` so a recompute never overwrites the negotiated number, and changing metal/weight/% clears that flag because the figure was for different gold), and a live "cash due". Rebuilt on each open so the karat labels follow a language switch. Capped at the sale: more gold than that is **buying** metal, and it says so and points at Add Metal rather than silently clamping.
  - **Everything downstream measures itself against what is LEFT.** `_splitPayable` and the owed cap subtract the trade-in (you cannot be owed money for gold already handed over), and `_splitResolve` takes an optional 5th `tradeInPart` — omitted by every existing caller, so identical.
  - **`_saleCashPart` deliberately EXCLUDES the trade-in** (no money hit the till, so counting it would overstate the cash) while **`_sbPayParts` adds it back into its cash bucket** — that is what keeps `Σcash + Σreceipt/(1+vat) === Σ_saleExVat` exact while letting the screen name the gold. **Verified to the cent (delta 0)** across a day mixing plain cash, plain card, gold+cash, gold+card, gold+split, certificate+gold+card, a scrap gain and a return.
  - **A cart spreads it proportionally** (€76.54 over €100/€60/€40 → 38.27/22.96/15.31, summing exactly), but the **physical metal is stamped on the FIRST record only** — grams, purity, melt, lot id and gain id. Splitting a 1.2 g jewel four ways would describe four pieces of gold that do not exist, and the reversal would then take the grams out four times.
  - **Reversal on DELETE, reporting on RETURN — deliberately different.** A delete is an erasure, so `_tradeInReverseForSale` takes the grams back out (`_metalRemoveFromLot`, refusing rather than guessing when the lot is gone or too small) and unbooks the gain via `deleteSaleRecord` (one level of recursion, no loop). A **return** does NOT touch stock: the gold is handed back over the counter, a partial refund does not map onto a partial weight, and by then it may be melted — so the record's `tradeInAmt` is scaled+negated (the books unwind under the right regime), the gold's share is **excluded from `_refundable`** so it can never become store credit, and a one-button `showConfirm` names the exact grams to hand over.
  - **`_metalRemoveFromLot` takes an optional `exact = {cost, melt}`** — found in testing: undoing a €76.54 buy out of a €1,276.54 pile scaled the cost to €72.26 and **stranded €4.28**. When a specific purchase is being undone those figures are known, so they come straight off; the generic "− Remove" flow, which cannot know what any gram cost, still scales. Verified the pile returns to exactly 20 g / 11.7 pure / €1200 / €1417.33.
  - **A failed stock write does not abort the sale** — the money genuinely changed hands and the customer is standing there. The record is written with **`tradeInNoStock`** (so a later delete does not go hunting for a lot and wrongly shrink metal this sale never added) and the toast says to add it by hand.
  - **Edit** reopens the control and corrects the value or the %; the metal's own description, lot link and gain id are left alone, because an edit is not moving gold. **Removing** a trade-in from a recorded sale is **refused** (`tiNoRemove`) — the metal is in the safe and a gain is booked against it, so clearing it from a money form would orphan both.
  - Surfaces: the sale-detail **Payment** row (`💵 €123.46 μετρητά + 🧱 €76.54 σε χρυσό`), a **🧱 Gold** row naming the metal (`1.20g Χρυσός 14K · αξία €85.04 −10%`), the **SPLIT badge** (correct in all cases: gold+cash is wholly cash-regime so it still reads CASH; gold+card genuinely is mixed), the transaction detail, an **"of which, in gold"** line in the sales breakdown, and the printed quote/certificate making **no VAT claim** (only part went through the till). Setting: **«Έκπτωση αξίας χρυσού»** / "Gold value deduction", cloud-synced, read through `_tradeInDeductPct()` so a device with an older saved state falls back to 10 rather than `undefined` (the v12.14 lesson).
  - **Extended to the order/repair COLLECTION dialog (v12.83)** — deferred in v12.82 and then asked for. The `repCollect` host already existed in `_SPLIT_HOSTS`, so the control mounted with one line; the work was the money path. `_recordRepairIncome` takes the trade-in exactly as `addSaleRecord` does (same clamp, same cash regime, threaded through `_splitResolve`/`_saleExVatRaw`, fields stored only when present) and now also queues to the **outbox** (v11.65), which it never did — income booked with no signal used to be droppable by the next pull.
    - **Offered on REPAIRS as well as orders, unlike the gift certificate**, which is gated to `repIsOrder` because a certificate buys jewelry. Metal is money-like and can pay for labour on the customer's own piece just as well.
    - **The gold enters stock OUTSIDE the "log as income" branch.** The customer handed metal over whether or not the job's money is booked, so it belongs in the safe either way — verified: with logging off, 0 income records but the lot and the scrap gain are still created. *(Consequence to know: in that case no sale record carries `tradeInLotId`, so there is no automatic reversal — exactly as when the Add Metal button is used directly, where "− Remove" is the undo.)*
    - **Reversal came free.** `deleteSaleRecord` → `_tradeInReverseForSale` runs for **any** record carrying a trade-in, whatever created it, so deleting the repair-income record takes the grams back out and unbooks the gain. Verified end to end.
    - **A real bug fixed in passing:** the ticket detail's Profit line recomputed its own ex-VAT as `cashSale ? price : price/(1+vat)`, which knows nothing about a trade-in — on a receipted €500 job part-paid with €179.89 of gold it read **€307.35 instead of €333.49**. It now goes through `_saleExVatRaw` ([[other-type-cost-in-qr]] generalised: one source for money). *(Still not passed there: the certificate portion, which that line has always ignored — pre-existing, the authoritative profit is on the sale record.)*
    - `_tiRefreshGift(p)` replaced the hard-coded `if (p === 'sale') _updateSaleGiftDisplay()` so whichever host owns a certificate box has its "still to collect" line follow the gold; `_repUpdateGiftDisplay` subtracts the trade-in like `_updateSaleGiftDisplay` does.
    - **"Worth" row + the gain made visible on the sale (v12.84).** The owner's first real trade — a €55 pair of earrings settled with 1.2 g of 14K — exposed two presentation problems, neither of them arithmetic. (1) The box showed the metal value and the deduction but never their difference, so the figure that matters (€84.91 − €8.49 = **€76.42**) had to be done in the head; a **`{p}TiNet` "Αξίζει" / "Worth"** row now sits under the deduction. The credit below can legitimately differ from it — capped by the sale, or hand-negotiated — and **the gap between the two IS the bargain**, which is what makes showing both worth the line. (2) The sale detail showed `Profit €30.85` and nothing else, so he reasonably asked whether the app had understated what he made. It had not — the transaction earned **€30.85 sale profit + €27.36 gain on the metal = €58.21**, which equals the direct calculation (€84.91 melt × 0.97 recoverable − €24.15 item cost = €58.21) to the cent — but the gain is a *separate record* in today's takings with nothing linking it. **`_tradeInGainRows(gain, profit, cur, mkRow)`** now draws «♻️ Κέρδος στον χρυσό» and «Σύνολο από τη συναλλαγή» under the Profit row, on both the single and the transaction view. **The Profit row itself deliberately still shows only the sale's own profit**: it has to agree with the sales list, and the gain must stay in the `scrap` segment or v12.74's jewelry-only margin breaks. **Answer to record for next time: the app is right to call this a €55 sale.** Booking €84.91 of revenue for a piece asking €56.51 would make the discount line nonsense, and would overstate profit by the refining loss he will actually pay. The extra value is a metal-buying gain, and that is where it belongs.
    - **The over-value warning was rewritten** because it was wrong for the commonest case. It said "record it with 🧱 Add Metal instead" — telling the owner he had made a mistake when he had made a good trade the customer accepted. It now explains that the credit was capped at the sale, that this is correct if the customer accepts it, that the difference stays as gain on the metal, and points at Add Metal **only** for the case that genuinely needs it: when change has to be handed back, which the app cannot express as one transaction.
    - Verified by driving the real functions: a €500 order collected with 2.82 g of 14K (melt €199.88 → credit €179.89 → due €320.11, lot created, gain €13.99, exVat €500 all-cash); the receipted variant at exVat **€453.49** matching the hand check exactly; a repair ticket showing the trade-in control and **no** certificate button; logging off; the reversal emptying the lot; a no-trade-in collection **byte-identical** in record shape with 0 lots and 0 gains; the ticket detail rendering `🧱 Χρυσός → −€179.89 · 2.82g Χρυσός 14K` with profit €333.49 in EL and EN; no overflow at 375 px; zero errors captured by an in-page listener.
  - **`renderSalesDashboard` finally removed (shipped inside v12.82).** Flagged as orphaned dead code in the v11.46 entry and again in v12.79 but never deleted, it threw `TypeError: Cannot set properties of null` on its very first line at **every** call, because the three ids it wrote to (`salesTodayAmt`/`salesMonthAmt`/`salesYearAmt`) — and the `salesTodayBadge`/`salesList` elements it filled — had all been taken out of the markup long ago. **Proven pre-existing by reproducing it on the pristine committed file**, not assumed. Its two live call sites are gone: `showPanel('settings')` and — **not `confirmSale` as previously recorded, but `confirmSaleEdit`**, so it fired when a sale was *corrected*, not when one was recorded. Its only helper, `getSaleSummary`, had no other caller and went with it; `fmtAmt` stays (22 other users). The Sales tab is rendered by `renderSalesPanel()`. **Left alone deliberately:** the now-unused i18n key `salesToday` and its already-orphaned siblings `salesTitle`/`salesMonth`/`salesYear` — four two-word strings sharing one dense line, worth sweeping together some day rather than churning that line now (`salesNoSales` is still used in 5 live places, `totalSales` in 2). Verified with an in-page `error`/`unhandledrejection` listener: opening Settings and editing a sale now capture **zero** errors, and a fresh tab loads with a clean console.
  - Verified by driving the real functions in a browser: the owner's exact case (melt €85.04 → −€8.50 → credit €76.54 → due €123.46, lot created 1.2 g/0.702 pure/€76.54 cost, gain €5.95, exVat €200); delete restoring the pile and removing the gain; merge into an existing pile; the cart spread; the over-credit cap; a full return (credit €123.46 **not** €200, net profit exactly 0, piece restored) and a half return (€61.73, 0.6 g); Edit reopen/correct/refuse-remove and a **plain sale round-tripping byte-identically**; all 19 new i18n keys in both dictionaries with whole-dictionary sync still 0/0; EL+EN; no clipping or overflow at 375 px; light and dark. *(The one console error, `renderSalesDashboard`, is pre-existing dead code — proven by reproducing it on the pristine committed file; flagged separately.)*
- **"Customer's gold" check on Calc — exchange vs cash (v12.85):** the owner spotted the limitation himself. The trade-in control lives INSIDE the sale dialog, so it could only answer "what is this gold worth?" **after** the customer had chosen a piece — while the real counter sequence is the reverse: they put gold down, ask what they will get, and the answer often decides whether they buy at all.
  - **TWO rates, which was his design and is the one the research supports.** Against a purchase the shop also earns retail margin on the piece going out, so metal can be taken at 85–95% of melt; a straight cash buy-out earns nothing but the metal and runs 60–80%. New setting **`cashBuyDeduct`** (default **20**, `_cashBuyDeductPct()`, cloud-synced) beside the existing `tradeInDeduct` (10), both relabelled «Έκπτωση χρυσού — ανταλλαγή / μετρητά». One rate would either rob the exchanging customer or leave money on the table when buying outright.
  - **Both prices shown at once**, because that IS the conversation — "this much if you exchange it, this much if you want money". `openGoldCheck()` sits under the Live Market card on Calc (his choice of placement: it is the same question from the other side of the counter), top-anchored per the typing-sheet rule, sharing `_tiKaratOptions` and the same melt maths as the sale control so the two can never disagree.
  - **Exchange → HOLD, nothing written.** `_gcheckArm` stores `_goldCheckPending` and `_consumePendingGoldCheck()` is picked up by **`openSaleModalWithBonus`** — the one funnel every sale-modal opener goes through, exactly where a held return credit is consumed. Single-use and ignored after 30 minutes, so a quote can never quietly attach to an unrelated customer later in the day. **A quote must be able to end in "no thanks" leaving no trace** — verified: arming writes 0 records and 0 lots.
  - **Cash → pays out and commits**, through `_metalAddToLot` — the *same* path as the Add Metal button, lot merge and buy-cheap gain included. This only saves walking to the Stock tab and working the price out by hand. `showConfirm`-gated with the amount and weight named; gated on `can('manage_stock')` like Add Metal, while the **quote itself is all-roles** (an employee at the counter has to be able to answer the question) — the Pay button simply does not render for them and the hint says so.
  - **Bug found in testing:** `_consumePendingGoldCheck` first went through `_tiSet`, which reopens a saved *record* and clamps the credit against **that record's own `amount`** — a synthetic object has none, so it clamped to zero and the hand-off silently did nothing. It now fills the control directly and marks the credit `manual` (the figure was agreed with the customer; a recompute must not move it), while `_tiSync` still caps it if the piece they choose is worth less than the gold.
  - Verified by driving the real functions with 4 g of 14K at €3768/oz: melt **€283.48**, exchange −10% → **€255.13**, cash −20% → **€226.78**, all matching the hand check. Exchange hand-off carries karat/weight/%/credit into a €400 sale with €144.87 still to collect, records €400 with a €255.13 trade-in and a €19.84 gain; a **cheaper €150 piece** correctly caps the credit to 150 with the over-value note. Cash pays €226.78, puts 4 g in stock and books a **€48.19** gain — bigger than the exchange gain, which is right, because buying outright earns only on the metal. Stale hold ignored at 31 minutes; employee sees both quotes, one Hold button, zero Pay buttons; EL + EN; no overflow at 375 px.
- **The deduction rate is never shown, and lives only in Settings (v12.86):** the owner turns the phone towards the customer, so a visible «−10%» hands them his margin. The percentage was removed from BOTH surfaces — the gold-check rows now read «🔄 Για ανταλλαγή €254.71» with no rate, and the sale box's editable `%` field became a **hidden input** (kept, not deleted, so `_tiSet` can still restore the rate a past sale was actually agreed at, and `_tiSpec` needed no change). The euro deduction line stays: the customer can subtract two numbers anyway, and the owner needs the arithmetic to add up.
  - **Per-sale flexibility survives without the rate** — the «Για τον χρυσό» amount is still editable, so a better price for a big lot is typed directly. Only the *percentage* is now owner-only, which is what he asked for.
  - **Research he asked for, with real Greek numbers** (spot-relative, so ±2 points as gold moves): an Athens gold buyer's own published buy list — 24K €110/g, 22K €95, 18K €78, 14K €61, 9K €34.50 — works out at roughly **91% of melt for 24K, ~86% for 14–22K, and ~76% for 9K**, i.e. deductions of **9% / 14% / 24%**. Internally consistent regardless of spot: their implied pure-gold rate is ~€110/g for 24K, ~€104 for the mid karats and ~€92 for 9K. International sources put local jewellers at 40–60% of melt, specialist buyers at 70–85%, pawn shops ~40%. **So the recommendation given: exchange 8–10% (he also earns retail margin on the piece, and this is the number that closes the sale), cash 15–20% (≈14% is the Athens benchmark; an island with no competing buyer justifies the top of that).**
  - **Flagged, not built: the deduction arguably belongs PER KARAT.** The Athens list deducts 9K nearly three times as hard as 24K, because low-karat gold is mostly alloy to refine away per gram of gold recovered. The app uses one rate for every karat, which is generous on 9K and stingy on 24K. One rate per karat in `METAL_STOCK_KARATS` would fix it; do not build unprompted.
  - **Also flagged (Greek law, relevant now that he buys for cash):** buying gold from a private individual requires seeing ID/passport **and** ΑΦΜ for ANY amount, and issuing a purchase document with item, weight, purity, price/g and date-time; over €10,000 the payment must go to the customer's bank account. The app records the metal and the price but does **not** capture the seller's identity — worth knowing before the cash path is used often.
- **"Add similar items" demanded a weight from a piece that has none (v12.87):** the owner cloned an "other"-type piece (a kid's picture frame, `PRIKO-8031-18.5`) in a different colour and got «Βάλτε βάρος» — with nothing to type it into, since an **other-type piece has no weight at all**: its cost is encoded in the QR and its price is `cost × multiplier`, never grams. `invAddSimilar` had recognised this in one place and not the other — `_canRegenQR` is already `source.metal !== 'other'` (so the code is never rebuilt from a weight), yet the form still rendered the weight box and `addBatch` still blocked on `isNaN(w) || w <= 0`. `_effWeight()` falls back to `parseFloat(source.weight)`, which is `NaN` for such a piece, so the flow was **unreachable for every other-type item** — the batch-create path simply did not exist for the ~47 fashion pieces in stock.
  - **Fix:** one `_isOtherSrc = source.metal === 'other'` gate. The weight block is not rendered (every `wEl()` use is `?.`-guarded, and the focus falls to Quantity instead), the Quantity label drops its «(ίδιο βάρος)» qualifier since there is no weight to be the same as, and the guard is skipped. The clone **inherits the source's stored weight verbatim** (`_newW`), so a piece that stores no `weight` key gets none — never `NaN`, which would have poisoned `calcInvItemPrice`, the CSV export and the pure-grams totals.
  - **The colour still earns its own reference code**, and here that is load-bearing rather than incidental: `_canRegenQR` is false for other-type, so the pink and the blue frame carry the **identical descriptive `qrCode`** and the ref code is the ONLY thing that can tell them apart on a tag ([[one-code-one-piece]]). Verified: the blue clone minted its own `seqId` while an identical-colour clone still shared the source's, so "12 identical pieces, one code, N copies of one label" is untouched.
  - Verified by driving the real function in a browser: the reported case adds (no weight key on the saved record, own ref code, both pieces sharing a `groupId`); a 3-piece batch prints **one job with `copies: 3`** carrying the ref code and stamps all three via `alsoIds`; an identical clone shares `XX0900`; and the **metal path is unchanged in every respect** — field present with its «ίδιο με ομάδα (2.7g)» placeholder, blank → uses the group's weight and shares the code (v10.79), typed 3.1 → `AMA925S3.1` and its own number, and a piece with no usable weight **still refuses** with «Βάλτε βάρος» and writes nothing. EL + EN, no overflow at 375px, console clean.
  - **The vendor code is editable there too (same version).** The owner's follow-up: a different colour is a different article to the supplier, so it carries a different vendor code — and `invAddSimilar` copied `vendorCode` blindly through `Object.assign` with no field to change it, which nearly pushed him back to adding each colour as a brand-new item. **Note which code that is:** for an other-type piece the descriptive code's middle segment (`PRIKO-`**`8031`**`-18.5`) is NOT a supplier reference — `buildLabelStrings` sets it as `String(Date.now()).slice(-4)`, a uniqueness token — so the thing he means is the separate free-text `item.vendorCode` field («Κωδικός Προμηθευτή», `SK02001`-style) that Edit Item and label creation already carry and the card shows in gold. A prefilled input was added to the sheet, read **once per batch** (like `_swapped`, so a field touched mid-save can't give two pieces of one batch different codes), and **cleared → the key is deleted**, never left inheriting an article number that belongs to another piece. Offered for **every** item type, not just other-type — the reasoning is about the supplier's numbering, not the metal. Reuses the existing `vendorCodeLabel`/`vendorCodePh` i18n keys, so no new strings. **`_refIdentityKey` was deliberately NOT extended to include it** — it is used by `_printAsRefTag` and `_refCheckSplitAfterEdit` across the app, and in practice a vendor-code change arrives alongside a colour change, which already splits the ref code. Verified: prefilled from the source, lowercase input uppercased, the blue clone saved `SK02002` while the source kept `SK02001`, an untouched field inherits exactly as before, a cleared field leaves no key, a 3-piece metal batch all took `AM-7782` with the correct weight and QR, and the field's right edge measures identical to the Quantity box in EL and EN at 375px with the sheet still not needing to scroll.
- **The cart is editable, and a catalog quote has somewhere to go (v12.88):** the owner built a 4-piece quote (€12,525.26) and wanted to shave the prices before printing it — but a cart line was write-once, so changing one price meant **clearing the whole cart and re-scanning every piece**. Three of those four pieces were **not in stock at all**: he was quoting from a vendor CATALOG and would order them once the customer agreed. Both halves were real gaps.
  - **Per-line price edit.** The price in each cart row is now a button (dashed underline + ✏️) → `openCartLineEdit(idx)`: a top-anchored dialog showing the **list price** and the **calculated price**, a prefilled price field, a live «−3.2% (−€245.86) έκπτωση από την τιμή καταλόγου» preview, and — only when the line has actually been changed — **↩️ Reset** back to the calculated figure and its own original discount.
  - **`_cartApplyLinePrice` is deliberately the SAME rule as `applyRoundedPrice`** (the calc's rounding box), so the two can never drift: the typed figure IS the price, and the discount is back-calculated against that piece's `retail`. **Rounding UP clamps the discount at 0** rather than storing a negative one that would print as "-0.24% discount" on the quote (verified). It also refreshes `preVat`/`vatAmount`, which `applyRoundedPrice` updates only on screen — a cart line's are **stored** by `_quoteHistItem`, so a stale pre-VAT would describe a price that no longer exists.
  - **Nothing else in the line is touched** — `retail`, `_originalFinalPrice`/`_originalDiscount(Amt)`, `invId`, `baseCost` and the bulk-chain fields all stay, so the edited line still reserves/sells/prints as itself. `profit`/`margin` are left alone on purpose: they are calc-time breakdown values and **no code reads them off a cart line** (checked).
  - **Cart → Order (`orderFromCart`), the missing step.** `markCartAsSold` and `reserveCartItems` both resolve every line to a record in stock (`_resolveCart`) and can only **drop** the ones they cannot place — so for a catalog quote neither had anything to work with, and `_cartMissingOk` just refused. The step that actually follows is "the customer said yes, now order it", which lives in **Desk → Orders** and had to be retyped by hand. A **📦 Παραγγελία / Order** button in the cart now opens the order modal prefilled: the cart's lines become the **description** (numbered, with metal · weight · price — so they print on the ticket and go out in the quote message) and the subtotal becomes **`estLow`**, the agreed price. Leaving the **deposit blank still saves it as a `quote` ticket** (v12.65 behaviour, untouched) and opens the detail so it can be sent; entering one makes it an order.
  - **The pieces are linked when they ARRIVE**, via the order's existing "Add pieces" (`openOrderAddPieces`) — the only moment a record for them exists. Collect then sells them at the agreed price via `_ordCompleteSale`. Nothing new was needed there.
  - **`ov.dataset.fromCart`, not a global flag** — it lives on the order overlay and dies with it, so it cannot go stale; `_repairSave` reads it beside `ov.dataset.type` and, when set, **clears the cart** after saving. Same reasoning as a hold made from the cart (v12.26 `_resDialogSave`): the deal has moved to the Desk, and a full cart would invite a later "Sell all" on pieces that are on order. Verified a plain Desk → new order leaves the cart untouched.
  - **`_cartMissingOk` now points somewhere** when nothing at all is in stock («Κανένα είδος στο απόθεμα — για κατάλογο πατήστε «📦 Παραγγελία»») instead of only refusing.
  - **New theme token `--order`** (`#c9a0e0` dark / `#6B3E8F` light). The purple the Desk uses for orders measures **2.19:1** on the light theme's white — the same trap as `--debt` (v12.78); now 8.36 / 7.75. *(The pre-existing literal `#c9a0e0` uses inside the Desk order modal were left alone.)*
  - **The by-the-gram chain sale had a bare name box (same version).** Reported from the counter: he typed `M` for Miranda Beshiri and nothing autocompleted, with no phone or email field anywhere — `openBulkSellSheet` was the ONE sale dialog still carrying a hand-rolled `<input id="bulkSellCustomer">` instead of the shared **`_saleCustBlockHtml`** (v12.72, autocomplete off the Clients book + `_phoneFieldHtml` + email) that the sale, sale-edit, gift and return dialogs all use. Swapped in with the sheet's own field styling passed as `o.fld`, so it is visually identical to the boxes above it (**verified: weight, name, email, phone and date all end at the same right edge, 354px, in EL and EN at 375px**). `confirmBulkSell` now reads `_saleCustRead('bulkSell')`, passes the **email** into `addSaleRecord`'s 9th argument (it was hardcoded `''`), and — the part that actually loses data — calls **`upsertClientFromSale(name, phone, email)`** instead of `autoSaveClientFromQuote(customer, '')`, **which takes no phone at all**, so a number typed here went nowhere. Verified end to end: typing `M` suggests «Miranda Beshiri +30 694…» and picking her fills all three fields; the sale records `customer` + `email`; the batch drops 42.2 → 38.5 g; a **new** customer is created with the typed phone; an **existing** phone-less client is updated in place (same id, no duplicate); no customer at all still records the sale and creates nothing; the country picker opens above the sheet (z 1000000 vs 99997) and writes back (`+47 41234567`); and **Add to cart** is unaffected.   - **A stored amount is always cents (same version).** The bulk path wrote an unrounded `amount` — **€484.3001891** against a screen reading €484.30 — because `_bulkSellCalc`'s `final` is a per-gram product (`3.7 × 184.527…`) and nothing rounded it; every display used `.toFixed(2)`, so it was invisible until a total came out a cent short. Fixed at **three** levels rather than only at the report: (1) `_bulkSellCalc` rounds `final` to the cent and re-derives `discAmt = base − final` from it, so the preview, the badge's "Save €X", the recorded sale, the cart line's `finalPrice`/`_originalFinalPrice` and the cost all come from ONE figure; (2) **`addSaleRecord` rounds `_amt` once at the top** and the record, the split, the ex-VAT value and the profit all derive from it — a structural net so no future caller can store a fraction of a cent ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]); (3) `confirmSaleEdit` rounds its typed amount the same way. **Proven a no-op for every existing caller: 432 combinations** (9 amounts × 4 costs × cash × gift portion × 3 VAT rates) — **0 records differ**, because every other caller already passes a `.toFixed(2)` value. The rounding-target branch still honours the entered figure exactly (450 / 475.50 / 150, verified). *An old unrounded record cleans itself up if the sale is opened in Edit and saved — the form prefills `.toFixed(2)`.*
  - Verified by driving the real functions on his exact cart: subtotal reproduces **€12,525.26**, four individual edits → **€12,150.00** with only the edited line changing each time, discounts back-calculated, `preVat × 1.17 === finalPrice` on every line, the cart persisted to `localStorage`, and `buildMultiQuoteHTML` printing all four new prices with per-line list-price/discount rows and a correct VAT split. Plus: zero/blank refused with the dialog still open and nothing written; Reset restoring 2865.25 / 0% / €2448.93; round-up clamping to 0%; the Reset button hidden on an untouched line; the order bridge creating **O-0012** at €12,303.37 with a €3,000 deposit, all 4 lines in the description, the client saved, and the cart cleared (memory + localStorage + panel); blank deposit → `quote` status; a catalog cart making both Sell all and Reserve refuse with the new message, no dialog opened and the cart intact. EL + EN, dark + light, no clipping or horizontal overflow at 375px, the dialog fully reachable at a 400px-tall viewport (keyboard up), console clean.
- **The cart price dialog is the price bar, in miniature (v12.89):** the v12.88 "Change price" dialog had ONE box — a bare final price — so the owner could not take a percentage off, and could not see what the figure was before VAT while a customer was standing there asking. Every other place he sets a price (the calc's customer price bar) has all three: a **Discount %** field, a **rounding** box, and the ex-VAT line under the amount. The dialog now has the same three, in the same order and with the same labels (`t('discount')` / `t('roundLabel')`), so it reads as the control he already knows rather than a fourth way of setting a price ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]).
  - **One rule decides the figure, shared by the preview and the save** — new **`_cartLineFigure()`**: a **round target wins** (the owner saying "the price is exactly this"), otherwise the price is **list less the typed discount**. That is the calc's own order (discount first, rounding on top), so the two cannot disagree; and because the preview and `_cartLineSave` call the *same* function, the dialog can never save a figure other than the one on screen.
  - **Typing a discount clears the round target**, exactly as re-calculating does in the calc — without it, a stale round figure would silently swallow the % just typed.
  - **The round box prefills only when a figure was genuinely typed** (`|now − list×(1−disc/100)| > 0.005`); an untouched line opens with a blank round box and its own discount in the % field, which is the honest state. Reopening a line saved with a round target shows that target **and** the effective discount it works out to.
  - `_cartApplyLinePrice` is unchanged in rule (it still back-calculates the discount against `retail` and refreshes `preVat`/`vatAmount`) but now **rounds the incoming figure to the cent** — a discount-derived price (2870.82 less 10% = 2583.738) is not a round number, and the v12.88 lesson is that a stored fraction of a cent puts a total out later.
  - **A line with no list price** (an older saved cart, or anything whose `retail` is 0) shows **no discount field** — a percentage of nothing is meaningless — and falls back to its stored price, never `NaN`.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's exact line (€2870.82, 18K, 10.90g, VAT 17%): opens at €2870.82 / «χωρίς ΦΠΑ €2453.69»; 10% → €2583.74 / €2208.32 with «−10.0% (−€287.08)»; rounding to 2550 → €2550.00 / €2179.49 / −11.2%; clearing the round box falls back to the discount price; above list reads «Πάνω από την τιμή καταλόγου κατά €29.18»; saving stores `finalPrice`/`discount`/`discountAmt`/`preVat`/`vatAmount` consistently (preVat + VAT = the price to the cent); reopen prefills correctly in both directions; Reset still restores €2870.82 / 0%; the retail-less line shows one field and saves; EL + EN labels and placeholders; clear button matches the input height exactly; no horizontal overflow at 375px and the Save button still reachable at a 400px-tall viewport (keyboard up); light-theme contrast 4.86:1 on the large price; console clean.
- **The cart had no way in without a calculation (v12.90):** the owner reopened the app for the v12.89 update, the day's 4-item cart came back as designed — and he could not open it. Not user error: **the 🛒 button lives INSIDE `#customerPriceBar`, and that bar only exists once something has been calculated** (`showPanel` shows it only `if (lastCalcResult)`, and `_showPriceBar` returns early otherwise). After a reload `lastCalcResult` is null, so the one control that calls `toggleCartPanel()` was not on screen and the cart was genuinely unreachable. The restore toast is a toast — it announces, it does not open anything.
  - **`#cartFloatPill`** — a floating `🛒 4 είδη` pill on the Calc tab, shown by **`_syncCartFloatPill()`** whenever the cart has items and neither the price bar nor the cart panel is on screen to carry the button itself. Same pattern and same slot as the existing `#cpbRestorePill` / `#invShareBarPill` ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]) rather than a new kind of control.
  - **It deliberately stays hidden while the bar is merely MINIMIZED** (`!lastCalcResult` is part of the test): `#cpbRestorePill` occupies that exact position, and one tap on it brings back the bar with its own 🛒. Two pills stacked in one corner would be worse than the bug.
  - **Kept in step from four places** so it cannot go stale: `updateCartUI()` (every cart mutation funnels there — the same reasoning as `_saveQuoteCart`'s single save point), `showPanel` (it is `position:fixed`, so it must be hidden off-Calc exactly like the price bar), `_showPriceBar` (a calc just landed — the bar carries the 🛒 again, so the pill steps aside) and `toggleCartPanel`.
  - Verified by driving the real app with a seeded saved cart, reproducing the reported state exactly (4 items, `lastCalcResult` null, price bar hidden): the pill shows «4 είδη», a real click opens the panel with all 4 rows and the €4101.32 subtotal and hides the pill; ✕ brings it back; it vanishes on Stock and returns on Calc; the label follows removals («3 είδη» → «1 είδος») and it goes when the cart empties; a calculation hides it and minimizing the bar leaves only the restore pill; English reads "4 items"; it clears the bottom nav (bottom 747 vs nav top 754) with nothing off screen; console clean.
- **A cancelled hold can be brought back (v12.91):** a customer cancelled a €1750 two-piece hold, then came back for it — and there was nothing to do but build the reservation again from scratch, losing the agreed price, the payment log and the history that the hold ever existed. A **CANCELLED** hold was locked in every way: `_resCanEdit` allows only `active`/`expired`, so the detail sheet showed no ✏️ either. Only an **expired** one could be revived, and only sideways (edit the date → a confirm), which is a path nobody would find looking at a cancelled hold.
  - **The machinery already existed and was extracted rather than copied** ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]): `_resOfferRevive`'s body became **`_resReviveApply(id, holdUntil)`**, now shared by both entry points — the expired-via-edit confirm (unchanged in behaviour) and a new **↩️ Reactivate** button on the detail sheet of a cancelled/expired hold.
  - **It asks for the new hold-until date up front** (`_resReactivate` → `_resReviveConfirm`), because a hold revived with a past date would simply be expired again by the next `_resCheckExpiry` sweep. Default = the existing date if still future, else +14 days; a past date is refused with the form still open and **nothing written**. Invisible-native-input-over-`fmtDate`, never a bare visible `<input type="date">` (v11.42).
  - **Which pieces can go back on hold is worked out BEFORE the status flips** — `_holdFor` only looks at *active* holds and tickets, so this one must not yet be one of them. Anything sold, or now held by another reservation **or an order ticket**, is **NAMED by its reference code**, never silently dropped: the record keeps listing it (the history stays true) and only the free pieces are re-flagged. `_resOfferRevive` used `_resHoldFor`, which could not see an order ticket holding the piece — now `_holdFor`, so both kinds are caught.
  - **Nothing else is touched**: agreed price, payments, `itemIds`, items and the customer stay exactly as they were; only `status`, `holdUntil` and `dateCompleted` change. A failed inventory write is reported and rolled back, as `_resMarkItems` has done since v12.35 ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]]).
  - **Who may:** `_resCanRevive` — owner/manager always; an **expired** hold can also be brought back by whoever made it (the same rule as editing it), but a **cancelled** one was a deliberate owner/manager decision, so only they can undo it. A **completed** hold is never revivable: its pieces were sold.
  - **New theme token `--revive`** (`#7fd6b0` dark / `#1F6E4E` light). The pale mint measured **1.73:1** on the light theme's white — the same trap as `--debt` (v12.78) and `--order` (v12.88); now 10.60 / 6.18.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: the reported case (cancelled → reactivated to 15/09, piece re-held, the sold second piece named as `NK0999`, agreed price and payments untouched); a clean two-piece revive with the success toast and no warning; a piece taken by another reservation, by an order ticket, and a forced write failure — each named and the rest still re-held; a past date refused with 0 writes and the status still `cancelled`; the full permission matrix driven with real `creds` (employee revives their own expired hold, not a cancelled one, not someone else's); completed/active/null never revivable; the expired-via-edit path still asking and still working; the whole journey by real clicks ending with the sheet showing «Ολοκλήρωση & Πώληση» again; EL + EN; dark + light; no overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **A ticket's pieces need the stock cache, and nobody loaded it (v12.92):** the owner's manager opened a custom order and the pendant that goes into it was simply not there — the block read "Materials from stock" with the empty-state prompt, indistinguishable from a job that has none. **Not a permission gate** (managers hold `see_cost`, and the piece rows were never gated anyway): `_ordMaterialEntries` and `_ordLinkedEntries` resolve their pieces out of `_inventoryCache`, **nothing loads that cache at boot**, and `openRepairDetail` was synchronous and never loaded it. So the blocks worked on a device that had opened the Stock tab and were blank on one that went straight to Desk. Reproduced exactly against the real code on a manager device: the sheet rendered his screenshot byte for byte with **0** inventory fetches. Same family as the stocktake scanner (v10.72), the export sheet (v10.74) and the exchange-card scan (v11.59) — a resolver that assumes a cache someone else populated.
  - **It was also a money bug, which is the half that matters.** `_repairCollectOpen` costs the job from `_ordMaterialsCost(r)`, and `_repairCollectConfirm` re-derives it before writing — but on a cold cache both see **no materials at all**, while `_ordConsumeMaterials` (which loads the cache itself) still takes the piece out of stock. So collecting from a manager's phone would have removed the €197.89 pendant and booked the job's cost as €0, overstating the profit by exactly that. `_repairCollectOpen` is now `async` and awaits the stock first. The `_ordCompleteSale` path already loaded it; the detail's Collect button choice (`_ordLinkedInStock(r).length`) was wrong on a cold cache too, and is fixed by the redraw.
  - **The sheet does not wait for a download.** `_ordEnsureStockThenRedraw(r)` starts the load **before the first paint** and redraws only if the overlay is still showing the same ticket (`ov.dataset.rid`). `_ordEnsureStock(r)` — awaited by anything that decides money — goes through `_invEnsureForScan`, which keeps the last good cache when the network is down rather than reporting an empty stock (v11.74).
