  - **The redraw calls the same function again, so a FAILED load must not start another one**, or a phone with no signal would loop on the network. `_ordStockFailedAt` + `ORD_STOCK_RETRY_MS` (20 s) cool it down; a later reopen retries. Verified: exactly **1** fetch on failure, and **1** more refused on an immediate reopen.
  - **A block that cannot resolve its pieces SAYS so** (`_ordStockColdNote`): «Θα εμφανιστούν μόλις φορτώσει το απόθεμα…» while loading, «⚠️ Δεν φόρτωσε το απόθεμα» after — and the empty-state prompt is **suppressed** while cold, since "there is nothing here" is precisely the misreading that hid this bug for a version.
  - **Tap a piece → its card in Stock** (`_ordOpenPiece`, the owner's second request): photo + text become one clickable area in both blocks while the ✕ unlink stays a sibling outside it (no `stopPropagation` needed — measured non-overlapping at 375px). Goes through `_jumpToInvCard`, which waits for the list, pages it in and widens the filters for a piece not in the current view (v12.51), so a **consumed** material — now marked sold — is reached as easily as one on the shelf.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a manager device with a cold cache: the reported sheet reproduced, then fixed (1 fetch, row `PE0543 · Μενταγιόν`, cost €197.89, "would have sold for" €242.09); a warm cache renders the row instantly with **0** fetches; the Collect box opened straight from a cold cache now costs €197.89 instead of €0; the sheet closed before the load lands does **not** reopen itself; a repair ticket still hides the Pieces block; EN + EL; the tap lands on the pendant's own card (correctly badged «🔒 ΚΡΑΤΗΜΕΝΟ», since the ticket holds it); no horizontal overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **The second metal on the customer-facing price view (v12.93):** a piece can be made of two metals (`secondaryMetal`/`secondaryKarat`/`secondaryWeight` — e.g. 1.80 g of 925 silver with a 0.60 g 14K gold face). The **fullscreen price overlay** (`togglePriceFullscreen`, the screen the owner turns towards the customer) named only the primary, so a two-metal piece read as plain «Ασήμι · 925 / 1.80g» and half of what the customer was holding went unmentioned — the same gap v12.29 closed for the showroom. The Metal and Weight rows now carry both, **in the same order** so the pairing is obvious without extra rows: «Ασήμι · 925 + Χρυσός 14K» and «1.80g + 0.60g».
  - **Shared helpers `secondMetalLabel(o, isEl)` / `secondMetalWeightStr(o)`** next to `metalWithPlating`, mirroring `secondMetalLabel`/`secondMetalWeight` in `showroom.htm`. They read the same three field names whether handed an **inventory item** or a **calc result**, so one helper serves both, and they return `''` when there is no second metal so a caller can concatenate blind ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]] — the inline copy in `buildCard`'s meta line now goes through them too).
  - Silver purity is a millesimal fineness, so **no `K` on silver** («Ασήμι 925», «Χρυσός 14K»). A weight of 0 or a missing second metal renders nothing.
  - **`lastCalcResult` already carried the three fields** (set in `calculate()` from `_secMetal` + the secondary-metal panel, which `lookupCode` fills from the scanned item) — nothing new is stored and no pricing changed.
  - **The discount badge only ever names a REAL discount now (same version).** Two faults on the same customer-facing line. (1) `#pfsDiscount` copied the price bar's badge with a raw `.textContent`, but `.badge-pct` is `display:block` above `.badge-saved` — so what reads as two lines in the bar flattened to **`-15.0%Save €120.00`**. (2) More important on a screen the owner turns towards the customer: the **automatic rounding** (v12.14) shaves a few cents off, and the badge dutifully announced **«-0.0% Εξοικονόμηση €0.12»** — a 12-cent "discount", which reads as pettiness rather than a concession. The owner's instruction was to decide it whichever way looks better for the customer.
  - **One shared writer, `_setDiscountBadge(pct, amt, cur)`** (next to `applyRoundedPrice`), replacing **five identical copies** of the badge markup ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]) — `calculate()`, `applyRoundedPrice`, `resetRoundedPrice`, the bulk-chain sale and the Other-type path — plus the two places that only clear it. Three honest states: **pct ≥ `DISC_BADGE_MIN_PCT` (0.5)** → as before; **pct below that but amount ≥ `DISC_BADGE_MIN_AMT` (€1)** → the euros lead instead (`Εξοικονόμηση €5.00`, not a meaningless `-0.1%` — reachable on an expensive piece rounded by €5); **neither** → nothing is shown. A **trailing `.0` is dropped**, so a plain 15% reads `-15%`, not `-15.0%`.
  - **A saving of €0.50 on a €5 item still shows**, because there the PERCENTAGE (−10%) is the point — which is why the rule is not a flat money threshold.
  - The helper also writes a flat **`dataset.line`** (`-15% · Εξοικονόμηση €120.00`) that the fullscreen view reads, instead of scraping the two spans, and **clears the markup when hiding** — leaving it in place is what let a discount that no longer applied reappear on the fullscreen (the ex-VAT line above it already made that check).
  - Verified by driving the real functions: 12 cases × EL/EN through the helper (both boundaries, string arguments, an odd 1.749%, the cheap-item case); then the real `applyRoundedPrice`/`resetRoundedPrice` — the reported €150.12→€150 now shows **nothing**, €800→€680 shows `-15% · Εξοικονόμηση €120.00`, €4000→€3995 shows `Εξοικονόμηση €5.00`, rounding **UP** still shows nothing, and reset restores the original 15%; `clearCalc` leaves display/line/markup all empty and the fullscreen blank. Call-site variables checked in scope at all seven sites; no console errors.
  - Verified by driving the real `togglePriceFullscreen` on the owner's exact piece: EL + EN, gold-primary/silver-secondary, no second metal, a zero second weight, a karat-less second metal, and with stones present — plus **18 of 20** `buildCard` meta-line cases byte-identical to the old inline expression, the 2 that differ being the karat-less improvement («Χρυσός K» → «Χρυσός»). At 375 px: 0 horizontal overflow, every row one line, value right edges aligned at 349. Console clean.
- **A certificate can be printed WITHOUT the price (v12.94):** a customer bought an 18K cross with emeralds as a gift and asked for a certificate with no price on it. Until now the certificate always stated the price — which is right for the usual case (people take it to their insurer) and wrong for a gift. Both certificate paths now offer the choice: the **quote/certificate modal** reached from Calc (a **«🙈 ΧΩΡΙΣ ΤΙΜΗ» / "🙈 WITHOUT PRICE"** toggle that appears only in certificate mode, revealed by `openCertificateModal` after `openQuoteModal` has reset it) and the **sale-document picker** (`openSaleDocPicker`, a toggle inside the certificate section only).
  - **One flag, read by the builders, exactly like `_quoteIsCash`** — `window._certNoPrice`. `buildCertificateHTML` builds its whole `.price-section` into a `priceSection` variable that is `''` when set, and `buildMultiQuoteHTML` does the same for the per-item price line, the per-item list-price/discount rows and the whole `totalsSection`. **Both gate on `isCert`** (`noPrice = !!(isCert && window._certNoPrice)`), so a QUOTE can never lose its prices — it is a price document by definition.
  - **Only the money goes.** Metal, weight, secondary metal, stones, reference code, the item QR, the photo, the 10x5 cm stamp and the signature line all stay — it still certifies the piece, it just never names what was paid. The **handmade note is reworded** for this case, because its usual wording is «Η τιμή αντικατοπτρίζει…» / "The price reflects…" and a price-free certificate must not mention a price either.
  - **It always starts OFF and never leaks.** `openQuoteModal` resets the toggle, hides its wrap and clears the flag on every open (so the gift setting cannot ride into the next customer), `closeQuoteModal` hides it, `_saleDocBuild(saleId, groupId, isCert, noPrice)` sets the flag from its own argument on every call (clearing a stale value), and `reprintQuote` clears it — history holds quotes only.
  - **The picker's toggle is read BEFORE the sheet closes.** The buttons' onclick used to remove the overlay and then call the builder; the toggle lives inside that overlay, so both now go through **`_sdpGo(saleId, gid, isCert, share)`**, which reads the state, closes the sheet and dispatches.
  - **New shared control `_optToggleHtml` / `_setOptBtnState` / `_toggleOptBtn` / `_isOptBtn`** next to the cash-toggle helpers — the same pressed/unpressed visual language ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]) but with its own caption, taken from an i18n key held on the element (`data-opt-key`) so `setLang`/`applyTranslations` relabel it and the ✓ still tracks the state. `_setCashBtnState` could not be reused: it overwrites the label with `_cashLabel`.
  - **Verified by driving the real builders and the real UI:** the no-price certificate renders **0** price elements and no euro figure anywhere while keeping metal/weight/stones/ref/stamp/signature and fitting one page (813px vs 952px); the multi-item certificate drops both item prices and the whole totals block but keeps both items and both refs; a plain quote built with the flag still set keeps every price. **The regression that matters: 10 documents** (certificate EL/EN, multi certificate, multi quote, single quote × cash and receipt) are **byte-identical to the committed version** when the option is untouched. Plus: the option is absent from the quote modal and present in certificate mode; it resets on reopen; the language switch relabels it and keeps the state; the picker shows exactly one toggle, in the certificate section, above its buttons, and the quote buttons receive `isCert:false, noPrice:false` even while it is pressed; EL + EN, dark + light, no overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **The job's cost is worked out BEFORE the customer arrives (v12.95):** the owner turned a customer's pendant (PE0543, €197.89, already linked as a material) into a ring; the ring shank plus the goldsmith's work cost him €320, and there was **nowhere on the ticket to put it**. The materials block showed €197.89 and nothing else, so the screen he was looking at said the job cost €197.89. His own framing is the requirement: *"I would like to have everything calculated and ready before the client comes."*
  - **The field already existed and was simply asked for too late.** `extraCost` has been stored on the ticket since v12.80 and `_repairCollectOpen` **already prefills from it** — it was only ever WRITTEN at the moment of collection, i.e. with the customer standing at the counter. So the fix is an earlier way in, not a new number: whatever is set on the ticket is exactly what Collect opens with, and the two can never disagree.
  - **`_ordJobCostBlock(r)`** — a panel under the materials block on every live ticket (owner/manager; hidden for a quote, which has committed nothing, and after collection, where the top box already shows the real figures). It rolls up **materials from stock** + **ordered pieces that have arrived** + **your cost** (a tappable row) into a **Total cost**, then previews the profit at the price already quoted. Mirrors the Collect box line for line, so the same three rows appear in both places ([[consistent-ui-patterns]]).
  - **Ordered pieces are counted in the PREVIEW only.** Their cost rides on the sale records at collection, so adding it to the ticket's booked cost as well would count it twice — the preview and the booked figure still agree because each side counts it exactly once (verified: preview €717.89, booked €717.89).
  - **Cash AND receipt profit are both shown**, because which one it will be is only decided at Collect and the gap between them is the whole point when settling on a price (€582.11 vs €422.28 on his ticket at €1100). Both go through **`_saleExVatRaw`**, the one place that knows the rule — never a local `price/(1+vat)` ([[other-type-cost-in-qr]] generalised: one source for money).
  - **A real money bug found in the same pass, on the OTHER collect path.** An order whose pieces arrived goes `_ordCompleteSale` → `_ordFinalizeCollect`, straight to the sale — **it never opens the Collect box**, and it added only `_mcost.total` to the first record's cost. So `extraCost` would have been silently ignored there and the profit would have read high by exactly the goldsmith's invoice. `_ordFinalizeCollect` now books `materials + extraCost`; **with no `extraCost` set it is byte-identical to before** (verified: €597.89 either way).
  - **A small overlay, not an inline input** (`_repCostOpen`/`_repCostSave`/`_repCostSync`): the detail sheet redraws itself when the stock cache lands (`_ordEnsureStockThenRedraw`, v12.92), which would wipe half-typed text. A **blank box clears the value** — the field is deliberate, so an emptied one means "nothing on top".
  - **New theme token `--profit`** (`#7fd6b0` dark / `#12664A` light). The pale mint measures **1.43:1** on the light theme's panels — the fourth colour to hit this after `--debt` (v12.78), `--order` (v12.88) and `--revive` (v12.91); now 9.82 / 5.74. *(The 33 pre-existing `#7fd6b0` literals elsewhere have the same problem and were left alone, as the earlier passes did.)*
  - **Layout caught by measurement:** the tappable row's own padding put its figure **11px out of line** with the rows above it — three money figures stacked with one off is what a sloppy panel looks like. Once a value is set the row goes full-bleed to the panel's 12px padding so all three land in one column (verified: right edges 328/328/328, 34px tap target); while still empty there is no figure to align and the dashed box keeps inviting the first tap. A roll-up of nothing is also suppressed — a plain repair using no stock shows the button alone, not "Total cost €0.00".
  - Verified by driving the real functions on his exact ticket: €197.89 + €320 → **€517.89**, profit preview 💵 €582.11 / 🧾 €422.28, Collect opening prefilled at 320 with its own total €517.89, and the full collection at €1050 cash recording **cost €517.89 / profit €532.11** with the pendant leaving stock marked `usedInOrder`. Plus: blank clears; an employee sees no block and `_repCostOpen` refuses; quote and collected tickets show nothing; a materials-less repair renders correctly; EL + EN; both themes; no overflow at 375px; console clean.
- **A notification names the pieces, so nothing may narrow it (v12.96):** an employee messaged the owner about a piece she had just sold; he pressed **View** on the notification and landed on an empty Stock list — "0 pieces · No items in inventory" — with the notification banner sitting above nothing. Reproduced exactly against the committed build, then fixed. **`notifViewItem` narrows `allItems` to the notified QR codes but the rest of the filter chain still ran on top**, and the everyday default is `_invFilters.status = ['in_stock']` — so a notified piece that has since been **SOLD** was filtered straight back out. The bug is not really about "sold": any pill the owner happened to have on (vendor, metal, karat, a date range) would have swallowed it the same way, and a notified **coin or raw-metal lot** was dropped by the separate list-drop below, which only yields to the Coin/Metal type filter or a live search.
  - **The notification is an explicit list of record identities**, i.e. an answer already given — every filter below it can only subtract from that answer, and when it does the screen reads "not found". So `_notifOn` now short-circuits the whole chain (`if (_notifOn) return true;`) and the coin/metal list-drop as well. Same rule the Desk lists follow for a search (v11.45) and the same reasoning as `_jumpToInvCard` widening its filters (v12.51).
  - **The one thing deliberately kept is the SEARCH box** — typing while the banner is up should still narrow within the notified set, or the box looks broken. The search block was **moved to the top of the filter callback** (it is a pure predicate, so the move is semantically free) and the bypass placed after it, rather than extracting a helper. `notifViewItem` **clears a stale term** on open, so a leftover search can never be what hides the piece.
  - **Nothing is written and no filter is changed** — the bypass is at read time, so dismissing the banner (`clearNotifFilter`) returns the owner to his own view exactly as he left it. Clearing his pills instead would have been the v12.51 mistake.
  - Codes are matched through **`_qrKey`** on both sides now (the notif filter and `handleDeepLinkItem`'s lookup), so the four legacy `+`-form codes resolve either way and the single-item flash can't report "Not found" for a card that is on screen.
  - Verified by driving the real functions, A/B against the committed file with the same fixture: old build → **0 cards + "No items in inventory"** (the owner's screenshot); new build → the sold card, through a **real click on the panel's View button**, with the banner carrying the employee's note and the vendor/metal/status pills he had on left untouched. Plus: a notified coin shows; a legacy `+` code resolves from either spelling; a multi-item notification shows both; typing narrows within the set and an unmatched term correctly shows none; dismissing restores the previous view. **Regression: 14 no-notification cases (default / sold / coin / 6 search forms incl. ref code, raw code, comma-OR and seqId / vendor / metal) return byte-identical results on both builds.** EL + EN, 0 horizontal overflow at 375px, console clean. *(The deep-link flash could not be observed — `requestAnimationFrame` never fires in this browser pane, so the highlight is untestable here; the code path is unchanged and was confirmed to find its target and raise no "Not found" toast.)*
- **A sale opens the job it came from (v12.97):** an ordinary sale has always had **View Item**; a repair or custom order collected as income had **nothing** — the record named its ticket in the note («Ειδική κατασκευή O-0002 — Δαχτυλίδι…») and reading a number is not the same as being able to open it. The owner's case: a €1000 custom order whose sale detail showed no piece, no photo and no way through to the ticket where the pendant that went into it is listed.
  - **The link is a RECORD ID, stored on the sale** ([[link-records-by-id-not-string]]): `repairTicketId`, set by `_recordRepairIncome` (via a new `ticketId` in its opts, passed from `_repairCollectConfirm`) and by **`_ordFinalizeCollect`** — which stamps **every line of the transaction**, not just the first, because any one of them can be opened on its own.
  - **Sales already written keep working.** `_saleTicket(s)` prefers the stored id and otherwise reads the ticket **number** back out of the note (`/\b([RO]-\d{1,6})\b/` → matched against `repNoFmt`). It is a **read-time** fallback — nothing stored is rewritten (the v10.19 lesson) — and only `R-`/`O-` are ticket prefixes, so a note naming a gift certificate (`G-`) or an exchange card (`X-`) can never be mistaken for a job. A number that names no live ticket simply yields no button.
  - **`_saleTicketBtn` builds it for BOTH the single and the transaction view** ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]), labelled `repTypeIcon` + «Δες» / "View" + the ticket number (`✨ Δες O-0002`, `🔧 Δες R-0007`), sitting beside **View Item** because it answers the same question: what was actually sold here.
  - **The pieces come free.** The ticket sheet already lists the materials and the linked pieces with photos, each tappable through `_ordOpenPiece` → `_jumpToInvCard` (which pages in and widens the filters, so a **consumed** material — now marked sold — is reached as easily as one on the shelf). Nothing was duplicated into the sale detail: a material is what went INTO the piece, not what was sold, and showing its photo as the sale's would misrepresent it (the same rule v11.67 applies to a bulk chain's batch photo).
  - **✕ goes back one step** — `_repBackToSale` (consumed in `_clientCloseDetail`, cleared by all three Desk-detail openers), the same one-step-back rule as v11.79/v11.80. A ticket opened from Desk never bounces. `showSaleDetail` also makes **one** attempt per session to pull the repairs list when this device has not seen it (`_saleEnsureTickets`), so a missing button can't mean "never loaded" (v11.61).
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the owner's exact case: the legacy €1000 record resolves **O-0002** from its note alone, the button opens the ticket showing `PE0543 · Μενταγιόν · Κωνσταντινάτο · αναλώθηκε` with the piece tappable, and ✕ returns to the same sale. Plus: an ordinary sale is **byte-identical** (no ticket button, View Item intact); a `G-`/`X-` note and an unknown ticket number both yield nothing; an empty repairs list yields null without crashing; the group view and one line of it both offer it; `🔧 R-0007` and `✨ View O-0002` in EN. **Money untouched:** `_recordRepairIncome` reproduces €1000/€520.49/**€479.51** exactly and writes **no key at all** without a ticket, a receipt + certificate split still lands on the hand-computed €349.15, and `_ordFinalizeCollect` leaves costs and profits byte-identical with no extra cost while still moving only the first line's (€600→€920, profit €105.64) with one. 6 buttons at 375px: no clipping, no overflow; console clean.
- **A notification names the RECORD, not a code (v12.98):** the owner sent ER0895 (a sold pair of “Greek Meander” earrings) to his employee; she pressed **View** and got **ER0933** — a different, in-stock pair. Reproduced exactly against the committed build, then fixed. **The two pieces share the descriptive code `DIA925S02`**, which is legitimate since v11.32 dropped the duplicate-code guard — and the whole notification path travelled on that code alone. A code is a DESCRIPTION; it cannot say which of two pieces is meant ([[link-records-by-id-not-string]]).
  - **The two halves disagreed, which is what made it look so odd.** The panel's description came from `Object.values(...).find()` — the FIRST match, ER0895, so the card correctly read “Greek Meander” — while `handleDeepLinkItem` resolved the jump with a `forEach` that **overwrote its answer on every match**, so the LAST match won: ER0933. Verified on the old build: `oldDescPicks: inv_895`, `oldFlashPicks: inv_933`. The filter matched on the code too, so both cards were listed and the wrong one sat on top.
  - **Fix:** the notification payload gains **`invId`/`invIds`** — the record ids the sender actually picked — threaded from the share sheet (`showInvShareOptions(items, ids)` → `overlay._idList`) through `invShareToTeam(qrInput, idInput)` and `notifDoSend(qrCodeArg, itemDesc, idArg)`. The receiving side prefers them: `_notifFilterIds` narrows the Stock list **by id**, and the flash goes to that exact record. `qrCode`/`qrCodes` are still written unchanged, so the payload stays byte-compatible and an employee on an older app is unaffected.
  - **A notification sent BEFORE this falls back to the code**, which is the best that can be done for it — the identity was never recorded. Nothing is migrated. An id naming a piece that has since been deleted also falls back rather than showing an empty list.
  - **`_invFlashCard(id)` was extracted** from `handleDeepLinkItem` so the notification path never has to go back through a code to find a record it already knows ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]), and `handleDeepLinkItem` now resolves through **`_descCodePool`** — so a `?item=` deep link to a shared code opens the piece **on the shelf** ([[shelf-rule-for-scanned-codes]]) instead of whichever the loop saw last. A legacy record carrying only `code` (not `qrCode`) is still caught by an explicit fallback.
  - **SCANNING WAS NEVER AFFECTED, and it is worth knowing why.** Every printed tag carries the **reference** code in its QR (v11.31), and two pieces that differ earn different reference codes (v11.83) — so scanning either tag resolves straight to its own record with no chooser. **Verified** by driving `lookupCode` on the owner's actual pair with both in stock: `ER0895` → `inv_895`, `ER0933` → `inv_933`, sell target correct, sheet never opened. The chooser appears only for the raw DESCRIPTIVE code (`DIA925S02`), which no tag prints. The share/notification path was simply the last place still keyed on that descriptive code — an internal lookup, not a scan.
  - Verified by driving the real functions, A/B against the committed file with the same two-piece fixture: old build lists **ER0933 first** (the owner's screenshot), new build lists **ER0895 alone**. Plus — sending either twin opens that twin; a legacy code-only notification still works and now prefers the in-stock piece; a 2-item notification shows both with the right count; a deleted id falls back to the code; dismissing the banner restores the owner's own filters; single / multi-select / group shares all carry ids parallel to their items; a share called with no ids still resolves them; and an ordinary unique-code piece is byte-identical end to end. EL + EN, no overflow at 375px, console clean.
- **One piece, two sales — the cold-start baseline (v12.99):** an employee recorded a sale at the wrong price; the owner deleted it and asked her to redo it; the Sales tab then showed **the same item sold twice**. **Both** of the possible causes turned out to be real, and both were reproduced against the committed build before being fixed.
  - **Cause 1 — a stale phone re-uploaded the deleted sale.** `_syncListToCloud` writes only what differs from `_cloudSnap[path]`, the baseline of "what the cloud held when we last pulled". That baseline is **in-memory only, so it is undefined on every app start until the boot pull lands** — and `autoSync()` runs once, is not awaited, and swallows its own errors, while `_refreshSharedLists` is throttled to 20 s **and skipped entirely while the sale dialog is open**. When the baseline is missing, the lazy branch fetched the node **on the spot** and diffed against it. Her list came from `localStorage` (via `getSales()`, which never pulls when a local copy exists) and still held the deleted sale — so it read as *"a record I have that the cloud is missing"* → **PUT**, and the sale the owner had just deleted came straight back. Reproduced exactly: the old build issues `PUT <deleted id>` alongside the new sale.
  - **The same branch could DESTROY a sale, which is worse and was never reported.** A record the cloud held that her stale list lacked (one the owner had just made on his phone) read as *"deleted"* → **DELETE**. Same few-second window.
  - **Fix — on an untrusted baseline, act only on provable intent.** A `_lazy` flag marks a baseline that had to be fetched on the spot. On that one save: a PUT of a record **absent from the cloud** is allowed only if the **outbox** vouches for it or its id was **minted this session** (`_idThisSession`, from `_stampId()`'s `Date.now()*1000 + salt`; anything unrecognised counts as OLD, the safe direction); a **DELETE** is allowed only if the outbox holds a `del` for it. Skipped records are not silent: a phantom is **dropped from the device's list and localStorage** (`_dropStaleLocal` — otherwise the *next*, now-trusted, save would upload it after all), and an unverifiable delete has its id **removed from the baseline** so the same inference cannot be drawn again. Both log a `console.warn`. Skips are refusals, not failures, so they do not affect the return value and cannot raise a false "not synced" alarm.
  - **`_queueRemoval(path, id)` at the six deliberate-delete sites** (`deleteSaleRecord`, `_undoReturn`, `_gcDelete`, `_resDelete`, `_repairDelete`, `deleteClient`) records the intent in the outbox **before** the write is attempted — mirroring what `addSaleRecord` has done for a new sale since v11.65. Without it a legitimate cold-start delete would be refused by the guard above and the record would walk back in on the next refresh (the v11.66 symptom). It also means an offline delete now survives the app being closed.
  - **Cause 2 — no double-submit guard on the sale.** `closeSaleModal()` runs only *after* the records and stock updates have gone out, so the dialog stays open for the whole write and a second tap re-ran the lot, writing a **complete second sale under a new id**. The piece is already sold, so `saveInventoryItem` is a no-op — **stock stays correct and only the money doubles**, which is exactly why it is easy to miss. Reproduced: two concurrent `confirmSale()` calls on the old build create **two €300 sales**. Fixed with the v11.09 wrapper pattern — `confirmSale` is now a thin guard (`_confirmingSale`, set synchronously before the first await) around `_confirmSaleInner`, with the button disabled and relabelled «Καταχώριση…» / "Recording…" and restored in a `finally`, so a refused sale never leaves it locked.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions** against a fake Firebase honouring per-record PUT/DELETE: the reported case now emits **one** write (the new sale), does not resurrect the deleted one, and cleans the phantom out of the list and localStorage; another phone's sale survives a stale list with **0** DELETEs; a deliberate cold-start delete still goes through with the outbox left empty; the same holds for the gift-card list. Regression on the trusted path is **byte-identical** — 1 PUT for an add, 1 PUT for an edit, 1 DELETE for a delete, **0** writes for no change — and the offline path still queues, flushes and leaves other records untouched.
  - **Left alone deliberately:** on the lazy path a PUT for an id the cloud **already has** (a modification) still goes through. Restricting it would break the ordinary "edit a sale right after opening the app", and the only risk is the per-record last-writer-wins the app accepts everywhere.
- **A scrap purchase is not a sale — show the arithmetic (v13.00):** the owner bought 1.10 g of 14K from a customer and opened the record: it read **«Ποσό (χωρίς ΦΠΑ) €30.00»** over **«Κέρδος €24.48 (81.6%)»**, with the note as the only clue what had happened. Two complaints, both right — a cash metal purchase has no VAT split to state, and the price paid (the one figure that decides everything) was nowhere on screen.
  - **The figures are FROZEN on the record at the moment of purchase.** `_recordScrapGain(gain, note, extra)` now stores `scrapPaid`/`scrapMelt`/`scrapRecover`/`scrapLossPct`/`scrapGrams`/`scrapPure`/`scrapMetal`/`scrapKarat`/`scrapLotId`, written by `_metalAddToLot` — so both entry points (the **Add Metal** button and the gold check's **Pay & add to stock**) carry them. Deliberately **not** re-derived from today's metal price: gold moves daily, so a melt value computed when the record is later opened would no longer reconcile with the gain sitting beside it — the same reason a sale's `listPrice` is frozen (v12.79).
  - **The detail box now reads:** `Μέταλλο 1.10g Χρυσός 14K · 0.64g καθαρό` / `Αξία μετάλλου €92.78` / `Απώλεια καθαρισμού 3% −€2.78` / `Ανακτήσιμο (−3%) €90.00` / `Πλήρωσα −€60.00` / `Κέρδος αγοράς €30.00`. Each figure is **rounded to the cent BEFORE the subtraction** in `_metalAddToLot` (and in `_metalUpdatePreview`, so preview and booked figure cannot differ by a cent), which is what makes the rows add up exactly on screen rather than being a cent out on the last line.
  - **`_saleIsGainOnly(s)`** (`scrapGain || giftBreakage`) is the shared rule: a gain-only record is not a sale — nothing went out, nothing went through the till, and the amount **is** the profit by construction — so the **Amount excl. VAT / VAT rows and the profit percentage are omitted** (the percentage is always 100%). The sheet title reads **«♻️ Αγορά Παλαιού Μετάλλου» / "♻️ Scrap Metal Purchase"**, because calling it a sale is what made the VAT row look like it belonged.
  - **Only Edit and Delete remain** on such a record. **«Επιστροφή» was a real footgun** — `_saleCanReturn` allowed it, and `processReturn` would have written a negative sale and could have **issued store credit for gold the shop BOUGHT**; Print (a quote/certificate of a metal purchase) and Exchange Card are meaningless. An ordinary sale still shows all five buttons.
  - **The amount/profit mismatch is fixed at the source:** `confirmSaleEdit` kept the old profit whenever `cost` was null, so correcting the amount on a gain-only record left the two disagreeing — exactly the «€30.00 / κέρδος €24.48» on the owner's screen. Profit now follows the amount for `_saleIsGainOnly` records, which also means **opening Edit and pressing Save re-syncs a record already written**. A manual sale with no cost still keeps `profit: null`, and a sale with a cost is untouched.
  - **A record written before this shows only the gain plus «Παλαιότερη εγγραφή — δεν είχε καταγραφεί η ανάλυση»** — the price paid was never stored and cannot be recovered (gain = recover − paid is one equation in two unknowns), and inventing a melt value from today's spot would be a plausible number that was never true.
  - **Known, deliberately not built:** deleting a scrap-gain record does **not** take the grams back out of the metal lot (only a sale trade-in does, via `_tradeInReverseForSale`) — the manual undo is Stock → the lot → **− Remove**. (`_recordGiftBreakage`'s bare `Date.now()` id, flagged here, was fixed in v13.01.)
  - Verified by driving the real functions in a browser: `_metalAddToLot` on the owner's exact purchase stores every field, `melt − loss − paid === amount` to the cent, `amount === profit`, one inventory write; the box renders correctly in EL + EN for gold and for silver (`850‰`), the legacy record shows the honest note, an ordinary sale's rows are unchanged, gift breakage shows one Profit row; `confirmSaleEdit` re-syncs a gain-only record (24.48 → 30) and follows a changed amount, while a normal sale (600 cash − 340 cost = 260) and a costless manual sale (`profit: null`) behave exactly as before; buttons correct on all three kinds; 0 horizontal overflow at 375px with all six values sharing one right edge; both dictionaries in sync (0/0); console clean.
- **The last unsalted record id, and a missing outbox queue (v13.01):** `_recordGiftBreakage` — the record written when an expired gift certificate's unspent balance is booked as profit — minted its id with a bare **`Date.now()`**. Every other sale-record writer (`addSaleRecord`, `_recordScrapGain`, `_recordRepairIncome`, `processReturn`, the giveaway) already used **`_nextSaleId(sales)`**, which steps past an id already taken and carries the per-device salt from `_stampId()`; this was the last one left, so two phones booking a breakage in the same millisecond would mint the SAME id — and an id is what every lookup, delete and return keys on (v11.64/v12.99).
  - **A second, live bug in the same function: it never queued to the outbox.** `_syncListToCloud` queues on a *failed write*, but on a phone that booted with no signal it cannot establish a baseline to diff against at all and **`return false`s having queued nothing** — which is exactly why `addSaleRecord` and `_recordScrapGain` queue the record BEFORE attempting the push (v11.65). Without it a breakage booked offline lived only in that device's `localStorage` and the next successful `pullSalesFromCloud` erased it. One line, same fix as everywhere else.
  - **Checked, and NOT the problem it first looked like:** a bare-`Date.now()` id is still recognised by `_idMintedMs` (`n > 1e11` → legacy plain timestamp), so the v12.99 cold-start `_lazy` guard would *not* have dropped such a record as a phantom. The bug was the collision, not the guard.
  - **Found in the same sweep and deliberately deferred: `processReturn` also never queued its record to the outbox** — fixed in v13.06 below, which is where that ordering decision was made.
  - Verified by driving the real function with the clock frozen: two breakages booked in the **same millisecond** (`ts` identical) get **distinct** salted ids (`…052816` / `…053816` — `_stampId` steps by a full 1000 so the salt digits survive), both `_idThisSession`, both queued `put` to `/jewelprice_sales`; every other field byte-identical to before (amount, note, date, ts, `cost: null`, profit, `cashSale: true`, `giftBreakage: true`). Sale detail still renders it as a gain-only record (one Profit row, Edit + Delete only), dictionaries in sync (0/0), console clean.
- **Gain on the metal held — the profit the app was never showing (v13.02):** the owner opened NK0001 (73.3 g 18K, added 16/06/2026, «Initial price (+8.4%)») and asked the right question: the app prices a per-gram piece's COST live, so the profit it reports is measured against what the piece would cost to buy **today** — but he bought it when gold was cheaper, so what he actually made is more. On that necklace: sale ex-VAT €16,142.63 − replacement cost €9,495.66 = **€6,646.97 recorded**, while the cost when it was added back-solves to €8,758.63, so the real profit is **€7,384.00**. The €737.03 gap is exactly the gold appreciating on the shelf.
  - **They are not competing answers — they ADD UP**, which is the whole framing: *trading margin* (sale − replacement cost) + *holding gain* (replacement cost − cost when added) = what the piece really made. Same shape, and the same helper, as the trade-in gain (v12.82).
  - **`initialBaseCost`** — the piece's cost FROZEN at add time — is captured at the same three create paths that set `initialRetail`/`initialMetalValue` (the `renderInvList` self-heal, scan-to-inventory, "add similar"), inherited by restock, and rescaled by the same ratio at the v11.29 weight/vendor/category edit so a correction cannot fake a gain.
  - **`heldCost`** is stamped on the SALE record beside `listPrice` (both the first record and every cart line), so the gain is derivable forever from the record's own numbers — no dependence on the piece surviving or on where the metal price has gone since (v12.79's rule). Stamped **only** when the cost really was a live recomputation: `_itemHeldCost` returns null for a stored `purchaseCost` (the recorded cost is already historical), other-type (cost fixed in the QR), coins and raw metal (their own melt baseline and gain line — folding them in would report the same money twice) and bulk.
  - **A sale recorded before this falls back to an ESTIMATE and says so.** `_saleHoldGain` converts the piece's `initialRetail` with today's `baseCost / retail` ratio — algebraically the same as dividing by (1+margin)(1+VAT) as they stand now — and marks the row **≈**. Measured on the real 25/07 backup, that is right to a few percent but no better: `initialRetail` is a *retail* figure that depends on the margin and the VAT rate then (24% → 17%), and it has been deliberately re-baselined more than once (rate fixes, the v12.32 wastage introduction, identity edits) — **two pieces added the same day in June imply metal prices of €104.95 and €115.45**. `initialGoldPrice` (on 256 of 740 in-stock pieces) looked ideal and is **unusable**: it is frozen at ~113.47 on *every* piece from 16/06 to 16/07, correlation 0.08 with the per-piece implied price. It is referenced nowhere in the code.
  - **The recorded profit stays the headline, deliberately.** Every sale already stored carries it and none can be recomputed; the breakdown's margin is a TRADING margin that a holding gain would distort exactly as a bullion coin did (v12.74); and employee bonuses are paid on profit — nobody should earn a bonus on the gold price. So the gain is an extra line, never a redefinition.
  - **`_tradeInGainRows` was replaced by `_saleGainRows(recs, profit, cur, mkRow)`** (used by both the single and the transaction view), which draws the trade-in gain, the holding gain and **ONE** combined total — two functions would have printed two contradicting "total" lines on a sale that has both.
  - **Sales breakdown** gains its own «🧱 Κέρδος από το μέταλλο που κρατούσατε» section under the tiles: the summed gain, the combined total, and a note that names how many of the sales were estimated. Kept **out** of the tiles and out of every per-activity margin. `_qrItemIndex()` memoizes code → item because most pre-v11.78 sales carry no `invId` and a year's breakdown would otherwise scan the whole stock once per record.
  - **Scale, measured on the real backup:** across 435 in-stock per-gram gold pieces, replacement cost **€183,378** vs cost-when-added **€169,301** — about **€14,077** of gain that would never have been reported as those pieces sold (median +8.6% per piece, one at −2.8%).
  - Verified by driving the real functions with his own 14-vendor config and the real NK0001 record: `calcInvItemPrice` reproduces his screen (metal €6,980.74 + wastage €698.07 + labour €1,832.50 → baseCost €9,511.31, retail €18,917.99 at €3,949/oz); a real `confirmSale` stamps `heldCost: 8758.63` and the sheet reads **Κέρδος €6,629.72 → 🧱 +€752.68 → Σύνολο €7,382.40**; the legacy fallback lands within €0.50 of that and is marked ≈; a piece with `purchaseCost`, a legacy piece with no baseline, and every excluded kind (return, scrap, repair, giveaway, breakage, bulk, no cost, other, coin, raw metal) all stamp and show nothing; a **cart** stamps both lines and sums them; a negative gain renders red and lowers the total; trade-in + holding gain draw both lines and one total; the breakdown section is absent when nothing applies, drops the ≈ when everything is exact, and names the estimated count otherwise; EL + EN; 0 horizontal overflow at 375px with every value on one right edge; console clean.
  - **Known:** the light-theme green contrast flagged here (2.58:1 in the breakdown, 3.93:1 in the sale detail) was fixed in v13.03. Also `_invSaleCost` stores an unrounded cost on the sale record (e.g. `9511.308836759465`); display rounds it, and the gain rounds correctly, but the stored figure is not to the cent.
- **Money colours are theme tokens now — the light theme was unreadable (v13.03):** flagged while building v13.02 and fixed here. Profit and loss figures were painted with hard-coded hex literals that know nothing about the theme, so on the light theme's ivory they measured **2.58:1** (`#4CAF50`), **2.70:1** (`#4caf84`), **3.63:1** (`#e05a5a`) and **3.93:1** (`var(--success)`, whose light value was `#2E8B57`) — all under the 4.5:1 needed for normal text. The app's amber was worse still at **2.12:1** and was swept in the same pass.
  - **Three new tokens**, following the `--debt`/`--order`/`--revive`/`--profit` pattern exactly: **`--gain`** (`#4caf84` dark / `#12664A` light), **`--loss`** (`#ff8a80` / `#9B2314`), **`--warn`** (`#e0a85a` / `#8A5A00`). Plus `--success`'s LIGHT value changed `#2E8B57` → `#12664A`; its dark value is untouched, and its three background uses (two status dots and one button with white text) only get better for being darker.
  - **Every literal swept, but only where it is TEXT.** `#7fd6b0`→`var(--profit)` (31, dark byte-identical), `#4CAF50`→`--gain` (7), `#4caf84`→`--gain` (13), `#ff8a80`→`--loss` (15), `#ff6b6b`→`--loss` (5), `#e05a5a`→`--loss` (11), `#e0a85a`/`#e0b050`/`#e0b25c`→`--warn` (38). The 13 `linear-gradient(135deg,#4caf84,#2e8b62)` button backgrounds, the 8px status dot and the multicolour conic-gradient swatch were **protected and left alone** — those are backgrounds carrying white text, and a token there would have been wrong. Checked that no swept value reaches a `ctx.fillStyle` or jsPDF call, where a CSS variable would silently fail.
  - **Dark theme is unchanged except by design:** near-identical shades were unified so they cannot drift apart again — 7 green text sites move `#4CAF50`→`#4caf84` (only the blue channel, 80→132) and 16 red text sites move `#ff6b6b`/`#e05a5a`→`#ff8a80` (the salmon already used for debts and returns). Everything else is byte-identical.
  - **Measured on real rendered elements in both themes, page ground and card white.** Light: `--gain` 6.43/6.94, `--loss` 7.36/7.94, `--warn` 5.49/5.93, `--profit` 6.43/6.94, `--success` 6.43/6.94. Dark: 6.85–11.23 across the same tokens. `--danger` (5.04/5.44) was already fine and was left alone. Verified on the sale-detail Profit and gain-on-metal rows, the breakdown Profit tile, the per-activity lines, the `.sb-num.sb-p/.sb-n` table columns and the inventory-card gain %, in both themes; all five panels rendered in both themes with **zero** elements resolving a new token to black, and a clean console.
  - **Rule going forward: a colour that carries meaning goes in the `:root` palette with a light-theme value, never as a hex literal in a style string.** The light theme is a first-class theme, and a pale-on-dark colour is almost always unreadable on ivory.
- **A percentage on the transaction total (v13.04):** the owner's request — the Profit row carries `(46.6%)` and the "Total from this transaction" row below it carried none, so the two could not be read against each other. `_saleGainRows` gained a 5th argument, the sale's **ex-VAT value**, and both call sites pass the SAME variable the Profit row's own percentage divides by (`amtExVat` in the single and the transaction view). Any other denominator would make the second number look like it answers the first when it does not. His €700 cross now reads `Κέρδος €326.42 (46.6%)` → `Σύνολο από τη συναλλαγή €351.34 (50.2%)`.
  - **A scoping trap caught before shipping:** the single view's `base` is a `const` declared INSIDE `if (s.profit != null)`, so passing it at the call site below would have thrown a ReferenceError on every sale. The enclosing `amtExVat` is the same value and is what is passed.
  - A base of 0, a missing base, a null profit and a trade-in-only gain were all driven: no percentage and no crash, and the total row is still absent entirely when there is no gain to add.
  - **Sign before the symbol on these rows too.** A negative total printed `€-40.00` while the app's rule (`fmtAmt`, v11.46) is `−€40.00`. New `_sdMoney(v, cur)` applies it to the Profit row and the total row — it takes the caller's own `cur` rather than calling `fmtAmt`, because these rows read `state.currency` while `fmtAmt` reads `state.currencySymbol`, and the two are separate settings. **Byte-identical for any positive figure.** (The percentage itself still prints a plain `-28.6%` from `toFixed`; left alone.)
- **The metal gain gets its own tiles (v13.05):** the owner's request — the gain and the real total were only in their own section further down the Sales Breakdown, so the summary at the top still showed the trading profit alone. The tile grid (`.sb-tiles`, 2 columns) gains a **third row in the tiles' own gold**: «Κέρδος μετάλλου» / "Metal gain" and «Συνολικό κέρδος» / "Total profit". `_holdGainTotal` moved above the tiles (`_sbHoldShow`) and the section below now reuses that one result rather than recomputing it.
  - **Gold is the DEFAULT tile colour**, so the two are simply left uncoloured — which is what sets them apart from the green trading figures above without implying they are the same kind of number. **A negative goes red even so**: gold on a loss reads as a gain at a glance, and would contradict the same figure in its own section below. Verified across positive / negative-gain / both-negative.
  - The row is **absent entirely when the period holds no gain on metal**, so an ordinary period still shows exactly the 4 tiles it always did. The `≈` estimate marker rides in the tile's `sub` slot (the same slot "jewelry only" uses on the Margin tile).
  - The Profit and Margin tiles and every per-activity margin are **untouched** — they measure the trading margin, which a holding gain would distort exactly as a bullion coin did (v12.74).
  - Verified by driving `_sbRender` on a fixture reproducing the owner's own 2026 figures (Sales €55,711.01, Profit €23,197.25): 6 tiles with the gold pair, 4 tiles when nothing applies, the `≈ εκτίμηση` / `≈ estimate` sub, EL + EN labels, light and dark, and nothing clipped or overflowing at 375px.
- **A return survives no signal, and says so (v13.06):** the last member of the v11.65 outbox family. `processReturn` wrote the return record to `_salesCache` and localStorage and then `await`ed `pushSalesToCloud` — but **queued nothing first**, so on a phone that booted with no signal `_syncListToCloud` could not even establish a cloud baseline to diff against, `return false`d having queued nothing, and the next successful `pullSalesFromCloud` erased the return. Worse than a lost sale: `_restoreStockForSale` had already put the piece back on the shelf and `_gcIssueReturnCredit` may already have handed the customer a printed credit — so the money came back and the record of it did not. **Reproduced against the committed build** before changing anything.
  - **Fix, in the order that matters:** `_outboxAdd` for the return record **before** the attempt (the same thing `addSaleRecord`, `_recordScrapGain`, `_recordRepairIncome`, the giveaway and `_recordGiftBreakage` all do), then the push inside a `try/catch`. A throw must not abort what follows: the record and the queue entry are already safe, the credit note may already exist, and **the piece still has to go back on the shelf** — previously a throw propagated out of `processReturn`, skipping the restock entirely and leaving `_returnDlgConfirm` to report a plain failure for a return that had in fact half happened.
  - **The ORIGINAL record is queued too when a debt was cancelled.** `processReturn` mutates `orig.owedAmt`/`orig.owedLog` in place (v12.78), and that lives on its own record path — without queuing it the money owed would quietly come back on the next pull. `_undoReturn` got the mirror of the same two gaps: the restored debt on the original, and the cancelled credit card.
  - **Same gap on `/jewelprice_giftcards`, and it is the half that invents money.** `_gcIssueReturnCredit` and `_gcApplyRedemption` both saved locally and pushed with nothing queued — so a credit issued offline could vanish, and a certificate drawn down offline could **come back showing a balance that had already been spent**. Both now queue the one card they changed and swallow a throw.
  - **The caller is told the truth**: `processReturn`/`processReturnGroup` return `synced`, and `_returnDlgConfirm` shows «⏳ Επιστροφή καταχωρήθηκε — θα συγχρονιστεί…» instead of a clean tick that implies the other phones already know. Tested `=== false`, so a legacy caller with no such key still reads as OK.
  - **Verified by driving the real functions in a browser** (server on `--directory` — note a bare `python -m http.server` run from a worktree can silently serve the parent repo, which cost a round of convincingly wrong results): with the push **throwing**, `processReturn` no longer throws, the record is in the list, the outbox holds a `put` whose JSON is the return, the piece is restocked, and `synced` is false; with it returning **false**, the same; on a **good** connection everything behaves as before. The credit path queues **both** nodes; a **3-line group** queues 3 sales + 1 card and re-queues every line **after** the card number is stamped on it. `_undoReturn` queues the `del` plus the cancelled card. With the **real** `_syncListToCloud` and a stubbed `fetch`: a landed write **clears** the entry (no debris on a good connection), a failed write keeps it, and the cold-start-no-baseline path makes one GET and queues nothing of its own — the gap this change closes, shown directly.
  - **Regression: byte-identical on a good connection.** Seven cases (cash-full-restock, credit-full-restock, partial-no-restock, gift-certificate-part-paid, split payment, trade-in, owed debt) run on the patched build and on the committed build with the clock and the device salt frozen produce the **same SHA-256** (`bc79681e…`) over the return record, the mutated original, the issued card, the credit amount and the inventory writes; the 3-line group return likewise (`d95e6ae0…`). v11.46 re-checked: a full restocked return nets to **zero** profit, a half refund of an unrestocked piece books the whole ex-VAT half as the loss, and `_undoReturn` removes the record, cancels the card and re-sells the piece.
- **Metal kept from your own piece (v13.07):** a customer bought a bracelet and asked for it shortened; the 1.2g of 14K that came off is the owner's, and goes to his scrap. Until now the only way to file it was **Add Metal** with a "price paid", and **whatever he entered there was wrong**: enter the metal's value and the gain is €0 — but taking grams back out of a lot books nothing either (v11.72: the piece made from it is priced on its own), so that value would never appear as profit **anywhere**; leave it blank and the gate `if (paid > 0 && meltNow > 0)` skipped the gain entirely, same result. Measured on his real case: ~€86 silently missing from the day.
  - **New "♻️ Κρατήθηκε από κόσμημα" / "Kept from a piece" toggle** in the Add Metal modal (add mode only, gold **and** silver — the whole path reads `spec.metal`, so silver needed nothing extra). Pressing it **hides the price field** (and clears anything typed in it) rather than leaving it alongside — two controls answering one question is how a dialog ends up half-answered ([[consistent-ui-patterns]], the v12.68 split-payment rule). Built on the shared `_optToggleHtml`/`_isOptBtn` pattern; its own handler is attached after insertion (`.onclick` wins over the helper's inline attribute) so it can also hide the field and redraw the preview, without teaching the shared helper about callers.
  - **The arithmetic is the existing one with a price paid of ZERO**, not a new formula: `_metalAddToLot(spec, paid, noteLbl, opts)` gained `opts.kept`, which only widens the gain gate to `(paid > 0 || kept)`. **Omitted by every other caller**, so the Add Metal price path, the sale trade-in and the gold check are byte-identical — verified (paid €60 → gain €26.90 with no `scrapKept`; blank price → no gain; overpaid → no gain; take and edit modes untouched, no toggle present).
  - **`purchaseCost` is deliberately NOT written** for kept metal — nothing was paid in cash. Verified merging into a bought lot: grams and `initialMelt` rise, `purchaseCost` stays put. The card's gain % is unaffected because `metalStockBaseline` reads `initialMelt`, not `purchaseCost`.
  - **Why the full recoverable value is the right figure to book:** the piece's WHOLE cost (all 16.90g) was charged against its own sale, so the 1.2g has already been expensed. Bringing it in at zero cost and booking `melt − refining loss` restores the true total: `sale profit + gain = 2650 + R − C`. Entering the value as "price paid" instead permanently understates by R.
  - Record carries **`scrapKept: true`**; the sale detail titles it **«♻️ Μέταλλο από Κόσμημα» / "♻️ Metal Kept from a Piece"**, drops the "You paid −€0.00" row and labels the last row **«Αξία που κρατήσατε» / "Value you kept"** (a separate `metalGainKept` key — "Buy gain" reads wrong when nothing was bought). The gain-only rules from v13.00 apply unchanged: no VAT row, no profit %, only Edit and Delete.
  - Verified by driving the real functions at the live spot (€3969.16/oz): 1.2g 14K → 0.702g pure, melt €89.58, recoverable €86.90, gain €86.90, lot created with no `purchaseCost`, record queued to the outbox, preview matching the booked figures to the cent; silver 5g of 925 → 4.625g pure, €8.47; a forced write failure books **nothing** and leaves the modal open; language switch mid-modal keeps the pressed state and relabels; dictionaries in sync (0/0); 375×812 with no clipping or horizontal overflow; console clean.
- **Kept metal is tied to the sale it came off (v13.08):** the owner's first real use of v13.07 — a €2650 bracelet whose profit read **48.3%** when it was really **51.6%**. v13.07 got the money right but left the €86.90 in a **separate record with no link to the sale**, so it landed in the `scrap` segment: out of the jewellery margin by design (v12.74), and invisible to the sale's own percentage.
  - **A "♻️ Κράτησα μέταλλο" button on the SALE**, not another way in from Stock — that is the moment it happens, and the only place the app can know which sale the metal came from. `keptMetalFromSale(saleId)` opens the ordinary Add Metal form already in kept mode (`openMetalModal('add', {kept, saleId})`); `window._metalSaleId` is set on that open **and nulled on every other**, so a link can never leak onto the next unrelated pile. Shown only for a real item sale (`invId||invQr`, not a return, not gain-only) with `can('manage_stock')`, and **gone once booked** so it cannot be recorded twice by accident.
  - **`_linkKeptMetalToSale` stamps BOTH directions** — `keptMetalGain`/`keptMetalGainId` on the sale, `scrapForSaleId` on the gain — queued to the outbox before the push (v11.65). **The sale's amount, cost and profit are never touched** (the owner's explicit choice between the two options offered: link it, don't rewrite the cost). `_saleGainRows` then draws `♻️ Μέταλλο που κρατήσατε` and the combined total beside the existing trade-in and metal-held rows, with **one** total when several apply.
  - **Deleting the gain entry clears the row from the sale** (inside `deleteSaleRecord`, so every entry point is covered) — otherwise the transaction would keep claiming a gain that exists nowhere. Deleting the **sale** deliberately leaves the metal entry alone: the grams are in the safe.
  - **The breakdown treats it as a COST RECOVERY, not a second sale.** `_sbSegment` moves a gain carrying `scrapForSaleId` from `scrap` to `jewelry`, and the row carries `keptLink` so `_sbRender` adds its **profit** to the segment but **not its amount to `knownAmt`**. Without that second half the margin read **49.9%** — the €86.90 landing in the denominator of a jewel whose revenue was already there. **Totals stay whole** (Sales €2,736.90 still agrees with the hero); only the margin's denominator changes.
  - Verified by driving the real functions on his exact transaction: 48.3% → **`Κέρδος €1280.00 (48.3%)` · `♻️ Μέταλλο που κρατήσατε +€86.90` · `Σύνολο €1366.90 (51.6%)`**, and the day's margin **51.6%** to match. A genuine scrap purchase is **untouched** (own activity, jewelry still 48.3%); the two together give jewelry 51.6% + scrap 100%; a legacy unlinked entry still reads 48.3%; deleting the gain clears the row and leaves the sale byte-identical; deleting the sale keeps the metal; the standalone Stock → Add Metal path links nothing and stays `scrap`; group view sums across members and shows trade-in + kept as two rows with one total; button hidden for return / gain-only / no-piece / already-kept / employee; coin segmentation (v12.74) unchanged; 7 buttons wrap to 3 rows at 375px with no clipping; EL + EN; console clean.
- **Metal kept is not a discount (v13.09):** the owner's framing of the same transaction, and he is right — *"the reason I went so low to 2650 is because I held the piece I removed, so in reality I sold the item 2736.87."* The app's total already agreed with him (€1,278.93 + €86.87 = €1,365.80 against €2,736.87 received), but the **discount row did not**: it read **−€558.31 (−17.4%)** when the concession he actually made was **−€471.44 (−14.7%)**. `_saleListPriceRows` gained a 5th arg `kept` and subtracts it before computing the discount, with a dim sub-line «μετά το μέταλλο που κρατήσατε (€86.87)» / "after the metal you kept (€86.87)" under the label (`discAfterKept`; `mkRow` renders the label as HTML, so a `<br><span>` nests cleanly). Both call sites pass it — the single view from `s.keptMetalGain`, the group view summed across members. **Amounts, cost and profit are untouched**; a sale with no kept metal is byte-identical (verified: −€558.31 / −17.4% unchanged).
  - **The alternative was offered and declined:** recording the sale AS €2,736.87 with the €86.87 settled in metal — literally his sentence, and the shape the v12.82 trade-in already implements. He chose to keep the amount at what the customer handed over, so any certificate or quote printed for her states €2,650. *(Correction to the v13.07 entry's reasoning: the claim that the trade-in route "overstates profit by D" was wrong — the trade-in and the v13.08 link produce the identical total. What actually understates is reducing the sale price and carrying the metal at that cost, since a metal lot's basis never resurfaces as profit.)*
  - Verified on his exact record: **−€471.44 (−14.7%)** with the note, `€1278.93 + €86.87 + €113.31 = €1479.11 (55.8%)`, and the same total from his own framing (`2650 + 86.87 − 1257.76`); group view sums list prices and kept metal across members; no-kept regression byte-identical; EL + EN at 375px with no collision (Greek label/value gap 6px) or overflow, dark and light; console clean. *(Testing note: `calcInvItemPrice` is meaningless without the owner's real vendor config loaded — a fixture without it made the held-gain row read −€492.27. Drive the held gain through a frozen `heldCost` instead.)*
- **A percentage on the Total Profit tile (v13.10):** the owner's request — the Sales Breakdown's **Margin** tile carries `51.1%` and the **Total profit** tile beside it carried none, so the two could not be read against each other. The tile now shows the percentage in its `sub` slot (the same slot the Margin tile uses for «μόνο κοσμήματα»), rather than appended to the money — `€3,000.10 (48.1%)` at 17px mono overflows a 169px tile.
  - **The base is EVERY activity whose cost is known (`knownAmtAll`), because the numerator is every activity's profit.** The Margin tile is deliberately jewelry-only (v12.74), and dividing a total that includes the coins by a jewelry-only revenue would overstate it. So when the two differ in basis — i.e. whenever the period splits by activity — the tile's sub says **«συνολικά» / "overall"**, or the pair would look like they answer the same question on the same basis when they do not (the v13.04 rule, one step further). On an ordinary jewelry-only day both tiles share the same base and no caption appears on either.
  - **Nothing else moved**: the Profit and Margin tiles, every per-activity margin and the "Gain on the metal held" section below are untouched, and the row is still absent entirely when the period holds no gain on metal (`_sbHoldShow`).
  - Verified by driving the real `_sbRender` on a fixture reproducing his day (Sales €6,239.07, Profit €2,844.86, jewelry vs coins split, a linked kept-metal gain): **«47.6% συνολικά»**, matching the hand-computed `(2844.86 + 126.79) / 6236.87`. Plus a jewelry-only day showing the bare **«45.0%»** with no caption; a loss rendering **−€350.00 / -35.0%** in `--loss`; a period with no holding gain rendering the original 4 tiles unchanged; and a base of 0 (no cost known anywhere) printing no percentage rather than a dash or `NaN`. At 375px both subs measure 151px in a 169px tile — one line, no clipping, no horizontal overflow, tiles even — in EL and EN; console clean.
- **A group card is a statement about STOCK (v13.11):** the owner filtered Stock to Gold + Coin + **Sold** to review his sold coins, and GC1162 — two Quarter Sovereigns, both sold — rendered as ONE bright card badged **«🔗 2 σε απόθεμα»**, over a «Δες 2 κομμάτια» button that opened nothing («Κανένα διαθέσιμο κομμάτι»). Reproduced against the committed build before changing anything.
  - **Cause:** the fold in `renderInvList` collapsed every member of a `groupId` present in the filtered view, **whatever its status** — but the group card and everything under it are written about pieces on the shelf. The badge says "N in stock"; the price range, the colour breakdown and `_grpAppreciation` are summed over the members shown (that line's own comment already assumes only in-stock pieces, so it was wrong here too); the `🔗 Grouped` pill counts through `_grpLive`, which is `status === 'in_stock'`; and `openInvGroup` lists non-sold members only, which is why the button had nothing to open. Every one of those said "shelf" except the fold.
  - **Fix, at the fold:** a piece whose status is `sold` or `returned` no longer folds — it gets its own card, exactly like an ungrouped one, which is also the card carrying the actions that apply to it (💰 View Sale / 🗑 Delete, and ↩️ back-to-stock for a returned piece). **Tested the negative way** (`status === 'sold' || status === 'returned'`), so a legacy record with no status counts as on the shelf, matching `renderInvSummary` and the status filter. **Display-time only** — the `groupId` stays on the record, so the group re-forms by itself (the v11.82 rule).
  - **It fixes the count in a MIXED view too**, which is the half nobody had reported: 2 on the shelf + 1 sold read **«3 σε απόθεμα»** on the old build *and swallowed the sold piece entirely*, since it was folded into a card whose only action opens in-stock pieces. Now: a group card of 2, plus the sold piece's own card.
  - Verified by driving the real `renderInvList`, A/B against the committed file with the same fixture: old → one bright `inv-group-card` badged «🔗 2 σε απόθεμα» + «Δες 2 κομμάτια» (his screenshot) whose button toasts «Κανένα διαθέσιμο κομμάτι»; new → **three dim `.sold` cards**, «Πουλήθηκε», each with View Sale / Delete. Plus 3-in-stock still folding as one card of 3; mixed 2+1 giving the true 2 and a separate sold card; 1-in-stock + 1-sold unwrapping to two plain cards (the group-of-one rule); a **returned** member standing alone; a status-less legacy record still folding; the `🔗 Grouped` pill returning 0 for an all-sold group (agreeing with what is drawn); EL + EN; no horizontal overflow; console clean.
- **Profit % is measured ON COST, not on the sale (v13.12):** the owner opened a €880 bracelet reading `Profit €417.71 (47.5%)` and said plainly that the shop has never counted it that way — *"if I buy something 100 euro and I sell it 200 we say we sold it with 100% profit, since we made the whole cost price."* He is describing **markup on cost**; the app was showing **margin on revenue**. Both are correct and they answer different questions, but only one is the language spoken in the shop.
  - **The app was already inconsistent, which settles it.** The Calc price breakdown has ALWAYS divided by cost (`profitAfterDiscPct = profitAfterDisc / baseCost`), so the same piece read ~90% on one screen and 47.5% on another. This is not a new convention, it is the sale side catching up with the Calc side.
  - **One helper, seven sites.** `_profitBase()` / `_profitPct(profit, exVat, cost)` / `_profitPctStr(...)` / `_profitPctLabel()` / `_profitPctSub()` replace seven separately-written divisions ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]): the single sale sheet, the transaction sheet, `_saleGainRows`' combined-total row (which took a 6th `baseCost` arg so its percentage keeps sharing the Profit row's denominator, the v13.04 rule), the breakdown's per-activity margin, its headline Margin tile, its Total-profit tile, and its per-transaction table. They cannot drift apart again.
  - **The two guard rules were kept APART on purpose.** The sale sheet has always required a **positive** base (so a return shows its money and no percentage) while the breakdown table allowed any **non-zero** base (so a return mirrors the sale it reverses). A single rule looked tidier and changed 28 of 420 cases; `_profitPctStr` therefore carries the stricter rule and `_profitPct` the looser one. **With the setting on "on sale" the result is byte-identical to before: 420 of 420 cases, and the owner's own sale reproduces 47.5% / 51.9% exactly.**
  - **Display only.** The profit in euros is computed by `_saleExVat` at sale time and stored; nothing here touches it, nothing is recomputed, and no record is rewritten. Sales, Cost and Profit totals are unchanged in both bases — only the percentages and the tile's LABEL move.
  - **The label follows the basis**, because a tile headed «Περιθώριο» / "Margin" showing 90% is the wrong *word*, not merely a bigger number: «Κέρδος %» / "Markup" with a «στο κόστος» / "on cost" caption. On the breakdown tile that caption sits under the existing "jewelry only" line (measured at 375px: 151px in a 169px tile, two lines, no clipping, both tiles in the row equal height).
  - **A setting, not a silent switch** ([[prefers-self-service-controls]]): Settings → Tax & Margin → «Το κέρδος % υπολογίζεται», **defaulting to on-cost**. Read through `_profitBase()` and never off `state` directly — an existing device's saved `jpro_state` predates the key, so a bare read would be `undefined` (the v12.14 lesson); a junk value also falls back to `'cost'` rather than becoming a third basis. Cloud-synced via `cloudConfig`/`applyCloudConfig`, so all three phones agree.
  - **The CSV carries BOTH columns, always, whatever the setting says** — «Περιθώριο %» for the accountant, «Κέρδος % (κόστος)» for the owner. Verified the exported file is byte-identical under either setting, so his display preference can never reach the accountant's spreadsheet.
  - **The denominator excludes what it should.** In the breakdown a new `knownCost` accumulator mirrors `knownAmt` (cost summed only over rows whose profit is known), so a sale with no recorded cost still adds nothing to either side. The v13.08 kept-metal `keptLink` exclusion needs **no special case** on the cost basis: such an entry carries no cost, so it stays out of that denominator by itself.
  - Verified by driving the real functions: his exact sale renders `€417.71 (90.4%)` → `Σύνολο €457.06 (98.9%)`; his own 100→200 example returns exactly 100.0%; a mixed jewelry+coin day gives jewelry 90.4% / coins 6.9% with the money totals untouched; a manual sale with no cost shows `—` and is excluded; a giveaway reads −100%; a sale and its return net to zero with `—`; the Settings control loads, saves, persists to localStorage, round-trips through the cloud and relabels on a language switch; dictionaries in sync (0/0); no clipping or horizontal overflow at 375px in EL or EN; console clean. *(Screenshots were unavailable in this session's browser pane, so layout was confirmed by measuring `scrollWidth`/`getBoundingClientRect`, not by eye.)*
- **A “temporary” role — counter cover for a few days (v13.13):** the owner’s employee was away and a friend was standing in, so he asked for a user with **less** access than an employee: **Calc, Stock and Vendors only**, and inside Stock **no sold pieces, no bullion coins, no scrap metal**. New role **`temporary`**, added to `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` with every permission false.
  - **Two separate ideas, deliberately.** *What you may DO* stays in `ROLE_PERMISSIONS`; *which SCREENS you may open* is a different question, and inventing a permission per tab (`sales`, `clients`, `desk`, `find`, `about`…) would have been five names carrying no meaning beyond “this tab”. So a second table states it directly: **`ROLE_PANELS`** (a role listed there may open ONLY the panels named; a role that is not listed — owner, manager, employee — is unaffected) plus **`canOpenPanel(name)`**. The whole navigation definition of the role is one line: `temporary: ['calc', 'inventory', 'vendors']`.
  - **Hiding a nav button is presentation; the guard is in `showPanel`** (`if (!canOpenPanel(name)) name = 'calc'`). `jpro_lastPanel` is restored on open, deep links call in from outside, and `applyRoleUI`’s rescue only ever covered three panels — so without it a limited role could be put on a screen it does not have. That rescue now asks `canOpenPanel` too, so someone standing on Sales who is demoted mid-session is moved to Calc.
  - **One new permission, `stock_full`** — “see the whole stock: sold pieces, bullion coins, raw/scrap metal”. **true for owner, manager AND employee**, so nothing about the existing roles moved (verified: an employee’s tab set, status pills and search results are byte-identical). Only `temporary` has it false. Coins and scrap were *already* stripped from the Type filter for anyone without `manage_stock` (v10.51), so this adds the sold/returned half and closes the gaps.
  - **Three places, because a filter is not a guard.** The filter sheet stops offering the **Sold / Returned** pills; `renderInvList` **refuses** a sold or returned piece outright; and the coin/metal list-drop becomes unconditional — for this role a **search and a notification are not exceptions**, which is the difference between a default and a guard. `renderInvSummary` stops counting them as pieces for the same reason.
  - **A role name can arrive in a URL**, and `ROLE_PERMISSIONS['constructor']` is truthy. `isKnownRole(r)` asks `hasOwnProperty`, and both the invite link and the `?role=` parameter go through it — anything unknown falls back to `employee` rather than being honoured (verified: `bogus` and `constructor` both land on `employee`). `shareUserLink` previously collapsed anything that was not owner/manager to `employee`, which would have silently invited a temporary as a full employee.
  - **A real hole closed in passing:** the sale detail’s `canEdit` was `creds.role !== 'employee' || …`, i.e. gated on the role STRING — so **every role that is not literally ‘employee’ passed**, and a temporary would have been able to edit or delete anyone’s sale. Now `can('sales_admin') || s.employee === creds.userName`. **Proven identical for owner, manager and employee** across own/others’ sales; the single changed cell is the one being fixed.
  - A temporary still does the full counter job: the Stock card offers the **identical** action set as an employee (Calc, Share, Reserve, Gift, Sell, Print Tag) — verified string-equal. *(They can create a hold but cannot open Desk to manage it; the owner sees it there. Flagged, not changed.)*
  - Assignable by the **owner** (any role) and by a **manager** (employee or temporary only — `canAssignRole`), from the same 3-way picker, which is now 4-way. Labels: «⏱️ Προσωρινός» / “⏱️ Temporary”. When the friend leaves, ⏸ **deactivate** — never delete (that would orphan their sales, v11.33).
  - Verified by driving the real functions: the full 4×11 role×tab matrix; every forbidden panel redirecting to Calc; the demote-while-standing-on-Sales rescue; a real `?role=temporary` setup link resolving end to end with only three tabs visible; the Stock matrix (default / sold pill forced / all statuses forced / searching a coin, scrap and a sold piece — **all empty for temporary, all unchanged for employee**); the filter sheet’s status pills and type options per role; the invite link’s `role=` for six inputs; `_roleChoices` in EL and EN; every panel opened as every role with **zero** JS errors; dictionaries in sync (0/0); the 2-button bottom nav sharing the width evenly at 375px with no overflow and only Vendors left in ☰ More.
- **A by-the-gram chain can be given away too (v13.14):** the owner wanted to hand over 1.5 g of a silver chain as a gift and found the 🎁 button only on discrete pieces — `buildCard` excluded `item.isBulk`, and `invGiveaway` had no way to ask for a quantity. So a cut of chain had to be recorded as a €0 *sale*, which is a different thing, or left out of the books entirely (and the grams then read as missing at the end-of-season stocktake).
  - **ONE gift dialog, which asks for grams when the piece is a batch** — not a second gift flow beside the bulk sell sheet ([[fix-the-pattern-not-the-instance]]). `invGiveaway` renders a weight field for `item.isBulk` (max = the remaining weight), and the customer block, the note and the cost box are the ones every other gift already uses.
  - **A batch is a QUANTITY, not a piece**, so what the gift costs is the cost of the GRAMS handed over, never of the whole remaining batch: `_invGiveawayCost(item, grams)` routes a bulk item through **`_bulkSoldCost`** — literally the same helper the by-the-gram SALE uses, so a cut of chain can never be costed two different ways. It returns **null** when it cannot be worked out (no spot price) and the box says «— άγνωστο» rather than showing a fabricated €0.00. New `_giftCostText` is shared by the first paint and the live update as grams are typed (`_invGiveawayGrams`), so the figure on screen and the figure booked are one function.
  - **The batch is not "sold".** The bulk branch deducts the grams (`weight -= grams`) and leaves `status`/`dateSold` alone and writes no `giveaway` flag on the item — only some grams left, exactly as `confirmBulkSell` does it. The record carries **`bulkInvId` + `bulkSoldWeight`** (and no `invId`), which is the proven shape of an ordinary by-the-gram sale, so **deleting the record puts the grams back** through the shared `_restoreStockForSale` with no code of its own. Verified the permanent **Sale Link Check** does not flag it: `_saleLinkBadRecords` resolves such a record's own identity to the batch, which `isBulk`, and returns.
  - Guards run **before** anything is read or written and leave the dialog open on a refusal so the number can simply be corrected (blank / 0 / more than the batch holds → 0 writes, 0 records). A failed inventory write **rolls the weight back** and says so ([[verify-dont-assume-in-live-app]], the v11.62 lesson); the record is queued to the outbox before the push, so an offline gift is not lost (v11.65).
  - The money model is unchanged and inherited: €0 revenue, `cost` = the cut's cost, `profit = −cost`, `giveaway: true` — so the day's takings do not move and the period profit falls by exactly what the gift cost. It lands in the **`gift`** activity of the Sales Breakdown (`_sbSegment` reads `giveaway` before the coin check), shows the 🎁 GIFT tag in the list, and appears in the customer's history when a name is given.
  - **Fixed in passing (visible in the owner's own screenshot): the duplicated unit.** `bulkRemaining` already carries the unit («γρ. υπόλοιπο» / "g remaining") and all three call sites prepended another `g`, so the sell sheet, the stock card and the restock toast read «46.5g γρ. υπόλοιπο» / "46.5g g remaining". Now "46.5 g remaining".
  - Verified by driving the real functions on a 46.5 g / 925 batch with the live silver price: 1.5 g costs **€6.46 and equals `_bulkSoldCost` exactly**; the cost box tracks 1.5 → 10 → 0 → blank → 46.5 g live; the gift books €0 / cost 6.46 / profit −6.46 with the batch at **45.0 g, still `in_stock`, no giveaway flag**, and deleting restores it to 46.5 g («1.5g επαναφέρθηκαν στην παρτίδα»); all three refusals write nothing; a forced write failure leaves the batch at 46.5 g with nothing booked. **The discrete-piece path is byte-identical** (no weight field, ref-code subtitle, €40 from `purchaseCost` whether or not the new argument is passed, record with `invId`/`seqRef` and no `bulkInvId`, status `sold`). EL + EN, no overflow at 375px, the button reachable at a 420px-tall viewport (keyboard up), console clean.
  - **Flagged, pre-existing, NOT changed:** a €0 gift's detail sheet offers Return / Exchange card / Kept-metal buttons, which are meaningless on a gift. Confirmed identical on the discrete gift (v12.16), so it predates this change; a "return" of a €0 sale refunds €0, so no credit can be created. The owner's call whether to hide them.
- **Switch user on a shared device, with a per-person PIN (v13.15):** the owner left his phone at home and worked the counter on the desktop, and asked whether users could be PIN-protected and switched quickly instead of re-installing the app per person. He had assumed separate installations kept people out of each other's data. **They never did:** `shareUserLink` puts the master DB secret in every setup link (`if (creds.dbSecret) url += '&secret='`), so any installed device already holds the key to the whole database — what actually hides costs and vendors is the app's own role table, not the installation. So switching inside one app is **exactly as safe as installing it twice**, which is what made the feature worth building rather than a compromise.
