  - **The switch itself is nearly free**, and that is a property worth preserving: all ~84 places that record "who did this" read `creds.userName`/`creds.displayName` **live, at the moment of use** — nothing caches the identity at startup. So `_applyUserIdentity(slug, u)` is four fields plus `applyRoleUI()`, which already hides the right tabs and already moves someone off a panel their new role may not open (verified: switching to a `temporary` user leaves exactly Calc/Stock/Vendors).
  - **NEVER reset on a switch** — these belong to the DEVICE, not the person, and the comment above the module says so: `jpro_device_salt` (record-id uniqueness, v11.64), `jpro_outbox` (unsent offline work, v11.65), `_cloudSnap` (this device's cloud baseline, v12.99), and `creds.binId`/`creds.dbSecret` (same shop). Verified untouched across a switch.
  - **PINs are stored HASHED** (`pinHash`, SHA-256 via the existing `_sha256Hex`) on `/jewelprice_users/<slug>` — **because that node is in `BACKUP_PATHS`**, so a plain PIN would sit in every JSON backup on the owner's Google Drive. Four digits is not cryptography (10,000 guesses) and a device with the DB secret can read everything anyway; it stops a casual switch at the counter, which is the actual threat, and nothing in the UI claims more.
  - **One PIN overlay, two kinds of PIN.** `openPinOverlay(storedPin, onSuccess, opts)` gained `opts.hash` (compare against a hash, making `confirmPinOverlay` async), `opts.title` and `opts.onCancel`; all three are **reset on every open** so an Owner-mode prompt can never inherit a hash left by a switch (verified: the plain owner PIN still works and `_pinStoredHash` is `''`). The async compare re-checks `_pinCallback` after the await, so a closed-and-reopened overlay can't fire a stale callback. The overlay is z-index **9999** — far below the sheet — so `_switchToUser` closes the sheet first and reopens it on cancel (the v11.44 precedent).
  - **Optional per person, blank = no PIN** (owner's choice). The field is `type="text"` + `.masked-field`, **never `type="password"`** (v12.02). It opens blank because a hash cannot be shown back, so the form has three states to make legible: no PIN / has one (kept unless you type) / being removed by this save (`_pinFormRemove`, `_syncUserPinUI`).
  - **The v12.75 trap, twice.** `saveUser` rebuilds `entry` from scratch, so `pinHash` had to be carried forward explicitly or every edit would silently drop it. **`pushSelfToUsersCloud` had the same shape and was already dropping `telegramChatId`** — fixed by spreading `existing` first. Both verified: editing a name keeps the hash, editing a bonus keeps hash + Telegram id.
  - **Auto-return to a "home" user** (owner's choice over "stay on the last person"): without it, checking a cost and walking away files the next sale under the wrong name, the wrong leaderboard and the wrong bonus. Per **device** (`jpro_home_user`, `jpro_home_idle`, default 5 min, `0` = never), set from the sheet itself; unset = the feature is simply off, which is every phone. It fires on idle **and** on app open (from `autoSync`, after `syncIdentityFromCloud`, because it needs the users list), and going **down** to the home user never asks for a PIN — it is climbing INTO an account that is gated. A home user who has since been deactivated is ignored rather than stranding the device.
  - **`_dialogBusy(ids)` — a guard that had never once fired.** The auto-return must not change the salesperson mid-transaction, so it reuses `_refreshSharedLists`' busy check… which tests `el.offsetParent !== null`, and **every dialog in this app is `position:fixed`, whose `offsetParent` is null whether it is visible or not**. Measured: an open `#saleModal` reports `offsetParent` null with a **1265x720** rect, a closed one **0x0** — the rect is what tells them apart. Extracted into one helper used by both (`_refreshSharedLists`' guard was dead since v11.64 — a real pre-existing bug, less harmful there). Verified: shared-list refresh now runs when idle and stops when a sale dialog is open; the user switch is blocked by an open sale/return/gift/repair dialog, by the PIN overlay and by the switch sheet itself.
  - **The ☰ menu entry doubles as the "who is this app right now" indicator** (`#moreCurrentUser`, filled by `renderIdentityCard`, which `setLang` now calls so the name follows the language) — on a shared counter machine that is the question people actually need answered.
  - Verified by driving the real functions with a stubbed users node: the sheet lists active users only (a deactivated one is excluded and `_switchToUser` refuses it), marks the current user, flags PIN'd ones with 🔒; a wrong PIN refuses and stays put, the right one switches and grants the owner tabs, landing on Calc; a PIN-less switch goes straight through; cancel reopens the sheet; `saveUser` hashes (the plain PIN appears nowhere in the node), carries forward, removes, and refuses a non-4-digit PIN **writing nothing**; all four home-box controls driven by real clicks with the idle default/junk/`0` cases; all 11 panels open with **zero** errors; dictionaries in sync (824/824, 0/0). Layout at 375px: no horizontal overflow, 55–57px rows, the ☰ sub-line on its own line, nothing clipped; contrast measured in **both** themes (the explanatory line was `--text-dim` at 2.81/3.25 and is now `--text-muted` at 5.95/6.26 — the v13.03 rule). *(Screenshots were unavailable in this session's browser pane, so layout was confirmed by measuring rects, not by eye.)*
- **The Clients tab states how many clients there are (v13.16):** the owner asked to see the total number of clients somewhere on the tab. It was only ever visible as a side effect of filtering — the one summary line above the list appeared **solely when a date range was active**, and even then it counted the FILTERED list, so "how many clients do I have" had no answer on screen.
  - **The header badge is the whole database and nothing narrows it** (`#clientTotalCount`, next to the «👥 Πελάτες» title) — that is the question being asked, and a count that shrank while searching would answer a different one. It is set from `getClients()` **before every early return** in `renderClients`, so an empty search result and an empty database both still state the real total.
  - **It is a SIBLING of the title, never a child**: `applyTranslations` sets `el.textContent` for every `[data-i18n]` element, so a span nested inside the title would be wiped on the first language switch.
  - **The list summary now answers the other question** — how much of the total you are looking at — so it reads `4 από 7 πελάτες` / `4 of 7 clients` and appears for a **search** as well as a date range (it previously ignored the search box entirely).
  - **`_clientsWord(n)`** is the one place both get their plural, so the badge and the summary cannot disagree; it replaced the inline double-ternary the summary carried.
  - **`setLang` now re-renders the Clients panel** alongside vendors/sync/inventory. Pre-existing and worth knowing: the client CARDS carry Greek/English text («Προστέθηκε»/«Added») and were already going stale on a language switch — this fixes the count word and the cards together.
  - Verified by driving the real `renderClients` in a browser on a 7-client fixture: badge `7 clients` / «7 πελάτες», unchanged by a search (`4 of 7 clients`), by a date range (same), by a no-match search (`No results found` with the badge still `7 clients`) and on an empty database (`0 clients`); singular at 1 in both languages; a real `setLang` switch relabels it. Contrast measured **6.31 dark / 5.80 light** (`--text-muted`, the v13.03 rule); at 375px the count sits at x=129–193 with the + ADD button at 275 — no overlap, no horizontal overflow. Screenshots confirm both themes and the filtered state.
- **The metal price you PAID, not today's (v13.17):** the owner ordered from Italy with gold at €128.40/g of pure; the parcel landed weeks later with gold at €122.82 and he added the pieces then. The selling price should follow today's spot — it does, and must — but what the pieces COST him is the July invoice, and the app had no way to be told that. Everything it freezes at add time (`initialRetail`, `initialMetalValue` and, since v13.02, **`initialBaseCost`**) was captured at the live spot, lazily, on the piece's first card render.
  - **It is a MONEY bug, not a cosmetic one, and v13.02 is why.** `initialBaseCost` is stamped onto the sale as `heldCost`, and the sale then reports `profit = sale − replacement cost` + `metal gain = replacement cost − heldCost`, totalling `sale − what you paid`. With `heldCost` sitting at today's *replacement* cost the metal gain reads **€0.00** and the total overstates by the whole difference. Driven on his exact ring: total €375.96 before, **€358.77** after, which is ex-VAT €793.70 − €434.93 paid, exactly. So the machinery was already there; it was being fed the wrong number.
  - **`calcInvItemPrice(item, basis)`** gained an optional second argument — `{goldOz, silverOz}` — used ONLY by the four spot reads in the gold/silver jewelry path (primary + secondary metal). Omitted by every existing caller, so the default path is unchanged: **proven by A/B against the committed build on the owner's real 25/07 backup — all 1286 items, 7 price fields each, identical SHA-256** (`ff772d5d…`). Coins and raw metal return before it and never see it.
  - **The fix is applied AFTER adding, not before** (owner's choice when asked). A sticky "shipment basis" set before entering a delivery would automate it, but it stays switched on until cleared and would silently mis-cost next month's stock — and it could not fix the pieces he had *already* added that morning. So: add the delivery as usual, select it in Stock → **⋯ → «Τιμή μετάλλου που πλήρωσα»** → state the invoice price in **€ per gram of PURE metal** (the same €/g the Calc breakdown already shows beside the spot, and how an Italian supplier bills *oro fino*).
  - **Recomputed from the piece's own weight/karat/vendor at that price, never scaled** — so applying it twice, or correcting a mistyped price, lands on the same numbers (verified idempotent). `paidMetalGram`/`paidMetalAt` are stored for audit and prefill the field next time. NOT in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` — it is a cost fact.
  - **`_paidBasisSkip(item)` is deliberately the same shape as `_itemHeldCost`'s rule**: only a piece whose cost the app RECOMPUTES from metal has a gap to correct. A **per-item** category and a stored **`purchaseCost`** are euro figures off the invoice already (and a per-item piece's `initialRetail` is the *typed target retail*, which must never be recomputed); a **sold** piece's baseline is frozen on its SALE record, where editing the item would fix nothing; coins, raw metal, bulk and other-type are excluded. The skipped count is **named on screen**, never silently dropped.
  - **The preview is the confirmation.** `_paidBasisSync` prices every eligible piece twice as you type — «12 κομμάτια · κόστος σήμερα €47,581.96 / Όσο πλήρωσα €49,305.17 / +€1,723.21» — and the sum matched the applied result to the cent. Blank → Apply disabled and **0 writes**. Owner/manager only (`can('see_cost')`), so the ⋯ row does not render for an employee.
  - Writes go through the existing **`_consignApply`**, so each is checked and **rolled back on failure** (v11.62): a forced failure on the 4th of 12 left that piece byte-for-byte as it was with no basis stamped, the other 11 applied, and the toast reported `11 · failed: 1`. Thousands separators are built inline from the dialog's own `cur` — **not `fmtAmt`, which reads `state.currencySymbol`, a different setting** (v13.04).
  - Verified by driving the real functions on the real backup and on his exact ring: the fixture reproduces the screenshot byte for byte (metal €354.21 + wastage €24.09, subtotal €417.74, pre-VAT €793.70, final €928.63); at €128.40/g the cost becomes **€434.93** and the card's gain line honestly reads **−4.0%**, because gold has fallen since he bought. The full eligibility matrix, the silver field appearing only when silver is present (hint at 3dp), Greek singular/plural, EL + EN, light + dark (gain figure 5.74:1), no overflow at 375px, 826/826 dictionary keys in sync, console clean.
  - **Note for next time:** his €435.07 implies ~€128.44/g, not the €128.40 he quoted — the exact invoice figure is what should be typed.
- About/Info page with the Lekkas Jewelry SVG logo, in-app Feature List (inline), a link to the full User Manual (`manual.htm`), and the current-version Changelog with a link to full history (`changelog.htm`) — currently at v13.17

> Note: the in-app "About" version badge derives from the `APP_VERSION` constant (currently `'13.17'`), not the stale "2.9" hardcoded fallback in the markup. The canonical version to bump is `APP_VERSION` plus the `changelog` arrays (both `el` and `en`) in the `ABOUT` object. The green update banner also reads those same `changelog` arrays, so a normal version bump keeps everything in sync.

> **Changelog "current entry inline, full history in `changelog.htm`" rule (v10.66+):** the inline `changelog` arrays in `jewelprice.htm` (both `el` and `en`) now hold **only the current `APP_VERSION` entry** — one entry each. The update banner (`checkForUpdate`) and the Info tab both read that single inline entry, so it MUST stay. The **full bilingual history lives in the separate `changelog.htm` page** (see below). **Per version bump:** (1) replace the single inline entry in BOTH language arrays with the new version's entry, (2) prepend the same entry to the **top** of `changelog.htm`'s embedded `CHANGELOG` array (newest-first; never trim it) — note the shape there differs from the inline one: `{version, date_el, items_el:[…], date_en, items_en:[…]}` (the compact array is one line in the page's `<script>`). This keeps `jewelprice.htm` flat forever while preserving all history. (History up to the switch was recovered from git — the repo's changelog spans v9.29→present; the earlier "keep 10" trimming had dropped older entries from the file but they remained in git history.)

## Owner preferences
- Clean, professional UI. **No emojis in printed documents** (quotes, labels).
- Keep changes minimal and targeted — this is a production app used in a live store.
- Increment the version number and update the in-app changelog with every user-visible change.
- Explain changes in plain language; the owner is not a professional developer.

## Workflow rules
- At the start of every session, if working in a local folder, run `git pull` to get the latest from GitHub before reading or changing anything.
- After completing a change, summarize what was modified before committing.
- Use clear, descriptive commit messages.
- Push to GitHub only when asked (GitHub Pages deploys automatically from main).
- **Update this CLAUDE.md as part of EVERY change**, in the same commit as the code. Whenever `jewelprice.htm` changes in a user-visible way, also: (1) bump the version reference(s) here to match the new `APP_VERSION`, and (2) if the change adds, removes, or meaningfully alters a feature, update the "Key features" section so it stays accurate. Do not treat CLAUDE.md as a separate follow-up task — it ships with the change.

## Deployment notes
- The live app is served by **GitHub Pages** (custom domain `www.lekkasjewelry.com`, preserved by the root `CNAME` file), and deploys automatically on every push to `main`.
- Deployment runs via a custom GitHub Actions workflow: **`.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml`**. It builds the site and **retries the Pages deploy up to 3 times** (with waits in between), so GitHub's occasional transient `Deployment failed, try again later` hiccup self-heals instead of turning the deploy red. This requires **Settings → Pages → Build and deployment → Source = "GitHub Actions"** (not "Deploy from a branch"); do not switch it back, or the retry protection is lost.
- You can also trigger a deploy by hand: **Actions** tab → "Deploy to GitHub Pages" → **Run workflow**.
- If all 3 attempts ever fail (a sustained GitHub Pages outage), just re-run the workflow later from the Actions tab.
- After a successful deploy, allow ~1–2 min for the CDN to update; the app's own green "new version" banner confirms the new `APP_VERSION` is live.

## Public website & data security
Alongside the private pricing app (`jewelprice.htm`) the repo also serves a small **public website** on the same GitHub Pages site:
- `index.html` — public landing page (hero, hours, contact, links to the showroom).
- `showroom.htm` — password-gated "Private Showroom": customers browse in-stock pieces (photo, description, type, metal, stones), select favourites, and send an inquiry via EmailJS. No prices shown. **Stone-name line on the grid card (showroom-only, no `APP_VERSION` bump):** every card shows its distinct stones as an elegant italic gold serif line under the name (Option A of the mock the owner picked) — NAME only, translated; the full carat/clarity stays inside on the lightbox detail. Helpers `cardStones(desc)` (aggregates by type across ` · ` **or** comma separators, parsing `N×`/`melee` prefixes and stripping carats) and `stoneLabelN(type, plural)` (`STONE_PLURAL_EN`/`STONE_PLURAL_EL` maps — plural shown when a stone is **melee or totals 2+**, e.g. "Diamonds" / «Διαμάντια», "Rubies" / «Ρουμπίνια»; a lone stone stays singular). Rendered in `renderGrid` as `.card-stone-line`; the font auto-shrinks (16→12px by joined length) and the line wraps (joined with spaced ` · ` so there are break points), so a piece with many stones stays compact and cards stay symmetric. `parseStonesFromDesc` (the stone FILTER) was also made comma-tolerant so multi-stone "other" items (chip-picker saves comma-joined) filter correctly. Verified in-browser EL+EN: plurals correct, melee→plural, no clipping, cards equal height. The item lightbox has **image zoom** (`_lbInitZoom`, called from `openLightbox`): desktop (fine pointer + hover) hovers to zoom and moves the cursor to pan; touch taps to zoom in centred on the tap, drags to pan, taps again to reset — for inspecting tiny pieces like children's bracelets. Purely `transform: scale()` + `transform-origin` on `.lb-img` inside an `overflow:hidden` `.lb-zoom` wrapper; showroom-only, no `APP_VERSION` bump.
- `item.htm` — single-item "view item" page opened from a QR/`?qr=` link (used in inquiry emails and physical tags). **Bilingual (EL/EN)** with a header language toggle: reads `localStorage 'jpro_lang'` (else browser language), persists on toggle, and re-renders the loaded item (`_item`/`_qr`) in the chosen language via `T`/`METAL_L`/`GENDER_L`/`TYPE_L` maps + `setLang(l)`. Reads `descEl`/`descEn` from the public feed. Jewelry-type labels mirror `INV_JEWELRY_TYPES`.
- `quote.htm` — standalone quote view. **Effectively unused:** quotes/certificates/orders/catalogs are all delivered as a **PDF** (`shareQuoteAsPDF`); the `viewerUrl` built in `shareQuote` is never shared (both `openQuoteWindow` callers pass `null`) and serves only as an "is Firebase configured?" guard for the history save. The DB secret was removed from that URL in v11.36 — an unused URL is no place for a master key — but this fixed no real leak, and **`/jewelprice_quotes_html` must stay PRIVATE** (a briefly-published world-readable rule was reverted the same day; it exposed stored quote HTML for a link that does not exist). Removing the dead quote-link machinery is a pending cleanup — see `SECURITY_PASS.md`. The quote **content** is baked HTML already in the language it was created in; only the viewer **chrome** (loading/error text, Share/Save-PDF buttons) is translated, auto-detected from `localStorage 'jpro_lang'`/browser language (`QT`/`qt()`, no toggle — the content language is fixed). `buildFallbackCard` is dead code.
- `changelog.htm` — standalone bilingual version history (v10.66+). Static — the changelog data is embedded in the page; reads no Firebase node, so it has no data-security surface.
- `manual.htm` — standalone bilingual User Manual (v10.68+). Static accordion; content embedded in the page (`MANUAL` object); reads no Firebase node.

**NEVER put a key, token or secret in `jewelprice.htm` (v11.85).** The file is served publicly from GitHub Pages — anyone can read every byte of it. The OpenRouter API key was hardcoded in **three** places as `atob('c2stb3It…')`; base64 is an *encoding*, not encryption, so the key was readable (and spendable) by anyone who opened the page source, and it was live on the public site. It now lives in the private Firebase config under `aiKey`, entered once via the **Sync tab → OpenRouter API Key** field (`saveAiKey`, which validates against `GET /api/v1/key` before storing, so a mistyped or revoked key can't be saved silently) and read at call time through the single helper **`_aiKey()`**. The three call sites `throw new Error('no-ai-key')` when it is absent, which their existing `catch` turns into the ordinary local-table fallback. Same pattern as the Telegram bot token. **If a future feature needs a credential, it goes in the config — never in the file.**

**Security model (important — do not regress):**
- These public pages must **never** carry the Firebase legacy **master DB secret** (it grants full read/write to the entire database) and must **never** read the private `/jewelprice_inventory` node (it contains cost, margin, vendor and supplier fields).
- Instead the app maintains a separate, **display-only public mirror** at Firebase node **`/jewelprice_public`**, containing only the safe fields in `PUBLIC_SAFE_FIELDS` (qrCode, descriptions, jewelryType, refType, metal, karat, goldColor, weight, gender, alsoGenders, handmade, stonesDesc, seqId, photoUrl, photoUrlHi, status, dateAdded). `jewelprice.htm` keeps it in sync automatically via `syncPublicItem` / `syncPublicDelete` (on every inventory save/delete) and a once-per-session self-healing `republishPublicShowroom()` (called from `renderInvList`). Only in-stock, non-bulk items are published (`publicProjection`).
- **Firebase Realtime Database rules** must keep everything private by default and expose **only** the two display nodes as world-readable, read-only:
  ```json
  { "rules": { ".read": false, ".write": false,
      "jewelprice_public": { ".read": true, ".write": false },
      "jewelprice_showroom_config": { ".read": true, ".write": false } } }
  ```
  (The app itself still reads/writes everything because it authenticates with the DB secret, which bypasses rules.) `jewelprice_showroom_config` was added in v12.04 and holds **only** `{enabled, hash}` for the showroom gate — never the readable code. The canonical copy of this JSON in code is `_srRulesSnippet()`; keep the two in step.
- `showroom.htm` and `item.htm` read `/jewelprice_public` **without any auth token**. If either ever needs the secret again, that's a security regression.
- Changes to the public website files do **not** bump `APP_VERSION`/changelog — that constant governs the pricing app (`jewelprice.htm`) and its in-app version banner only.

## First-session task
On the first session with this file, read `jewelprice.htm` fully and update the "Key features" section above to match the actual current state of the app, since this document may be behind the code.
